New York contained several husbands who eyed him askance, but could not verify their suspicions, and the apparent hopelessness of ever doing so made them look on Basil as a man who had carried smoothness into the realms of fine art. He was considered too gifted to be wholesome. The men of his set, being for the most part amiably stupid, resented his cleverness.

Bailey, just at present, was feeling strongly on the subject of Basil. He was at that stage of his married life when he would have preferred his Sybil to speak civilly to no other man than himself. And only yesterday Sybil had come to him to inform him with obvious delight that Basil Milbank had invited her to join his yacht party for a lengthy voyage.

This had stung Bailey. He was not included in the invitation. The whole affair struck him as sinister. It was true that Sybil had never shown any sign of being fascinated by Basil; but, he told himself, there was no knowing. He forbade Sybil to accept the invitation. To soothe her disappointment, he sent her off then and there to Tiffany’s with a roving commission to get what she liked; for Bailey, the stern, strong man, the man who knew when to put his foot down, was no tyrant. But he would have been indignant at the suggestion that he had bribed Sybil to refuse Basil’s invitation.

One of the arguments which Sybil had advanced in the brief discussion which had followed the putting down of Bailey’s foot had been that Ruth had been invited and accepted, so why should not she? Bailey had not replied to this—it was at this point of the proceedings that the Tiffany motive had been introduced, but he had not forgotten it. He thought it over, and decided to call upon Ruth. He did so.

===Chinese===
贝利恨巴兹尔。男人照例都恨他，却说不出个所以然。巴兹尔的名声不清不白，却也没坐实过什么劣迹。他是个从未被定罪的嫌疑犯。纽约城里有好几位做丈夫的斜着眼看他，却拿不出证据来坐实自己的疑心，而这疑心眼看着永无坐实之望，便使他们把巴兹尔视为一个把圆滑修炼到纯艺术境界的人物。众人认为他才华过人，过人得有损健康。他那个圈子里的男人大都和蔼可亲而头脑愚钝，对他的聪明怀恨在心。

眼下这个当口，贝利在巴兹尔这个题目上感情格外强烈。他正处在婚姻生活的这样一个阶段：恨不得他的西比尔除了自己之外，对任何别的男人连一句客气话都不要讲。而就在昨天，西比尔来告诉他，说巴兹尔·米尔班克邀她参加他的游艇出游，做一次长途航行——说这话时她那股欢喜劲儿明明白白写在脸上。

这可扎痛了贝利。请客名单里没有他。整件事在他看来透着凶险。诚然，西比尔从未流露过一丝为巴兹尔着迷的迹象；可是他对自己说，人心隔肚皮，谁保得准呢。他禁止西比尔接受这份邀请。为了抚慰她的失望，他当场打发她去蒂芙尼（Tiffany’s），授以全权，喜欢什么买什么。须知贝利这位严峻的强者、这位懂得何时该一脚踩定的汉子，可不是暴君。不过，倘若有人暗示他这是贿赂西比尔去回绝巴兹尔的邀请，他准会勃然大怒。

贝利一脚踩定之后随之而来的那场简短讨论里，西比尔提出的论据之一便是：露丝也在被邀之列，而且已经应了，凭什么她就不行？贝利没有回答——蒂芙尼这一招正是在这个节骨眼上使出来的——但他把这话记在了心里。他翻来覆去想了想，决定去拜访露丝。他去了。

===Original===
It was unfortunate that the nervous strain of being the Napoleon of Wall Street had had the effect of increasing to a marked extent the portentousness of Bailey’s always portentous manner. Ruth rebelled against it. There was an insufferable suggestion of ripe old age and fatherliness in his attitude which she found irritating in the extreme. All her life she had chafed at authority, and now, when Bailey set himself up as one possessing it, she showed the worst side of herself to him.

He struck this unfortunate note from the very beginning.

“Ruth,” he said, “I wish to speak seriously to you.”

Ruth looked at him with hostile eyes, but did not speak. He did not know it, poor man, but he had selected an exceedingly bad moment for his lecture. It so happened that, only half an hour before, she and Kirk had come nearer to open warfare than they had ever come.

It had come about in this way. Kirk had slept badly the night before, and, as he lay awake in the small hours, his conscience had troubled him.

===Chinese===
不幸的是，充当华尔街拿破仑的神经紧张，使贝利那份向来煞有介事的做派愈发煞有介事，达到了显著的地步。露丝对这一套反胃透了。他的态度里有一股德高望重的老者气、慈父气，令人无法忍受，她觉得窝火到了极点。她一辈子都对权威憋气，如今贝利自立为权威在握之人，她便把自己最坏的一面亮给了他。

他一开口就弹错了这个倒霉的调子。

「露丝，」他说，「我有正事要跟你谈。」

露丝敌意地看着他，一言不发。可怜的人，他自己并不知道，他挑来训话的这个时机糟糕透顶。偏偏就在半个钟头之前，她跟柯克刚刚闹到了有史以来最接近公开开战的地步。

事情是这样闹起来的。头天夜里柯克睡得很糟，凌晨时分他睁眼躺着，良心来搅扰他了。

===Original===
Had he done all that it was in him to do to bridge the gap between Ruth and himself? That was what his conscience had wanted to know. The answer was in the negative. On the following day, just before Bailey’s call, he accordingly sought Ruth out, and—rather nervously, for Ruth made him feel nervous nowadays—suggested that he and she and William Bannister should take the air in each other’s company and go and feed the squirrels in the park.

Ruth declined. It is possible that she declined somewhat curtly. The day was close and oppressive, and she had a headache and a general feeling of ill-will toward her species. Also, in her heart, she considered that the scheme proposed smacked too much of Sunday afternoon domesticity in Brooklyn. The idea of papa, mamma, and baby sporting together in a public park offended her sense of the social proprieties.

She did not reveal these thoughts to Kirk because she was more than a little ashamed of them. A year ago, she knew, she would not have objected to the idea. A year ago such an expedition would have been a daily occurrence with her. Now she felt if William Bannister wished to feed squirrels, Mamie was his proper companion.

She could not put all this baldly to Kirk, so she placed the burden of her refusal on the adequate shoulders of Lora Delane Porter. Aunt Lora, she said, would never hear of William Bannister wandering at large in such an unhygienic fashion. Upon which Kirk, whose patience was not so robust as it had been, and who, like Ruth, found the day oppressive and making for irritability, had cursed Aunt Lora heartily, given it as his opinion that between them she and Ruth were turning the child from a human being into a sort of spineless, effeminate exhibit in a museum, and had taken himself off to the studio muttering disjointed things.

Ruth was still quivering with the indignation of a woman who has been cheated of the last word when Bailey appeared and announced that he wished to speak seriously to her.

===Chinese===
在露丝和他自己之间的鸿沟上架桥，他已经竭尽自己所能了吗？这是他的良心想要知道的。答案是否定的。于是第二天，就在贝利到访之前，他去找了露丝，提议他、她，再加上威廉·班尼斯特，三个人结伴透透空气，到公园里去喂喂松鼠——说这话时他相当紧张，因为如今露丝总叫他心里发慌。

露丝回绝了。她回绝得恐怕还不免有点生硬。天气闷热难当，她头也疼，对整个人类都怀着一股泛泛的恶意。再者，在她心底，她认为这个提议未免太有点布鲁克林（Brooklyn）星期天下午居家过日子的味道。爸爸、妈妈加娃娃在一座公园里同乐——这幅图景触犯了她对社交体统的讲究。

这些心思她没有向柯克吐露，因为她对它们惭愧得不止一点点。她知道，一年之前，她是不会反对这个主意的。一年之前，这样的出游对她来说本是家常便饭。如今她觉得，威廉·班尼斯特要是想喂松鼠，玛米（Mamie）才是与他相配的同伴。

这一番话她没法照直摊给柯克，于是便把回绝的担子搁到了洛拉·德莱恩·波特那副足够宽厚的肩膀上。她说，洛拉姑妈绝不会答应让威廉·班尼斯特以这样不讲卫生的方式四处乱跑。柯克的耐性已不如当年结实，而且他和露丝一样觉得这天闷得人冒火，闻言便把洛拉姑妈狠狠咒骂了一通，并发表意见说，她们俩一个拉一个扯，正在把这孩子从一个活人变成博物馆里一件没骨头、女人气的展品，随后便嘟囔着些不连贯的话，独自回画室去了。

贝利到来并宣布有正事要同她谈的时候，露丝还在气得发抖，带着那种最后一句话被人抢走了的女人的义愤——凡是没有抢到最后一句话的女人，都要这样发抖的。

===Original===
Bailey saw the hostility in her eyes and winced a little before it. He was not feeling altogether at his ease. He had had experience of Ruth in this mood, and she had taught him to respect it.

But he was not going to shirk his duty. He resumed:

“I am only speaking for your own good,” he said. “I know that it is nothing but thoughtlessness on your part, but I am naturally anxious—”

“Bailey,” interrupted Ruth, “get to the point.”

Bailey drew a long breath.

===Chinese===
贝利看见她眼里的敌意，不由得微微一缩。他心里不很踏实。露丝这副心绪他领教过，她已教会了他对它敬而远之。

但他不打算临阵脱逃。他接着说下去：

「我完全是为了你好，」他说。「我知道你不过是一时欠考虑，可我自然免不了担忧——」

「贝利，」露丝打断他，「捡要紧的说。」

贝利深深吸了一口气。

===Original===
“Well, then,” he said, baulked of his preamble, and rushing on his fate, “I think you see too much of Basil Milbank.”

Ruth raised her eyebrows.

“Oh?”

The mildness of her tone deceived Bailey.

“I do not like to speak of these things,” he went on more happily; “but I feel that I must. It is my duty. Basil Milbank has not a good reputation. He is not the sort of man who—ah—who—in fact, he has not a good reputation.”

“Oh?”

===Chinese===
「好吧，」他说，开场白被生生夺去，便径直朝自己的命运冲去，「我看你跟巴兹尔·米尔班克来往太密了。」

露丝扬起了眉毛。

「哦？」

她语气的温和骗过了贝利。

「我不喜欢谈这类事，」他接着说，心情轻快了些；「可我感到非谈不可。这是我的责任。巴兹尔·米尔班克名声不好。他不是那种——啊——那种——总而言之，他名声不好。」

「哦？」

===Original===
“I understand that he has invited you to form one of his yacht party.”

“How did you know?”

“Sybil told me. He invited her. I refused to allow her to accept the invitation.”

“And what did Sybil say?”

“She was naturally a little disappointed, of course, but she did as I requested.”

===Chinese===
「我了解到，他邀你加入他游艇出游的行列。」

「你怎么知道的？」

「西比尔告诉我的。他邀了她。我没答应她接受这份邀请。」

「西比尔说什么了？」

「她自然有点失望，这是难免的，不过她照我的要求做了。」

===Original===
“I wonder she didn’t pack her things and go straight off.”

“My dear Ruth!”

“That is what I should have done.”

“You don’t know what you are saying.”

“Oh? Do you think I should let Kirk dictate to me like that?”

===Chinese===
「我真奇怪，她怎么不打点行李，径直就走。」

「我亲爱的露丝！」

「换了我就这么办。」

「你不知道自己在说什么。」

「哦？你以为我肯让柯克这样对我发号施令？」

===Original===
“He is certain to disapprove of your going when he hears of the invitation. What will you do?”

Ruth’s eyes opened. For a moment she looked almost ugly.

“What shall I do? Why, go, of course.”

She clenched her teeth. A woman’s mind can work curiously, and she was associating Kirk with Bailey in what she considered an unwarrantable intrusion into her private affairs. It was as if Kirk, and not Bailey, were standing there, demanding that she should not associate with Basil Milbank.

“I shall make it my business,” said Bailey, “to warn Kirk that this man is not a desirable companion for you.”

===Chinese===
「他一听说这份邀请，准会反对你去。到那时你怎么办？」

露丝睁大了眼睛。有一瞬间，她的模样几乎变得凶狠。

「我怎么办？嗐，当然是去。」

她咬紧了牙关。女人的头脑有时运作得莫名其妙：她竟把柯克和贝利归成了一伙，认为他们联手对她私事进行了一场她眼中毫无道理的侵扰。仿佛站在那儿、勒令她不许与巴兹尔·米尔班克来往的不是贝利，而是柯克本人。

「我会把这事当作分内之事，」贝利说，「去告诫柯克，此人不是与你交往的合适人选。」

===Original===
The discussion of this miserable yacht affair had brought back to Bailey all the jealousy which he had felt when Sybil had first told him of it. All the vague stories he had ever heard about Basil were surging in his mind like waves of some corrosive acid. He had become a leading member of the extreme wing of the anti-Milbank party. He regarded Basil with the aversion which a dignified pigeon might feel for a circling hawk; and he was now looking on this yacht party as a deadly peril from which Ruth must be saved at any cost.

“I shall speak to him very strongly,” he added.

Ruth’s suppressed anger blazed up in the sudden way which before now had disconcerted her brother.

“Bailey, what do you mean by coming here and saying this sort of thing? You’re becoming a perfect old woman. You spend your whole time prying into other people’s affairs. I’m sorry for Sybil.”

Bailey cast one reproachable look at her and left the room with pained dignity. Something seemed to tell him that no good could come to him from a prolongation of the interview. Ruth, in this mood, always had been too much for him, and always would be. Well, he had done his duty as far as he was concerned. It now remained to do the same by Kirk.

===Chinese===
这场关于游艇出游的倒霉讨论，把西比尔头一回说起此事时贝利心中泛起的那份醋意，原原本本召了回来。他历来听到的关于巴兹尔的种种含糊传言，此刻正像腐蚀酸的波浪在他脑子里翻涌。他已成了一名反米尔班克党极端派的领军人物。他以一种庄严的鸽子望着盘旋的鹰隼时的憎恶来看待巴兹尔；此刻他把这场游艇出游视为一个致命的危险，露丝必须不惜一切代价从中获救。

「我会极其严厉地跟他谈。」他补了一句。

露丝强压的怒火倏地蹿了起来，那来势之突然，从前就曾叫他这位兄长手足无措。

「贝利，你跑到这儿来说这种话，到底是什么意思？你简直变成个十足的老婆子了。你一天到晚就晓得刺探别人的私事。我真替西比尔难过。」

贝利朝她投去一道饱含责备的一瞥，带着满心委屈的尊严离开了房间。冥冥中似乎有什么告诉他，这场会见再拖下去决无好处。露丝使出这副心绪，他从来招架不住，今后也永远招架不住。好吧，就她这方面而言，他已尽了责任。接下来该对柯克如法炮制了。

===Original===
He hailed a taxi and drove to the studio.

Kirk was busy and not anxious for conversation, least of all with Bailey. He had not forgotten their last tête-à-tête.

Bailey, however, was regarding him with a feeling almost of friendliness. They were bound together by a common grievance against Basil Milbank.

“I came here, Winfield,” he said, after a few moments of awkward conversation on neutral topics, “because I understand that this man Milbank has invited Ruth to join his yacht party.”

“What yacht party?”

“This man Milbank is taking a party for a cruise shortly in his yacht.”

===Chinese===
他招了辆出租车，坐车去了画室。

柯克正忙着，不盼着有人来聊天，尤其是跟贝利。他们上一次的单独对谈，他可没忘。

然而贝利望着他，心里竟差不多怀着好感。对巴兹尔·米尔班克的共同宿怨把两人拴在了一起。

「我到这儿来，温菲尔德，」在不痛不痒的话题上尴尬地交谈了几句之后，他说，「是因为我了解到米尔班克这人邀了露丝参加他的游艇出游。」

「什么游艇出游？」

「米尔班克这人不久要用他的游艇带一批客人出游。」

===Original===
“Who is Milbank?”

“Surely you have met him? Yes, he was at my house one night when you and Ruth dined there shortly after your return.”

“I don’t remember him. However, it doesn’t matter. But why does the fact that he has asked Ruth on his yacht excite you? Are you nervous about the sea?”

“I dislike this man Milbank very much, Winfield. I think Ruth sees too much of him.”

Kirk stiffened. His eyebrows rose the fraction of an inch.

“Oh?” he said.

===Chinese===
「米尔班克是谁？」

「你总见过他吧？对了，你们回来后不久，有一晚你和露丝在我家吃饭，他就在座。」

「我不记得这个人。不过，这不要紧。可是他邀露丝上他的游艇，这件事怎么就让你激动起来了？你是替大海担心吗？」

「我非常不喜欢米尔班克这个人，温菲尔德。我认为露丝跟他见面太频繁了。」

柯克绷紧了。他的眉毛抬起了那么一丝。

「哦？」他说。

===Original===
It seemed to Bailey for an instant that he had been talking all his life to people who raised their eyebrows and said “Oh!” but he continued manfully.

“I do not think that Ruth should know him, Winfield.”

“Wouldn’t Ruth be rather a good judge of that?”

His tone nettled Bailey, but the man conscious of doing his duty acquires an artificial thickness of skin, and he controlled himself. But he had lost that feeling of friendliness, of sympathy with a brother in misfortune which he had brought in with him.

“I disagree with you entirely,” he said.

===Chinese===
有一刹那，贝利觉得他这辈子打交道的全是些扬起眉毛说「哦！」的人，但他硬着头皮继续说下去。

「我认为露丝不该与他相识，温菲尔德。」

「这一点，露丝自己不是挺有资格下判断吗？」

他的语气激恼了贝利，但自知正在尽责的人自会养成一层人造的厚脸皮，他按捺住了自己。不过，他进门时怀有的那份好感、那份同病相怜的同情，已经消失了。

「我完全不同意你的看法。」他说。

===Original===
“Another thing,” went on Kirk. “If this man Milbank—I still can’t place him—is such a thug, or whatever it is that he happens to be, how did he come to be at your house the night you say I met him?”

Bailey winced. He wished the world was not perpetually reminding him that Basil and Sybil were on speaking terms.

“Sybil invited him. I may say he has asked Sybil to make one of the yacht party. I absolutely forbade it.”

“But, Heavens! What’s wrong with the man?”

“He has a bad reputation.”

“Has he, indeed!”

===Chinese===
「还有一件事，」柯克接着说。「倘若这位米尔班克——我还是想不起他是谁——真是个恶棍，或者不管他到底是个什么货色，那他怎么会到你家做客，就在你说我见过他的那一晚？」

贝利哆嗦了一下。他真希望这个世界别没完没了地提醒他：巴兹尔和西比尔是说过话的交情。

「是西比尔请的他。我不妨告诉你，他邀了西比尔加入游艇出游。我断然禁止了。」

「可是，老天！这人到底有什么毛病？」

「他名声不好。」

「是嘛，真的！」

===Original===
“And I wish my wife to associate with him as little as possible. And I should advise you to forbid Ruth to see more of him than she can help.”

Kirk laughed. The idea struck him as comic.

“My good man, I don’t forbid Ruth to do things.”

Bailey, objecting to being called anyone’s good man, especially Kirk’s, permitted his temper to get the better of him.

“Then you should,” he snapped. “I have no wish to quarrel with you. I came in here in a friendly spirit to warn you; but I must say that for a man who married a girl, as you married Ruth, in direct opposition to the wishes of her family, you take a curious view of your obligations. Ruth has always been a headstrong, impulsive girl, and it is for you to see that she is protected from herself. If you are indifferent to her welfare, then all I can say is that you should not have married her. You appear to think otherwise. Good afternoon.”

===Chinese===
「还有，我希望我太太尽可能少与他来往。我也奉劝你，别让露丝与他多见面，能少见就少见。」

柯克笑了。这个主意在他看来滑稽得很。

「老兄，我可不禁止露丝做这做那。」

贝利不乐意被称作任何人的「老兄」，尤其是柯克的老兄，便听任自己的脾气占了上风。

「那你就应该禁止。」他厉声说。「我不想跟你吵架。我是抱着友好的精神来提醒你的；可我必须说，像你这样娶了一位姑娘——完完全全违背她家里人的意愿娶了露丝——的人，居然对自己的义务持这样一种古怪的见解。露丝素来是个任性、冲动的姑娘，保护她不至于害了自己，正是你的责任。你若对她的幸福漠不关心，那我只能说，你当初就不该娶她。你看来另有想法。再见。」

===Original===
He stalked out of the studio, leaving Kirk uncomfortably conscious that he had had the worst of the argument. Bailey had been officious, no doubt, and his pompous mode of expression was not soothing, but there was no doubt that he had had right on his side.

Marrying Ruth did not involve obligations. He had never considered her in that light, but perhaps she was a girl who had to be protected from herself. She was certainly impulsive. Bailey had been right there, if nowhere else.

Who was this fellow Milbank who had sprung suddenly from nowhere into the position of a menace? What were Ruth’s feelings toward him? Kirk threw his mind back to the dinner-party at Bailey’s and tried to place him.

Was it the man—yes, he had it now. It was the man with the wave of hair over his forehead, the fellow who looked like a poet. Memory came to him with a rush. He recalled his instinctive dislike for the fellow.

So that was Milbank, was it? He got up and put away his brushes. There would be no more work for him that afternoon.

===Chinese===
他昂首阔步走出了画室，留下柯克在那儿浑身不自在地意识到：这场争论里吃瘪的是自己。贝利固然是多管闲事，他那套装腔作势的说法也叫人窝火，可毫无疑问，理在他那一边。

娶露丝并不牵扯什么义务。他从来没有从这个角度考虑过她，可她或许真是个需要有人保护、免得害了自己的姑娘。她的确冲动。就算贝利别处全错，这一点上他没说错。

这个米尔班克究竟是何方神圣，居然平地一声雷冒出来，一下子成了个威胁？露丝对他是什么感情？柯克把思绪抛回贝利家那顿晚饭，努力回想此人的模样。

是不是那个人——对了，想起来了。就是那个额前垂着一绺波浪头发的男人，那个长得像诗人的家伙。记忆哗地涌了回来。他记起了自己当初对这家伙本能的厌恶。

原来那就是米尔班克，是吗？他站起身，收起画笔。那天下午，他是什么活也干不下去了。

===Original===
He walked slowly home. The heat of the day had grown steadily more oppressive. It was one of those airless, stifling afternoons which afflict New York in the summer. He remembered seeing something about a record in the evening paper which he had bought on his way to the studio, a whole column about heat and humidity. It certainly felt unusually warm even for New York.

It was one of those days when nerves are strained, when molehills become mountains, and mountains are all Everests. He had felt it when he talked with Ruth about Bill and the squirrels, and he felt it now. He was conscious of being extraordinarily irritated, not so much with any particular person as with the world in general. The very vagueness of Bailey’s insinuations against Basil Milbank increased his resentment.

What a pompous ass Bailey was! What a fool he had been to give Bailey such a chance of snubbing him! What an extraordinarily futile and unpleasant world it was altogether!

He braced himself with an effort. It was this heat which was making him magnify trifles. Bailey was a fool. Probably there was nothing whatever wrong with this fellow Milbank. Probably he had some personal objection to the man, and that was all.

And yet the image of Basil which had come back to his mind was not reassuring. He had mistrusted him that night, and he mistrusted him now.

===Chinese===
他慢慢走回家去。天气的燥热一阵紧似一阵地压下来。这是夏天折磨纽约的那种不透风、闷死人的下午之一。他记得在来画室路上买的那份晚报上看到过什么破纪录的消息，整整一栏全是讲气温和湿度。即使拿纽约的标准来说，这天也确实热得反常。

这是那种日子：人人神经绷紧，鼹鼠丘变成大山，而座座大山全成了珠穆朗玛峰。跟露丝谈比尔和松鼠的时候他就感到了，此刻又感到了。他意识到自己烦躁得非同小可——倒不怎么针对哪一个具体的人，而是冲着整个世界。贝利对巴兹尔·米尔班克的影射偏偏又含含糊糊，反倒愈发添了他的恼火。

贝利这头装腔作势的蠢驴！自己真是个傻瓜，竟给了贝利这么一个抢白他的机会！这整个世界又是何等莫名其妙地无聊、何等惹人厌！

他用力打起精神来。是这鬼天气害得他把鸡毛蒜皮看成了天塌地陷。贝利是个蠢货。这位米尔班克多半根本没什么毛病。多半只是他个人看此人不顺眼，仅此而已。

然而，回到他脑子里的巴兹尔的那个形象，并不令人安心。那天晚上他就不信任此人，此刻依然不信任。

===Original===
What should he do? Ruth was not Sybil. She was not the sort of woman a man could forbid to do things. It would require tact to induce her to refuse Basil’s invitation.

As he reached the door an idea came to him, so simple that he wondered that it had not occurred to him before. It was, perhaps, an echo of his conversation with Steve.

He would get Ruth to come away with him to the shack in the Connecticut woods. As he dwelt on the idea the heat of the day seemed to become less oppressive and his heart leaped. How cool and pleasant it would be out there! They would take Bill with them and live the simple life again, in the country this time instead of in town. Perhaps out there, far away from the overcrowded city, he and Ruth would be able to come to an understanding and bridge over that ghastly gulf.

As for his work, he could do that as well in the woods as in New York. And, anyhow, he had earned a vacation. For days Mr. Penway had been hinting that the time had arrived for a folding of the hands.

Mr. Penway’s views on New York and its record humidity were strong and crisply expressed. His idea, he told Kirk, was that some sport with a heart should loan him a couple of hundred bucks and let him beat it to the seashore before he melted.

===Chinese===
他该怎么办？露丝不是西比尔。她不是那种男人可以禁止其做这做那的女人。要劝得她自己回绝巴兹尔的邀请，得讲究点手腕。

走到家门口时，一个主意冒了出来，简单得让他纳闷怎么早没想到。这或许是他与史蒂夫那场谈话的回声。

他要带露丝跟他一起躲到康涅狄格（Connecticut）树林里的小木屋去。他细细琢磨着这个主意，暑气似乎也没那么难当了，他的心怦然一跳。到了那边该多么凉爽、多么惬意！他们要带上比尔，重新过上简朴的日子——这回是在乡间，不在城里。也许在那边，在远离这座拥挤不堪的城市的地方，他和露丝能够彼此说开，在那道可怕的鸿沟上把桥架起来。

至于他的工作，在林子里干跟在纽约干一样。再说了，他也确实挣来了一次休假。好些天来，佩尼韦先生（Mr. Penway）一直在暗示：抱起手来歇着的时辰到了。

关于纽约和它破纪录的湿度，佩尼韦先生观点鲜明，而且表达得干脆利落。他告诉柯克，他的想法是：哪位有点儿心肠的阔佬借他两百来块钱，放他在化成一摊水之前溜到海边去。

===Original===
In the drawing-room Ruth was playing the piano softly, as she had done so often at the studio. Kirk went to her and kissed her. A marked coolness in her reception of the kiss increased the feeling of nervousness which he had felt at the sight of her. It came back to him that they had parted that afternoon, for the first time, on definitely hostile terms.

He decided to ignore the fact. Something told him that Ruth had not forgotten, but it might be that cheerfulness now would blot out the resentment of past irritability.

But in his embarrassment he was more than cheerful. As Steve had been on the occasion of his visit to old John Bannister, he was breezy, breezy with an effort that was as painful to Ruth as it was to himself, breezy with a horrible musical comedy breeziness.

He could have adopted no more fatal tone with Ruth at that moment. All the afternoon she had been a complicated tangle of fretted nerves. Her quarrel with Kirk, Bailey’s visit, a conscience that would not lie down and go to sleep at her orders, but insisted on running riot—all these things had unfitted her to bear up amiably under sudden, self-conscious breeziness.

And the heat of the day, charged now with the oppressiveness of long-overdue thunder, completed her mood. When Kirk came in and began to speak, the softest notes of the human voice would have jarred upon her. And Kirk, in his nervousness, was almost shouting.

===Chinese===
客厅里，露丝正在轻柔地弹钢琴，一如她当年在画室里常做的那样。柯克走过去吻她。她接受这一吻时那份分明的冷淡，加深了他一看见她就生出的慌乱。他记起来了：那天下午，他们头一回在不折不扣的敌意中分了手。

他决定对这件事佯装不见。冥冥中有个声音告诉他，露丝并没有忘，可眼下的一团高兴兴许能把先前龃龉的怨气涂抹干净。

可是在窘迫之中，他岂止是高兴，简直兴高采烈过了头。就像史蒂夫当年去拜访老约翰·班尼斯特那回一样，他浑身轻快，那份硬撑出来的轻快，叫露丝难受，也叫他自己难受，是一种歌舞喜剧式的、可怕的轻快。

在那一刻，他对露丝再也挑不出一个更致命的腔调了。整个下午，她就是一团乱麻似的烦躁神经。跟柯克的口角、贝利的来访、一颗不肯奉命躺倒睡觉偏要撒野的良心——这一切都使她再没本领在和颜悦色中承受这种突如其来的、自觉的轻快。

而这天的高温，此刻又饱含着久拖不至的雷霆的郁沉，给她的心绪补上了最后一笔。柯克进来开口说话的时候，人声里最轻柔的音调于她都是刺耳的。而柯克心一慌，几乎是在喊叫。

===Original===
His voice rang through the room, and Ruth winced away from it like a stricken thing. From out of the hell of nerves and heat and interfering brothers there materialized itself, as she sat there, a very vivid hatred of Kirk.

Kirk, meanwhile, uneasy, but a little guessing at the fury behind Ruth’s calm face, was expounding his great scheme, his panacea for all the ills of domestic misunderstandings and parted lives.

“Ruth, old girl.”

Ruth shuddered.

“Ruth, old girl, I’ve had a bully good idea. It’s getting too warm for anything in New York. Did you ever feel anything like it is today? Why shouldn’t you and I pop down to the shack and camp out there for a week or so? And we would take Bill with us. Just we three, with somebody to do the cooking. It would be great. What do you say?”

===Chinese===
他的嗓音在屋里嗡嗡回荡，露丝像个挨了打的东西似的缩着躲开。正当她坐在那儿的时候，从这神经、酷暑加上多管闲事的兄长共同织成的地狱里，一个鲜明的形象凝成了：对柯克活生生的憎恨。

柯克这边呢，心里发虚，却多少猜到了露丝那张平静面孔背后的狂怒，只顾阐述他的宏伟计划——他那一剂包治家庭误会、劳燕分飞诸般沉疴的万灵药。

「露丝，好姑娘。」

露丝打了个哆嗦。

「露丝，好姑娘，我想出了个顶呱呱的好主意。纽约热得没法待了。你哪天领教过今天这样的天气？咱们俩何不溜到小木屋去，在那儿露营个把星期？咱们把比尔也带上。就咱们仨，再雇个人做饭。那才叫棒呢。你说好不好？」

===Original===
What Ruth said languidly was: “It’s quite impossible.”

It was damping; but Kirk felt that at all costs he must refuse to be damped. He clutched at his cheerfulness and held it.

“Nonsense,” he retorted. “Why is it impossible? It’s a great idea.”

Ruth half hid a yawn. She knew she was behaving abominably, and she was glad of it.

“It’s impossible as far as I’m concerned. I have a hundred things to do before I can leave New York.”

===Chinese===
露丝懒洋洋地答道：「根本不可能。」

这话像一盆冷水；可柯克觉得，无论如何他也不能被浇灭。他死死抓住自己的兴头不放。

「胡说，」他反驳道。「为什么不可能？这主意好得很。」

露丝掩住了半个哈欠。她知道自己行事可恶透了，而且正为此高兴。

「对我来说不可能。离开纽约之前，我有一百件事要办。」

===Original===
“Well, I could do with a day or two to clear up a few bits of work I have on hand. Why couldn’t we start this day week?”

“It is out of the question for me. About then I shall be on Mr. Milbank’s yacht. He has invited me to join his party. The actual day is not settled, but it will be in about a week’s time.”

“Oh!” said Kirk.

Ruth said nothing.

“Have you accepted the invitation?”

“I have not actually answered his letter. I was just going to when you came in.”

===Chinese===
「呃，我也正好需要一两天，把手头几件零碎活理一理。咱们何不一周后的今天动身？」

「对我来说免谈。到时候我会在米尔班克先生的游艇上。他邀我加入他那一伙。日子还没定死，不过就在大约一个星期之后。」

「哦！」柯克说。

露丝没说什么。

「你应了邀请没有？」

「他的信我还没正式回。你进来的时候我正要写呢。」

===Original===
“But you mean to accept it?”

“Certainly. Several of my friends will be there. Sybil for one.”

“Not Sybil.”

“Oh, I know Bailey has made some ridiculous objection to her going, but I mean to persuade her.”

Kirk did not answer. She looked at him steadily.

“So Bailey did call on you this afternoon? He told me he was going to, but I hoped he would think better of it. But apparently there are no limits to Bailey’s stupidity.”

===Chinese===
「可你是打算应下的？」

「当然。我有好几位朋友都在船上。西比尔就是一位。」

「西比尔去不成。」

「哦，我知道贝利对她去这事提了些荒唐的反对意见，可我打算把她劝过去。」

柯克没有答话。她定定地看着他。

「这么说，贝利今天下午果然来找过你？他跟我说他要来，可我还指望他回心转意呢。看来贝利的愚蠢当真是没有底的。」

===Original===
“Yes, Bailey came to the studio. He seemed troubled about this yacht party.”

“Did he advise you to forbid me to go?”

“Well, yes; he did.”

“And now you have come to do it?”

“Not at all. I told Bailey that you were not the sort of woman one forbade to do things.”

“I’m not.”

===Chinese===
「是的，贝利到画室来了。他好像对这次游艇出游忧心忡忡。」

「他是不是劝你禁止我去？」

「呃，是的，他劝了。」

「现在你就是来干这个的？」

「完全不是。我跟贝利说了，你不是那种可以任人禁止做这做那的女人。」

「我不是。」

===Original===
There was a pause.

“All the same, I wish you wouldn’t go.”

Ruth did not answer.

“It would be very jolly out at the shack.”

Ruth shuddered elaborately and gave a little laugh.

“Would it? It’s rather a question of taste. Personally, I can’t imagine anything more depressing and uncomfortable than being cooped up in a draughty frame house miles away from anywhere. There’s no reason why you should not go, though, if you like that sort of thing. Of course, you must not take Bill.”

===Chinese===
出现了一阵沉默。

「话虽这么说，我还是希望你别去。」

露丝没有作声。

「在小木屋那边会很快活的。」

露丝夸张地打了个哆嗦，轻笑了一声。

「会吗？这恐怕是个口味问题。就我个人而言，我想不出还有什么比关在一幢四面透风的木板屋里、前不着村后不着店更郁闷更难受的了。不过你要是好这一口，倒也没有理由不去。当然，比尔你是万万不能带的。」

===Original===
“Why not?”

Kirk spoke calmly enough, but he was very near the breaking point. All his good resolutions had vanished under the acid of Ruth’s manner.

“I couldn’t let him rough it like that. Aunt Lora would have a fit.”

Conditions being favourable, it only needs a spark to explode a powder magazine; and there are moments when a word can turn an outwardly calm and patient man into a raging maniac. This introduction of Mrs. Porter’s name into the discussion at this particular point broke down the last remnants of Kirk’s self-control.

For a few seconds his fury so mastered him that he could not speak. Then, suddenly, the storm passed and he found himself cool and venomous. He looked at Ruth curiously. It seemed incredible to him that he had ever loved her.

===Chinese===
「为什么不能带？」

柯克说得很平静，可他离崩溃的边缘已近在咫尺。他的种种好决心，在露丝态度的酸液里蒸发得一干二净。

「我可不能让他去受那份野罪。洛拉姑妈会犯病的。」

条件既已备齐，火药库只欠一颗火星；而有些时刻，一个词就能把一个外表平静、耐心的人变成狂怒的疯子。波特夫人的名字偏偏在这个节骨眼上被引了进来，砸塌了柯克自制力最后的残余。

有几秒钟，怒火烧得他一句话也说不出来。随后，风暴陡然过去，他发现自己冷静了下来，冷静得带毒。他好奇地看着露丝。他竟曾爱过她——这在他看来已难以置信。

===Original===
“We had better get this settled,” he said in a hard, quiet voice.

Ruth started. She had never heard him speak like this before. She had not imagined him capable of speaking in that way. Even in the days when she had loved him most she had never looked up to him. She had considered his nature weak, and she had loved his weakness. Except in the case of her father, she had always dominated the persons with whom she mixed; and she had taken it for granted that her will was stronger than Kirk’s. Something in his voice now told her that she had underestimated him.

“Get what settled?” she asked, and was furious with herself because her voice shook.

“Is Mrs. Porter the mother of the child, or are you? What has Mrs. Porter to do with it? Why should I ask her permission? How does it happen to be any business of Mrs. Porter’s at all?”

Ruth felt baffled. He was giving her no chance to take the offensive. There was nothing in his tone which she could openly resent. He was not shouting at her, he was speaking quietly. There was nothing for her to do but answer the question, and she knew that her answer would give him another point in the contest. Even as she spoke she knew that her words were ridiculous.

===Chinese===
「咱们最好把这件事弄弄清楚。」他用一种冷硬而平静的声音说。

露丝一惊。她从没听他用这种口吻说过话。她从没想过他能有这样说话的本事。即使在她最爱他的那些日子里，她也从没仰视过他。她一向认为他天性软弱，而她爱的正是他的软弱。除了对她父亲，她与交往的人相处从来都是她说了算；她一直想当然地认定，自己的意志比柯克的强。此刻他嗓音里的某种东西告诉她：她小瞧他了。

「弄清楚什么？」她问，又因为自己的声音发颤而恼恨自己。

「这孩子的母亲是波特夫人，还是你？这事与波特夫人何干？凭什么要请示她？这究竟是怎么变成波特夫人的业务的？」

露丝感到无计可施。他不给她任何出击的机会。他的语气里没有任何她可以公然记恨的东西。他没冲她喊叫，他平静地说着。她别无选择，只能回答问题，而她知道自己的回答会让他在较量中再得一分。话出口的时候她就知道，自己的话荒唐得很。

===Original===
“Aunt Lora has been wonderful with him. No child could have been better looked after.”

“I know she has used him as a vehicle for her particular form of insanity, but that’s not the point. What I am asking is why she was introduced at all.”

“I told you. When you were away, Bill nearly—”

“Died. I know. I’m not forgetting that. And naturally for a time you were frightened. It is just possible that for the moment you lost your head and honestly thought that Mrs. Porter’s methods were the only chance for him. But that state of mind could not last all the time with you. You are not a crank like your aunt. You are a perfectly sensible, levelheaded woman. And you must have seen the idiocy of it all long before I came back. Why did you let it go on?”

===Chinese===
「洛拉姑妈对孩子尽心尽力得了不得。没有哪个孩子能被照顾得更好了。」

「我知道她一直拿他当运载工具，装运她那一款特制的疯狂，可这不是重点。我问的是，当初究竟为什么要把她引进来。」

「我跟你说过了。你不在家那阵子，比尔差一点——」

「死掉。我知道。我没有忘。你自然有一阵子吓坏了。完全有可能，你当时昏了头，真心以为波特夫人那一套是救他的唯一机会。可那种心境不可能在你身上一直持续下去。你不是你姑妈那样的怪人。你是个通情达理、头脑冷静得不能再冷静的女人。早在回国之前，你必定早就看清了这一切的愚蠢。你为什么由着它继续下去？」

===Original===
Ruth did not answer.

“I will tell you why. Because it saved you trouble. Because it gave you more leisure for the sort of futile waste of time which seems to be the only thing you care for nowadays. Don’t trouble to deny it. Do you think I haven’t seen in these last few months that Bill bores you to death? Oh, I know you always have some perfect excuse for keeping away from him. It’s too much trouble for you to be a mother to him, so you hedge with your conscience by letting Mrs. Porter pamper him and sterilize his toys and all the rest of it, and try to make yourself think that you have done your duty to him. You know that, as far as everything goes that matters, any tenement child is better off than Bill.”

“I—”

“You had better let me finish what I have got to say. I will be as brief as I can. That is my case as regards Bill. Now about myself. What do you think I am made of? I’ve stood it just as long as I could; you have tried me too hard. I’m through. Heaven knows why it should have come to this. It is not so very long ago that Bill was half the world to you and I was the other half. Now, apparently, there is not room in your world for either of us.”

===Chinese===
露丝没有回答。

「我来告诉你为什么。因为那样省事。因为那样能给你腾出更多的闲暇，去挥霍在那种无聊的营生上——而那似乎是你如今唯一上心的事。不必费神否认了。你以为这几个月我没看出来，比尔烦得你要死吗？哦，我知道你总有十全十美的借口躲着他。给他当母亲太费事，于是你便让波特夫人去娇惯他、给他的玩具消毒，还有那一整套，以此跟自己的良心打马虎眼，再想法子让自己相信你已经对他尽了责。你知道，凡是真正要紧的事，随便哪个贫民公寓里的孩子都比比尔过得强。」

「我——」

「你最好让我把话说完。我尽量拣短的讲。关于比尔，我的话就这些。现在说说我自个儿。你以为我是什么做的？我能忍的都忍了；你把我逼得太狠了。我受够了。天知道事情怎么会走到这一步。就在不算太久之前，比尔是你世界的一半，我是另一半。如今，看起来，你的世界里已经装不下我们俩中的任何一个了。」

===Original===
Ruth had risen. She was trembling.

“I think we had better end this.”

He broke in on her words.

“End it? Yes, you’re right. One way or the other. Either go back to the old life or start a new one. What we are living now is a horrible burlesque.”

“What do you mean? How start a new life?”

===Chinese===
露丝已经站了起来。她在发抖。

「我想我们最好到此为止。」

他打断了她的话。

「到此为止？对，你说得对。不走这条路，就走那条路。要么回到原来的日子，要么另起一摊新的。我们现在过的这种日子，是一场可怕的滑稽戏。」

「你什么意思？怎么个另起法？」

===Original===
“I mean exactly what I say. In the life you are living now I am an anachronism. I’m a survival. I’m out of date and in the way. You would be freer without me.”

“That’s absurd.”

“Is the idea so novel? Is our marriage the only failure in New York?”

“Do you mean that we ought to separate?”

“Only a little more, a very little more, than we are separated now. Never see each other again instead of seeing each other for a few minutes every day. It’s not a very big step to take.”

===Chinese===
「我就是我说的那个意思。在你眼下过的这种生活里，我是个不合时宜的东西。我是个时代残余。我过了时，还碍手碍脚。没有我，你会更自由。」

「荒唐。」

「这个想法很新鲜吗？我们这桩婚姻是纽约唯一的失败吗？」

「你是说我们应当分开？」

「不过比我们现在的分离再多走一小步，很小的一步。从每天见几分钟面，变成从此不再见面。这一步不算多大。」

===Original===
Ruth sat down and rested her chin on her hand, staring at nothing. Kirk went to the window and looked out.

Over the park the sky was black. In the room behind him the light had faded till it seemed as if night were come. The air was heavy and stifling. A flicker of lightning came and went in the darkness over the trees.

He turned abruptly.

“It is the only reasonable thing to do. Our present mode of life is a farce. We are drifting farther apart every day. Perhaps I have changed. I know you have. We are two strangers chained together. We have made a muddle of it, and the best thing we can do is to admit it.

“I am no good to you. I have no part in your present life. You’re the queen and I’m just the prince consort, the fellow who happens to be Mrs. Winfield’s husband. It’s not a pleasant part to have to play, and I have had enough of it. We had better separate before we hate each other. You have your amusements. I have my work. We can continue them apart. We shall both be better off.”

===Chinese===
露丝坐下来，用手支着下巴，两眼望着虚空。柯克走到窗前，望着外面。

公园上空的天是黑的。他身后的房间里，光线已经暗得仿佛黑夜降临。空气滞重窒闷。一道闪电在树丛上方的黑暗里忽明忽灭。

他猛地转过身。

「这是唯一讲得通的做法。我们眼下的生活方式是场闹剧。我们一天比一天飘得更远。也许我变了。我知道你变了。我们是拴在一根链子上的两个陌生人。我们把这桩事搞砸了，最好的办法就是承认这一点。

「我对你没什么用处。你眼下的生活里没有我的份。你是女王，我不过是王夫，是那个碰巧当了温菲尔德太太丈夫的人。这个角色演起来不愉快，我也演够了。我们最好趁还没彼此怨恨的时候分开。你有你的消遣。我有我的工作。分开之后两不耽误。我们俩的日子都会好过些。」

===Original===
He stopped. Ruth did not speak. She was still sitting in the same attitude. It was too dark to see her face. It formed a little splash of white in the dusk. She did not move.

Kirk went to the door.

“I’m going up to say goodbye to Bill. Have you anything to say against that? And I shall say goodbye to him in my own way.”

She made no sign that she had heard him.

“Goodbye,” he said again.

===Chinese===
他停住了。露丝没有说话。她仍以原先的姿势坐着。天太暗了，看不清她的脸。那张脸在暮色里成了一小片白色的影子。她一动不动。

柯克向门口走去。

「我上楼去跟比尔道个别。你可有半句反对？道别的方式，也要按我自己的来。」

她没有任何听见了的表示。

「再见。」他又说了一遍。

===Original===
The door closed.

Up in the nursery Bill crooned to himself as he played on the floor. Mamie sat in a chair, sewing. The opening of the door caused them to look up simultaneously.

“Hello,” said Bill.

His voice was cordial without being enthusiastic. He was glad to see Kirk, but tin soldiers were tin soldiers and demanded concentrated attention. When you are in the middle of intricate manoeuvres you cannot allow yourself to be more than momentarily distracted by anything.

“Mamie,” said Kirk hoarsely, “go out for a minute, will you? I shan’t be long.”

===Chinese===
门关上了。

楼上育儿室里，比尔在地板上玩耍，嘴里哼哼唧唧地自得其乐。玛米坐在椅子上做针线。门一开，两人同时抬头。

「哈罗。」比尔说。

他的声音亲切而不热烈。见到柯克他是高兴的，可是锡兵就是锡兵，容不得分心。当你正处于复杂的调兵遣将之中，任何东西都不许让你分神超过一瞬。

「玛米，」柯克哑着嗓子说，「出去一会儿，好吗？我用不了多长时间。」

===Original===
Mamie obediently departed. Later, when Keggs was spreading the news of Kirk’s departure in the servants’ hall, she remembered that his manner had struck her as strange.

Kirk sat down in the chair she had left and looked at Bill. He felt choked. There was a mist before his eyes.

“Bill.”

The child, absorbed in his game, did not look up.

“Bill, old man, come here a minute. I’ve something to say.”

===Chinese===
玛米乖乖地出去了。后来，当凯格斯（Keggs）在下人饭厅里传播柯克出走的消息时，她想起当时就觉得他的样子有点异样。

柯克在她空出来的椅子上坐下，看着比尔。他觉着喉咙哽住。眼前蒙着一层雾。

「比尔。」

孩子专心致志玩着他的游戏，没有抬头。

「比尔，小伙子，过来一下。我有话跟你说。」

===Original===
Bill looked up, nodded, moved a couple of soldiers, and got up. He came to Kirk’s side. His chosen mode of progression at this time was a kind of lurch. He was accustomed to breathe heavily during the journey, and on arrival at the terminus usually shouted triumphantly.

Kirk put an arm round him. Bill stared gravely up into his face. There was a silence. From outside came a sudden rumbling crash. Bill jumped.

“Funder,” he said in a voice that shook a little.

“Not afraid of thunder, are you?” said Kirk.

Bill shook his head stoutly.

“Bill.”

===Chinese===
比尔抬起头，点点头，挪动了两三个锡兵，站起身来。他走到柯克身边。他这个时期选定的行进方式，是一种连冲带撞的蹒跚。旅途中他惯于呼哧呼哧喘大气，抵达终点站时通常要得胜地呐喊一声。

柯克伸出手臂搂住他。比尔严肃地仰视着他的脸。一时无声。外面忽然传来一声隆隆巨响。比尔跳了起来。

「雷雷。」他说话的声音有点发颤。

「不怕打雷，嗯？」柯克说。

比尔斩钉截铁地摇了摇头。

「比尔。」

===Original===
“Yes, daddy?”

Kirk fought to keep his voice steady.

“Bill, old man, I’m afraid you won’t see me again for some time. I’m going away.”

“In a ship?”

“No, not in a ship.”

“In a train?”

===Chinese===
「什么事，爸爸？」

柯克竭力让声音保持平稳。

「比尔，小伙子，恐怕有一阵子你见不着我了。我要出远门。」

「坐船吗？」

「不，不坐船。」

「坐火车吗？」

===Original===
“Perhaps.”

“Take me with you, daddy.”

“I’m afraid I can’t, Bill.”

“Shan’t I ever see you again?”

Kirk winced. How direct children are! What was it they called it in the papers? “The custody of the child.” How little it said and how much it meant!

===Chinese===
「也许吧。」

「带我一块儿去，爸爸。」

「恐怕不行，比尔。」

「我以后再也见不着你了吗？」

柯克浑身一震。孩子的话多么直截了当！报纸上管这个叫什么来着？「孩子的监护权。」这几个字说得多么少，含的分量又多么重！

===Original===
The sight of Bill’s wide eyes and quivering mouth reminded him that he was not the only person involved in the tragedy of those five words. He pulled himself together. Bill was waiting anxiously for an answer to his question. There was no need to make Bill unhappy before his time.

“Of course you will,” he said, trying to make his voice cheerful.

“Of course I will,” echoed Bill dutifully.

Kirk could not trust himself to speak again. The old sensation of choking had come back to him. The room was a blur.

He caught Bill to him in a grip that made the child cry out, held him for a long minute, then put him gently down and made blindly for the door.

===Chinese===
比尔那双睁大的眼睛和哆嗦的小嘴提醒他：在这五个字的悲剧里，卷进去的不止他一个人。他振作起来。比尔正急切地等着他的问题的答案。犯不着让比尔提前难过。

「当然见得着。」他说，竭力让声音听起来欢快些。

「我当然见得着。」比尔乖乖地跟着说。

柯克不敢担保自己还能开口。那种喉咙哽住的老感觉又回来了。房间成了一片模糊。

他一把将比尔紧紧搂住，搂得孩子叫出声来，抱了长长的一分钟，然后轻轻把他放下，恍恍惚惚地朝门口走去。

===Original===
The storm had burst by the time Kirk found himself in the street. The thunder crashed and great spears of lightning flashed across the sky. A few heavy drops heralded the approach of the rain, and before he had reached the corner it was beating down in torrents.

He walked on, raising his face to the storm, finding in it a curious relief. A magical coolness had crept into the air, and with it a strange calm into his troubled mind. He looked back at the scene through which he had passed as at something infinitely remote. He could not realize distinctly what had happened. He was only aware that everything was over, that with a few words he had broken his life into small pieces. Too impatient to unravel the tangled knot, he had cut it, and nothing could mend it now.

“Why?”

The rain had ceased as suddenly as it had begun. The sun was struggling through a mass of thin cloud over the park. The world was full of the drip and rush of water. All that had made the day oppressive and strained nerves to breaking point had gone, leaving peace behind. Kirk felt like one waking from an evil dream.

“Why did it happen?” he asked himself. “What made me do it?”

A distant rumble of thunder answered the question.

===Chinese===
柯克走到街上时，暴风雨已经发作了。雷声隆隆炸响，一道道巨大的闪电长矛划过天空。几颗沉甸甸的雨点先行宣告了大雨的来临，还没等他走到街角，倾盆大雨已经泼了下来。

他继续往前走，仰起脸迎着暴风雨，从中觉出一种奇异的解脱。一种魔法似的清凉潜入了空气，随之潜入他纷乱心底的，是一种奇特的平静。他回望自己刚刚穿过的那一幕，如同望着一件无限遥远的东西。他无法真切地意识到究竟发生了什么。他只知道一切都完了，只用寥寥数语，他把自己的生活摔成了碎片。那团乱麻，他不耐烦去一缕缕解开，而是一刀斩断了它——如今再没有什么能把它接上了。

「为什么？」

雨来得突然，停得也突然。太阳正在公园上空的一团薄云里挣扎。满世界都是水的滴沥与奔流。曾使这一天郁闷难当、把神经绷到断裂边缘的一切，都已散去，留下了安宁。柯克觉得自己像一个从噩梦中醒来的人。

「为什么会走到这一步？」他问自己。「是什么让我这么做的？」

远处一声隆隆的雷声，回答了这个问题。

## XII Dolls with Souls / 第十二章 有灵魂的玩偶

===Original===

Ruth had not seen Bailey since the afternoon when he had called to warn her against Basil Milbank. Whether it was offended dignity that kept him away, or merely pressure of business, she did not know.
That pressure of business existed, she was aware. The papers were full, and had been full for several days, of wars and rumours of wars down in Wall Street; and, though she understood nothing of finance, she knew that Bailey was in the forefront of the battle. Her knowledge was based partly on occasional references in the papers to the firm of Bannister & Co. and partly on what she heard in society.
She did not hear all that was said in society about Bailey’s financial operations﻿—which, as Bailey had the control of her money, was unfortunate for her. The manipulation of money bored her, and she had left the investing of her legacy entirely to Bailey. Her father, she knew, had always had a high opinion of Bailey’s business instincts, and that was good enough for her.
She could not know how completely revolutionized the latter’s mind had become since the old man’s death, and how freedom had turned him from a steady young man of business to a frenzied financier.

===Chinese===

自那天下午贝利登门提醒她提防巴兹尔·米尔班克之后，露丝就再没有见过他。究竟是他那份受了冒犯的尊严使他却步，还是仅仅因为公务缠身，她不得而知。
公务缠身确有其事，这一点她是知道的。连日来，报上满篇都是华尔街那边「打仗和打仗的风声」；她虽对金融一窍不通，却也明白贝利正身处战局的最前沿。她这一份了解，一半得自报上偶尔提及的班尼斯特公司，一半得自社交场中的耳闻。
社交场上关于贝利金融运作的议论，她并未听全——这于她实在不幸，因为她的钱财正由贝利一手掌管。摆弄金钱令她厌倦，她索性把那笔遗产的投资全权交给了贝利。她知道，父亲生前一向对贝利的生意头脑评价极高，有这一条，于她便已足够。
她无从知道，老人去世之后，贝利的头脑已经翻了个彻底的身；更无从知道，自由如何把一个循规蹈矩的青年生意人，变成了一名狂热的金融赌徒。

===Original===

It was common report now that Bailey was taking big chances. Some went so far as to say that he was “asking for it,” “it” in his case being presumably the Nemesis which waits on those who take big chances in an uncertain market. It was in the air that he was “going up against” the Pinkey-Dowd group and the Norman-Graham combination, and everybody knew that the cemeteries of Wall Street were full of the unhonoured graves of others who in years past had attempted to do the same.
Pinkey, that sinister buccaneer, could have eaten a dozen Baileys. Devouring aspiring young men of the Bailey type was Norman’s chief diversion.
Ruth knew nothing of these things. She told herself that it was her abruptness that had driven Bailey away.

===Chinese===

如今人人都说，贝利正下大注冒险。有人索性说他是在「自讨苦吃」——就他而言，这「苦吃」多半便是指那位专候在不稳市况里下大注冒险者身后的复仇女神（Nemesis）了。风传他这是要去碰一碰平基-多德集团（Pinkey-Dowd group）和诺曼-格雷厄姆联合（Norman-Graham combination），而人人都知道，华尔街的墓园里早已填满了历年胆敢行此一举的先人，一座座坟茔，无名无主。
平基——那个阴鸷的海盗——就是再吃一打贝利也绰绰有余。而把贝利这路野心勃勃的年轻人当点心吞掉，正是诺曼的头号消遣。
这些事露丝一概不知。她只认定，是自己说话唐突，把贝利气走了。

===Original===

Weariness and depression had settled on Ruth since that afternoon of the storm. It was as if the storm had wrought an awakening in her. It had marked a definite point of change in her outlook. She felt as if she had been roused from a trance by a sharp blow.
If Steve had but known, she had had the “jolt” by which he set such store. She knew now that she had thrown away the substance for the shadow.
Kirk’s anger, so unlike him, so foreign to the weak, easygoing person she had always thought him, had brought her to herself. But it was too late. There could be no going back and picking up the threads. She had lost him, and must bear the consequences.
The withdrawal of Bailey was a small thing by comparison, a submotive in the greater tragedy. But she had always been fond of Bailey, and it hurt her to think that she should have driven him out of her life.
It seemed to her that she was very much alone now. She was marooned on a desert island of froth and laughter. Everything that mattered she had lost.

===Chinese===

自那个暴风雨的下午起，疲惫与消沉便落在了露丝身上。仿佛那场暴风雨在她心里催出了一场觉醒，在她的处世之见上划出分明的转折之点。她只觉自己像被人迎头一记猛击，从恍惚的迷梦里打醒了。
史蒂夫若是有幸知道，定会说她总算挨了他素来看重的那记「猛击」。她如今明白，自己抛下的是实体，捞起的不过是影子。
柯克的震怒——那样不像他素日的为人，那样全然有别于她一贯以为的那个软弱随和的人——把她打回了神。可是太迟了。再不能回头去接那些断了的线。她已失去了他，只得自吞苦果。
两相比较，贝利的抽身只是件小事，是大悲剧里的一条次要动机。可她素来喜欢贝利，一想到竟是自己把他从生活里撵了出去，心里便隐隐作痛。
她只觉得自己眼下形单影只到了极处。她被抛逐在一座浮沫与欢笑筑成的荒岛上。凡是真正要紧的东西，她都已尽数失去。

===Original===

Even Bill had gone from her. The bitter justice of Kirk’s words came home to her now in her time of clear thinking. It was all true. In the first excitement of the new life he had bored her. She had looked upon Mrs. Porter as a saviour who brought her freedom together with an easy conscience. It had been so simple to deceive herself, to cheat herself into the comfortable belief that all that could be done for him was being done, when, as concerned the essential thing, as Kirk had said, there was no child of the streets who was not better off.
She tramped her round of social duties mechanically. Everything bored her now. The joy of life had gone out of her. She ate the bread of sorrow in captivity.
And then, this morning, had come a voice from the world she had lost﻿—little Mrs. Bailey’s voice, small and tearful.

===Chinese===

连比尔也从她身边失去了。在这头脑清明的时刻，柯克那番话中苦涩的公道，如今字字敲在她心上。那全是真的。新生活初来的兴奋里，他曾令她厌倦。她把波特夫人看作救星，给她带来自由，还捎带一份心安理得。自欺竟是这样容易：她哄着自己，舒舒服服地相信能替孩子做的都已做到了——而在那件最要紧的事上，正如柯克所说，街上随便哪个野孩子都比他过得强。
她机械地巡回履行着社交义务。如今什么都叫她厌倦。生活的欢愉已离她而去。她在幽禁中吃着忧愁的面包。
而后，就在今天早晨，一个声音从她已失去的那个世界里传来——贝利小太太的声音，细细的，含着哭腔。

===Original===

Could she possibly come out by the next train? Bailey was very ill. Bailey was dying. Bailey had come home last night looking ghastly. He had not slept. In the early morning he had begun to babble﻿—Mrs. Bailey’s voice had risen and broken on the word, and Ruth at the other end of the wire had heard her frightened sobs. The doctor had come. The doctor had looked awfully grave. The doctor had telephoned to New York for another doctor. They were both upstairs now. It was awful, and Ruth must come at once.
This was the bad news which had brought about the pallor which had impressed Mr. Keggs as he helped Ruth into her cab.
Little Mrs. Bailey was waiting for her on the platform when she got out of the train. Her face was drawn and miserable. She looked like a beaten kitten. She hugged Ruth hysterically.
“Oh, my dear, I’m so glad you’ve come. He’s better, but it has been awful. The doctors have had to *fight* him to keep him in bed. He was crazy to get to town. He kept saying over and over again that he must be at the office. They gave him something, and he was asleep when I left the house.”

===Chinese===

她能不能搭下一班火车赶来？贝利病得厉害。贝利要死了。贝利昨夜回到家时脸色吓人。他一夜未曾合眼。今天一清早他开始说起胡话——说到这个词，贝利太太的声音陡然拔高、随即哽住，露丝在电话那头听见她惊惶的抽泣。医生来过了。医生的脸色沉重极了。医生又打电话到纽约另请了一位医生。这会儿他们俩都在楼上。真是太可怕了，露丝务必马上就来。
便是这桩凶讯，映出了那阵苍白——凯格斯先生扶露丝上马车时看在眼里的那阵苍白。
她走下火车时，贝利小太太正在月台上等她。她一脸愁苦，面容扭曲，活像一只挨了打的小猫。她歇斯底里地搂住露丝。
「哦，亲爱的，你来了就好了。他好些了，可先前真是吓死人。医生们只好*强按*着他，才把他按在床上。他一门心思疯着要进城，翻来覆去只说他必须到办公室去。他们给他用了点药，我离家的时候他已经睡着了。」

===Original===

She began to cry helplessly. The fates had not bestowed upon Sybil Bannister the same care in the matter of education for times of crisis which they had accorded to Steve’s Mamie. Her life till now had been sheltered and unruffled, and disaster, swooping upon her, had found her an easy victim.
She was trying to be brave, but her powers of resistance were small like her body. She clung to Ruth as a child clings to its mother. Ruth, as she tried to comfort her, felt curiously old. It occurred to her with a suggestion almost of grotesqueness that she and Sybil had been debutantes in the same season.
They walked up to the house. The summer cottage which Bailey had taken was not far from the station. On the way, in the intervals of her sobs, Sybil told Ruth the disjointed story of what had happened.
Bailey had not been looking well for some days. She had thought it must be the heat or business worries or something. He had not eaten very much, and he had seemed too tired to talk when he got home each evening. She had begged him to take a few days’ rest. That had been the only occasion in the whole of the last week when she had heard him laugh; and it had been such a horrid, ugly sort of laugh that she wished she hadn’t.

===Chinese===

她又束手无策地哭了起来。命运女神们对西比尔·班尼斯特，从没像对史蒂夫的玛米那样，在危难关头的教养上头下过同样的工夫。她至今的人生一路有人遮风挡雨、波澜不惊，灾祸凌空扑下，轻易便将她擒获。
她竭力想勇敢些，可她的抵抗力同她的身量一样小。她偎着露丝，像孩子偎着母亲。露丝一面安慰她，一面觉出一种奇异的苍老。她蓦地想起，自己同西比尔竟是同一年进入社交界的——这念头几乎透着几分荒诞。
她们步行回宅。贝利租下的避暑别墅离车站不远。一路上，西比尔在抽泣的间隙里，断断续续把事情的始末讲给露丝听。
好几天了，贝利的气色一直不好。她原以为无非是暑热，或是生意上的烦心事，或是诸如此类的什么。他吃得很少，每晚到家也累得懒于开口。她求过他歇上几天。那竟是整个上礼拜里她唯一一回听他笑；而那笑又那样难听、那样狰狞，倒叫她宁愿没听见过。

===Original===

He had said that if he stayed away from the office for some time to come it would mean love in a cottage for them for the rest of their lives﻿—and not a summer cottage at Tuxedo at that. “ ‘My dear child,’ ” he had gone on, “and you know when Bailey calls me that,” said Sybil, “it means that there is something the matter; for, as a rule, he never calls me anything but my name, or baby, or something like that.”
Which gave Ruth a little shock of surprise. Somehow the idea of the dignified Bailey addressing his wife as baby startled her. She was certainly learning these days that she did not know people as completely as she had supposed. There seemed to be endless sides to people’s characters which had never come under her notice. A sudden memory of Kirk on that fateful afternoon came to her and made her wince.
Mrs. Bailey continued: “ ‘My dear child,’ he went on, ‘this week is about the most important week you and I are ever likely to live through. It’s the showdown. We either come out on top or we blow up. It’s one thing or the other. And if I take a few days’ holiday just now you had better start looking about for the best place to sell your jewellery.’
“Those were his very words,” she said tearfully. “I remember them all. It was so unlike his usual way of talking.”
Ruth acknowledged that it was. More than ever she felt that she did not know the complete Bailey.
“He was probably exaggerating,” she said for the sake of saying something.

===Chinese===

他说，往后若再离开办公室些时日，他们后半辈子就只好去过「茅屋里的爱情」了——而且还是不带塔克西多（Tuxedo）避暑小屋的那一种。「『亲爱的孩子，』」他接下去又说，「而且你知道，」西比尔说，「贝利一这么叫我，准是出了什么事；因为平素他除了叫我的名字，就只叫我宝宝，或是这一类的叫法。」
这话让露丝小小吃了一惊。庄重的贝利竟唤太太作宝宝——这念头不知怎的叫她错愕。这些天她着实领教了：自己对人的了解，远不如自以为的那样透彻。人的性情里似乎藏着数不尽的侧面，从来不曾入过她的眼。柯克在那个决定命运的下午的模样蓦地浮上来，她不由得瑟缩了一下。
贝利太太继续道：「『亲爱的孩子，』他接下去说，『这个礼拜，差不多是你我这辈子最要紧的礼拜了。是摊牌的时候了。咱们要么压倒他们，要么一起炸上天。非此即彼。这会儿我要是歇上几天假，你最好趁早去打听打听，哪家铺子收珠宝最划算。』」
「他就是这么说的，一字不差，」她眼里含着泪说，「我全记得。那口气，同他平常说话太不一样了。」
露丝承认确是如此。她比以往任何时候都更觉得，自己并不识得一个完整的贝利。
「他多半是夸大其词吧。」她为着说句话而说。

===Original===

Sybil was silent for a moment.
“It isn’t that that’s worrying me,” she went on then. “Somehow I don’t seem to care at all whether we come out right or not, so long as he gets well. Last night, when I thought he was going to die, I made up my mind that I couldn’t go on living without him. I wouldn’t have, either.”
This time the shock of surprise which came to Ruth was greater by a hundredfold than the first had been. She gave a quick glance at Sybil. Her small face was hard, and the little white teeth gleamed between her drawn lips. It was the face, for one brief instant, of a fanatic. The sight of it affected Ruth extraordinarily. It was as if she had seen a naked soul where she had never imagined a soul to be.
She had weighed Sybil in the same calm, complacent almost patronizing fashion in which she had weighed Bailey, Kirk, everybody. She had set her down as a delightful child, an undeveloped, featherbrained little thing, pleasant to spend an afternoon with, but not to be taken seriously by anyone as magnificent and superior as Ruth Winfield. And what manner of a man must Bailey be, Bailey whom she had always looked on as a dear, but as quite a joke, something to be chaffed and made to look foolish, if he was capable of inspiring love like this?
A wave of humility swept over her. The pygmies of her world were springing up as giants, dwarfing her. The pinnacle of superiority on which she had stood so long was crumbling into dust.

===Chinese===

西比尔沉默了片刻。
「叫我心里难受的倒不是这个，」她随后又开了口，「也不知怎么，只要他能好起来，我们到底是输是赢，我半点儿也不在乎。昨天夜里，我以为他就要死了，我便拿定了主意：没有他，我活不成。我也真会那么做的。」
这一回露丝所受的震动，比先前那次大了百倍。她飞快地瞥了西比尔一眼。那张小脸绷得紧紧的，抿住的唇间闪出一排细白的小牙齿。有那么短短一瞬，那是一张狂信者的脸。这景象给露丝的触动异乎寻常。她仿佛在一处从未想到会有灵魂的所在，蓦然看见了一个赤裸裸的灵魂。
她掂量西比尔，用的正是她掂量贝利、柯克、以及世人的那副平静、自得、近乎居高临下的做派。她给她下的断语是：一个可人的孩子，一个未长成的、满脑子绒毛的小东西，同她消磨一个下午固然愉快，但在露丝·温菲尔德这般光彩夺目、高人一等的人物眼里，断断当不得真。那么贝利又该是个怎样的人物呢——贝利，那个她一向视作亲爱的小丑、专门用来打趣出洋相的活宝——倘若他竟能激起这样的爱？
一阵谦卑之情席卷了她。她那个世界里的侏儒正纷纷长成巨人，反把她衬成了矮子。她久踞其上的那座优越绝伦的巅峰，正簌簌崩作尘土。

===Original===

She was finding herself. She winced again as the thought stabbed her that she was finding herself too late.
They reached the house in silence, each occupied with her own thoughts. The defiant look had died out of Sybil’s face and she was once more a child, crying because unknown forces had hurt it. But Ruth was not looking at her now.
She was too busy examining this new world into which she had been abruptly cast, this world where dolls had souls and jokes lost their point.

===Chinese===

她正在找回自己。可刀子似的又一个念头扎了进来：这自己找得太迟了——她重新瑟缩了一下。
她们默默走到宅前，各怀心事。那副决绝的神色已从西比尔脸上褪尽，她又还原成一个孩子，只因被莫名的力量弄痛了而啼哭。可露丝这会儿没有看她。
她正忙着审视这个把她猛然掷入的新世界——一个玩偶也有灵魂、而玩笑不再可笑的世界。

===Original===

At the cottage good news awaited them. The crisis was past. Bailey was definitely out of danger. He was still asleep, and sleeping easily. It had just been an ordinary breakdown, due to worrying and overwork, said the doctor, the bigger of the doctors, the one who had been summoned from New York.
“All your husband needs now, Mrs. Bannister, is rest. See that he is kept quiet. That’s all there is to it.”
As if by way of a commentary on his words, a small boy on a bicycle rode up with a telegram.
Sybil opened it. She read it, and looked at Ruth with large eyes.
“From the office,” she said, handing it to her.
Ruth read it. It was a CQD, an SOS from the front; an appeal for help from the forefront of the battle. She did not understand the details of it, but the purport was clear. The battle had begun, and Bailey was needed. But Bailey lay sleeping in his tent.

===Chinese===

别墅里有好消息正等着她们。危机过去了。贝利确已脱离险境。他仍睡着，睡得安稳。这不过是忧劳交攻之下寻常的一次垮倒，医生说——两位医生里块头大的那位，就是从纽约请来的那一位。
「班尼斯特太太，您先生眼下只需静养。务必别让他劳神。要说的就这么些。」
仿佛特意要给他的话作个注脚，一个骑自行车的男孩蹬着车送来一封电报。
西比尔拆开电报，读了，睁着一双大眼睛望向露丝。
「办公室打来的。」她说着把电报递过去。
露丝读了。那是一封 CQD（呼救电），一封来自前线的 SOS（求救信号）；是鏖战最前沿发出的求援。个中细节她不懂，可大意清楚：大战已经开场，贝利不可或缺。而贝利正躺在他的帐篷里酣睡。

===Original===

She handed it back in silence. There was nothing to be done.
The second telegram arrived half an hour after the first. It differed from the first only in its greater emphasis. Panic seemed to be growing in the army of the lost leader.
The ringing of the telephone began almost simultaneously with the arrival of the second telegram. Ruth went to the receiver. A frantic voice was inquiring for Mr. Bannister even as she put it to her ear.
“This is Mrs. Winfield speaking,” she said steadily, “Mr. Bannister’s sister. Mr. Bannister is very ill and cannot possibly attend to any business.”
There was a silence at the other end of the wire. Then a voice, with the calm of desperation, said: “Thank you.” There was a pause. “Thank you,” said the voice again in a crushed sort of way, and the receiver was hung up. Ruth went back to Sybil.

===Chinese===

她默默把电报递了回去。无可奈何。
第二封电报在头一封之后半小时抵达。它与头一封的分别，只在语气更急。这支群龙无首的军队里，恐慌似乎正越酿越浓。
电话铃几乎与第二封电报同时响起。露丝走向听筒。她刚把听筒凑近耳边，一个狂乱的声音已经在喊班尼斯特先生。
「我是温菲尔德太太，」她从容地说，「班尼斯特先生的妹妹。班尼斯特先生病得很重，无论如何料理不了任何事务。」
线路那端静了片刻。而后一个声音，带着绝望者才有的平静，说道：「谢谢您。」顿了顿。「谢谢您。」那声音又说了一遍，蔫蔫的，随即听筒被挂上了。露丝回到西比尔身边。

===Original===

The hours passed. How she got through them Ruth hardly knew. Time seemed to have stopped. For the most part they sat in silence. In the afternoon Sybil was allowed to see Bailey for a few minutes. She returned thoughtful. She kissed Ruth before she sat down, and once or twice after that Ruth, looking up, found her eyes fixed upon her. It seemed to Ruth that there was something which she was trying to say, but she asked no questions.
After dinner they sat out on the porch. It was a perfect night. The cool dusk was soothing.
Ruth broke a long silence.
“Sybil!”
“Yes, dear?”
“May I tell you something?”

===Chinese===

几个钟头挨了过去。露丝几乎不知道自己是怎么熬过来的。时间仿佛停了。她们多半时候对坐无言。午后，西比尔获准去见贝利几分钟。她回来时若有所思。坐下之前她吻了吻露丝，此后有一两回，露丝抬头，正撞见她的目光定定落在自己身上。露丝觉得她有话要说，却没有问。
晚饭后，她们坐在屋外的游廊上。夜色再好不过。清凉的薄暮沁人心脾。
露丝打破了久长的沉默。
「西比尔！」
「什么事，亲爱的？」
「我有件事告诉你，可以吗？」

===Original===

“Well?”
“I’m afraid it’s bad news.”
Sybil turned quickly.
“You called up the office while I was with Bailey?”
Ruth started.
“How did you know?”

===Chinese===

「你说。」
「恐怕是个坏消息。」
西比尔霍地转过身来。
「我在贝利房里的时候，你给办公室挂了电话？」
露丝一怔。
「你怎么知道的？」

===Original===

“I guessed. I have been trying to do it all day, but I hadn’t the pluck. Well?”
“I’m afraid things are about as bad as they can be. A Mr. Meadows spoke to me. He was very gloomy. He told me a lot of things which I couldn’t follow, details of what had happened, but I understood all that was necessary, I’m afraid﻿—”
“Bailey’s ruined?” said Sybil quietly.
“Mr. Meadows seemed to think so. He may have exaggerated.”
Sybil shook her head.
“No. Bailey was talking to me upstairs. I expected it.”

===Chinese===

「我猜的。我倒想打这一整天呢，只是没这份胆量。怎么样？」
「恐怕事情坏得不能再坏了。一位米多斯先生（Mr. Meadows）同我讲的。他沮丧得很。他说了一大堆我听不明白的事，出了什么事的种种细节，可是该懂的我全懂了，恐怕——」
「贝利垮台了？」西比尔静静地说。
「米多斯先生看来是这么想的。他也许言过其实。」
西比尔摇了摇头。
「不会言过其实的。贝利在楼上同我说过了。我早有这个准备。」

===Original===

There was a long silence.
“Ruth.”
“Yes?”
“I’m afraid﻿—”
Sybil stopped.
“Yes?”

===Chinese===

又是长久的沉默。
「露丝。」
「嗯？」
「我怕——」
西比尔停住了。
「嗯？」

===Original===

A sudden light of understanding came to Ruth. She knew what it was that Sybil was trying to say, had been trying to say ever since she spoke with Bailey.
“My money has gone, too? Is that it?”
Sybil did not answer. Ruth went quickly to her and took her in her arms.
“You poor baby,” she cried. “Was that what was on your mind, wondering how you should tell me? I knew there was something troubling you.”
Sybil began to sob.
“I didn’t know how to tell you,” she whispered.

===Chinese===

露丝心头霎时透亮。她知道西比尔想说的、从同贝利谈过话起就一直想说而没说出口的是什么了。
「我的钱也赔进去了？是这话吗？」
西比尔没有回答。露丝快步走到她跟前，将她搂进怀里。
「你这可怜的小宝贝，」她喊道，「你心里悬的就是这个？一直琢磨着该怎么对我说？我早知道你有桩心事压着你。」
西比尔呜咽起来。
「我不知道怎么开口，」她低声说。

===Original===

Ruth laughed excitedly. She felt as if a great weight had been lifted from her shoulders﻿—a weight which had been crushing the life out of her. In the last few days the scales had fallen from her eyes and she had seen clearly.
She realized now what Kirk had realized from the first, that what had forced his life apart from hers had been the golden wedge of her father’s money. It was the burden of wealth that had weighed her down without her knowing it. She felt as if she had been suddenly set free.
“I’m dreadfully sorry,” said Sybil feebly.
Ruth laughed again.
“I’m not,” she said. “If you knew how glad I was you would be congratulating me instead of looking as if you thought I was going to bite you.”

===Chinese===

露丝激动地笑了。她只觉仿佛一副千斤重担从肩上卸了下来——一副险些把她整个人压瘪了的重担。这几日，她眼上的鳞片纷纷剥落，她看清了一切。
她如今明白了柯克从第一天起就明白的事：硬把他的生活从她的生活里劈出去的，正是她父亲那笔钱的金楔子。是财富的重负把她压得沉了下去，她本人却始终不察。她只觉自己仿佛蓦地被松了绑。
「真是抱歉极了。」西比尔有气无力地说。
露丝又笑了。
「我可一点不，」她说，「你要知道我有多高兴，这会儿你早该向我道贺了，何至于摆出这副样子，倒像怕我要咬你一口。」

===Original===

“Glad!”
“Of course I’m glad. Everything’s going to be all right again now. Sybil dear, Kirk and I had the most awful quarrel the other day. We﻿—we actually decided it would be better for us to separate. It was all my fault. I had neglected Kirk, and I had neglected Bill, and Kirk couldn’t stand it any longer. But now that this has happened, don’t you see that it will be all right again? You can’t stand on your dignity when you’re up against real trouble. If this had not happened, neither of us would have had the pluck to make the first move; but now, you see, we shall just naturally fall into each other’s arms and be happy again, he and I and Bill, just as we were before.”
“It must be lovely for you having Bill,” said little Mrs. Bailey wistfully. “I wish﻿—”
She stopped. There was a corner of her mind into which she could not admit anyone, even Ruth.
“Having him ought to have been enough for any woman.” Ruth’s voice was serious. “It was enough for me in the old days when we were at the studio. What fools women are sometimes! I suppose I lost my head, coming suddenly into all that money﻿—I don’t know why; for it was not as if I had not had plenty of time, when father was alive, to get used to the idea of being rich. I think it must have been the unexpectedness of it. I certainly did behave as if I had gone mad. Goodness! I’m glad it’s over and that we can make a fresh start.”

===Chinese===

「高兴！」
「我当然高兴。这下一切又要好起来了。西比尔，亲爱的，前几天柯克同我大吵了一场，吵得可怕极了。我们——我们竟当真商量好了，还是分开过的好。全怪我。我冷落了柯克，也冷落了比尔，柯克再受不住了。可如今出了这桩事，你不明白吗，一切都会好起来的？人当着真正的祸事，是端不起架子的。若不是出了这桩事，我们俩谁也没那份胆量先迈一步；可现在你瞧，我们自自然然就会重新落进彼此怀里，重新快活起来——他，我，还有比尔，同从前一模一样。」
「身边有个比尔，你多有福气啊，」贝利小太太满怀想望地说，「但愿我——」
她住了口。她心里有个角落，谁也放不进去，连露丝也不行。
「有这样的孩子，对哪个女人本该就是足够的了。」露丝的声音很郑重。「从前我们住画室那阵子，他于我便是足够的。女人有时候多傻啊！我想是那一大笔钱来得太骤，把我冲昏了头——我也说不上为什么；因为父亲在世时，我原有大把光阴去习惯做个有钱人的念头。想必是那份猝不及防闹的。我那阵子的做派，实在跟疯了没什么两样。天哪！总算过去了，我们还能重新开头，我真高兴。」

===Original===

“What is it like being poor, Ruth? Of course, we were never very well off at home, but we weren’t really poor.”
“It’s heaven if you’re with the right man.”
Mrs. Bailey sighed.
“Bailey’s the right man, as far as I’m concerned. But I’m wondering how he will bear it, poor dear.”
Ruth was feeling too happy herself to allow anyone else to be unhappy if she could help it.

===Chinese===

「露丝，穷人过日子是什么滋味？我们家从前自然也说不上宽裕，可到底不算真穷。」
「只要跟的是对的男人，那便是天堂。」
贝利太太叹了口气。
「就我来说，贝利正是那个对的男人。我只是惦记着，他可怎么受得住，可怜的亲人。」
露丝自己快活得很，只要力所能及，便不忍让旁人接着愁苦。

===Original===

“Why, of course he will be splendid about it,” she said. “You’re letting your imagination run away with you. You have got the idea of Bailey and yourself as two broken creatures begging in the streets. I don’t know how badly Bailey will be off after this smash, but I do know that he will have all his brains and his energy left.”
Ruth was conscious of a momentary feeling of surprise that she should be eulogizing Bailey in this fashion, and﻿—stranger still﻿—that she should be really sincere in what she said. But today seemed to have changed everything, and she was regarding her brother with a newborn respect. She could still see Sybil’s face as it had appeared in that memorable moment of self-revelation. It had made a deep impression upon her.
“A man like Bailey is worth a large salary to anyone, even if he may not be able to start out for himself again immediately. I’m not worrying about you and Bailey. You will have forgotten all about this crash this time next year.” Sybil brightened up. She was by nature easily moved, and Ruth’s words had stimulated her imagination.
“He *is* awfully clever,” she said, her eyes shining.
“Why, this sort of thing happens every six months to anybody who has anything to do with Wall Street,” proceeded Ruth, fired by her own optimism. “You read about it in the papers every day. Nobody thinks anything of it.”

===Chinese===

「哎呀，他当然会拿得起放得下，漂亮得很，」她说，「你这是任着想象撒蹄子跑了。你满脑子都是你和贝利沦作两个残躯断臂沿街讨饭的光景。这一跤摔下来贝利究竟会落到哪一步，我不知道；可我知道，他的脑子和干劲，一根毫毛也少不了。」
露丝一时有些诧异，自己竟这样褒扬贝利——更奇的是，说的句句出自真心。可今天似乎把一切都改变了，她正怀着一种新生的敬意打量自己的哥哥。西比尔那张脸，在那个自我剖白的难忘一刻里的模样，依旧历历在目。那给了她极深的印象。
「贝利这样的人，给谁做事都值一份厚薪，哪怕一时半刻还开不得自己的铺子。你和贝利，我才不犯愁呢。到明年这时候，这场崩盘你们早忘得干干净净了。」西比尔神色一振。她天生容易受感染，露丝这番话把她的想象也点燃了。
「他*确实*聪明极了。」她说，眼睛亮亮的。
「再说，但凡同华尔街沾边的人，哪半年不摊上这么一回？」露丝被自己的乐观烧得正旺，接着说道，「这种事报上天天登，谁也不当回事。」

===Original===

Sybil, though anxious to look on the bright side, could not quite rise to these heights of scorn for the earthquake which had shaken her world.
“I hope not. It would be awful to go through a time like this again.”
Ruth reassured her, though it entailed a certain inconsistency on her part. She had a true woman’s contempt for consistency.
“Of course you won’t have to go through it again. Bailey will be careful in future not to﻿—not to do whatever it is that he has done.”
She felt that the end of her inspiring speech was a little weak, but she did not see how she could mend it. Her talk with Mr. Meadows on the telephone had left her as vague as before as to the actual details of what had been happening that day in Wall Street. She remembered stray remarks of his about bulls, and she had gathered that something had happened to something which Mr. Meadows called G.R.D.’s, which had evidently been at the root of the trouble; but there her grasp of high finance ended.
Sybil, however, was not exigent. She brightened at Ruth’s words as if they had been an authoritative pronouncement from an expert.

===Chinese===

西比尔虽一心想往亮处看，可对那场把她整个世界摇撼过的地震，终究登不上这般鄙夷的高处。
「但愿别再有了。再熬这么一回，可真要人的命。」
露丝又安慰了她几句，虽说这话不免自相矛盾。对于「始终如一」，她怀有的正是一个地道女人的轻蔑。
「你们自然不会再熬第二回了。往后贝利自会当心，再不——再不干出那件不管是什么的营生来。」
她自觉这篇鼓舞人心的高论收尾收得软了些，却也想不出法子补救。同米多斯先生在电话里的那番话，并没让她对当天华尔街究竟出了什么事多明白半分。她记得他零星说到过什么多头（bulls），又依稀听出有个他叫作 G.R.D.'s 的东西出了点什么事，那显然正是祸根；她对高等金融的全部把握，到此为止。
好在西比尔并不苛细。露丝的话音一落，她便喜上眉梢，仿佛这是哪位行家权威的裁断。

===Original===

“Bailey is sure to do right,” she said. “I think I’ll creep in and see if he’s still asleep.”
Ruth, left alone on the porch, fell into a pleasant train of thought. There was something in her mental attitude which amused her. She wondered if anybody had ever received the announcement of financial ruin in quite the same way before. Yet to her this attitude seemed the only one possible.
How simple everything was now! She could go to Kirk and, as she had said to Sybil, start again. The golden barrier between them had vanished. One day had wiped out all the wretchedness of the last year. They were back where they had started, with all the accumulated experience of those twelve months to help them steer their little ship clear of the rocks on its new voyage.

===Chinese===

「贝利一定拿得出办法，」她说，「我想这会儿就溜进去，瞧瞧他还睡不睡。」
露丝独自留在游廊上，思绪淌进一条惬意的河道。她此刻的心境里有点什么，连她自己都觉着好笑。她不知道世上可曾有人这样接过破产的凶信。可在她，这份心境又仿佛是唯一可能的。
如今一切多么简单！她可以回到柯克身边，像她对西比尔说的那样，从头再来。横亘在两人之间的黄金壁垒消失了。一天的工夫，便把这一整年的愁苦抹了个干净。他们又回到了出发的地方，还添上那十二个月攒下的全部阅历，好扶着他们的小船，在新航程上绕开礁石。

---

===Original===

She was roused from her dream by the sound of an automobile drawing up at the door. A voice that she recognised called her name. She went quickly down the steps.
“Is that you, Aunt Lora?”
Mrs. Porter, masterly woman, never wasted time in useless chatter.
“Jump in, my dear,” she said crisply. “Your husband has stolen William and eloped with that girl Mamie (whom I never trusted) to Connecticut.”

===Chinese===

一阵汽车停在门前的声响把她从梦里惊起。一个她认得出声音的人唤着她的名字。她快步走下台阶。
「是您吗，洛拉姑妈？」
波特夫人，干练的女人，从不在无谓的闲话上虚耗光阴。
「上车吧，亲爱的，」她干脆利落地说，「你丈夫拐走了威廉，带着玛米那丫头（我从没信过她）私奔到康涅狄格去了。」

## I Empty-Handed / 第一章 空手而归

===Original===

The steamship *Santa Barbara*, of the United Fruit Line, moved slowly through the glittering water of the bay on her way to dock. Out at quarantine earlier in the morning there had been a mist, through which passing ships loomed up vague and shapeless; but now the sun had dispersed it and a perfect May morning welcomed the *Santa Barbara* home.
Kirk leaned on the rail, looking with dull eyes on the city he had left a year before. Only a year! It seemed ten. As he stood there he felt an old man.
A drummer, a cheery soul who had come aboard at Puerto Rico, sauntered up, beaming with well-being and good-fellowship.
“Looks pretty good, sir,” said he.
Kirk did not answer. He had not heard.
“Some burg,” ventured the drummer.
Again encountering silence, he turned away, hurt. This churlish attitude on the part of one returning to God’s country on one of God’s own mornings surprised and wounded him.
To him all was right with the world. He had breakfasted well; he was smoking a good cigar; and he was strong in the knowledge that he had done well by the firm this trip and that bouquets were due to be handed to him in the office on lower Broadway. He was annoyed with Kirk for having cast even a tiny cloud upon his contentment.

===Chinese===

联合果品公司（United Fruit Line）航线的轮船*圣巴巴拉号*（Santa Barbara），正缓缓犁开湾中粼粼的波光，朝码头驶去。清晨在外港检疫区的时候，海上还笼着一层薄雾，过往船只从雾里浮现出来，影影绰绰、不成形状；此刻太阳已把雾气驱散，一个完美无瑕的五月清晨，正迎接*圣巴巴拉号*归家。
柯克倚着船栏，用一双黯淡的眼睛，望着这座他一年前离开的城市。不过一年！却恍如十年。站在那儿，他觉得自己已经是个老人。
一个在波多黎各（Puerto Rico）上船的旅行推销员——位乐呵呵的先生——溜达过来，红光满面，一团和气。
「这地方看着挺不赖吧，先生。」他说。
柯克没有答话。他压根儿没听见。
「好一座大城。」推销员又试着搭了句腔。
再次碰了哑巴亏，他受了伤似的转身走开了。人家明明是在上帝自个儿的早晨，回上帝自个儿的国度，竟摆出这么一副爱答不理的架势，这叫他既吃惊又痛心。
在他看来，这世上样样都顺遂。他早饭吃得好；抽着一支上好的雪茄；而且他心里笃定得很：这一趟为公司立了大功，回下百老汇（Broadway）的办公室，一准有人给他捧花道贺。柯克往他这份心满意足上投了哪怕一小片阴云，他自然要着恼。

===Original===

He communicated his feelings to the third officer, who happened to come on deck at that moment.
“Say, who *is* that guy?” he asked complainingly. “The big son of a gun leaning on the rail. Seems like he’d got a hangover this morning. Is he deaf and dumb or just plain grouchy?”
The third officer eyed Kirk’s back with sympathy.
“I shouldn’t worry him, Freddie,” he said. “I guess if you had been up against it like him you’d be shy on the small talk. That’s a fellow called Winfield. They carried him on board at Colon. He was about all in. Got fever in Colombia, inland at the mines, and nearly died. His pal did die. Ever met Hank Jardine?”
“Long, thin man?”
The other nodded.
“One of the best. He made two trips with us.”
“And he’s dead?”
“Died of fever away back in the interior, where there’s nothing much else except mosquitoes. He and Winfield went in there after gold.”

===Chinese===

他把这番感受讲给了三副——那位恰在此时上了甲板。
「哎，那家伙*到底*是什么人？」他抱怨道。「就是倚着船栏的那个大块头。瞧那样子，今早像是宿醉还没醒。他是又聋又哑呢，还是干脆脾气坏？」
三副带着同情，打量着柯克的背影。
「我要是你，就不去招他，弗雷迪（Freddie），」他说。「换了你遭过他那号罪，怕也懒得讲闲话。那位先生叫温菲尔德，是在科隆（Colon）被他们抬上船的，来的时候差不多垮掉了。他在哥伦比亚的内陆矿上害了热病，险些死掉。他那位伙伴是真死了。你见过汉克·贾丁没有？」
「瘦高条儿的？」
三副点了点头。
「顶好的一个人。他搭我们的船跑过两趟。」
「他死了？」
「死在内陆老远的地方，热病送了命——那地方除了蚊子，旁的东西也不多见。他和温菲尔德就是奔金子才进去的。」

===Original===

“Did they get any?” asked the drummer, interested.
The third officer spat disgustedly over the rail.
“You ask Winfield. Or, rather, don’t, because I guess it’s not his pet subject. He told me all about it when he was getting better. There was gold there, all right, in chunks. It only needed to be dug for. And somebody else did the digging. Of all the skin games! It made me pretty hot under the collar, and it wasn’t *me* that was stung.
“Out there you can’t buy land if you’re a foreigner; you have to lease it from the natives. Poor old Hank leased his bit, all right, and when he’d got to his claim he found somebody else working on it. It seemed there had been a flaw in his agreement and the owners had let it over his head to these other guys, who had slipped them more than what Hank had done.”
“What did he do?”
“He couldn’t do anything. They were the right side of the law, or what they call law out there. There was nothing to do except beat it back again three hundred miles to the coast. That’s where they got the fever which finished Hank. So you can understand,” concluded the third officer, “that Mr. Winfield isn’t in what you can call a sunny mood. If I were you, I’d go and talk to someone else, if conversation’s what you need.”

===Chinese===

「他们挖到了没有？」推销员来了兴致。
三副一脸厌恶，朝船栏外啐了一口。
「你去问温菲尔德吧。哦，还是别问了，我料想这算不得他心爱的话题。他养病的时候，原原本本都讲给我听了。金子那儿确实有，成块成块的，只消有人去挖。可挖的偏偏是别人。天底下竟有这样的骗局！连我这不相干的人都火冒三丈，挨宰的又不是*我*。」
「在那边，外国人要买地是买不成的，只能向土著租。可怜的老汉克，他那块地是租妥了的，等赶到自己的矿地上，却发现别人正在上头开干。原来他的契约里出了纰漏，地主们背着他把地又租给了另一伙人——那几位塞过去的钱，比汉克给的足。」
「那他怎么办？」
「他能有什么办法。人家站在法律那一边——或者说，站在那边所谓的法律那一边。除了再一路退回三百英里回到海岸，没有第二条路。他们俩的热病就是在那一路上招上的，汉克的命也是它要去的。所以你该明白，」三副归结道，「温菲尔德先生此刻的心情，实在算不上晴朗。我要是你，要是想聊天，就找别人聊去。」

===Original===

Kirk stood motionless at the rail, thinking. It was not what was past that occupied his thoughts, as the third officer had supposed; it was the future.
The forlorn hope had failed; he was limping back to Ruth wounded and broken. He had sent her a wireless message. She would be at the dock to meet him. How could he face her? Fate had been against him, it was true, but he was in no mood to make excuses for himself. He had failed. That was the beginning and the end of it. He had set out to bring back wealth and comfort to her, and he was returning empty-handed.
That was what the immediate future held, the meeting with Ruth. And after? His imagination was not equal to the task of considering that. He had failed as an artist. There was no future for him there. He must find some other work. But he was fit for no other work. He had no training. What could he do in a city where keenness of competition is a tradition? It would be as if an unarmed man should attack a fortress.
The thought of the years he had wasted was very bitter. Looking back, he could see how fate had tricked him into throwing away his one talent. He had had promise. With hard work he could have become an artist, a professional﻿—a man whose work was worth money in the open market. He had never had it in him to be a great artist, but he had had the facility which goes to make a good worker of the second class. He had it still. Given the time for hard study, it was still in him to take his proper place among painters.
But time for study was out of his reach now. He must set to work at once, without a day’s delay, on something which would bring him immediate money. The reflection brought his mind back abruptly to the practical consideration of the future.
Before him, as he stood there, the ragged battlements of New York seemed to frown down on him with a cold cruelty that paralysed his mind. He had seen them a hundred times before. They should have been familiar and friendly. But this morning they were strange and sinister. The skyline which daunts the emigrant as he comes up the bay to his new home struck fear into Kirk’s heart.

===Chinese===

柯克一动不动地立在船栏边，想着心事。占住他心思的不是过去——三副猜错了——而是将来。
那场孤注一掷的拼搏失败了；他带着内伤外创，一步一瘸地退回露丝身边来。他已经给她发了无线电报。她会到码头上来接他。他拿什么去见她？命运诚然与他作对，可他全无心绪替自己开脱。他败了。这就是全部，从头到尾。他当初立志要给她带回财富与安逸，如今却在两手空空地往回走。
眼面前的将来就是这个：与露丝相见。往后呢？他的想象力担不起这桩思量。作为艺术家，他失败了。那上面再没有他的前程。他必须另找别的活计。可别的活计他一样也干不来。他没有受过训练。在一座以竞争酷烈为传统的城市里，他能做什么呢？那就好比叫一个手无寸铁的人去攻打一座堡垒。
一想起那些白白荒掷的年头，他心里就泛起苦味。回头看去，他瞧得分明：命运是怎样诓骗着他，哄他亲手扔掉了自己唯一的天分。他本来是有指望的。只要肯下苦功，他本可以成为一名画家，一个职业画家——一个作品在公开市场上真金白银值钱的人。他天生做不成大画家，可他具备那股手上的娴熟，足以造就一名出色的二流画匠。这功夫如今还在。只要给他潜心苦学的时间，他仍旧能在画家之列挣得应有的一席。
可是苦学的光阴，如今已非他所能企及。他必须立刻动手干活，一天也不能耽搁，干一桩能立刻换来钱的事。这一转念，把他的心思猛地拽回到对将来的实际盘算上。
他站在那儿，眼前纽约那道参差如雉堞的楼影，仿佛正以一种冷冷的残暴朝他蹙眉俯压，慑得他的头脑都麻痹了。这样的楼影他见过一百回了，本该熟稔而亲切。可是今天早晨，它们陌生而阴森。那条天际线，移民顺着海湾驶向新家园时望之生畏的天际线，此刻也把恐惧扎进了柯克心里。

===Original===

He turned away and began to walk up and down the deck.
He felt tired and lonely. For the first time he realized just what it meant to him that he should never see Hank again. It had been hard, almost impossible, till now to force his mind to face that fact. He had winced away from it. But now it would not be avoided. It fell upon him like a shadow.
Hank had filled a place of his own in Kirk’s life. Theirs had been one of those smooth friendships which absence cannot harm. Often they had not seen each other for months at a time. Indeed, now that he thought of it, Hank was generally away; and he could not remember that they had ever exchanged letters. Yet even so there had been a bond between them which had never broken. And now Hank had dropped out.
Kirk began to think about death. As with most men of his temperament, it was a subject on which his mind had seldom dwelt, never for any length of time. His parents had died when he was too young to understand; and circumstances had shielded him from the shadow of the great mystery. Birth he understood; it had forced itself into the scheme of his life; but death till now had been a stranger to him.
The realization of it affected him oddly. In a sense, he found it stimulating; not stimulating as birth had been, but more subtly. He could recall vividly the thrill that had come to him with the birth of his son. For days he had walked as one in a trance. The world had seemed unreal, like an opium-smoker’s dream. There had been magic everywhere.
But death had exactly the opposite effect. It made everything curiously real﻿—himself most of all. He had the sensation, as he thought of Hank, of knowing himself for the first time. Somehow he felt strengthened, braced for the fight, as a soldier might who sees his comrade fall at his side.

===Chinese===

他转过身，开始在甲板上走来走去。
他又疲倦，又孤单。今生再也见不到汉克了——这话对他意味着什么，他是头一回真切地掂量出来。直到此刻，要强迫自己的心去正视这件事，一直是难的，几乎不可能。他一直缩身躲着它。可如今它躲不掉了。它像一道影子似的，罩落在他身上。
汉克在柯克的生命里，自占着一席之地。他们那份交情，属于离别磨损不了的一种。两人常常一连几个月见不上一面。真的，此刻想起来，汉克照例总在远处；他也记不得他们两个什么时候通过信。然而即便如此，他们之间系着一根纽带，从来没有断过。如今，汉克退场了。
柯克开始想死亡这件事。大多数与他同性情的人一样，这是个他的头脑很少驻留的题目，从不曾久留。他父母去世时他年纪太小，还不懂得是什么意思；种种境遇又一直把他护在那桩大奥秘的影子之外。出生，他是懂得的；它硬挤进了他的人生格局里来；可死亡，直到今天，于他还是个陌生人。
死亡这一层领悟，在他身上激起的反应颇为古怪。在某种意义上，他觉得它令人振奋；不像出生那种振奋法，而是更微妙的一种。儿子出生时他得到的那阵战栗，他还能活生生地记起来。好几天里，他走路都像踩在梦里。世界显得不真实，像鸦片客的一场梦。到处都是魔法。
死亡的效用却恰恰相反。它使一切都出奇地真实起来——他自己尤其如此。一想到汉克，他便有一种头一回认识了自己的感觉。不知怎么，他觉得身上添了力量，抖擞起来迎向战斗，像一个眼见身旁战友倒下的士兵。

===Original===

There was something almost vindictive in the feeling that came to him. It was too vague to be analysed, but it filled him with a desire to fight, gave him a sense of determination of which he had never before been conscious. It toughened him, and made the old, easygoing Kirk Winfield seem a stranger at whom he could look with detachment and a certain contempt.
As he walked back along the deck the battlements of the city met his gaze once more. But now they seemed less formidable.
In the leisurely fashion of the homecoming ship the *Santa Barbara* slid into her dock. The gangplank was thrust out. Kirk walked ashore.
For a moment he thought that Ruth had not come to meet him. Then his heart leaped madly. He had seen her.

===Chinese===

涌上他心头的这股情绪里，几乎带着一点报复的意味。它太模糊，无从剖析，可它把他灌满了求战的欲望，给了他一种从未意识到过的决绝。它把他炼硬了，叫那个旧日闲散的柯克·温菲尔德，看上去倒像个陌生人，他可以置身事外地打量他，还带着几分轻蔑。
他沿着甲板踱回来，城市的雉堞又一次撞进他的眼帘。可这一回，它们显得没那么狰狞了。
*圣巴巴拉号*以归航船只的从容，滑进了泊位。跳板搭了出来。柯克走上岸。
有一瞬，他以为露丝没有来接他。随即他的心狂跳起来。他看见她了。
---

===Original===

There are worse spots in the world than the sheds of the New York customs, but few more desolate; yet to Kirk just then the shadowy vastness seemed a sunlit garden. A flame of happiness blazed up in his mind, blotting out in an instant the forebodings which had lurked there like evil creatures in a dark vault. The future, with its explanations and plans, could take care of itself. Ruth was a thing of the present.
He put his arms round her and held her. The friendly drummer, who chanced to be near, observed them with interest and a good deal of pleasure. The third officer’s story had temporarily destroyed his feeling that all was right with the world, and his sympathetic heart welcomed this evidence that life held compensations even for men who had been swindled out of valuable goldmines.
“I guess he’s not feeling so worse, after all,” he mused, and went on his way with an easy mind to be fawned upon by his grateful firm.
Ruth was holding Kirk at arm’s length, her eyes full of tears at the sight.
“You poor boy, how thin you are!”
“I had fever. It’s an awful place for fever out there.”
“Kirk!”
“Oh, I’m all right now. The voyage set me up. They made a great fuss over me on board.”

===Chinese===

世上比纽约海关货棚更糟的地方有的是，可比它更荒凉的却不多；然而此刻在柯克眼里，这片幽暗空旷竟像一座洒满阳光的花园。一团幸福的火焰在他心里轰然烧起，霎时间把那些预感——它们像黑暗地窖里的恶物一样潜伏在那里——烧了个干净。将来，连同它那一堆解释和盘算，自个儿去操心自个儿吧。露丝是眼面前的。
他伸开双臂把她搂住了。那位友善的推销员恰好在近旁，饶有兴味、也满心欢喜地观看了这一幕。三副讲的故事一时毁掉了他「世界万事顺遂」的好心境，此刻他那颗富于同情的心欣然领受了这份证据：生活纵使叫人被骗走了值钱的金矿，也自有补偿。
「我瞧他到底也不像难受得那么厉害。」他这样寻思着，心安理得地上路了，去领受那家感恩的公司对他的殷勤奉承。
露丝把柯克放在一臂开外端详着，目睹此状，她眼里噙满了泪水。
「可怜的孩子，你瘦成什么样了！」
「我害了热病。那边是个闹热病的地方。」
「柯克！」
「哦，我如今没事了。这一程航行把我养了回来。船上他们把我伺候得周到极了。」

===Original===

Ruth’s hand was clinging to his arm. He squeezed it against his side. It was wonderful to him, this sense of being together again after these centuries of absence. It drove from his mind the thought of all the explanations which sooner or later he had got to make. Whatever might come after, he would keep this moment in his memory golden and untarnished.
“Don’t you worry about me,” he said. “Now that I’ve found you again I’m feeling better than I ever did in my life. You wait till you see me sparring with Steve tomorrow. By the way, how is Steve?”
“Splendid.”
“And Bill?”
Ruth drew herself up haughtily.
“You dare to ask about your son after Steve? How clumsy that sounds! I mean you dare to put Steve before your son. I believe you’ve only just realized that you have a son.”
“I’ve only just realized there’s anybody or anything in the world except my wife.”
“Well, after that I suppose I’ve got to forgive you. Since you have asked after Bill at last, I may tell you that he’s very well indeed.”

===Chinese===

露丝的手攀着他的胳膊不放。他把它贴在自己身侧紧紧握住。这一场世纪般漫长的离别之后重又相守，这感觉于他真是妙不可言。它把他心里那些迟早躲不掉的解释，一时全赶了出去。往后无论怎样，他都要把这一刻存进记忆，让它金光灿灿，永不蒙尘。
「你别替我操心，」他说。「这会儿重新找着了你，我觉得比这辈子哪一天都好。你等着瞧，明天我跟史蒂夫过上几招给你看。对了，史蒂夫怎么样？」
「好极了。」
「那比尔呢？」
露丝傲然把身子一挺。
「你竟敢问完了史蒂夫才问你的儿子？这话听着多别扭！我是说，你竟敢把史蒂夫摆在儿子前头。我看你只怕才刚刚想起来自己有个儿子。」
「我只是刚刚才明白，这世上除了我的太太，居然还有别人、还有别的东西。」
「好吧，话说到这份上，我只好饶了你。你总算问到比尔了，那我不妨告诉你，他实在好得很。」

===Original===

Kirk’s eyes glowed.
“He ought to be a great kid by now.”
