“He is.”
“And Mamie? Have you still got her?”
“I wouldn’t lose her for a million.”

===Chinese===

柯克两眼放光。
「他现在该是个了不起的小家伙了吧。」
「正是。」
「那玛米呢？还用着她吗？」
「一百万我也不肯放她走。」

===Original===

“And Whiskers?”
“I’m afraid Whiskers is gone.”
“Not dead?”
“No. I gave him away.”
“For Heaven’s sake! Why?”
“Well, dear, the fact is, I’ve come around to Aunt Lora’s way of thinking.”
“Eh?”
“About germs.”

===Chinese===

「那『小胡子』呢？」
「只怕『小胡子』已经不在了。」
「死了？」
「没有。我把他送人了。」
「我的天！为什么？」
「这个么，亲爱的，实情是，我改信了洛拉姑妈那一套想法。」
「嗯？」
「关于细菌。」

===Original===

Kirk laughed, the first real laugh he had had for a year.
“That insane fad of hers!”
Ruth was serious.
“I have,” she said. “We’re taking a great deal more care of Bill than in the old days. I hate to think of the way I used to let him run around wild then. He might have died.”
“What nonsense! He was simply bursting with health all the time.”
“I had a horrible shock after you left,” Ruth went on. “The poor little fellow was awfully ill with some kind of a fever. The doctor almost gave him up.”
“Good heavens!”
“Aunt Lora helped me to nurse him, and she made me see how I had been exposing him to all sorts of risks, and﻿—well, now we guard against them.”

===Chinese===

柯克笑了——这是他一年来头一场发自真心的笑。
「她那套走火入魔的怪念头！」
露丝却是一本正经。
「我是当真的，」她说。「如今我们照看比尔，比从前仔细了不知多少。一想起从前我竟放任他满世界野跑，我就后怕。他险些儿就没命了。」
「胡说！他那时候从头到脚健康得都要胀破了。」
「你走以后我受了一场大惊吓，」露丝接着说。「可怜的小家伙得了不知什么热病，病得好重。医生几乎都要放弃他了。」
「老天爷！」
「是洛拉姑妈帮我护理他的。她让我看清了自己从前怎样把他暴露在种种风险底下，于是——嗯，如今我们都一一提防着了。」

===Original===

There was a silence.
“I grew to rely on her a great deal, Kirk, when you were away. You know I always used to before we were married. She’s so wonderfully strong. And then when your letters stopped coming﻿—”
“There aren’t any postal arrangements out there in the interior. It was the worst part of it﻿—not being able to write to you or hear from you. Heavens, what an exile I’ve been this last year! Anything may have happened!”
“Perhaps something has,” said Ruth mysteriously.
“What do you mean?”
“Wait and see. Oh, I know one thing that has happened. I’ve been looking at you all this while trying to think what it was. You’ve grown a beard, and it looks perfectly horrid.”
“Sheer laziness. It shall come off this very day. I knew you would hate it.”
“I certainly do. It makes you look so old.”
Kirk’s face clouded.
“I feel old.”

===Chinese===

有一阵子谁都没说话。
「你不在家的时候，柯克，我渐渐事事都倚重她了。你知道，结婚以前我一向就是这样。她那个人坚强得出奇。后来，你的信又一封封断了来——」
「那内陆深处压根儿没有邮政这回事。那是最难熬的一桩——不能给你写信，也听不到你的音信。天哪，这一年我流放得多远啊！什么事都可能有！」
「也许真有件事。」露丝神秘地说。
「你什么意思？」
「等着瞧吧。哦，对了，有一件事确实发生了。我这半天一直瞧着你，就想不起来到底是什么。你蓄了胡子，难看极了。」
「纯粹是懒。今天当天就把它刮掉。我就知道你讨厌它。」
「岂止是讨厌。它叫你显得那么老。」
柯克的脸阴了下来。
「我也觉得自己老。」

===Original===

For the first time since he had left the ship the memory of Hank had come back to him. The sight of Ruth had driven it away, but now it swept back on him. The golden moment was over. Life with all its troubles and its explanations and its burdening sense of failure must be faced.
“What’s the matter?” asked Ruth, startled by the sudden change.
“I was thinking of poor old Hank.”
“Where is Mr. Jardine? Didn’t he come back with you?”
“He’s dead, dear,” said Kirk gently. “He died of fever while we were working our way back to the coast.”
“Oh!”
It was the idea of death that shocked Ruth, not the particular manifestation of it. Hank had not touched her life. She had begun by disliking him and ended by feeling for him the tolerant sort of affection which she might have bestowed upon a dog or a cat. Hank as a man was nothing to her, and she could not quite keep her indifference out of her voice.
It was only later, when he looked back on this conversation, that Kirk realized this. At the moment he was unconscious of it, significant as it was of the fact that there were points at which his mind and Ruth’s did not touch.
When Ruth spoke again it was to change the subject.

===Chinese===

下船以来，汉克的影子头一回又回到他心里。见到露丝时，它曾被赶走，此刻却又整个席卷回来。金色的时刻结束了。生活，连同它全部的烦恼、它要作的种种解释、它那压得人喘不过气的挫败感，都必须面对了。
「怎么了？」露丝被这骤然的变化吓了一跳，问道。
「我在想可怜的老汉克。」
「贾丁先生呢？他没跟你一道回来？」
「他去世了，亲爱的。」柯克轻声说。「我们一路往海岸挣回去的时候，他害热病死了。」
「哦！」
震动了露丝的是死亡这个念头本身，而不是它落在这一个人身上。汉克不曾进过她的生活。她起先厌恶他，到头来对他也不过是怀着一种宽容的喜爱，就像可能给一条狗、一只猫的那种。汉克作为一个人，于她什么都不是，因此她的话音里，怎么也免不了带出一丝淡漠。
柯克是后来回顾这段谈话时，才明白这一点的。当时他毫无所觉，尽管此事意味深长——它说明，他和露丝的心思之间，有些地方是碰不到一处去的。
露丝再开口时，已经换了话题。

===Original===

“Well, Kirk,” she said, “have you come back with your trunk crammed with nuggets? You haven’t said a word about the mine yet, and I’m dying to know.”
He groaned inwardly. The moment he had been dreading had arrived more swiftly than he had expected. It was time for him to face facts.
“No,” he said shortly.
Ruth looked at him curiously. She met his eyes and saw the pain in them, and intuition told her in an instant what Kirk, stumbling through his story, could not have told her in an hour. She squeezed his arm affectionately.
“Don’t tell me,” she said. “I understand. And it doesn’t matter. It doesn’t matter a bit.”
“Doesn’t matter? But﻿—”
Ruth’s eyes were dancing.
“Kirk, dear, I’ve something to tell you. Wait till we get outside.”
“What do you mean?”
“You’ll soon see?”

===Chinese===

「哎，柯克，」她说，「你是揣着一箱子金块回来的吗？矿上的事你还一个字没提呢，我都快急死了。」
他心里暗暗叫苦。他一直怕着的这个时刻，来得比他预想的还快。该面对事实的时候到了。
「没有。」他简短地说。
露丝好奇地看着他。她迎上他的眼睛，看见了里面的痛楚；直觉霎时告诉她的，比柯克磕磕绊绊讲一个钟头能讲清的还多。她怜爱地紧了紧他的胳膊。
「别说出来，」她说。「我明白。那不要紧。一点儿也不要紧。」
「不要紧？可是——」
露丝的眼睛雀跃地转了起来。
「柯克，亲爱的，我有件事要告诉你。等出去以后再说。」
「你是什么意思？」
「很快你就知道了？」

===Original===

They went out into the street. Against the kerb a large red automobile was standing. The chauffeur touched his cap as he saw them. Kirk stared at him dumbly.
“In you get, dear,” said Ruth.
She met his astonished gaze with a smile of triumph. This was her moment, the moment for which she had been waiting. The chauffeur started the machine.
“I don’t understand. Whose car is this?”
“Mine. Yours. Ours. Oh, Kirk, darling, I was so afraid that you would come back bulging with a fortune that would make my little one look like nothing. But you haven’t, you haven’t, and it’s just splendid.” She caught his hand and pressed it. “It’s simply sweet of you to look so astonished. I was hoping you would. This car belongs to us, and there’s another just as big besides, and a house, and﻿—oh﻿—everything you can think of. Kirk, dear, we’ve nothing to worry us any longer. We’re rich!”

===Chinese===

他们走到街上。路缘石边停着一辆硕大的红色汽车。司机一见他们便碰了碰帽檐。柯克哑然望着他。
「上车吧，亲爱的。」露丝说。
她迎着他惊愕的目光，绽出一个得胜的微笑。这是她的时刻，她一直等着的那一刻。司机发动了车子。
「我不明白。这是谁的车？」
「我的。你的。咱们的。哦，柯克，亲爱的，我先前真怕你回来时鼓鼓囊囊装着一大笔财，把我这一小份比得没了影儿。可你没有，你没有，这可太好了。」她抓住他的手，紧紧一握。「你这副大吃一惊的样子，真是可爱极了。我就盼着这样。这辆车是咱们家的，此外还有一辆一样大的，还有一幢房子，还有——哦——你想到的一切。柯克，亲爱的，咱们再也没有什么可发愁的了。咱们发财了！」

## XI Mr. Penway on the Grill / 第十一章 佩尼韦先生受审

===Original===

Fate moves in a mysterious way. Luck comes hand in hand with misfortune. What we lose on the swings we make up on the roundabouts. If Keggs had not seen twenty-five of his hard-earned dollars pass at one swoop into the clutches of the croupier at the apparently untenanted house on Forty-First Street, and become disgusted with the pleasing game of roulette, he might have delayed his return to the house on Fifth Avenue till a later hour; in which case he would have missed the remarkable and stimulating spectacle of Kirk driving to the door in an automobile with Mamie at his side; of Mamie, jumping out and entering the house; of Mamie leaving the house with a suitcase; of Kirk helping her into the automobile, and of the automobile disappearing with its interesting occupants up the avenue at a high rate of speed.
Having lost his money, as stated, and having returned home, he was enabled to be a witness, the only witness, of these notable events, and his breast was filled with a calm joy in consequence. This was something special. This was exclusive, a scoop. He looked forward to the return of Mrs. Porter with an eagerness which, earlier in the day, he would have considered impossible. Somehow Ruth did not figure in his picture of the delivery of the sensational news that Mr. Winfield had eloped with the young person engaged to look after her son. Mrs. Porter’s was one of those characters which monopolize any stage on which they appear. Besides, Keggs disliked Mrs. Porter, and the pleasure of the prospect of giving her a shock left no room for other thoughts.
It was nearly seven o’clock when Mrs. Porter reached the house. She was a little tired from the journey, but in high good humour. She had had a thoroughly satisfactory interview with her publishers﻿—satisfactory, that is to say, to herself; the publishers had other views.
“Is Mrs. Winfield in?” she asked Keggs as he admitted her.
Ruth was always sympathetic about her guerrilla warfare with the publishers. She looked forward to a cosy chat, in the course of which she would trace, step by step, the progress of the late campaign which had begun overnight and had culminated that morning in a sort of Gettysburg, from which she had emerged with her arms full of captured flags and all the other trophies of conquest.

===Chinese===

命运的行事方式神秘莫测。好运与厄运携手同行。在秋千上输掉的，到旋转木马上找补回来。倘若凯格斯没有眼睁睁看着自己辛苦挣来的二十五美元，在第四十一街那座看似无人的宅子里一口气落进轮盘赌荷官的魔爪，又没有因此对轮盘这项可人的游戏心生厌弃，他也许会把回第五大道那座宅子的时辰再往后拖一拖；若是那样，他就要错过那幕精彩提神的奇观了：柯克驾着汽车驶到门前，玛米坐在他身旁；玛米跳下车，走进宅子；玛米拎着一只手提箱走出宅子；柯克扶她上了汽车，然后那辆汽车载着两位耐人寻味的乘客，沿着大道一路风驰电掣，消失而去。
如前所述，钱既然输了，人也到家了，他便得以成为这一连串大事的见证人——唯一的见证人，胸中随即充满一种安详的喜悦。这可是特别的货色。这是独一份，是一条独家新闻。他盼着波特夫人回来，那份急切劲儿，要是放在当天早些时候，他自己都会觉得断无可能。不知怎么，在他设想播报那桩轰动新闻——温菲尔德先生同受雇照料她儿子的年轻姑娘私奔了——的场面时，露丝偏偏不在画面里。波特夫人属于那一类人物：不管在哪儿露面，整座舞台都归她一个人。何况凯格斯本就不喜欢波特夫人，一想到即将让她大吃一惊，那份快活便占满心胸，再容不下别的念头。
波特夫人回到宅子时，已近七点钟。旅途让她略有倦意，心情却极佳。她同出版商们谈了一回，谈得彻头彻尾地满意——所谓满意，是对她自己而言；出版商们另有一番见解。
「温菲尔德太太在家吗？」凯格斯开门迎她进去时，她问道。
对于她同出版商之间的游击战，露丝向来是同情的。她盼着来一场舒舒服服的闲谈，把那场刚刚打完的战役一步步细细回顾——那场仗头天夜里打响，当天上午以一场葛底斯堡式的大决战收尾，她从战场上凯旋，怀抱里满是缴获的旗帜和其余种种胜利的战利品。

===Original===

“No, madam,” said Keggs. “Mrs. Winfield has not yet returned.”
Keggs was an artist in tragic narration. He did not give away his climax; he led up to it by degrees as slow as his audience would permit.
“Returned? I did not know she intended to go away. Her yacht party is next week, I understand.”
“Yes, madam.”
“Where has she gone?”
“To Tuxedo, madam.”

===Chinese===

「没有，夫人，」凯格斯说。「温菲尔德太太还没有回来。」
凯格斯是惨事叙述这门艺术的行家。他不肯一上来就抖出高潮，而是一层一层往上垫，垫得多慢，全看听众容得下多慢。
「回来？我可不知道她要出门。据我了解，她的游艇出游是下礼拜的事。」
「是，夫人。」
「她去哪儿了？」
「去塔克西多了，夫人。」

===Original===

“Tuxedo?”
“Mrs. Winfield has just rung us up from there upon the telephone to request that necessaries for an indefinite stay be despatched to her. She is visiting Mrs. Bailey Bannister.”
If Mrs. Porter had been Steve, she would probably have said “For the love of Mike!” at this point. Being herself, she merely repeated the butler’s last words.
“If I may be allowed to say so, madam, I think that there must have been trouble at Mrs. Bannister’s. A telephone-call came from her very early this morning for Mrs. Winfield which caused Mrs. Winfield to rise and leave in a taximeter-cab in an extreme hurry. If I might be allowed to suggest it, it is probably a case of serious illness. Mrs. Winfield was looking very disturbed.”
“H’m!” said Mrs. Porter. The exclamation was one of disappointment rather than of apprehension. Sudden illnesses at the Bailey home did not stir her, but she was annoyed that her recital of the squelching of the publishers would have to wait.

===Chinese===

「塔克西多？」
「温菲尔德太太刚从那边打电话来，吩咐给她递送可供无限期居留的日用必需品。她正探访贝利·班尼斯特太太。」
波特夫人若是换了史蒂夫，此刻多半要来一声「看在迈克的份上！」。但她终究是她自己，只把男管家最后那句话重复了一遍。
「恕我斗胆进言，夫人，我想班尼斯特太太府上必定出了乱子。今天一大早，那边来了电话找温菲尔德太太，温菲尔德太太闻讯起身，坐着出租车火急火燎地走了。恕我斗胆揣测，多半是有人得了重病。温菲尔德太太当时神色十分不安。」
「哼！」波特夫人说。这声惊叹，失望的成分多于忧虑。贝利家里闹突然急病的事撼不动她，叫她气恼的是，压扁出版商的战绩汇报不得不往后搁了。

===Original===

She went upstairs. Her intention was to look in at the nursery and satisfy herself that all was well with William Bannister. She had given Mamie specific instructions as to his care on her departure; but you never knew. Perhaps her keen eye might be able to detect some deviation from the rules she had laid down.
It detected one at once. The nursery was empty. According to schedule, the child should have been taking his bath.
She went downstairs again. Keggs was waiting in the hall. He had foreseen this return. He had allowed her to go upstairs with his story but half heard because that appealed to his artistic sense. This story, to his mind, was too good to be bolted at a sitting; it was the ideal serial.
“Keggs.”
“Madam?”

===Chinese===

她上了楼。本打算到育儿室瞧一眼，亲眼确认威廉·班尼斯特一切安好。临行前，她就孩子的照管问题给玛米下过具体指示；可是谁说得准呢。没准她那双锐眼能查出什么越出她所定章程的地方。
果然一查便着。育儿室空空如也。按时刻表，这孩子此刻本该在洗澡。
她又下了楼。凯格斯正在门厅里候着。他早料到有这一趟回转。他方才故意让她带着只听了一半的故事上楼去，因为这正合他的艺术趣味。在他看来，这故事太好了，万万不可一口囫囵吞下；这是最理想的连载。
「凯格斯。」
「夫人？」

===Original===

“Where is Master William?”
“I fear I do not know, madam.”
“When did he go out? It is seven o’clock; he should have been in an hour ago.”
“I have been making inquiries, madam, and I regret to inform you that nobody appears to have seen Master William all day.”
“What?”
“It not being my place to follow his movements, I was unaware of this until quite recently, but from conversation with the other domestics, I find that he seems to have disappeared!”

===Chinese===

「威廉少爷在哪儿？」
「恕我不知，夫人。」
「他什么时候出去的？眼下七点钟了；一个钟头前他就该回来了。」
「我做了一番查询，夫人，很遗憾地禀报您，似乎一整天都没有人见过威廉少爷。」
「什么？」
「追踪他的行踪本非我分内之事，故而我直到方才才得知。不过同府里其他仆役交谈过后，我发现他似乎是失踪了！」

===Original===

“Disappeared?”
A glow of enjoyment such as he had sometimes experienced when the ticker at the Cadillac Hotel informed him that the man he had backed in some San Francisco fight had upset his opponent for the count began to permeate Keggs.
“Disappeared, madam,” he repeated.
“Perhaps Mrs. Winfield took him with her to Tuxedo.”
“No, madam. Mrs. Winfield was alone. I was present when she drove away.”

===Chinese===

「失踪了？」
一阵酣畅的快意开始在凯格斯周身弥漫——从前在凯迪拉克酒店，行情收报机传来消息，说他在旧金山某场拳赛里押中的那位拳手把对手打翻在地、数完了秒也没能爬起来，那时他也体味过这种快意。
「失踪了，夫人。」他重复道。
「兴许是温菲尔德太太带他一道去塔克西多了。」
「不是的，夫人。温菲尔德太太是独自一人。她动身乘车离开时，我在场。」

===Original===

“Send Mamie to me at once,” said Mrs. Porter.
Keggs could have whooped with delight had not such an action seemed to him likely to prejudice his chances of retaining a good situation. He contented himself with wriggling ecstatically. “The young person is not in the house, madam.”
“Not in the house? What business has she to be out? Where is she?”
“I could not tell you, madam.” Keggs paused, reluctant to deal the final blow, as a child lingers lovingly over the last lick of ice-cream in a cone. “I last saw her at about five o’clock, driving off with Mr. Winfield in an automobile.”
“What!”

===Chinese===

「马上叫玛米来见我。」波特夫人说。
若不是顾虑这样做恐怕会妨害他保住这份好差事的机会，凯格斯真要纵声欢呼了。他只满足于心醉神迷地扭了几扭。「那位年轻姑娘不在宅子里，夫人。」
「不在宅子里？她出去做什么？她人呢？」
「我说不上来，夫人。」凯格斯顿了顿，迟迟不肯发出最后一击，活像孩子恋恋不舍地舔着蛋卷冰淇淋的最后一口。「我最后一次见到她，约莫在五点钟，她同温菲尔德先生坐着一辆汽车走了。」
「什么！」

===Original===

Keggs was content. His climax had not missed fire. Its staggering effect was plain on the face of his hearer. For once Mrs. Porter’s poise had deserted her. Her one word had been a scream.
“She did not tell me her destination, madam,” went on Keggs, making all that could be made of what was left of the situation after its artistic finish. “She came in and packed a suitcase and went out again and joined Mr. Winfield in the automobile, and they drove off together.”
Mrs. Porter recovered herself. This was a matter which called for silent meditation, not for chitchat with a garrulous butler.
“That will do, Keggs.”
“Very good, madam.”

===Chinese===

凯格斯心满意足了。他的高潮没有哑火。那五雷轰顶的效果，明明白白写在听众脸上。波特夫人的镇定破天荒地弃她而去。她那一个字，是尖叫出来的。
「她没有告诉我她的目的地，夫人。」凯格斯接着说，趁这故事在艺术上功德圆满之后，把局面的剩余价值榨了个干净。「她进来收拾了一只手提箱，又出去了，同温菲尔德先生一起上了汽车，两人一道驱车走了。」
波特夫人回过神来。这是一桩需要静默思量的事，不是同一个饶舌的男管家闲磕牙的事。
「行了，凯格斯。」
「是，夫人。」

===Original===

Keggs withdrew to his pantry, well pleased. He considered that he had done himself justice as a raconteur. He had not spoiled a good story in the telling.
Mrs. Porter went to her room and sat down to think. She was a woman of action, and she soon reached a decision.
The errant pair must be followed, and at once. Her great mind, playing over the situation like a searchlight, detected a connection between this elopement and the disappearance of William Bannister. She had long since marked Kirk down as a malcontent, and she now labelled the absent Mamie as a snake in the grass who had feigned submission to her rule, while meditating all the time the theft of the child and the elopement with Kirk. She had placed the same construction on Mamie’s departure with Kirk as had Mr. Penway, showing that it is not only great minds that think alike.
A latent conviction as to the immorality of all artists, which had been one of the maxims of her late mother, sprang into life. She blamed herself for having allowed a nurse of such undeniable physical attractions to become a member of the household. Mamie’s very quietness and apparent absence of bad qualities became additional evidence against her now, Mrs. Porter arguing that these things indicated deep deceitfulness. She told herself, what was not the case, that she had never trusted that girl.

===Chinese===

凯格斯退回配膳室，心满意足。他认为，作为一名讲故事的人，自己这回总算尽展所长。他没有把一个好故事讲砸。
波特夫人回到自己房里，坐下来思索。她是说干就干的女人，很快就拿定了主意。
那对出逃的男女必须追查，而且立刻就追。她那颗伟大的头脑像探照灯似的把整个局面扫了一遍，当即察觉这桩私奔同威廉·班尼斯特的失踪之间有干系。她早就把柯克记作心怀不满的一号分子，如今又给不在眼前的玛米定了性：一条草丛里的蛇，表面上对她俯首听命，暗地里无时不在盘算着偷走孩子、同柯克私奔。她对玛米随柯克出走一事所下的判语，同佩尼韦先生如出一辙——可见「英雄所见略同」，并非伟大头脑的专利。
一条蛰伏的信念——凡艺术家无不道德败坏，这本是她已故母亲的箴言之一——此刻勃然苏醒。她责怪自己，竟容许一个肉体魅力如此昭彰的保姆进入这个家。玛米的安静，以及她表面上看不出的坏品性，如今反倒成了追加的罪证；波特夫人的道理是：这些东西恰恰表明其欺诈之深。她对自己说，她从来就没信任过那姑娘——这自然不是实情。

===Original===

But Lora Delane Porter was not a woman to waste time in retrospection. She had not been in her room five minutes before her mind was made up. It was improbable that Kirk and his guilty accomplice had sought so near and obvious a haven as the studio, but it was undoubtedly there that pursuit must begin. She knew nothing of his way of living at that retreat, but she imagined that he must have appointed some successor to George Pennicut as general factotum, and it might be that this person would have information to impart.
The task of inducing him to impart it did not daunt Mrs. Porter. She had a just confidence in her powers of cross-examination.
She went to the telephone and called up the garage where Ruth’s automobiles were housed. Her plan of action was now complete. If no information were forthcoming at the studio, she would endeavour to find out where Kirk had hired the car in which he had taken Mamie away. He would probably have secured it from some garage near by. But this detective work would be a last resource. Like a good general, she did not admit of the possibility of failing in her first attack.

===Chinese===

不过，洛拉·德莱恩·波特可不是肯把光阴虚掷在追悔上的女人。她进房不到五分钟，主意已定。柯克同他那罪责难逃的女同伙，未必会躲进画室这么个又近又扎眼的避风港，但追查无疑必须从那里开头。那处退居之地里的生活情形她一无所知，但她料想乔治·佩尼卡特走后，他总该安排了谁来接任打点一切的总杂役，而此人手头或许握有可供吐露的情报。
至于怎么逼他吐露，这份差事吓不倒波特夫人。她对自己盘问的本领，怀有充分有据的自信。
她走到电话机前，给存放露丝那些汽车的车库打了电话。她的行动计划至此完备。倘若画室那边问不出情报，她就设法查明柯克载走玛米的那辆汽车是在哪儿雇的。他多半是从附近哪家车行弄来的车。不过这类侦探活计是最后一招。正如一位良将，她不承认首度进攻有失手的可能。

===Original===

And, luck being with her, it happened that at the moment when she set out, Mr. Penway, feeling pretty comfortable where he was, abandoned his idea of going out for a stroll along Broadway and settled himself to pass the next few hours in Kirk’s armchair.
Mr. Penway’s first feeling when the bell rang, rousing him from his peaceful musings, was one of mild vexation. A few minutes later, when Mrs. Porter had really got to work upon him, he would not have recognized that tepid emotion as vexation at all.
Mrs. Porter wasted no time. She perforated Mr. Penway’s spine with her eyes, reduced it to the consistency of summer squash, and drove him before her into the studio, where she took a seat and motioned him to do the same. For a moment she sat looking at him, by way of completing the work of subjection, while Mr. Penway writhed uneasily on his chair and thought of past sins.
“My name is Mrs. Porter,” she began abruptly.

===Chinese===

而且天从人愿：恰在她动身的当口，佩尼韦先生正觉得眼下坐处相当受用，打消了出门沿百老汇遛弯儿的念头，安顿下来，预备在柯克的扶手椅里消磨接下来这几个钟头。
门铃响起，把他从安详的冥想中惊起时，佩尼韦先生的头一感觉是几分不快。几分钟后，等波特夫人真正对他下了手，他简直认不出刚才那点温吞的情绪也算得上不快。
波特夫人分秒不浪费。她用目光把佩尼韦先生的脊椎戳了个对穿，碾成西葫芦一般的软烂，然后像赶牲口似的把他赶在自己前头押进画室。她落了座，抬手示意他也照办。她坐着盯了他片刻，算是收尾这道驯服的工序；佩尼韦先生则在椅子上不安地扭动，心里默数着自己既往的罪愆。
「本人是波特夫人。」她劈头就说。

===Original===

“Mine’s Penway,” said the miserable being before her. It struck him as the only thing to say.
“I have come to inquire about Mr. Winfield.”
As she paused Mr. Penway felt it incumbent upon him to speak again.
“Dear old Kirk,” he mumbled.
“Nothing of the kind,” said Mrs. Porter sharply. “Mr. Winfield is a scoundrel of the worst type, and if you are as intimate a friend of his as your words imply, it does not argue well for your respectability.”
Mr. Penway opened his mouth feebly and closed it again. Having closed it, he reopened it and allowed it to remain ajar, as it were. It was his idea of being conciliatory.

===Chinese===

「本人姓佩尼韦。」她面前那可怜的活物说。他觉得，这是唯一说得出口的话。
「我来打听温菲尔德先生的事。」
她停了一停，佩尼韦先生觉得自己责无旁贷，得再开一回口。
「亲爱的老柯克。」他嗫嚅道。
「根本不是那么回事，」波特夫人厉声道。「温菲尔德先生是个头一等恶劣的恶棍，你若是像你的话里透出来的那样同他过从甚密，那你这人的体面，可就大有疑问了。」
佩尼韦先生有气无力地张开嘴，又合上。合上之后，又重新张开，由它那么半开半合地悬着——可以说是吧。这是他心目中表示好商好量的法子。

===Original===

“Tell me.” Mr. Penway started violently. “Tell me, when did you last see Mr. Winfield?”
“We went to Long Beach together this afternoon.”
“In an automobile?”
“Yes.”
“Ah! Were you here when Mr. Winfield left again?”
For the life of him Mr. Penway had not the courage to say no. There was something about this woman’s stare which acted hypnotically upon his mind, never at its best as early in the evening.

===Chinese===

「你说。」佩尼韦先生猛地一惊。「你说，你最后一次见到温菲尔德先生是什么时候？」
「今天下午我们一道去了长滩。」
「坐汽车去的？」
「是。」
「啊！温菲尔德先生再次动身的时候，你在这儿吗？」
佩尼韦先生说什么也提不起勇气答个「不」字。这女人的瞪视里自有一种东西，对他的心智施着催眠术——况且一到傍晚，他的心智本就从来不在最佳状态。

===Original===

He nodded.
“There was a young woman with him?” pursued Mrs. Porter.
At this moment Mr. Penway’s eyes, roving desperately about the room, fell upon the bottle of Bourbon which Kirk’s kindly hospitality had provided. His emotions at the sight of it were those of the shipwrecked mariner who see a sail. He sprang at it and poured himself out a stiff dose. Before Mrs. Porter’s disgusted gaze he drained the glass and then turned to her, a new man.
The noble spirit restored his own. For the first time since the interview had begun he felt capable of sustaining his end of the conversation with ease and dignity.

===Chinese===

他点了点头。
「他身边带着一位年轻姑娘？」波特夫人追问。
正在这时，佩尼韦先生那双在屋里拼命乱转的眼睛，落在了那瓶波本威士忌上——那是柯克好心的待客之谊备下的。一见此物，他心头涌起的情感，恰似落难的水手望见一片帆。他扑过去，给自己斟了结结实实的一剂。在波特夫人嫌恶的瞪视之下，他把杯中酒一饮而尽，然后转向她——已然脱胎换骨，换了个人。
这高贵的酒魂把他自己的魂还给了他。自这场会面开场以来，他头一回觉得，自己撑得住谈话的这一头，而且撑得从容、有尊严。

===Original===

“How’s that?” he said.
“There was a young woman with him?” repeated Mrs. Porter.
Mr. Penway imagined that he had placed her by this time. Here, he told himself in his own crude language, was the squab’s mother camping on Kirk’s trail with an axe. Mr. Penway’s moral code was of the easiest description. His sympathies were entirely with Kirk. Fortified by the Bourbon, he set himself resolutely to the task of lying wholeheartedly on behalf of his absent friend.
“No,” he said firmly.
“No!” exclaimed Mrs. Porter.

===Chinese===

「您说什么？」他说。
「他身边带着一位年轻姑娘？」波特夫人又问一遍。
佩尼韦先生自觉这会儿已经把她摸清了。用他自己那套粗话来说：这就是那小崽子的娘，拎着斧头沿柯克的行踪扎下了营。佩尼韦先生的道德准则属于最宽松的那一档。他的同情完全在柯克一边。有波本威士忌壮胆，他毅然着手替不在场的朋友全心全意地撒谎。
「没有。」他说得斩钉截铁。
「没有！」波特夫人失声叫道。

===Original===

“No,” repeated Mr. Penway with iron resolution. “No young woman. No young woman whatsoever. I noticed it particularly, because I thought it strange, don’t you know﻿—what I mean is, don’t you know, strange there shouldn’t be!”
How tragic is a man’s fruitless fight on behalf of a friend! For one short instant Mrs. Porter allowed Mr. Penway to imagine that the victory was his, then she administered the *coup de grâce*.
“Don’t lie, you worthless creature,” she said. “They stopped at my house on their way while the girl packed a suitcase.”
Mr. Penway threw up his brief. There are moments when the stoutest-hearted, even under the influence of old Bourbon, realize that to fight on is merely to fight in vain.
He condensed his emotions into four words.

===Chinese===

「没有。」佩尼韦先生以钢铁般的决心重申。「没有年轻姑娘。什么样的年轻姑娘都没有。这一点我特别留意过，因为我觉得奇怪，您知道吗——我是说，您知道吗，居然一个都没有，这才叫奇怪！」
一个人为朋友打的这场徒劳之战，何其悲壮！波特夫人容许佩尼韦先生有短短一瞬，以为胜利属于自己，随即施出了 *coup de grâce*（致命一击）。
「不许撒谎，你这废物东西，」她说。「他们路上在我家停了一站，好让那姑娘收拾一只手提箱。」
佩尼韦先生交出了辩护状。总有些时刻，纵然有陈年波本威士忌撑腰，连心肠最硬的人也会明白：再战下去，不过是枉自再战。
他把满腔情绪浓缩成四个字。

===Original===

“Of all the chumps!” he remarked, and, pouring himself out a further instalment of the raw spirit, he sat down, a beaten man.
Mrs. Porter continued to harry him.
“Exactly,” she said. “So you see that there is no need for any more subterfuge and concealment. I do not intend to leave this room until you have told me all you have to tell, so you had better be quick about it. Kindly tell me the truth in as few words as possible﻿—if you know what is meant by telling the truth.”
A belated tenderness for his dignity came to Mr. Penway.

===Chinese===

「傻到家了！」他说着，又给自己斟了生烈烈的一份原酒，坐了下来——一个被打垮了的人。
波特夫人继续对他穷追猛打。
「正是，」她说。「所以你明白，不必再支吾遮掩了。你不把该说的全说出来，我就不离开这间屋子，所以你最好抓紧些。劳驾，用尽可能少的话告诉我实话——如果你知道什么叫实话的话。」
一份迟来的、对自身尊严的怜惜之情，在佩尼韦先生心里油然而生。

===Original===

“You are insulting,” he remarked. “You are﻿—you are﻿—most insulting.”
“I meant to be,” said Mrs. Porter crisply. “Now. Tell me. Where has Mr. Winfield gone?”
Mr. Penway preserved an offended silence. Mrs. Porter struck the table a blow with a book which caused him to leap in his seat.
“Where has Mr. Winfield gone?”
“How should I know?”
“How should you know? Because he told you, I should imagine. Where﻿—has﻿—Mr.﻿—Winfield﻿—gone?”

===Chinese===

「你这是在侮辱人，」他说。「你是——你是——侮辱到极点了。」
「我就是要侮辱，」波特夫人脆生生地说。「现在。说吧。温菲尔德先生去哪儿了？」
佩尼韦先生保持着受了冒犯的沉默。波特夫人拿一本书猛击桌面一下，惊得他从椅子上蹦了起来。
「温菲尔德先生去哪儿了？」
「我哪儿知道？」
「你哪儿知道？我想，是因为他告诉过你吧。温——菲——尔——德——先——生——去——哪——儿——了？」

===Original===

“C’nnecticut,” said Mr. Penway, finally capitulating.
“What part of Connecticut?”
“I don’t know.”
“What part of Connecticut?”
“I tell you I don’t know. He said: ‘I’m off to Connecticut,’ and left.” It suddenly struck Mr. Penway that his defeat was not so overwhelming as he had imagined. “So you haven’t got much out of me, you see, after all,” he added.
Mrs. Porter rose.

===Chinese===

「康涅狄格。」佩尼韦先生说，终于缴械投降。
「康涅狄格的哪个地段？」
「我不知道。」
「康涅狄格的哪个地段？」
「我告诉你我不知道。他说了句『我这就去康涅狄格』，就走了。」佩尼韦先生忽然省悟，自己的败北并不像他想的那样一败涂地。「所以你瞧，到头来你也没从我嘴里套出多少东西。」他补了一句。
波特夫人站起身来。

===Original===

“On the contrary,” she said; “I have got out of you precisely the information which I required, and in considerably less time than I had supposed likely. If it interests you, I may tell you that Mr. Winfield has gone to a small house which he owns in the Connecticut woods.”
“Then what,” demanded Mr. Penway indignantly, “did you mean by keeping on saying ‘What part of C’nnecticut? What part of C’nnecticut? What part﻿—’ ”
“Because Mr. Winfield’s destination has only just occurred to me.” She looked at him closely. “You are a curious and not uninteresting object, Mr. Penway.”
Mr. Penway started. “Eh?”
“Object lesson, I should have said. I should like to exhibit you as a warning to the youth of this country.”

===Chinese===

「恰恰相反，」她说。「我从你嘴里套出来的，正是我所需要的情报，而且费时比我料想的还短了不少。你若有兴致听，我不妨奉告：温菲尔德先生去了他在康涅狄格林子里置的一座小房子。」
「那你，」佩尼韦先生愤然质问，「翻来覆去地问『康涅狄格哪个地段？康涅狄格哪个地段？哪个地——』算是什么意思？」
「因为温菲尔德先生的目的地，我也是刚刚才想出来。」她凑近细细打量他。「你是一件稀奇的、也算不得乏味的物件，佩尼韦先生。」
佩尼韦先生一激灵。「嗯？」
「应当说，是一件实物教材。我很乐意把你陈列出来，给这个国家的青年作个前车之鉴。」

===Original===

“What!”
“From the look of your frame I should imagine that you were once a man of some physique. Your shoulders are good. Even now a rigorous course of physical training might save you. I have known more helpless cases saved by firm treatment. You have allowed yourself to deteriorate much as did a man named Pennicut who used to be employed here by Mr. Winfield. I saved him. I dare say I could make something of you. I can see at a glance that you eat, drink, and smoke too much. You could not hold out your hand now, at this minute, without it trembling.”
“I could,” said Mr. Penway indignantly.

===Chinese===

「什么！」
「看你这副骨架，我猜你从前也算有过几分体格。你的肩膀不坏。哪怕到如今，一门严格的体育锻炼说不定还救得回你。我见过比这更没救的病例，是靠铁腕手段挽救回来的。你由着自己败坏下去，那份光景，同从前温菲尔德先生雇的那个姓佩尼卡特的差不多。我救过他。我说不定也能把你调教出个人样。我一眼就看出来，你吃得太多，喝得太凶，烟也抽得太狠。就现在，就这一分钟，你伸出手来，它要是不哆嗦才怪。」
「我就能。」佩尼韦先生愤然道。

===Original===

He held it out, and it quivered like a tuning-fork.
“There!” said Mrs. Porter calmly. “What do you expect? You know your own business best, I suppose, but I should like to tell you that if you do not become a teetotaller instantly, and begin taking exercise, you will probably die suddenly within a very few years. Personally I shall bear the calamity with fortitude. Good evening, Mr. Penway.”
For some moments after she had gone Mr. Penway sat staring before him. His eyes wore a glassy look. His mouth was still ajar.
“Damn woman!” he said at length.

===Chinese===

他伸出手来，那只手抖得像一支音叉。
「瞧见没！」波特夫人不动声色地说。「不这样还能怎样？我想，你自己的底细你自己最清楚，不过我愿意告诉你：你要是不立刻戒酒、开始锻炼，不出几年八成会猝死。就我本人而言，这场灾祸来了，我会以坚忍承受。晚安，佩尼韦先生。」
她走后好一会儿，佩尼韦先生还直挺挺坐着，眼睁睁望着前方。他的眼神呆滞发直。嘴还那么半张着。
「该死的女人！」他终于开了口。

===Original===

He turned to his meditations.
“Damn impertinent woman!”
Another interval for reflection, and he spoke again.
“Damn impertinent, interfering woman that!”
He reached out for the bottle of Bourbon and filled his glass. He put it to his lips, then slowly withdrew it.
“Damn impertinent, inter﻿—I wonder!”

===Chinese===

他转回去继续寻思。
「该死的、无礼的女人！」
又寻思了一阵，他再度开口。
「该死的、无礼的、多管闲事的女人！」
他伸手够到那瓶波本威士忌，斟满了酒杯。他把杯子举到唇边，又缓缓挪开。
「该死的、无礼的、多管闲——咦，还真难说！」

===Original===

There was a small mirror on the opposite wall. He walked unsteadily toward it and put out his tongue. He continued in this attitude for a time, then, with increased dejection, turned away.
He placed a hand over his heart. This seemed to depress him still further. Finally he went to the table, took up the glass, poured its contents carefully back into the bottle, which he corked and replaced on the shelf.
On the floor against the wall was a pair of Indian clubs. He picked these up and examined them owlishly. He gave them little tentative jerks. Finally, with the air of a man carrying out a great resolution, he began to swing them. He swung them in slow, irregular sweeps, his eyes the while, still glassy, staring fixedly at the ceiling.

===Chinese===

对面墙上有一面小镜子。他脚步踉跄地走过去，伸出舌头。他保持着这姿势端详了一阵，然后带着愈发深重的沮丧，转身走开。
他把一只手按在心口。这似乎让他愈发消沉。最后他走到桌边，端起酒杯，把杯中物仔仔细细倒回瓶里，塞上瓶塞，把瓶子放回了架子上。
靠墙的地板上放着一对印度体操棒。他把它们拾起来，猫头鹰似的呆呆端详。他试着把棒子小小地颠了几下。最后，带着一个执行重大决断的人的气概，他抡了起来。他抡得又慢又不成章法，一圈又一圈，那双眼睛依旧呆滞，死死盯着天花板。

## XV Mrs. Porter’s Waterloo / 第十五章 波特夫人的滑铁卢

===Original===

Of the little band of revellers it would be hard to say which was the most taken aback at this invasion. The excitement of the moment had kept them from hearing the sound of the automobile which Mrs. Porter, mistrusting the rough road that led to the shack, had stopped some distance away.
Perhaps, on the whole, Kirk was more surprised than either of his companions. Their guilty consciences had never been quite free from the idea of the possibility of pursuit; but Kirk, having gathered from Mamie that neither Ruth nor her aunt was aware of what had happened, had counted upon remaining undisturbed till the time for return came on the morrow.
He stood staring at Ruth, who had followed Mrs. Porter into the room.
Mrs. Porter took charge of the situation. She was in her element. She stood with one hand resting on the table as if she were about to make an after-dinner speech﻿—as indeed she was.

===Chinese===

在这一小撮寻欢作乐的人当中，很难说谁被这场突如其来的入侵弄得最为措手不及。方才的兴奋热闹让他们没能听见汽车的声响——波特夫人信不过那条通往小木屋的崎岖道路，把车停在了老远的地方。
总体而论，也许柯克比他的两个同伴更感意外。他们俩做贼心虚，心里从未彻底排除过人家追上门来的可能；柯克则从玛米口中得知露丝和她姑妈对发生的事一无所知，便指望能安安稳稳不受惊扰，直到明日该回去的时候。
他站在那里，直勾勾地望着跟在波特夫人身后进屋来的露丝。
波特夫人接管了局面。这正是她的用武之地。她站着，一只手按在桌上，仿佛即将发表一篇餐后演说——事实上也确乎如此。

===Original===

Lora Delane Porter was not dissatisfied with the turn events had taken. On the whole, perhaps, it might be said that she was pleased. She intended, when she began to speak, to pulverize Kirk and the abandoned young woman whom he had selected as his partner in his shameful escapade, but in this she was swayed almost entirely by a regard for abstract morality.
As concerned Ruth, she felt that the situation was, on the whole, the best thing that could have happened. To her Napoleonic mind, which took little account of the softer emotions, concerning itself entirely with the future of the race, Kirk had played his part and was now lagging superfluous on the stage. His tendency, she felt, was to retard rather than to assist William Bannister’s development. His influence, such as it was, clashed with hers. She did not forget that there had been a time when Ruth, having practically to choose between them, had chosen to go Kirk’s way and had abandoned herself to a life which could only be considered unhygienic and retrograde. Her defeat in the matter of Whiskers, the microbe-harbouring dog from Ireland, still rankled.
It was true that in what might be called the return match she had utterly routed Kirk; but until this moment she had always been aware of him as an opponent who might have to be reckoned with. She was quite convinced that it would be in the best interests of everybody, especially of William Bannister, if he could be eliminated. There were signs of human weakness in Ruth which sometimes made her uneasy. Ruth, she told herself, might “bear the torch,” but when it came to “not faltering” she was less certain of her.
Ruth, it was true, had behaved admirably in the matter of the upbringing of William from the moment of her conversion till now, but might she not at any moment become a backslider and fill the white-tiled nursery with abominable long-haired dogs? Most certainly she might. In a woman who had once been a long-haired dogist there are always possibilities of a relapse into long-haired dogism, just as in a converted cannibal there are always possibilities of a return to the gods of wood and stone and the disposition to look on his fellow-man purely in the light of breakfast-food.
For these reasons Mrs. Porter was determined to push home her present advantage, to wipe Kirk off the map as an influence in Ruth’s life. It was her intention, having recovered William Bannister and bathed him from head to foot in a weak solution of boric acid, to stand over Ruth while she obtained a divorce. That done, she would be in a position to defy Kirk and all his antagonistic views on the subject of the hygienic upbringing of children.

===Chinese===

洛拉·德莱恩·波特对事态的这般转折并非不满。大体而论，或许可以说她还相当满意。她打算待到开口之时，把柯克连同他在那桩无耻荒唐事里挑作搭档的堕落女子一并碾成齑粉；不过在这一层上，驱动她的几乎全然是对抽象道德的顾念。
至于露丝，她觉得这场局面，大体而论，是所能发生的最好之事。在她那颗拿破仑式的头脑看来——这颗头脑甚少顾及柔情，一门心思全在种族的前途上——柯克已经演完了他的戏份，如今纯属滞留台上的多余角色。她感到，他的趋向与其说是在助力、不如说是在拖累威廉·班尼斯特的发展。他那点影响力，不论大小，总归与她的相抵触。她不曾忘记，曾几何时，露丝实际上不得不在两人之间择一而从，结果选了柯克那条路，纵身陷入一种只能称之为不讲卫生、开历史倒车的生活。她在「小胡子」——那条来自爱尔兰、浑身窝藏微生物的狗——一事上的败绩，至今犹自隐痛。
诚然，在那场堪称复赛的交锋里，她已把柯克打得一败涂地；但直到此刻，她始终把他视为一个说不定仍须认真对付的对手。她深信，若能把他清除出去，于所有人、尤其于威廉·班尼斯特，都最有裨益。露丝身上时有软弱人性的征兆，常常令她不安。她对自己说，露丝或可「高擎火炬」，但说到「决不动摇」，她就不那么有把握了。
诚然，自皈依归正那天起直到如今，露丝在抚育威廉一事上表现堪称楷模，可她难道不会随时变成一个叛道者，把那间白瓷砖育儿室塞满可恶的长毛狗吗？那是万分可能的。一个做过长毛狗主义者的女人，永远有复发长毛狗主义的可能，正如一个皈依了的食人族，永远有可能重回木石之神的座下，重新生出纯把同胞当早点粮看待的心思。
缘于此故，波特夫人决意将眼下的优势乘胜推尽，把柯克作为一名影响人物，从露丝的生活地图上整个抹掉。她的打算是：先夺回威廉·班尼斯特，用稀硼酸溶液把他从头到脚浸洗一遍，然后亲自监守着露丝把离婚办妥。此事一了，她便有资本公然叫板，向柯克连同他在儿童卫生抚育问题上的一切敌对主张开战。

===Original===

She rapped the table and prepared to speak.
Even a Napoleon, however, may err from lack of sufficient information; and there was a flaw in her position of which she was unaware. From the beginning of the drive to the end of it Ruth had hardly spoken a word, and Mrs. Porter, in consequence, was still in ignorance of what had been happening that day in Wall Street and the effect of these happenings on her niece’s outlook on life. Could she have known it, the silent girl beside her had already suffered the relapse which she had feared as a remote possibility.
Ruth’s mind during that drive had been in a confusion of regrets and doubts and hopes. There were times when she refused absolutely to believe the story of Kirk’s baseness which her aunt poured into her ear during the first miles of the journey. It was absurd and incredible. Yet, as they raced along the dark roads, doubt came to her and would not be driven out.
A single unfortunate phrase of Kirk’s, spoken in haste, but remembered at leisure, formed the basis of this uncertainty. That afternoon when he had left her he had said that Mamie was the real mother of the child. Could it be that Mamie’s undeviating devotion to the boy had won the love which she had lost? It was possible. Considered in the light of what Mrs. Porter had told her, it seemed, in her blackest moments, certain.
She knew how wrapped up in the boy Kirk had been. Was it not a logical outcome of his estrangement from herself that he should have turned for consolation to the one person in sympathy with him in his great love for his child?

===Chinese===

她敲了敲桌子，准备开口。
然而，即便是拿破仑，也会因情报不足而失误；她的阵地上便有一处破绽，而她并不自知。从驱车启程直到抵达终点，露丝一路上几乎不发一言，因此，当天华尔街发生了什么、这些变故又对侄女的人生观产生了何种影响，波特夫人始终蒙在鼓里。倘若她能够知晓便会明白：身旁这个沉默的姑娘，早已经历了那场她唯恐发生、却只当机会渺茫的复萌。
一路驱车前来，露丝的心神在悔恨、疑虑与希望的乱流中打转。旅程的头几英里，姑妈往她耳中灌注的那个柯克卑劣行径的故事，她有时断然拒信。荒唐透顶，匪夷所思。然而，当车子在黑暗的道路上疾驰，疑虑还是袭上心头，再也驱之不散。
柯克一句失言——说时仓促，记起时从容——正是这份疑心的基石。那天下午他离家出门时曾说过，玛米才是孩子真正的母亲。难道玛米那份始终不渝的疼爱，竟赢走了她已失去的爱？这是有可能的。再拿姑妈告诉她的话一并考量，在她心情最黯淡的时刻，这似乎已是定论。
她知道柯克从前把整颗心都扑在这孩子身上。他与她日渐疏远，转而向唯一与他同样深爱孩子的人寻求慰藉，这难道不是顺理成章的结局吗？

===Original===

She tried to read his face as he stood looking at her now, but she could find no hope in it. The eyes that met hers were cold and expressionless.
Mrs. Porter rapped the table a second time.
“Mr. Winfield,” she said in the metallic voice with which she was wont to cow publishers insufficiently equipped with dash and enterprise in the matter of advertising treatises on the future of the race, “I have no doubt you are surprised to see us. You appear to be looking your wife in the face. It speaks well for your courage but badly for your sense of shame. If you had the remnants of decent feeling in you, you would be physically incapable of the feat. If you would care to know how your conduct strikes an unprejudiced spectator, I may tell you that I consider you a scoundrel of the worst type and unfit to associate with any but the low company in which I find you.”
Steve, who had been listening with interest, and indeed, a certain relish while Kirk was, as he put it to himself, “getting his” in this spirited fashion, started at the concluding words of the address, which, in his opinion, seemed slightly personal. He had long ago made up his mind that Lora Delane Porter, though an entertaining woman and, on the whole, more worth while than a moving-picture show, was quite mad; but, he felt, even lunatics ought to realize that there is a limit to what they may say.
He moaned protestingly, and rashly, for he drew the speaker’s attention upon himself.

===Chinese===

此刻他站在那里望着她，她竭力想从他脸上读出些讯息，却寻不到半点希望。迎上她目光的那双眼睛，冷漠而无表情。
波特夫人第二次敲响了桌子。
「温菲尔德先生，」她开口了，用的是她惯于震慑出版商的那种金属般的声音——专震慑那些在为探讨种族前途的专著做广告一事上魄力与闯劲不足的出版商——「你见到我们，想必吃惊不小。你居然还敢直视你妻子的脸。这于你的胆量是美谈，于你的羞恶之心却是劣评。你身上若还剩得一星半点体面的感情，这番绝技在生理上便无从施展。你若想知道你的行径落在一位不偏不倚的旁观者眼中是何光景，我可以告诉你：我认为你是个顶恶劣的恶棍，除了我此刻撞见你厮混其中的这伙下等人，不配与任何人为伍。」
史蒂夫一直听得津津有味，柯克这般「领训」——用他心里的话说——他甚至有几分幸灾乐祸的滋味；及至这篇训词的收尾之句，他猛然一惊：在他看来，这话似乎有点冲着自己来。他早已拿定主意：洛拉·德莱恩·波特虽然是个有趣的女人，大体上比看一场电影还值当，脑子却是全然疯了；不过他觉得，就连疯子也该明白，出言是有限度的。
他哼了一声，以示抗议——此举鲁莽，因为这把演讲者的注意力引到了他自己身上。

===Original===

“This person,” went on Mrs. Porter, indicating Steve with a wave of her hand which caused him to sidestep swiftly and throw up an arm, as had been his habit in the ring when Battling Dick or Fighting Jack endeavoured to blot him out with a right swing, “who, I observe, retains the tattered relics of a conscience, seeing that he winces, you employed to do the only dangerous part of your dirty work. I hope he will see that he gets his money. In his place I should be feeling uneasy.”
“Ma’am!” protested Steve.
Mrs. Porter silenced him with a gesture.
“Be quiet!” she said.
Steve was quiet.

===Chinese===

「这一位，」波特夫人继续说道，手朝史蒂夫一挥——这一挥害得他疾忙侧步闪避、扬臂护头，正是当年拳台上「战斗迪克」或「格斗杰克」试图用一记右摆拳把他抹掉时他的老习惯——「据我观察，尚存良知的几缕褴褛残余，可见他还知道畏缩。你雇他来干你那桩龌龊勾当里唯一危险的部分。我希望他能盯牢了，把工钱拿到手。我要是处在他的位置，早该忐忑不安了。」
「夫人！」史蒂夫抗议道。
波特夫人一个手势叫他噤了声。
「安静！」她说。
史蒂夫安静了。
===Original===

Mrs. Porter returned to Kirk.
Of all her burning words, Kirk had not heard one. His eyes had never left Ruth’s. Like her, he was trying to read a message from a face that seemed only cold. In this crisis of their two lives he had no thought for anybody but her. He had a sense of great issues, of being on the verge of the tremendous; but his brain felt numbed and heavy. He could not think. He could see nothing except her eyes.
His inattention seemed to communicate itself to Mrs. Porter. She rapped imperatively upon the table for the third time. The report galvanized Steve, as, earlier in the day, a similar report had galvanized Mr. Penway; but Kirk did not move.
“Mr. Winfield!”
Still Kirk made no sign that he had heard her. It was discouraging, but Lora Delane Porter was not made of the stuff that yields readily to discouragement. She resumed:

===Chinese===

波特夫人把火力转回了柯克身上。
她那番烈火般的言辞，柯克一个字也没有听进去。他的目光从未离开过露丝。和她一样，他也想从那张只余冷漠的脸上读出一个讯息。在两人生命的这场危局中，他心里容不下任何人，只有她。他感觉到事态重大，感觉到自己正站在某桩惊天动地之事的边沿；可他的脑子又麻又沉。他无法思考。除了她的眼睛，他什么也看不见。
他的心不在焉似乎传染给了波特夫人。她第三次敲桌，带着不容违拗的威势。这一记脆响让史蒂夫如遭电击般惊跳——当日早些时候，一记类似的脆响也曾这样惊跳过佩尼韦先生；柯克却纹丝未动。
「温菲尔德先生！」
柯克依旧没有半点听见了的表示。这固然令人气馁，但洛拉·德莱恩·波特可不是那种一气馁便松劲的材料。她重新开了腔：

===Original===

“As for this wretched girl”﻿—she indicated the silent Mamie with a wave of her hand﻿—“this abandoned creature whom you have led astray, this shameless partner of your﻿—”
“Say!”
The exclamation came from Steve, and it stopped Mrs. Porter like a bullet. To her this interruption from one whom she had fallen upon and wiped out resembled a voice from the tomb. She was not accustomed to having her victims rise up and cut sharply, even peremptorily, into the flow of her speech. Macbeth, confronted by the ghost of Banquo, may have been a little more taken aback, but not much.
She endeavoured to quell Steve with a glance, but it was instantly apparent that he was immune for the time being to quelling glances. His brown eyes were fixed upon her in a cold stare which she found arresting and charged with menace. His chin protruded and his upper lip was entirely concealed behind its fellow in a most uncomfortable manner.
She had never had the privilege of seeing Steve in the active exercise of his late profession, or she would have recognized the look. It was the one which proclaims the state of mind commonly known as “being fighting mad,” and in other days had usually heralded a knockout for some too persistent opponent.

===Chinese===

「至于这个可悲的姑娘，」——她手一挥，指向默然无语的玛米——「这个被你引上歧途的堕落女子，你这个恬不知耻的——」
「喂！」
这一声断喝出自史蒂夫之口，像一颗子弹把波特夫人钉在了原地。在她听来，这个已被她扑倒歼灭之人竟敢出声打岔，无异于墓穴中传来的语声。她可不惯于见她的牺牲品站起来，凌厉地、甚至不留情面地插进她的滔滔议论。麦克白面对班柯的鬼魂之时，或许比这更吃惊些，但也仅此而已。
她试图用一道眼风把史蒂夫镇住，但立刻便可看出，他此刻对镇人的眼风自有免疫。他棕色的眼睛冷冷地盯住她，那凝视令她也不由得屏息，且满蓄威胁。他的下巴向前突出，上唇整个没入下唇之后，那模样瞧着极不自在。
她从未有幸见识过史蒂夫活跃施展旧日本行的风采，否则她准能认出这副神情。这是宣告那种通常被称作「打性大发」的心境的表情；想当年，它往往预示着某个纠缠过甚的对手即将挨上一记击倒。

===Original===

“Say, ma’am, you want to cut that out. That line of talk don’t go.”
Great is the magic of love that can restore a man in an instant of time from being an obsequious wreck to a thing of fire and resolution. A moment before Steve’s only immediate object in life had been to stay quiet and keep out of the way as much as possible. He had never been a man of ready speech in the presence of an angry woman; words intimidated him as blows never did, especially the whirl of words which were at Lora Delane Porter’s command in moments of emotion.
But this sudden onslaught upon Mamie, innocent Mamie who had done nothing to anybody, scattered his embarrassment and filled him with much the same spirit which sent bantamweight knights up against heavyweight dragons in the Middle Ages. He felt inspired.
“Nix on the ‘abandoned creature,’ ” he said with dignity. “You’re on the wrong wire! This here lady is my affianced wife!”
He went to Mamie and, putting his arm round her waist, pressed her to him. He was conscious, as he did so, of a sensation of wonderment at himself. This was the attitude he had dreamed of a thousand times and had been afraid to assume. For the last three years he had been picturing himself in precisely this position, and daily had cursed the lack of nerve which had held him back. Yet here he was, and it had all happened in a moment. A funny thing, life.

===Chinese===

「我说，夫人，这套您可得收起来。这种话行不通。」
爱情的魔力何其伟大，竟能在一瞬之间，把一个男人从卑躬屈膝的废人还原成一团挟着决断的烈火。片刻之前，史蒂夫眼下唯一的人生目标，还是尽量安安静静、少抛头露面。在动怒的女人面前，他从来不是个口角锋利的人；拳头从不曾吓住过他，言辞却吓得住——尤其是洛拉·德莱恩·波特情绪激动时驱遣自如的那一阵言辞的旋风。
可是对玛米——对谁也没有招惹的无辜的玛米——的这场突袭，驱散了他的窘迫，使他胸中充溢起一股气概，与中世纪驱使雏量级骑士迎击重量级恶龙的那股气概如出一辙。他只觉如有神助。
「『堕落女子』这话免了吧，」他端着架子说道，「您接错线啦！这位小姐是我的未婚妻！」
他走到玛米跟前，伸臂搂住她的腰，把她拥向自己。这么做的时候，他心头对自己涌起一阵惊奇。这正是他梦过千百回、却始终不敢摆出的姿态。整整三年来，他无时不在心中描画自己恰是这样站着，又天天咒骂那拖住他后腿的胆怯。然而此刻他真的这样站着了，而这一切都发生在一瞬之间。人生，真是桩妙事。

===Original===

“What!” exclaimed Mrs. Porter.
“Sure thing,” said Steve. His coolness, the ease with which he found words astonished him as much as his rapidity of action.
“I stole the kid,” he said, “and it was my idea at that. Kirk didn’t know anything about it. I wired to him today what I had done and that he was to come right along. And,” added Steve in a burst of inspiration, “I said bring along Mamie, too, as the kid’s used to her and there ought to be a woman around. And she could be here, all right, and no harm, she being my affianced wife.” He liked that phrase. He had read it in a book somewhere, and it was the goods.
He eyed Mrs. Porter jauntily. Mrs. Porter’s gaze wavered. She was not feeling comfortable. Hers was a nature that did not lend itself easily to apologies, yet apologies were obviously what the situation demanded. The thought of all the eloquence which she had expended to no end added to her discomfort. For the first time she was pleased that Kirk had so manifestly not been listening to a word of it.
“Oh!” she said.

===Chinese===

「什么！」波特夫人失声叫道。
「可不，」史蒂夫说。他自己的沉着，连同觅词达意的轻省，令他自己吃惊的程度，不亚于他行动的迅疾。
「孩子是我拐的，」他说，「主意也是我出的。柯克什么也不知道。我今天打了电报给他，说我干了这事，叫他就势动身过来。还有，」史蒂夫灵机一动，补上一句，「我说，把玛米也一并带来，因为孩子习惯了她，这边也总得有个女人。她在这里就名正言顺，毫无不是之处，因为她是我订聘在身的未婚妻。」他喜欢这个措辞。它是他从哪本书上读来的，地道得很。
他一脸轻快得意地打量着波特夫人。波特夫人的目光动摇了。她浑身不自在。她的天性不惯于赔不是，而眼下的局面，显然正缺一个赔不是。想到自己那一番雄辩尽数付诸东流，她愈发不自在。平生头一回，她庆幸柯克明摆着一个字也没有听进去。
「哦！」她说。

===Original===

She paused.
“That puts a different complexion on this affair.”
“Betcha life!”
She paused once more. It was some moments before she could bring herself to speak. She managed it at last.
“I beg your pardon,” she said.

===Chinese===

她顿了顿。
「这么一来，这件事便得另换一副面孔了。」
「那还用说！」
她又顿了顿。过了好一会儿，她才勉强让自己开了口。最后总算办到了。
「请您原谅。」她说。
===Original===

“Mine, ma’am?” said Steve grandly. Five minutes before, the idea that he could ever speak grandly to Lora Delane Porter would have seemed ridiculous to him; but he was surprised at nothing now.
“And the young wom﻿—And the future Mrs. Dingle’s,” said Mrs. Porter with an effort.
“Thank you, ma’am,” said Steve, and released Mamie, who forthwith bolted from the room like a scared rabbit.
Steve had started to follow her when Mrs. Porter, magnificent woman, snatching what was left from defeat, stopped him.

===Chinese===

「求我原谅，夫人？」史蒂夫气派十足地答道。五分钟之前，若说他竟能对洛拉·德莱恩·波特讲出气派十足的话来，他自己听着都要觉得荒唐；可如今他对什么都不再惊讶了。
「还有这位年轻女——还有这位未来的丁格尔太太的。」波特夫人费力地说道。
「多谢您了，夫人。」史蒂夫说，随即松开玛米——她当即如一只受惊的兔子，窜出了房间。
史蒂夫正要跟出去，波特夫人——好一位气概非凡的女人——从败局中抢救出残余的一切，叫住了他。

===Original===

“Wait!” she said. “What you have said alters the matter in one respect; but there is another point. On your own confession you have been guilty of the extremely serious offence, the penal offence of kidnapping a child who﻿—”
“Drop me a line about it, ma’am,” said Steve. “Me time’s rather full just now.”
He disappeared into the outer darkness after Mamie.

===Chinese===

「且慢！」她说。「你方才所言，在一个方面改换了此事的性质；但还有一点。照你亲口招认，你已犯下一桩极其严重的罪行，一桩刑事罪行——拐带儿童，而这孩子——」
「这事您写封信跟我细说吧，夫人，」史蒂夫说，「我这阵子工夫实在排得满。」
他追着玛米而去，消失在外面的黑暗之中。

---

===Original===

In the room they had left, Kirk and Ruth faced each other in silence. Lora Delane Porter eyed them grimly. It was the hour of her defeat, and she knew it. Forces too strong for her were at work. Her grand attack, the bringing of these two together that Ruth might confront Kirk in his guilt, had recoiled upon her. The Old Guard had made their charge up the hill, and it had failed. Victory had become a rout. With one speech Steve had destroyed her whole plan of campaign.
She knew it was all over, that in another moment if she remained, she would be compelled to witness the humiliating spectacle of Ruth in Kirk’s arms, stammering the words which intuition told her were even now trembling on her lips. She knew Ruth. She could read her like a primer. And her knowledge told her that she was about to capitulate, that all her pride and resentment had been swept away, that she had gone over to the enemy.
Elemental passions were warring against Lora Delane Porter, and she bowed before them.
“Mr. Winfield,” she said sharply, her voice cutting the silence like a knife, “I beg your pardon. I seem to have made a mistake. Good night.”

===Chinese===

在他们留下的那间屋子里，柯克与露丝默默相对。洛拉·德莱恩·波特冷冷地盯着他们俩。这是她折戟沉沙的时刻，她心里有数。有某种她敌不过的力量正在起作用。她的大举进攻——把这两个人弄到一处，好让露丝当面直击负罪的柯克——竟反噬到了她自己身上。老近卫军向着山头发起了冲锋，冲锋败下阵来。胜局化作溃逃。史蒂夫一席话，摧毁了她的整套战役方略。
她知道大势已去；她若再待下去，下一刻便不得不亲眼目睹那丢人的一幕：露丝偎在柯克怀里，讷讷吐出那些直觉告诉她此刻已在这姑娘唇边打颤的话语。她了解露丝。读露丝，就像读一本启蒙课本。而她的这份了解告诉她：露丝就要开城投降了，她全部的骄傲与怨怼已被扫荡一空，她已经投到敌方去了。
原始的激情正与洛拉·德莱恩·波特交战，她向它们俯首了。
「温菲尔德先生，」她尖声说道，嗓音如刀，划破寂静，「请您原谅。我似乎是弄错了。晚安。」

===Original===

Kirk did not answer.
“Good night, Ruth.”
Ruth made no sign that she had heard.
Mrs. Porter, grand in defeat, moved slowly to the door.
But even in the greatest women there is that germ of feminine curiosity which cannot be wholly eliminated, that little grain of dust that asserts itself and clogs the machinery. It had been Mrs. Porter’s intention to leave the room without a glance, her back defiantly toward the foe. But, as she reached the door, there came from behind her a sound of movement, a stifled cry, a little sound whose meaning she knew too well.

===Chinese===

柯克没有回答。
「晚安，露丝。」
露丝没有露出听见了的表示。
波特夫人败得堂皇，缓缓向门口走去。
然而，即便在最伟大的女性身上，也埋着那粒无法根除净尽的女性的好奇心胚芽，那粒会自行发作、滞涩机器的微尘。波特夫人本打算一眼也不回地离开这间屋子，把不肯屈服的脊背甩向敌人。可是，就在她走到门口时，身后传来一阵响动，一声强抑的呼喊——一记小小的声响，其中含义，她再清楚不过。

===Original===

She hesitated. She stood still, fighting herself. But the grain of dust had done its work. For an instant she ceased to be a smoothly working machine and became a woman subject to the dictates of impulse.
She turned.
Intuition had not deceived her. Ruth had gone over to the enemy. She was in Kirk’s arms, holding him to her, her face hidden against his shoulder, for all the world as if Lora Delane Porter, her guiding force, had ceased to exist.
Mrs. Porter closed the door and walked stiffly through the scented night to where the headlights of her automobile cleft the darkness. Birds, asleep in the trees, fluttered uneasily at the sudden throbbing of the engine.

===Chinese===

她犹豫了。她站定不动，与自己搏斗。可那粒微尘已经完成了它的工作。有一瞬，她不再是一台运转圆滑的机器，重新变成了一个受冲动支使的女人。
她回过了头。
直觉没有骗她。露丝果真投到敌方去了。她已在柯克怀里，紧紧把他抱住，脸埋在他的肩窝里，那副光景，全然好像她的引路神明洛拉·德莱恩·波特已经不在人世。
波特夫人关上门，僵硬地穿过香馥馥的夜色，走向她的汽车那劈开黑暗的车头灯旁。栖在树上酣睡的鸟儿，被发动机骤然的震颤搅得不安地扑棱着翅膀。

## XIII Pastures New / 第十三章 新牧场

===Original===

Steve had arrived at the Connecticut shack in the early dawn of the day which had been so eventful to most of his friends and acquaintances. William Bannister’s interest in the drive, at first acute, had ceased after the first five miles, and he had passed the remainder of the journey in a sound sleep from which the stopping of the car did not awaken him.

Steve jumped down and stretched himself. There was a wonderful freshness in the air which made him forget for a moment his desire for repose. He looked about him, breathing deep draughts of its coolness. The robins which, though not so well advertised, rise just as punctually as the lark, were beginning to sing as they made their simple toilets before setting out to attend to the early worm. The sky to the east was a delicate blend of pinks and greens and yellows, with a hint of blue behind the grey which was still the prevailing note.

A vaguely sentimental mood came upon Steve. In his heart he knew perfectly well that he could never be happy for any length of time out of sight and hearing of Broadway cars; but at that moment, such was the magic of the dawn, he felt a longing to settle down in the country and pass the rest of his days a simple farmer with beard unchecked by razor. He saw himself feeding the chickens and addressing the pigs by their pet names, while Mamie, in a cotton frock, called cheerfully to him to come in because breakfast was ready and getting cold.

Mamie! Ah!

His sigh turned into a yawn. He realized with the abruptness which comes to a man who stands alone with nature in the small hours that he was very sleepy. The excitement which had sustained him till now had begun to ebb. The free life of the bearded farmer seemed suddenly less attractive. Bed was what he wanted now, not nature.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫抵达康涅狄格的小木屋，是在那天最初的晨光里——这一天，对他的大多数朋友和相识来说，都是格外多事的。威廉·班尼斯特对乘车出游起初兴致盎然，头五英里一过便兴味索然，余下的路程都在酣睡中度过，连汽车停下也没能唤醒他。

史蒂夫跳下车，伸展了一下身子。空气里有一种奇妙的清新，令他一时忘却了对歇息的渴望。他环顾四周，大口大口地吸入那份凉爽。知更鸟——虽不像云雀那样广有名声，起得却同样准时——已经开腔歌唱，一面梳着它们简单的晨妆，一面准备动身去照应那条早起的虫子。东边的天空是粉、绿、黄三色精微的交融，仍是主调的灰色背后，隐隐透出一抹蓝。

一种影影绰绰的伤感情绪涌上史蒂夫心头。他心底里一清二楚：只要看不见、听不着百老汇的电车，他就没法长久快活；可就在那一刻，晨光的魔力如此了得，他竟生出一股渴望，想在农村安家落户，做个简朴农夫度过余生，胡子任其生长、不劳剃刀约束。他看见自己在喂鸡，用爱称呼唤猪猡们，而玛米穿着棉布裙，欢快地喊他进屋，因为早饭好了，再不吃就要凉了。

玛米！啊！

他的叹息变成了一声呵欠。他猛然醒悟——独自在后半夜与大自然做伴的人，都会有这种突如其来的清醒——自己实在困极了。迄今支撑着他的那股兴奋劲已开始退潮。大胡子农夫的自由生涯忽然显得不那么诱人了。此刻他想要的是床，不是自然。

===Original===

He opened the door of the car and lifted William Bannister out, swathed in rugs. The White Hope gurgled drowsily, but did not wake. Steve carried him on to the porch and laid him down. Then he turned his attention to the problem of effecting an entry.

Once an honest man has taken to amateur burgling he soon picks up the tricks of it. To open his knife and shoot back the catch of the nearest window was with Steve the work, if not of a moment, of a very few minutes. He climbed in and unlocked the front door. Then he carried his young charge into the sitting-room and laid him down on a chair, a step nearer his ultimate destination﻿—bed.

Steve’s faculties were rapidly becoming numb with approaching sleep, but he roused himself to face certain details of the country life which till now had escaped him. His earnest concentration on the main plank of his platform, the spiriting away of William Bannister, had caused him to overlook the fact that no preparations had been made to welcome him on his arrival at his destination. He had treated the shack as if it had been a summer hotel, where he could walk in and engage a room. It now struck him that there was much to be attended to before he could, as he put it to himself, hit the hay. There was the White Hope’s bed to be made, and, by the way of a preliminary to that, sheets must be found and blankets, not to mention pillows.

===Chinese===

他打开车门，把裹在毯子里的威廉·班尼斯特抱了出来。「白人希望」睡意朦胧地咕哝了一声，却没有醒。史蒂夫把他抱上门廊，放平。然后他便把心思转向了如何进屋这个难题。

一个老实人一旦改行干起业余盗窃，很快便能摸到这一行的门道。掏出小刀，拨开最近一扇窗户的窗栓，这在史蒂夫手里就算不是一眨眼的工夫，也顶多几分钟。他爬进屋，打开了前门的锁，然后把他照看的小家伙抱进起居室，放在一把椅子上——离他最终的目的地床，又近了一步。

睡意压来，史蒂夫的各项官能正迅速麻木下去，但他强打精神，去应付乡村生活中若干此前被他忽略的细节。他全副心思都扑在政纲的主干——神不知鬼不觉地掳走威廉·班尼斯特——上面，以致忽略了一个事实：迎接他们抵达目的地的各项准备，一样也没有做。他一直把小木屋当成消夏旅馆，仿佛只消走进去就能订下一间房。此刻他才省悟，照他对自己说的那样「上草铺」之前，还有好些事要料理。「白人希望」的床要铺；而铺床的第一步，得先找到床单和毯子，更别提枕头了。

===Original===

Yawning wearily he set out on his search.

He found sheets, but mistrusted them. They might or might not be perfectly dry. He did not care to risk his godson’s valuable health in the experiment. A hazy notion that blankets were always safe restored his spirits, and he became cheerful on reflecting that a child with William Bannister’s gift for sleep would not be likely to notice the absence of linen in his bed.

The couch which he finally passed adequate would have caused Lora Delane Porter’s hair to stand erect, but it satisfied Steve. He went downstairs, and, returning with William Bannister, placed him carefully on it and tucked him in. The White Hope slept on.

Having assured himself that all was well, Steve made up a similar nest for himself, and, removing his coat and shoes, crawled under the blankets. Five minutes later rhythmical snores proclaimed the fact that nature had triumphed over all the discomforts of one of the worst-made beds in Connecticut.

===Chinese===

他疲惫地打着呵欠，出发搜寻去了。

他找到了床单，却信不过。它们也许干透了，也许没有。他可不愿意拿教子金贵的健康去冒这个险。「毯子永远保险」这么一个模糊的念头让他重新振作，转念一想，他又快活起来：凭威廉·班尼斯特那份睡眠的天赋，孩子多半不会察觉床上缺了床单。

那张他最终验收合格的睡榻，若是让洛拉·德莱恩·波特看了，准保叫她头发根根倒竖，史蒂夫却心满意足。他下楼去，抱着威廉·班尼斯特回来，小心地把他放上去，掖好被角。「白人希望」继续酣睡。

确认一切妥当之后，史蒂夫照式照样给自己也铺了一个窝，脱掉上衣和鞋，钻进毯子底下。五分钟后，一阵有节奏的鼾声宣告：大自然战胜了康涅狄格做工最糟糕的床铺之一的全部不适。

---

===Original===

The sun was high when Steve woke. He rose stiffly and went into the other room. William Bannister still slept.

Steve regarded him admiringly.

“For the dormouse act,” he mused, “that kid certainly stands alone. You got to hand it to him.”

An aching void within him called his mind to the question of breakfast. It began to come home to him that he had not planned out this expedition with that thoroughness which marks the great general.

“I guess I’ll have to get out to the nearest village in the bubble,” he said. “And while I’m there maybe I’d better send Kirk a wire. And I reckon I’ll have to take the kid. If he wakes up and finds me gone he’ll throw fits. Up you get, squire.”

He kneaded the recumbent form of his godson with a large hand until he had massaged out of him the last remains of his great sleep. It took some time, but it was effective. The White Hope sat up, full of life and energy. He inspected Steve gravely for a moment, endeavouring to place him.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫醒来时，太阳已经老高。他僵手僵脚地爬起来，走进另一间屋子。威廉·班尼斯特还在睡。

史蒂夫满怀钦佩地打量着他。

「论睡鼠这门功夫，」他沉吟道，「这孩子真是独一份。不服不行。」

体内一处发痛的空虚把他的心思唤到了早饭问题上。他这才开始痛感：筹办这趟远征时，自己实在缺少名将的那份周密。

「看来我只好开着『泡泡』去最近的村子跑一趟，」他说，「人在那儿的时候，顺便最好给柯克发封电报。我寻思还得带上这孩子。他要是醒来发现我不在，非闹个天翻地覆不可。起来吧，老爷。」

他用一只大手揉捏教子横躺的躯体，直到把那场大觉的最后残余也从他身上揉捏出去。这费了些时间，却很见效。「白人希望」坐起身来，浑身是劲。他神情庄重地端详了史蒂夫片刻，努力想认出这是何方人物。

===Original===

“Hello, Steve,” he said at length.

“Hello, kid.”

“Where am I?”

“In the country. In Connecticut.”

“What’s ’Necticut?”

“This is. Where we are.”

===Chinese===

「哈罗，史蒂夫。」他终于开了口。

「哈罗，小家伙。」

「我在哪儿？」

「在乡下。在康涅狄格。」

「『涅狄格』是什么？」

「就是这儿。咱们待的地方。」

===Original===

“Where are we?”

“Here. In Connecticut.”

“Why?”

Steve raised a protesting hand.

“Not so early in the day, kid; not before breakfast,” he pleaded. “Honest, I’m not strong enough. It ain’t as if we was a vaudeville team that had got to rehearse.”

“What’s rehearse?”

===Chinese===

「咱们待的地方在哪儿？」

「这儿。在康涅狄格。」

「为什么？」

史蒂夫举起一只手表示抗议。

「别这么一大早就来这套，小家伙；别赶在早饭前头，」他恳求道，「说真的，我撑不住。咱们又不是搭档演杂耍的班子，还非得排练不可。」

「排练是什么？」

===Original===

Steve changed the subject.

“Say, kid, ain’t you feeling like you could bite into something? I got an emptiness inside me as big as all outdoors. How about a mouthful of cereal and a shirred egg? Now, for the love of Mike,” he went on quickly, as his godson opened his mouth to speak, “don’t say ‘What’s shirred?’ It’s something you do to eggs. It’s one way of fixing ’em.”

“What’s fixing?” inquired William Bannister brightly.

Steve sighed. When he spoke he was calm, but determined.

“That’ll be all the dialogue for the present,” he said. “We’ll play the rest of our act in dumb show. Get a move on you, and I’ll take you out in the bubble﻿—the automobile, the car, the chug-chug wagon, the thing we came here in, if you want to know what bubble is﻿—and we’ll scare up some breakfast.”

===Chinese===

史蒂夫岔开了话头。

「我说，小家伙，你是不是觉着能啃点儿什么了？我肚子里空得能装下整个大露天。来一口麦片，外加一个焗蛋，怎么样？好了好了，看在迈克的份上，」他见教子张嘴要问，赶紧接着说，「千万别问『焗』是什么。那是你对鸡蛋做的一件事，是拾掇它们的一个路数。」

「拾掇是什么？」威廉·班尼斯特兴致勃勃地问。

史蒂夫叹了口气。再开口时，他语气平静，却斩钉截铁。

「眼下的台词到此为止，」他说，「咱们这出戏剩下的部分，全用哑剧演。快着点儿，我带你坐『泡泡』出去——就是汽车、车子、突突车、咱们坐来这儿的那件家伙，你要是想知道『泡泡』是什么的话——咱们张罗点早饭去。」
===Original===

Steve’s ignorance of the locality in which he found himself was complete; but he had a general impression that farmers as a class were people who delighted in providing breakfasts for the needy, if the needy possessed the necessary price. Acting on this assumption, he postponed his trip to the nearest town and drove slowly along the roads with his eyes open for signs of life.

He found a suitable farm and, applying the brakes, gathered up William Bannister and knocked at the door.

His surmise as to the hospitality of farmers proved correct, and presently they were sitting down to a breakfast which it did his famished soul good to contemplate.

William Bannister seemed less enthusiastic. Steve, having disposed of two eggs in quick succession, turned to see how his young charge was progressing with his repast, and found him eyeing a bowl of bread-and-milk in a sort of frozen horror.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫对自己身处何地全然无知；但他有个笼统的印象：农夫这个阶级，就整体而言，是乐于为断粮者供应早饭的人——只要断粮者备有必要的价钱。本着这项假设，他推迟了去最近镇子的行程，沿着大路缓缓驱车，睁大眼睛留意人烟的迹象。

他找到一处合适的农家，踩下刹车，抱起威廉·班尼斯特，上前敲门。

他关于农夫好客的揣测证明无误，不多时两人已落座于一顿早饭之前——那光景望在他饿瘪了的灵魂眼里，实在大有滋补。

威廉·班尼斯特的热情似乎不高。史蒂夫接连解决了两个鸡蛋，转过头来看看他照看的小家伙进膳进展如何，却见他正以一种冻结般的惊恐，盯着一碗面包泡牛奶。

===Original===

“What’s the matter, kid?” he asked. “Get busy.”

“No paper,” said William Bannister.

“For the love of Pete! Do you expect your morning paper out in the woods?”

“No paper,” repeated the White Hope firmly.

Steve regarded him thoughtfully.

===Chinese===

「怎么了，小家伙？」他问，「快动手啊。」

「没有纸。」威廉·班尼斯特说。

「看在皮特的份上！你总不至于指望树林子里还给你送上晨报来吧？」

「没有纸。」「白人希望」语气坚定地重复道。

史蒂夫若有所思地看着他。

===Original===

“I didn’t have this trip planned out right,” he said regretfully. “I ought to have got Mamie to come along. I bet a hundred dollars she would have got next to your meanings in a second. I pass. What’s your kick, anyway? What’s all this about paper?”

“Aunty Lora says not to eat bread that doesn’t come wrapped up in paper,” said the White Hope, becoming surprisingly lucid. “Mamie undoes it out of crinkly paper.”

“I get you. They feed you rolls at home wrapped up in tissue-paper, is that it?”

“What’s tissue?”

===Chinese===

「这趟出行我没筹划周全，」他懊悔地说，「本来该把玛米一道带来的。我赌一百块，她一眨眼就能摸透你的意思。我认输了。你到底别扭个什么劲儿？这纸啊纸的，究竟是怎么回事？」

「洛拉姑妈说，没拿纸包着的面包不许吃，」「白人希望」说，明白得出奇，「玛米都是从皱皱巴巴的纸里把它解出来的。」

「我明白了。在家里，他们给你吃的是薄纸包好的小圆面包，是不是？」

「薄纸是什么？」

===Original===

“Same as crinkly. Well, see here. You remember what we was talking about last night about germs?”

“Yes.”

“Well, that’s one thing germs never do, eat bread out of crinkly paper. You want to forget all the dope they shot into you back in New York and start fresh. You do what I tell you and you can’t go wrong. If you’re going to be a regular germ, what you’ve got to do is to wrap yourself round that bread-and-milk the quickest you can. Get me? Till you do that we can’t begin to start out to have a good time.”

William Bannister made no more objections. He attacked his meal with an easy conscience, and about a quarter of an hour later leaned back with a deep sigh of repletion.

===Chinese===

「跟皱纸一个意思。听我说。昨天夜里咱们聊的细菌那些话，还记得不？」

「记得。」

「这就对了，细菌从来不干的一件事，就是从皱皱的纸里掏面包吃。你在纽约让他们灌进脑袋的那些方子，统统忘掉，从头来过。你照我吩咐的做，保准出不了错。你要打算当一条地道细菌，头一件事就是赶紧扑上去，把那碗面包牛奶整个裹住。懂了没？不办成这一步，咱们就没法动身开始好好找乐子。」

威廉·班尼斯特没再提异议。他问心无愧地向饭食发起进攻，约莫一刻钟后，带着一声深深的饱足长叹靠回椅背上。

===Original===

Steve, meanwhile had entered into conversation with the lady of the house.

“Say, I guess you ain’t got a kid of your own anywheres, have you?”

“Sure I have,” said the hostess proudly. “He’s out in the field with his pop this minute. His name’s Jim.”

“Fine. I want to get hold of a kid to play with this kid here. Jim sounds pretty good to me. About the same age as this one?”

“For the Lord’s sake! Jim’s eighteen and weighs two hundred pounds.”

===Chinese===

这厢史蒂夫已经同这家主妇攀谈起来。

「我说，您家里该没有同龄的小孩吧，哪儿都没有？」

「咋能没有，」女主人自豪地说，「这会儿他正跟他爹在地里干活呢。他叫吉姆。」

「好极了。我正想找个孩子陪我这个孩子玩玩。吉姆听着挺对路。跟这个差不多大？」

「老天爷！吉姆十八了，体重两百磅！」

===Original===

“Cut out Jim. I thought from the way you spoke he was a regular kid. Know anyone in these parts who’s got something about the same weight as this one?”

The farmer’s wife reflected.

“Kids is pretty scarce round here,” she said. “I reckon you won’t get one that I knows of. There’s that Tom Whiting, but he’s a bad boy. He ain’t been raised right.”

“What’s the matter with him?”

“I don’t want to speak harm of no one, but his father used to be a low prizefighter, and you know what they are.”

===Chinese===

「吉姆划掉。听您说话的口气，我还当他是个正经小孩呢。这左近可有谁家养着跟这个分量差不多的？」

农妇想了想。

「这一带孩子可稀罕呢，」她说，「依我看，您是找不着了。是有那么个汤姆·怀廷，可他是个坏孩子。没被调教好。」

「他有什么不好？」

「我不想说谁的坏话，可他爹从前当过低级职业拳手，那号人什么样，您是知道的。」

===Original===

Steve nodded sympathetically.

“Regular plug-uglies,” he said. “A friend of mine used to have to mix with them quite a lot, poor fellah! He used to say they was none of them truly refined. And this kid takes after his pop, eh? Kind of scrappy kid, is that it?”

“He’s a bad boy.”

“Well, maybe I’d better look him over, just in case. Where’s he to be found?”

“They live in the cottage by the big house you can see through them trees. His pop looks after Mr. Wilson’s prize dawgs. That’s his job.”

===Chinese===

史蒂夫同情地点点头。

「一水儿的粗坯恶棍，」他说，「我有个朋友从前不得不老跟他们打交道，可怜的家伙！他常说，那帮人没一个真正文雅的。这孩子随他爹，嗯？属于好斗的那类孩子，是不是？」

「他是个坏孩子。」

「好吧，没准儿我还是去相看相看，以防万一。上哪儿找得着他？」

「他们住在那所大宅子边上的小屋里，隔着那片树就能望见。他爹替威尔逊先生照看那些名犬。那就是他的差事。」

===Original===

“What’s Wilson?” asked the White Hope, coming out of his stupor.

“You beat me to it by a second, kid. I was just going to ask it myself.”

“He’s one of them rich New Yawkers. He has his summer place here, and this Whiting looks after his prize dawgs.”

“Well, I guess I’ll give him a call. It’s going to be lonesome for my kid if he ain’t got someone to show him how to hit it up. He’s not used to country life. Come along. We’ll get into the bubble and go and send your pop a telegram.”

“What’s telegram?” asked William Bannister.

“I got you placed now,” said Steve, regarding him with interest. “You’re not going to turn into an ambassador or an artist or any of them things. You’re going to be the greatest district attorney that ever came down the pike.”

===Chinese===

「威尔逊是什么？」「白人希望」从恍惚中醒转过来，问道。

「你抢在我前头一秒钟，小家伙。我正要问这个呢。」

「他是纽约来的阔佬。他的消夏别墅就在这儿，这个怀廷就替他照看那些名犬。」

「那么，我看我得去拜访他一趟。我这孩子要是没人教他怎么撒开了玩，可要寂寞坏了。他过不惯乡下的日子。走吧。咱们上『泡泡』里去，给你爸爸拍封电报。」

「电报是什么？」威廉·班尼斯特问。

「这回我给你定位定了，」史蒂夫说着，饶有兴味地端详他，「你将来成不了大使，也成不了画家，那一类行当都成不了。你要当的是有史以来顺着大路走来的最了不起的地区检察官。」

## II Ruth States Her Intentions / 第二章 露丝表明心意

===Original===
At about the time when Lora Delane Porter was cross-examining Kirk Winfield, Bailey Bannister left his club hurriedly.

Inside the club a sad, rabbit-faced young gentleman, who had been unburdening his soul to Bailey, was seeking further consolation in an amber drink with a cherry at the bottom of it. For this young man was one of nature’s cherry-chasers. It was the only thing he did really well. His name was Grayling, his height five feet three, his socks pink, and his income enormous.

So much for Grayling. He is of absolutely no importance, either to the world or to this narrative, except in so far that the painful story he has been unfolding to Bailey Bannister has so wrought upon that exquisite as to send him galloping up Fifth Avenue at five miles an hour in search of his sister Ruth.

Let us now examine Bailey. He is a faultlessly dressed young man of about twenty-seven, who takes it as a compliment when people think him older. His mouth, at present gaping with agitation and the unwonted exercise, is, as a rule, primly closed. His eyes, peering through gold-rimmed glasses, protrude slightly, giving him something of the dumb pathos of a codfish.

===Chinese===
大约就在洛拉·德莱恩·波特盘问柯克·温菲尔德的当口，贝利·班尼斯特正匆匆离开他的俱乐部。

俱乐部里，一位愁眉苦脸、长着兔子脸的年轻绅士——方才他一直在向贝利倾吐衷肠——这会儿正在一杯琥珀色的酒里寻求进一步的慰藉，杯底沉着一颗樱桃。因为这位年轻人是造化手笔里天生的樱桃追逐者，这是他唯一真正擅长的事。他姓格雷林（Grayling），身高五英尺三英寸，袜子粉红，进项惊人。

格雷林其人，交代如上。无论对这个世界还是对本书故事，他都毫不重要——只有一处例外：他向贝利·班尼斯特倾吐的那段伤心史，把那位雅人刺激得不轻，竟害得他沿着第五大道飞奔起来，时速五英里，直奔他的妹妹露丝而去。

现在让我们来端详端详贝利。他是个衣着无可指摘的年轻人，年约二十七岁，人家当他年长，他引以为恭维。他那张嘴，此刻因激动和难得的运动而张着，平时却是端端正正地抿紧的。他从金边眼镜后面向外窥探，双眼微微外凸，给他平添了几分鳕鱼式的、一言不发的哀婉。

===Original===
His hair is pale and scanty, his nose sharp and narrow. He is a junior partner in the firm of Bannister & Son, and it is his unalterable conviction that, if his father would only give him a chance, he could show Wall Street some high finance that would astonish it.

The afternoon was warm. The sun beat down on the avenue. Bailey had not gone two blocks before it occurred to him that swifter and more comfortable progress could be made in a taxicab than on his admirably trousered legs. No more significant proof of the magnitude of his agitation could be brought forward than the fact that he had so far forgotten himself as to walk at all. He hailed a cab and gave the address of a house on the upper avenue.

He leaned back against the cushions, trying to achieve a coolness of mind and body. But the heat of the day kept him unpleasantly soluble, and dismay, that perspiration of the soul, refused to be absorbed by the pocket-handkerchief of philosophy.

Bailey Bannister was a young man who considered the minding of other people’s business a duty not to be shirked. Life is a rocky road for such. His motto was “Let *me* do it!” He fussed about the affairs of Bannister & Son; he fussed about the welfare of his friends at the club; especially, he fussed about his only sister Ruth.

===Chinese===
他的头发色浅而稀疏，鼻子尖而窄。他是班尼斯特父子公司的初级合伙人，并且抱定一条雷打不动的信念：只要父亲肯给他一个机会，他就能让华尔街开开眼，见识一些足以使之震惊的高级金融。

午后天气暖热，太阳火辣辣地晒在大道上。贝利还没走出两个街区便想到，乘出租汽车赶路，总比支使他那两条裤子体面入时的腿要又快又舒适。要举证他心神激荡之烈，再没有比下面这事实更有分量的了：他竟失态到干脆步行了起来。他拦下一辆车，报了大道北段一处宅子的地址。

他往后一靠，倚着车垫，力求身心双方面的凉快。无奈当天的暑气弄得他浑身不自在，人几乎要化掉；而沮丧——灵魂的汗液——又怎么也不肯让哲学这块手帕吸掉。

贝利·班尼斯特这个年轻人，把操别人的心当作一份不容推诿的职责。这种人的日子是一条碎石路。他的座右铭是「让*我*来！」他替班尼斯特父子公司的业务瞎操心；他替俱乐部里朋友们的福祉瞎操心；尤其是，他替他唯一的妹妹露丝瞎操心。

===Original===
He looked on himself as a sort of guardian to Ruth. Their mother had died when they were children, and old Mr. Bannister was indifferently equipped with the paternal instinct. He was absorbed, body and soul, in the business of the firm. He lived practically a hermit life in the great house on Fifth Avenue; and, if it had not been for Bailey, so Bailey considered, Ruth would have been allowed to do just whatever she pleased. There were those who said that this was precisely what she did, despite Brother Bailey.

It is a hard world for a conscientious young man of twenty-seven.

Bailey paid the cab and went into the house. It was deliciously cool in the hall, and for a moment peace descended on him. But the distant sound of a piano in the upper regions ejected it again by reminding him of his mission. He bounded up the stairs and knocked at the door of his sister’s private den.

The piano stopped as he entered, and the girl on the music-stool glanced over her shoulder.

===Chinese===
他把自己看作露丝的半个监护人。他们的母亲在他们幼年时便去世了，而老班尼斯特先生身上的父性本能装备得马马虎虎。他身心全数扑在公司业务上，在第五大道那座大宅里过的差不多是隐士的日子。在贝利看来，若不是有他贝利，露丝早就随她高兴爱怎样就怎样了。可也偏有人说：尽管有贝利哥哥在，露丝干的恰恰就是随她高兴爱怎样就怎样。

世道对于一个恪尽职守的二十七岁年轻人来说，实在艰难。

贝利付了车钱，走进宅子。门厅里凉爽宜人，一时间，安宁降临到他身上。可楼上一带隐约传来的钢琴声又把安宁轰了出去——它提醒他想起了自己的使命。他三步并作两步蹿上楼梯，敲响了妹妹私人小书房的门。

他进去时琴声停了，琴凳上的姑娘回头瞥了一眼。

===Original===
“Well, Bailey,” she said, “you look warm.”

“I *am* warm,” said Bailey in an aggrieved tone. He sat down solemnly.

“I want to speak to you, Ruth.”

Ruth shut the piano and caused the music-stool to revolve till she faced him.

“Well?” she said.

===Chinese===
「嗬，贝利，」她说，「你看上去挺热。」

「我*就是*热。」贝利委屈巴巴地说。他一本正经地坐了下来。

「我要跟你谈谈，露丝。」

露丝合上琴盖，旋得琴凳转过来，直到正对着他。

「谈吧。」她说。

===Original===
Ruth Bannister was an extraordinarily beautiful girl, “a daughter of the gods, divinely tall, and most divinely fair.” From her mother she had inherited the dark eyes and ivory complexion which went so well with her mass of dark hair; from her father a chin of peculiar determination and perfect teeth. Her body was strong and supple. She radiated health.

To her friends Ruth was a source of perplexity. It was difficult to understand her. In the set in which she moved girls married young; yet season followed season, and Ruth remained single, and this so obviously of her own free will that the usual explanation of such a state of things broke down as soon as it was tested.

In shoals during her first two seasons, and lately with less unanimity, men of every condition, from a prince﻿—somewhat battered, but still a prince﻿—to the Bannisters’ English butler﻿—a good man, but at the moment under the influence of tawny port, had laid their hearts at her feet. One and all, they had been compelled to pick them up and take them elsewhere. She was generally kind on these occasions, but always very firm. The determined chin gave no hope that she might yield to importunity. The eyes that backed up the message of the chin were pleasant, but inflexible.

===Chinese===
露丝·班尼斯特是个美得出奇的姑娘，「天神之女，高挑得神圣，美丽得更神圣」。她从母亲那里继承了黑眼睛和象牙色的肌肤，同她一头浓密的乌发相得益彰；又从父亲那里继承了一个意志格外坚决的下巴和一口完美的牙齿。她身躯结实而柔韧，通体焕发着健康的光彩。

在朋友们看来，露丝是个令人费解的谜。她这人不好懂。在她走动的那个圈子里，姑娘们都早早出嫁；然而一季接着一季，露丝始终独身，而且明摆着完全出于自愿，以致对这种事态的惯常解释，一经验证便告失效。

头两个社交季里成群结队，近来虽不那么齐心了，各色身份的男人——从一位亲王（略经风霜，好歹是位亲王）到班尼斯特家的英国管家（是好人，只是当时受了茶色波特酒的影响）——都曾把心捧到她脚边。而他们无一例外，都只得把心拾起来，另送别处。这种场合她照例和气，却也照例毫不动摇。那个坚决的下巴一点不给人留下「软磨硬泡可以奏效」的希望；为下巴撑腰的那双眼睛虽然讨人喜欢，却绝不通融。

===Original===
Generally it was with a feeling akin to relief that the rejected, when time had begun to heal the wound, contemplated their position. There was something about this girl, they decided, which no fellow could understand: she frightened them; she made them feel that their hands were large and red and their minds weak and empty. She was waiting for something. What it was they did not know, but it was plain that they were not it, and off they went to live happily ever after with girls who ate candy and read bestsellers. And Ruth went on her way, cool and watchful and mysterious, waiting.

The room which Ruth had taken for her own gave, like all rooms when intelligently considered, a clue to the character of its owner. It was the only room in the house furnished with any taste or simplicity. The furniture was exceedingly expensive, but did not look so. The keynote of the colour-scheme was green and white. All round the walls were books. Except for a few prints, there were no pictures; and the only photograph visible stood in a silver frame on a little table.

It was the portrait of a woman of about fifty, square-jawed, tight-lipped, who stared almost threateningly out of the frame; exceedingly handsome, but, to the ordinary male, too formidable to be attractive. On this was written in a bold hand, bristling with emphatic downstrokes and wholly free from feminine flourish: “To my dear Ruth from her Aunt Lora.” And below the signature, in what printers call “quotes,” a line that was evidently an extract from somebody’s published works: “Bear the torch and do not falter.”

===Chinese===
待到时光开始愈合伤口，那些遭拒的人回顾自己的处境，多半竟近乎如释重负。他们断定，这姑娘身上有种哪个爷们儿也参不透的东西：她让他们害怕；她让他们觉得自己双手又大又红，头脑空虚孱弱。她在等待某样东西。是什么他们不知道，但显而易见他们不是那东西，于是一个个掉头而去，从此跟吃糖果、读畅销书的姑娘们过上了幸福日子。而露丝继续走她的路，冷静、警觉、神秘，等着。

露丝据为己有的那间屋子，正如一切屋子只要聪明地加以审视都会的那样，给出了主人性格的线索。它是全宅唯一陈设得算得上有品位、有简趣的房间。家具昂贵之至，看着却不像。色调设计的主调是绿与白。四壁皆是书。除了几幅版画，别无图画；唯一可见的照片立在小几上的一只银相框里。

那是一位五十岁上下的妇人的肖像，方下巴，紧闭着嘴，几乎是威吓似的从相框里瞪视出来；风仪极美，但在寻常男人看来威势太盛，引不起爱慕。照片上题着一手遒劲的字，笔笔下捺铿锵有力，全无闺秀花哨：「赠我亲爱的小露丝——洛拉姑妈」。署名底下，用排字工人所谓的「引号」引着一行字，显然摘自某人的出版著作：「高擎火炬，切勿踌躇。」

===Original===
Bailey inspected this photograph with disfavour. It always irritated him. The information, conveyed to him by amused friends, that his Aunt Lora had once described Ruth as a jewel in a dustbin, seemed to him to carry an offensive innuendo directed at himself and the rest of the dwellers in the Bannister home. Also, she had called him a worm. Also, again, his actual encounters with the lady, though few, had been memorably unpleasant. Furthermore, he considered that she had far too great an influence on Ruth. And, lastly, that infernal sentence about the torch, which he found perfectly meaningless, had a habit of running in his head like a catchphrase, causing him the keenest annoyance.

He pursed his lips disapprovingly and averted his eyes.

“Don’t sniff at Aunt Lora, Bailey,” said Ruth. “I’ve had to speak to you about that before. What’s the matter? What has sent you flying up here?”

===Chinese===
贝利不大领教地打量着这张照片。它一贯叫他冒火。爱看热闹的朋友们传话给他，说他的洛拉姑妈有回把露丝形容成垃圾箱里的一颗珠宝；这话在他听来，暗含着冲他本人以及班尼斯特宅中其余住户而来的无礼影射。何况，她叫过他一条虫子。何况又何况，他与这位夫人当面打过的交道虽则寥寥，却无不令人难忘地不愉快。再者，他认为她对露丝的影响实在太大。最后，那句关于火炬的混账话——他只觉得全无意义——还惯会像口头禅似的在他脑袋里打转，惹得他恼怒至极。

他不以为然地嘬起嘴唇，把目光挪开了。

「别冲洛拉姑妈嗤之以鼻，贝利，」露丝说。「这个我以前就不得不说过你了。出什么事了？什么风把你飞也似的刮上楼来了？」

===Original===
“I have had a shock,” said Bailey. “I have been very greatly disturbed. I have just been speaking to Clarence Grayling.”

He eyed her accusingly through his gold-rimmed glasses. She remained tranquil.

“And what had Clarence to say?”

“A great many things.”

“I gather he told you I had refused him.”

“If it were only that!”

===Chinese===
「我受了一场惊吓，」贝利说，「我给搅得心神大乱。我刚才在同克拉伦斯·格雷林说话。」

他从金边眼镜后面拿控诉的眼光看她。她纹丝不动。

「克拉伦斯说了些什么？」

「好大一堆话。」

「我猜他是告诉你，我回绝了他。」

「要是仅仅如此倒好了！」

===Original===
Ruth rapped the piano sharply.

“Bailey,” she said, “wake up. Either get to the point or go or read a book or do some tatting or talk about something else. You know perfectly well that I absolutely refuse to endure your impressive manner. I believe when people ask you the time you look pained and important and make a mystery of it. What’s troubling you? I should have thought Clarence would have kept quiet about insulting me. But apparently he has no sense of shame.”

Bailey gaped. Bailey was shocked and alarmed.

“Insulting you! What do you mean? Clarence is a gentleman. He is incapable of insulting a woman.”

“Is he? He told me I was a suitable wife for a wretched dwarf with the miserably inadequate intelligence which nature gave him reduced to practically a minus quantity by alcohol! At least, he implied it. He asked me to marry him.”

===Chinese===
露丝啪的敲了一下钢琴。

「贝利，」她说，「醒醒。要么直奔正题，要么走人，要么找本书读读，要么做做梭结花边，要么换个话题聊聊。你清楚得很，你这种煞有介事的派头我绝对拒绝忍受。我看人家向你问个时间，你都要摆出又沉痛又重大的神色，把这事弄成一桩悬案。到底什么事烦着你？我还以为克拉伦斯侮辱了我，总该知趣闭嘴呢。看来他显然毫无羞耻之心。」

贝利张大了嘴。贝利又惊又惧。

「侮辱你！你这话什么意思？克拉伦斯是位绅士。他不可能侮辱一位女士。」

「是吗？他倒是让我明白了，我是配一个可怜侏儒的合适妻子——那侏儒天生那点智力本就可怜巴巴地不敷使用，又叫酒精折损得几乎成了负数！至少，他是暗示了这个意思。他向我求了婚。」

===Original===
“I have just left him at the club. He is very upset.”

“I should imagine so.” A soft smile played over Ruth’s face. “I spoke to Clarence. I explained things to him. I lit up Clarence’s little mind like a searchlight.”

Bailey rose, tremulous with just wrath.

“You spoke to him in a way that I can only call outrageous and improper, and﻿—er﻿—outrageous.”

He paced the room with agitated strides. Ruth watched him calmly.

“If the overflowing emotion of a giant soul in torment makes you knock over a table or smash a chair,” she said, “I shall send the bill for repairs to you. You had far better sit down and talk quietly. What *is* worrying you, Bailey?”

===Chinese===
「我刚才在俱乐部同他分手。他难受得很。」

「这我料想得到。」一抹温柔的微笑在露丝脸上掠过。「我同克拉伦斯谈了话。我向他解释了种种事情。我像一盏探照灯，照亮了克拉伦斯那颗小小的头脑。」

贝利站起身来，义愤填膺，浑身发颤。

「你同他说话的那副腔调，我只能称之为蛮横无礼、有失体统，而且——呃——蛮横无礼。」

他迈着激动的步子在屋里踱来踱去。露丝静静地看他。

「倘若一个受煎熬的伟大灵魂情感四溢，弄得你撞翻一张桌子、砸坏一把椅子，」她说，「修理费账单我就给你寄去。你还是乖乖坐下、安安静静说话的好。你*究竟*怎么了，贝利？」

===Original===
“Is it nothing,” demanded her brother, “that my sister should have spoken to a man as you spoke to Clarence Grayling?”

With an impassioned gesture he sent a flower-vase crashing to the floor.

“I told you so,” said Ruth. “Pick up the bits, and don’t let the water spoil the carpet. Use your handkerchief. I should say that that would cost you about six dollars, dear. Why will you let yourself be so temperamental? Now let me try and think what it was I said to Clarence. As far as I can remember it was the mere A.B.C. of eugenics.”

Bailey, on his knees, picking up broken glass, raised a flushed and accusing face.

“Ah! Eugenics! You admit it!”

===Chinese===
「我妹妹用你对克拉伦斯·格雷林说话的那种方式去同一位男士说话，」她哥哥质问，「难道是小事一桩吗？」

一个激愤的手势过去，他把一只花瓶扫落在地，摔了个粉碎。

「我说什么来着。」露丝说。「把碎片捡起来，别让水毁了地毯。用你的手帕。亲爱的，我估计这一下要花你六美元上下。你干吗由着自己这么感情用事？来，让我想想我当初同克拉伦斯说了些什么。就我记忆所及，不过是优生学的区区 ABC。」

贝利跪在地上捡碎玻璃，抬起一张涨红的、兴师问罪的脸。

「啊哈！优生学！你招了！」

===Original===
“I think,” went on Ruth placidly, “I asked him what sort of children he thought we were likely to have if we married.”

“A nice girl ought not to think about such things.”

“I don’t think about anything else much. A woman can’t do a great deal, even nowadays, but she can have a conscience and feel that she owes something to the future of the race. She can feel that it is her duty to bring fine children into the world. As Aunt Lora says, she can carry the torch and not falter.”
