Kirk got up and began to walk quickly up and down. Steve watched him furtively.
“You want to take your mind off it,” he said. “You’ll be all in if you keep on worrying about it in that way.”
Kirk stopped in his stride.
“That’s what the doctor said,” he snapped savagely. “What do you two fools think I’m made of?” He recovered himself quickly, ashamed of the outburst. “I’m sorry, Steve. Don’t mind anything I say. It’s awfully good of you to have come here, and I’m not going to forget it.”

===Chinese===

柯克没有作答。画室里此刻静极了。外头街上，一辆重型货车隆隆驶过。有人扯着嗓子吼了几句流行歌。史蒂夫腾地站起身。
「我去收拾那小子。」他说。可歌声停了，他又坐了回去。
柯克站起来，开始快步来回走动。史蒂夫悄悄觑着他。
「你得分点心到别处去，」他说，「照这么个愁法，你非整个垮掉不可。」
柯克收住了脚步。
「医生也是这么说的，」他恶声恶气地抢白道，「你们两个傻瓜当我是铁打的不成？」他很快回过神来，为这通发作感到羞愧。「对不起，史蒂夫。我说什么都别往心里去。你能上这儿来，真是好得没法说，我不会忘的。」

===Original===

Steve scratched his chin reflectively.
“Say, I’ll tell you something,” he said. “My mother told me once that when I was born my old dad took it just like you. Found he was getting all worked up by having to hang around and do nothing, so he says to himself: ‘I’ve got to take my mind off this business, or it’s me for the foolish-house.’
“Well, sir, there was a big guy down on that street who’d been picking on dad good and hard for a mighty long while. And this guy suddenly comes into dad’s mind. He felt of his muscle, dad did. ‘Gee!’ he says to himself, ‘I believe the way I’m feeling, I could just go and eat up that gink right away.’ And the more he thought of it, the better it looked to him, so all of a sudden he grabs his hat and beats it like a streak down to the saloon on the corner, where he knew the feller would be at that time, and he goes straight up to him and hands him one.
“Back comes the guy at him﻿—he was a great big son of a gun, weighing thirty pounds more than dad﻿—and him and dad mixes it right there in the saloon till the barkeep and about fifty other fellers throws them out, and they goes off to a vacant lot to finish the thing. And dad’s so worked up that he gives the other guy his till he hollers that that’s all he’ll want. And then dad goes home and waits quite quiet and happy and peaceful till they tell him I’m there.”

===Chinese===

史蒂夫若有所思地挠着下巴。
「我说，我给你讲桩事儿，」他说，「我妈有一回告诉我，我出生那会儿，我爸的架势跟你一模一样。他发现自己非得干守着没事干不可，心火越熬越旺，就自言自语道：『这事我得想法子岔开去，不然就该送我进疯人院了。』
「嘿，你听着，我们那条街上有个大块头，天长日久地死命欺负我爸。这小子突然就冒进了爸的脑子。爸捏了捏自己的肌肉。『嚯！』他心里说，『照我现在这感觉，我看我能这就过去，把那家伙一口吞喽。』他越琢磨越觉得这主意妙不可言，于是冷不丁抓起帽子，一溜烟奔到街角的酒馆——他算准那个钟点那小子准在——径直走到人家跟前，抡手就给了他一下。
「那家伙反手就扑回来——他是个五大三粗的狠角色，比我爸重三十磅——俩人当场就在酒馆里打开了锅，直到酒保带着五十来号人把他们扔出门去，两人又转场到一块空地把事情做个了断。我爸那股火气憋得太足，把对方结结实实伺候了个遍，直打得他嗷嗷叫着告饶，说够了够了。然后我爸就回了家，安安静静、欢欢喜喜、心平气和地干等着，一直等到人家告诉他，我出世了。」

===Original===

Steve paused.
“Kirk,” he said then, “how would you like a round or two with the small gloves, just to get things off your mind for a spell and pass the time? My dad said he found it eased him mighty good.”
Kirk stared at him.
“Just a couple of rounds,” urged Steve. “And you can go all out at that. I shan’t mind. Just try to think I’m some guy that’s been picking on you and let me have it. See what I mean?”
For the first time that day the faint ghost of a grin appeared on Kirk’s face.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫停了停。
「柯克，」他这才说，「你乐意戴上小拳套来上一两个回合吗？就为把心事岔开一会儿，也消磨消磨时间。我爸说他发现这法子给他解乏解得特灵。」
柯克直勾勾地盯着他。
「就打两个回合，」史蒂夫怂恿道，「到时候你尽管放开手脚打。我不会介意的。你就只当我是哪个平日欺负你的家伙，可着劲儿招呼我。明白我的意思吧？」
那一天里，一抹笑意的淡淡幽影头一回浮上了柯克的脸。

===Original===

“I wonder if you’re right, Steve?”
“I know I’m right. And, say, don’t think I don’t need it, too. I ain’t known Miss Ruth all this time for nothing. You’ll be doing me a kindness if you knock my face in.”
The small gloves occupied a place of honour to themselves in a lower drawer. It was not often that Kirk used them in his friendly bouts with Steve. For ordinary occasions the larger and more padded species met with his approval. Steve, during these daily sparring encounters, was amiability itself; but he could not be counted upon not to forget himself for an occasional moment in the heat of the fray; and though Kirk was courageous enough, he preferred to preserve the regularity of his features at the expense of a little extra excitement.
Once, after a brisk rally, he had gone about the world looking as if he was suffering from mumps, owing to a right hook which no one regretted more than Steve himself.
But today was different; and Kirk felt that even a repetition of that lethal punch would be welcome.

===Chinese===

「不知你说得对不对，史蒂夫？」
「我知道我对。还有，我说，你可别以为我自己就不需要这个。我跟露丝小姐相识这么久，可不是白相识的。你要是把我的脸打开花，那就算帮了我一个大忙。」
小拳套在底层抽屉里独享一个尊位。柯克跟史蒂夫进行友谊切磋时，并不常动用它们。寻常场合，他认可的是那种更大、衬垫更厚的品类。这些每日一度的拳击雅集上，史蒂夫本是和气的化身；可谁也不能担保他在酣战之际不偶尔忘乎所以那么一小下；柯克胆量虽说足够，却宁可牺牲一点额外的刺激，也要保全五官的端正。
有一回，一场短促激烈的交手过后，他就顶着一张活像害了腮腺炎的脸在世上走了好些天，祸根是一记右勾拳——对这记拳最痛心疾首的，莫过于史蒂夫本人。
可今天不同；柯克觉得，哪怕那记致命重拳再来上一遍，也是受欢迎的。

===Original===

Steve, when the contest opened, was disposed to be consolatory in word as well as deed. He kept up a desultory conversation as he circled and feinted.
“You gotta look at it this way,” he began, sidestepping a left, “it ain’t often you hear of anything going wrong at times like this. You gotta remember”﻿—he hooked Kirk neatly on the jaw﻿—“that” he concluded.
Kirk came back with a swing at the body which made his adversary grunt.
“That’s true,” he said.
“Sure,” rejoined Steve a little breathlessly, falling into a clinch.

===Chinese===

开赛之后，史蒂夫存心在言行两面都予人宽慰。他一边绕圈子、施佯攻，一边有一搭没一搭地聊着。
「你得这么看问题，」他开腔道，同时侧步闪过一记左拳，「这种时候出岔子的事，可不常听说。你还得记住」——他干净利落地一记勾拳打中柯克下巴——「那个。」他如此作结。
柯克回敬一记扫向躯干的横拳，打得对手闷哼一声。
「这话不假。」他说。
「可不，」史蒂夫有点气喘吁吁地应道，顺势扭抱上去。

===Original===

They moved warily round each other.
“So,” said Steve, blocking a left, “that ought to comfort you some.”
Kirk nodded. He guessed correctly that the other was alluding to his last speech, not to the counter which had just made the sight of his left eye a little uncertain.
Gradually, as the bout progressed, Kirk began to lose the slight diffidence which had hampered him at the start. He had been feeling so wonderfully friendly toward Steve, so grateful for his presence, and his sympathy, that it had been hard, in spite of the other’s admonitions, to enter into the fray with any real conviction. Moreover, subconsciously, he was listening all the time for sounds from above which never came.
These things gave a certain lameness to his operations. It was immediately after this blow in the eye, mentioned above, that he ceased to be an individual with private troubles and a wandering mind, and became a boxer pure and simple, his whole brain concentrated on the problem of how to get past his opponent’s guard.

===Chinese===

两人小心翼翼地绕着圈子。
「所以说，」史蒂夫挡开一记左拳，说道，「这话该能安慰你几分。」
柯克点了点头。他猜得不错：对方援引的是自己上一句发言，而不是刚刚那记打得他左眼视线有点打晃的回拳。
随着战局展开，开场时掣肘着柯克的那点拘谨渐渐消退了。他对史蒂夫友善得了不得，感激他的到来、感激他的同情，感激到任凭对方再三鼓动，也难以真正放开手投入战斗。再者，他下意识里一直在竖着耳朵等楼上的动静，而那动静始终不来。
这些都使他的身手带了三分疲软。正是在上文提到的那记眼上打击之后，他不再是一个怀抱私愁、神思恍惚的个人，而成了一名彻头彻尾的拳手，全部脑力都倾注在如何攻破对手防守这道难题上。

===Original===

Steve, recognizing the change in an instant, congratulated himself on the success of his treatment. It had worked even more quickly than he had hoped. He helped the cure with another swift jab which shot over Kirk’s guard.
Kirk came in with a rush. Steve slipped him. Kirk rushed again. Steve, receiving a hard punch on a nose which, though accustomed to such assaults, had never grown really to enjoy them, began to feel a slight diminution of his detached attitude toward this encounter. Till now his position had been purely that of the kindly physician soothing a patient. The rapidity with which the patient was permitting himself to be soothed rendered the post of physician something of a sinecure; and Steve, as Kirk had done, began to slip back into the boxer.
It was while he was in what might be called a transition stage that an unexpected swing sent him with some violence against the wall; and from that moment nature asserted itself. A curious, set look appeared on his face; wrinkles creased his forehead; his jaw protruded slightly.
Kirk made another rush. This time Steve did not slip; he went to meet it, head down and hands busy.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫一眼认出了这个变化，暗自庆贺自己疗法的成功。见效之快，还超出了他的期望。他又补上一记越过柯克防线的迅捷刺拳，以助疗效。
柯克猛冲上来。史蒂夫侧身闪过。柯克再度猛冲。史蒂夫的鼻子结结实实吃了一拳——这鼻子虽惯经此等招待，却从未真正培养出对它们的喜好——他开始感到，自己对本场交锋的那份超然态度略有折损。在此之前，他的立场纯粹是慈心医师安抚病人。而病人被安抚时缴械之快，使医师这个职位颇有沦为闲差之势；于是史蒂夫也像柯克方才那样，开始滑回拳手本色。
正处在这种可以称之为过渡阶段的当口，一记始料未及的横拳把他相当凶猛地送上了墙；自那一刻起，天性大获全胜。他脸上现出一种奇特的、凝定的神色；额头上挤出了皱纹；下巴微微前凸。
柯克又发起一次猛冲。这一回史蒂夫不再闪避；他低头迎了上去，双手翻飞。
---

===Original===

Mrs. Lora Delane Porter came downstairs with the measured impressiveness of one who bears weighty news. Her determined face was pale and tired, as it had every right to be; but she bore herself proudly, as one who has fought and not been defeated.
“Mr. Winfield,” she said.
There was no answer. Looking about her, she found the studio empty.
Then, from behind the closed door of the inner room, she was aware of a strange, shuffling sound. She listened, astonished. She heard a gasp, then curious thuds, finally a bump louder than the thuds. And then there was silence.
These things surprised Mrs. Porter. She opened the door and looked in.

===Chinese===

洛拉·德莱恩·波特夫人走下楼来，步履之间带着身负重大消息者特有的庄重气派。她那张坚毅的脸苍白而疲惫——它完全有权利如此；但她的举止昂然自得，像一位战斗过而未被击垮的人。
「温菲尔德先生。」她说。
无人应答。她环顾四周，发现画室空无一人。
接着，从里间紧闭的门后，她觉察到一阵奇怪的拖踏之声。她诧异地侧耳倾听。她听见一声喘息，随后是几下古怪的闷响，最后是一声比闷响更响的撞击。然后便归于寂静。
这一切令波特夫人深感意外。她推开门往里看。

===Original===

It says much for her iron self-control that she remained quiet at this point. A lesser person, after a far less tiring ordeal than she had passed through, would have found relief in some cry or exclamation﻿—possibly even in a scream.
Against the far wall, breathing hard and fondling his left eye with a four-ounce glove, leaned Steve Dingle. His nose was bleeding somewhat freely, but this he appeared to consider a trifle unworthy of serious attention. On the floor, an even more disturbing spectacle, Kirk lay at full length. To Mrs. Porter’s startled gaze he appeared to be dead. He too, was bleeding, but he was not in a position to notice it.
“It’s all right, ma’am,” said Steve, removing the hand from his face and revealing an eye which for spectacular dilapidation must have rivalled the epoch-making one which had so excited his mother on a famous occasion. “It’s nothing serious.”
“Has Mr. Winfield fainted?”
“Not exactly fainted, ma’am. It’s like this. He’d got me clear up in a corner, and I seen it’s up to me if I don’t want to be knocked through the wall, so I has to cross him. Maybe I’d gotten a little worked up myself by then. But it was my fault. I told him to go all out, and he sure did. This eye’s going to be a pippin tomorrow.”
Mrs. Porter examined the wounded organ with interest.

===Chinese===

她此刻竟能保持安静，足证其钢铁般的自制力。换个成色稍逊的人，哪怕经受的考验远不如她方才这场劳神，也早就要喊一声、叹一句来求个宣泄了——没准还会尖叫出声。
史蒂夫·丁格尔靠着远处的墙，喘着粗气，用一只四盎司的拳套摩挲着自己的左眼。他的鼻子出血出得相当畅快，但他似乎认为这是桩不值一顾的小事。地板上还有一幅更为骇人的景象：柯克直挺挺地躺着。在波特夫人受惊的目光里，他看上去已经死了。他也在流血，只是眼下的处境使他无从留意此事。
「没大事，夫人，」史蒂夫说着，把捂在脸上的手挪开，露出一只眼睛——论蔚为大观的惨状，这只眼想必足可与某个著名场合里那只令他母亲激动不已的、划时代的眼睛媲美。「不碍事的。」
「温菲尔德先生晕过去了吗？」
「不算晕，夫人。是这么回事。他把我一直堵在角落里，我瞅明白了：再不还手，就得被打穿这堵墙，所以我只好回他一记交叉拳。兴许我那会儿自己也上了点火。不过这得怪我。是我叫他放开手脚打的，他也真放开了。这只眼明天准是个顶呱呱的稀罕物。」
波特夫人饶有兴味地检视了这只受创的器官。

===Original===

“That, I suppose Mr. Dingle, is what you call a blue eye?”
“It sure is, ma’am.”
“What has been happening?”
“Well, it’s this way. I see he’s all worked up, sitting around doing nothing except wait, so I makes him come and spar a round to take his mind off it. My old dad, ma’am, when I was coming along, found that dope fixed him all right, so I reckoned it would do as much good here. My old dad went and beat the block off a fellow down our street, and it done him a lot of good.”
Mrs. Porter shook his gloved hand.
“Mr. Dingle,” she said with enthusiasm, “I really believe that you are the only sensible man I have ever met. Your common sense is astonishing. I have no doubt you saved Mr. Winfield from a nervous breakdown. Would you be kind enough, when you are rested, to fetch some water and bring him to and inform him that he is the father of a son?”

===Chinese===

「那个，我猜丁格尔先生，就是你们所谓的乌眼青吧？」
「可不就是，夫人。」
「这里究竟出了什么事？」
「是这么回事，夫人。我瞧他心焦得不行，干坐着没事干、光知道等，就叫他过来打一轮拳，把心思岔开。我爸，夫人，当年快有我的时候，发现那剂偏方治他这毛病灵验得很，我就寻思它在这儿也能有同样的好处。我爸当年就跑去把我们街上一个家伙结结实实揍了个透，结果对他大有裨益。」
波特夫人握了握他那只戴拳套的手。
「丁格尔先生，」她热情地说，「我真心相信，你是我这辈子遇见过的唯一一个明白人。你的常识令人叹服。你把温菲尔德先生从一场精神崩溃里解救了出来，这一点我毫不怀疑。可否劳你的驾，等你缓过劲儿来，取些水来把他救醒，并通知他，他已是一个儿子的父亲？」

## III The Mates Meet / 第三章 情侣相聚

===Original===
Kirk Winfield was an amiable, if rather weak, young man with whom life, for twenty-five years, had dealt kindly. He had perfect health, an income more than sufficient for his needs, a profession which interested without monopolizing him, a thoroughly contented disposition, and the happy knack of surrounding himself with friends.

That he had to contribute to the support of the majority of these friends might have seemed a drawback to some men. Kirk did not object to it in the least. He had enough money to meet their needs, and, being a sociable person who enjoyed mixing with all sorts and conditions of men, he found the Liberty Hall regime pleasant.

He liked to be a magnet, attracting New York’s Bohemian population. If he had his preferences among the impecunious crowd who used the studio as a chapel of ease, strolling in when it pleased them, drinking his whisky, smoking his cigarettes, borrowing his money, and, on occasion, his spare bedrooms and his pyjamas, he never showed it. He was fully as pleasant to Percy Shanklyn, the elegant, perpetually resting English actor, whom he disliked as far as he was capable of disliking anyone, as he was to Hank Jardine, the prospector, and Hank’s prizefighter friend, Steve Dingle, both of whom he liked enormously.

It seemed to him sometimes that he had drifted into the absolutely ideal life. He lived entirely in the present. The passage of time left him untouched. Day followed day, week followed week, and nothing seemed to change. He was never unhappy, never ill, never bored.

===Chinese===
柯克·温菲尔德（Kirk Winfield）是个和蔼可亲、只是略欠刚强的年轻人，二十五年来，生活一向待他不薄。他身体无懈可击，收入远超所需，职业既让他兴致盎然又不至吞没他全副身心，脾性十足知足，还有一身把朋友们聚拢在周围的可喜本领。

这些朋友里的大多数还得靠他掏钱养活，这在某些人看来未免是个美中不足。柯克却毫不在意。他的钱足够应付他们的需求，何况他本是个爱交际的人，乐于同三教九流厮混，于是觉得这种人人来去自便、宾至如归的「自由宫」日子过得相当惬意。

他乐意做一块磁石，把纽约的波希米亚众生都吸过来。那帮囊中羞涩的家伙把他的画室当成一座随到随用的「方便堂」——高兴了就溜达进来，喝他的威士忌，抽他的香烟，借他的钱，兴致来时连备用的卧室和睡衣也一并借了。在这一路人马中间，他纵然存着什么偏心眼，也从不流露。他对珀西·尚克林（Percy Shanklyn）——那位风度翩翩、永远「歇业待聘」的英国演员，也是他以有限的本事最不喜欢的人——同他对探矿人汉克·贾丁（Hank Jardine）以及汉克那位职业拳手朋友史蒂夫·丁格尔（Steve Dingle）——这两位他都极其喜欢——态度同样和气。

有时候他觉得自己简直是漂进了一种尽善尽美的理想生活。他完完全全活在当下，光阴的流逝碰不着他一根汗毛。日子接着日子，星期接着星期，似乎万事一成不变。他从不烦闷，从不生病，从不无聊。

===Original===
He would get up in the morning with the comfortable knowledge that the day held no definite duties. George Pennicut would produce one of his excellent breakfasts. The next milestone would be the arrival of Steve Dingle. Five brisk rounds with Steve, a cold bath, and a rubdown took him pleasantly on to lunch, after which it amused him to play at painting.

There was always something to do when he wearied of that until, almost before the day had properly begun, up came George with one of his celebrated dinners. And then began the incursion of his friends. One by one they would drop in, making themselves very much at home, to help their host through till bedtime. And another day would slip into the past.

It never occurred to Kirk that he was wasting his life. He had no ambitions. Ambition is born of woman, and no woman that he had ever met had ever stirred him deeply. He had never been in love, and he had come to imagine that he was incapable of anything except a mild liking for women. He considered himself immune, and was secretly glad of it. He enjoyed his go-as-you-please existence too much to want to have it upset. He belonged, in fact, to the type which, when the moment arrives, falls in love very suddenly, very violently, and for all time.

Nothing could have convinced him of this. He was like a child lighting matches in a powder-magazine. When the idea of marriage crossed his mind he thrust it from him with a kind of shuddering horror. He could not picture to himself a woman who could compensate him for the loss of his freedom and, still less, of his friends.

===Chinese===
早晨起床时，他心里安安稳稳地知道：这一天没有任何非办不可的差事。乔治·佩尼卡特（George Pennicut）会端出他那些精美早餐中的一顿。下一个里程碑，便是史蒂夫·丁格尔的驾到。同史蒂夫干脆利落地打上五个回合，再来个冷水浴和浑身擦摩，他便舒舒服服地挨到了午饭；饭后，把画画当玩意儿耍一耍，也是一桩乐事。

等这一样玩腻了，总还有别的事可做，直到——这一天几乎还没正式开张——乔治便端着他那远近闻名的晚餐中的一款上楼来。跟着，朋友们的大举入侵开始了。他们一个接一个顺脚溜进来，俨然把这儿当成自己家，帮着主人一路厮混到就寝时分。于是，又一天悄悄溜进了过去。

柯克从来没有想过自己是在虚掷光阴。他没有任何抱负。抱负这东西，生于女人，而他平生遇见的女人，没有一个能让他心旌深摇。他从未恋爱过，还渐渐以为自己天生只懂得对女人存一分不冷不热的淡淡好感。他自认为有了免疫力，并为此暗自庆幸。他太留恋这种随心所欲的日子了，绝不肯让人搅了。而实际上，他属于那样一类人：时辰一到，便会爱得极突然、极猛烈，而且一爱便是终生。

任凭什么也说服不了他相信这一点。他就像在火药库里划火柴玩的小孩。每逢结婚的念头掠过脑海，他总怀着一种不寒而栗的恐怖把它一把推开。他想象不出世上有哪个女人能赔偿他失去的自由，更不用说失去的朋友了。

===Original===
His friends were men’s men; he could not see them fitting into a scheme of life that involved the perpetual presence of a hostess. Hank Jardine, for instance. To Kirk, the great point about Hank was that he had been everywhere, seen everything, and was, when properly stimulated with tobacco and drink, a fountain of reminiscence. But he could not talk unless he had his coat off and his feet up on the back of a chair. No hostess could be expected to relish that.

Hank was a bachelor’s friend; he did not belong in a married household. The abstract wife could not be reconciled to him, and Kirk, loving Hank like a brother, firmly dismissed the abstract wife.

He came to look upon himself as a confirmed bachelor. He had thought out the question of marriage in all its aspects, and decided against it. He was the strong man who knew his own mind and could not be shaken.

Yet, on the afternoon of the day following Mrs. Lora Delane Porter’s entry into his life, Kirk sat in the studio, feeling, for the first time in recent years, a vague discontent. He was uneasy, almost afraid. The slight dislocation in the smooth-working machinery of his existence, caused by the compulsory retirement of George Pennicut, had made him thoroughly uncomfortable. With discomfort had come introspection, and with introspection this uneasiness that was almost fear.

===Chinese===
他的朋友们个个都是须眉中之须眉；他没法设想他们能塞进一种终身有女主人当值的生活格局里去。就拿汉克·贾丁来说。在柯克看来，汉克最大的妙处在于他四海为家、什么都见识过，只要拿烟草和酒恰到好处地一催化，他就是一座往事的喷泉。可是，不脱外套、不把双脚翘上椅背，他就开不了腔。这做派，随便哪位女主人都不可能甘之如饴。

汉克是单身汉之友；他不属于有妇之夫的家庭。「抽象的妻子」跟他势难两立，而柯克既然爱汉克如手足，便断然回绝了「抽象的妻子」。

他渐渐把自己看成一个铁了心的单身汉。他把婚姻问题的方方面面都想了个透，做出了否决。他是那种自有主见、坚如磐石的强人。

然而，在洛拉·德莱恩·波特夫人（Mrs. Lora Delane Porter）闯进他生活的第二天下午，柯克坐在画室里，多年来头一回感到一种隐隐约约的不满足。他心神不定，几乎可以说是害怕。乔治·佩尼卡特的被迫挂冠，给他那部运转圆滑的生活机器造成了一点小脱节，把他弄得浑身不自在。不自在引出了自我反省，自我反省又引出了这份近乎恐惧的不安。

===Original===
A man, living alone, without money troubles to worry him, sinks inevitably into a routine. Fatted ease is good for no one. It sucks the soul out of a man. Kirk, as he sat smoking in the cool dusk of the studio, was wondering, almost in a panic, whether all was well with himself.

This mild domestic calamity had upset him so infernally. It could not be right that so slight a change in his habits should have such an effect upon him. George had been so little hurt﻿—the doctor gave him a couple of days before complete recovery﻿—that it had not seemed worth while to Kirk to engage a substitute. It was simpler to go out for his meals and make his own bed. And it was the realization that this alteration in his habits had horribly disturbed and unsettled him that was making Kirk subject himself now to an examination of quite unusual severity.

He hated softness. Physically, he kept himself always in perfect condition. Had he become spiritually flabby? Certainly this unexpected call on his energies would appear to have found him unprepared. It spoiled his whole day, knowing, when he got out of bed in the morning, that he must hunt about and find his food instead of sitting still and having it brought to him. It frightened him to think how set he had become.

Forty-eight hours ago he would have scorned the suggestion that he coddled himself. He would have produced as evidence to the contrary his cold baths, his exercises, his bouts with Steve Dingle. Today he felt less confidence. For all his baths and boxing, the fact remained that he had become, at the age of twenty-six, such a slave to habit that a very trifling deviation from settled routine had been enough to poison life for him.

===Chinese===
一个人独居独处，又没有钱粮的烦恼来搅扰，必然要沉进一潭按部就班的日子。养尊处优于人无益，它能把人的魂儿一点点吸出去。柯克坐在画室清凉的暮色里吞云吐雾，几乎带着恐慌地琢磨：自己这一切，究竟还正常不正常？

这么一场区区的家庭小灾，竟把他搅得如此天翻地覆。生活习惯里这样一点小小的变动就落得这般效果，总归说不过去。乔治伤得那样轻——医生说，过上一两天便可痊愈——柯克觉得犯不着另雇一个人。出去吃饭、自己铺床，岂不更省事。而正是意识到生活习惯上的一点变动竟把他搅得心慌意乱、六神无主，柯克此刻才对自己展开了一场严苛得异乎寻常的审查。

他憎恶松松垮垮。肉体上，他一向把自己保持在巅峰状态。那么，精神上他是不是已经软塌塌了？当然，这回对他精力的一场意外征调，看来是发现他毫无准备。清晨下床时想到必须四处奔走、自己觅食，而不是安坐家中等着饭来张口，他一整天就全毁了。一想到自己已经积习深重到这般田地，他不免心惊。

四十八小时以前，谁要是说他娇惯自己，他准会嗤之以鼻。他会搬出他的冷水浴、他的体操、他同史蒂夫·丁格尔的一场场拳斗来做反证。今天他可没有那么足的底气了。任凭他洗澡、打拳，事实俱在：他已在二十六岁的年纪变成了习惯的奴隶，日常定规哪怕偏离一星半点，也足以把他的人生搅得苦不堪言。

===Original===
Bachelors have these black moments, and it is then that the abstract wife comes into her own. To Kirk, brooding in the dusk, the figure of the abstract wife seemed to grow less formidable, the fact that she might not get on with Hank Jardine of less importance.

The revolutionary thought that life was rather a bore, and would become more and more of a bore as the years went on, unless he had someone to share it with, crept into his mind and stayed there.

He shivered. These were unpleasant thoughts, and in his hour of clear vision he knew whence they came. They were entirely due to the knowledge that, instead of sitting comfortably at home, he would be compelled in a few short hours to go out and get dinner at some restaurant. To such a pass had he come in the twenty-sixth year of his life.

Once the gods have marked a bachelor down, they give him few chances of escape. It was when Kirk’s mood was at its blackest, and the figure of the abstract wife had ceased to be a menace and become a shining angel of salvation, that Lora Delane Porter, with Ruth Bannister at her side, rang the studio bell.

===Chinese===
单身汉都有这种阴暗时刻，而正是在这种时刻，「抽象的妻子」才扬眉吐气。在暮色中郁郁沉思的柯克眼里，「抽象的妻子」的身影似乎不那么可怕了，她可能跟汉克·贾丁处不来这件事，也显得不那么要紧了。

一个革命性的念头悄悄爬进他心里，并且赖着不走了：人生未免乏味，而随着年齿渐增还会越来越乏味，除非有个人来同他分享。

他打了个寒噤。这些念头可不大妙，而在这一刻目光澄澈之际，他明白它们的来路。它们完全源自这样一个认知：再过短短几个小时，他将不得不出门到哪家饭馆去解决晚饭，而不是舒舒服服地坐在家里。他在人生的第二十六个年头，竟落到了这般田地。

诸神一旦盯上了一个单身汉，便很少再给他逃生的机会。正是在柯克心情最阴郁、「抽象的妻子」的身影已经卸下威胁、变成一尊光灿灿的救世天使的时刻，洛拉·德莱恩·波特携着露丝·班尼斯特（Ruth Bannister）按响了画室的门铃。

===Original===
Kirk went to the door. He hoped it was a tradesman; he feared it was a friend. In his present state of mind he had no use for friends. When he found himself confronting Mrs. Porter he became momentarily incapable of speech. It had not entered his mind that she would pay him a second visit. Possibly it was joy that rendered him dumb.

“Good afternoon, Mr. Winfield,” said Mrs. Porter. “I have come to inquire after the man Pennicut. Ruth, this is Mr. Winfield. Mr. Winfield, my niece, Miss Bannister.”

And Kirk perceived for the first time that his visitor was not alone. In the shadow behind her a girl was standing. He stood aside to let Mrs. Porter pass, and Ruth came into the light.

If there are degrees in speechlessness, Kirk’s aphasia became doubled and trebled at the sight of her. It seemed to him that he went all to pieces, as if he had received a violent blow. Curious physical changes were taking place in him. His legs, which only that morning he had looked upon as eminently muscular, he now discovered to be composed of some curiously unstable jelly.

He also perceived﻿—a fact which he had never before suspected﻿—that he had heart-disease. His lungs, too, were in poor condition; he found it practically impossible to breathe. The violent trembling fit which assailed him he attributed to general organic weakness.

===Chinese===
柯克走去开门。他盼着来的是个送货的，怕的是来的是个朋友。以他此刻的心境，朋友们毫无用处。待到发现门口站着的是波特夫人，他一时间竟说不出话来。他压根没想到她会二度光临。让他哑口无言的，或许是欢喜。

「午安，温菲尔德先生，」波特夫人说。「我是来探问佩尼卡特这个人的伤情的。露丝，这位是温菲尔德先生。温菲尔德先生，这是我侄女班尼斯特小姐。」

柯克这才头一回发觉，来客并非孤身一人。她身后的暗影里立着一位姑娘。他侧身让波特夫人过去，露丝走进了亮处。

如果说失语也分等级，那么一见她，柯克的失语症顿时翻了倍又翻了番。他觉得自己整个人散了架，仿佛挨了狠狠一记闷棍。他身上正发生着种种古怪的身体变化。他那双腿，就在今天早晨他还认定它健美刚劲无比，如今却发现是由某种奇异地站不稳的胶状物构成的。

他还察觉——这是一个他从未疑心过的事实——自己患有心脏病。他的肺也状况不佳；他发现呼吸这桩事几乎没法进行了。至于那阵袭遍全身的剧烈颤抖，他把它归咎于周身器官的普遍衰弱。

===Original===
He gaped at Ruth.

Ruth, outwardly, remained unaffected by the meeting, but inwardly she was feeling precisely the same sensation of smallness which had come to Mrs. Porter on her first meeting with Kirk. If this sensation had been novel to Mrs. Porter, it was even stranger to Ruth.

To think humbly of herself was an experience that seldom happened to her. She was perfectly aware that her beauty was remarkable even in a city of beautiful women, and it was rarely that she permitted her knowledge of that fact to escape her. Her beauty, to her, was a natural phenomenon, impossible to overlook. The realization of it did not obtrude itself into her mind, it simply existed subconsciously.

Yet for an instant it ceased to exist. She was staggered by a sense of inferiority.

It lasted but a pinpoint of time, this riotous upheaval of her nature. She recovered herself so swiftly that Kirk, busy with his own emotions, had no suspicion of it.

A moment later he, too, was himself again. He was conscious of feeling curiously uplifted and thrilled, as if the world had suddenly become charged with ozone and electricity, and for some reason he felt capable of great feats of muscle and energy; but the aphasia had left him, and he addressed himself with a clear brain to the task of entertaining his visitors.

===Chinese===
他目瞪口呆地凝视着露丝。

露丝表面上不为这次会面所动，内心却体验着与波特夫人初次见到柯克时如出一辙的自惭形秽之感。如果说这种感觉对波特夫人来说是件新鲜事，那么对露丝就更是闻所未闻了。

谦卑地看轻自己，这于她是难得一遇的经历。她清楚得很，自己的美貌即便在这满城佳丽之中也属出挑，而她极少让这份自知显露于外。美貌于她，是一种无从忽视的自然现象。这份自知并不强行挤进她的心头，只是潜藏在意识底层，静静存在。

然而有一瞬间，它不复存在了。一阵自卑感把她击得东倒西歪。

她天性里这场翻天覆地的骚动，只持续了针尖大的一瞬。她恢复得太快，忙于应付自己心头波澜的柯克对这一切毫无察觉。

片刻之后，他也恢复了常态。他只觉得自己奇异地振奋昂扬，仿佛天地之间骤然充满了臭氧与电，而且不知怎的，自觉筋肉与精力足以成就大大的伟业；好在失语症已经退去，他便头脑清明地着手招待起客人来。

===Original===
“George is better today,” he reported.

“He never was bad,” said Mrs. Porter succinctly.

“He doesn’t think so.”

“Possibly not. He is hopelessly weak-minded.”

Ruth laughed. Kirk thrilled at the sound.

“Poor George!” she observed.

===Chinese===
「乔治今天好些了。」他报告道。

「他根本就没病过。」波特夫人言简意赅地说。

「他自己可不这么想。」

「也许吧。此人意志薄弱，不可救药。」

露丝笑了。这笑声让柯克心头一颤。

「可怜的乔治！」她说。

===Original===
“Don’t waste your sympathy, my dear,” said Mrs. Porter. “That he is injured at all is his own fault. For years he has allowed himself to become gross and flabby, with the result that the collision did damage which it would not have done to a man in hard condition. You, Mr. Winfield,” she added, turning abruptly to Kirk, “would scarcely have felt it. But then you,” went on Mrs. Porter, “are in good condition. Cold baths!”

“I beg your pardon?”

“Do you take cold baths?”

“I do.”

“Do you do Swedish exercises?”

“I go through a series of evolutions every morning, with the utmost loathing. I started them as a boy, and they have become a habit like dram-drinking. I would leave them off if I could, but I can’t.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的，别滥用你的同情，」波特夫人说。「他之所以会受伤，全得怪他自己。多年来他放任自己变得又肥又松，结果这一撞便造成了在一个体格结实的人身上根本不会有的损伤。换了您，温菲尔德先生，」她陡然转向柯克，补了一句，「恐怕连感觉都感觉不到。不过话说回来，您，」波特夫人接着说，「体格良好。冷水浴嘛！」

「您说什么？」

「您洗冷水浴吗？」

「洗。」

「您做瑞典体操吗？」

「我每天早晨都要深恶痛绝地操演一整套动作。这习惯是小时候落下的，如今已像贪杯嗜酒一样成了瘾。能戒我早戒了，可惜戒不掉。」

===Original===
“Do nothing of the kind. They are invaluable.”

“But undignified.”

“Let me feel your biceps, Mr. Winfield,” said Mrs. Porter. She nodded approvingly. “Like iron.” She poised a finger and ran a meditative glance over his form. Kirk eyed her apprehensively. The finger darted forward and struck home in the region of the third waistcoat button. “Wonderful!” she exclaimed. “Ruth!”

“Yes, aunt.”

“Prod Mr. Winfield where my finger is pointing. He is extraordinarily muscular.”

“I say, really!” protested Kirk. He was a modest young man, and this exploration of his more intimate anatomy by the fingertips of the girl he loved was not to be contemplated.

===Chinese===
「万万戒不得。它们是无价之宝。」

「可有失体统。」

「让我摸摸您的二头肌，温菲尔德先生。」波特夫人说。她赞许地点点头。「像铁一样。」她竖起一根手指，若有所思地把他全身上下打量一番。柯克提心吊胆地瞧着她。那根手指猛地一伸，正戳在马甲第三颗纽扣一带。「妙极了！」她叫道。「露丝！」

「哎，姑妈。」

「照我手指的地方戳一戳温菲尔德先生。他的肌肉发达得非同寻常。」

「喂，这可使不得！」柯克抗议道。他是个拘谨的年轻人，任凭心爱的姑娘用指尖来勘察他身上更私密的部位，这种事想都不容想。

===Original===
“Just as you please,” said Mrs. Porter. “If I were a man of your physique, I should be proud of it.”

“Wouldn’t you like to go up and see George?” asked Kirk. It was hard on George, but it was imperative that this woman be removed somehow.

“Very well. I have brought him a little book to read, which will do him good. It is called *Elementary Rules for the Preservation of the Body*.”

“He has learned one of them, all right, since yesterday,” said Kirk. “Not to walk about in front of automobiles.”

“The rules I refer to are mainly concerned with diet and wholesome exercise,” explained Mrs. Porter. “Careful attention to them may yet save him. His case is not hopeless. Ruth, let Mr. Winfield show you his pictures. They are poor in many respects, but not entirely without merit.”

===Chinese===
「随您的便，」波特夫人说。「我要是有您这副体格，非引以为豪不可。」

「您不想上楼去看看乔治吗？」柯克问。这对乔治未免狠心，可当务之急是无论如何得把这女人从现场调开。

「好吧。我带了一本小书来给他读，对他有益处。书名叫*《保养身体的基本守则》*（*Elementary Rules for the Preservation of the Body*）。」

「从昨天到今天，他倒确实学会了其中一条，」柯克说。「别在汽车跟前乱走。」

「我说的那些守则，主要关乎饮食与有益健康的运动，」波特夫人解释道。「认真照办兴许还能救他。他的情形并非全无希望。露丝，让温菲尔德先生带你看看他的画。这些画在许多方面乏善可陈，但并非全无可取之处。」

===Original===
Ruth, meanwhile, had been sitting on the couch, listening to the conversation without really hearing it. She was in a dreamy, contented mood. She found herself curiously soothed by the atmosphere of the studio, with its shaded lights and its atmosphere of peace. That was the keynote of the place, peace.

From outside came the rumble of an elevated train, subdued and softened, like faintly heard thunder. Somebody passed the window, whistling. A barrier seemed to separate her from these noises of the city. New York was very far away.

“I believe I could be wonderfully happy in a place like this,” she thought.

She became suddenly aware, in the midst of her meditations, of eyes watching her intently. She looked up and met Kirk’s.

===Chinese===
这当口，露丝一直坐在长椅上，听着这场谈话却没有真听进去。她处在一种朦胧而满足的心境里。她发觉自己被画室的气氛奇异地抚慰着——灯影低垂，一片宁谧的气氛。这地方的基调，便是宁谧。

外面传来高架列车的隆隆声，被压得又低又柔，像隐隐约约听见的雷鸣。有人吹着口哨从窗前走过。仿佛有一道屏障把她和这些城市的喧嚣隔开了。纽约远在天边。

「我相信，在这样一个地方，我能快活似神仙。」她想。

正在遐想之际，她猛然察觉有一双眼睛正目不转睛地望着自己。她抬起头，正撞上柯克的目光。

===Original===
She could read the message in them as clearly as if he had spoken it, and she was conscious of a little thrill of annoyance at the thought of all the tiresome formalities which must be gone through before he could speak it. They seemed absurd.

It was all so simple. He wanted her; she wanted him. She had known it from the moment of their meeting. The man had found his woman, the woman her man. Nature had settled the whole affair in an instant. And now civilization, propriety, etiquette, whatever one cared to call it, must needs step in with the rules and regulations and precedents.

The goal was there, clear in sight, but it must be reached by the winding road appointed. She, being a woman and, by virtue of her sex, primeval, scorned the road, and would have ignored it. But she knew men, and especially, at that moment as their eyes met, she knew Kirk; and she understood that to him the road was a thing that could not be ignored. The mere idea of doing so would seem grotesque and impossible, probably even shocking, to him. Men were odd, formal creatures, slaves to precedent.

He must have time, it was the prerogative of the male; time to reveal himself to her, to strut before her, to go through the solemn comedy of proving to her, by the exhibition of his virtues and the careful suppression of his defects, what had been clear to her from the first instant, that here was her mate, the man nature had set apart for her.

===Chinese===
那目光里的讯息，她读得清清楚楚，就仿佛他已说出口一般；而一想到他要把这话真正说出口之前还须熬过的一大堆恼人的繁文缛节，她便感到一丝恼意的悸动。这一切显得荒唐透顶。

事情本再简单不过。他要她；她要他。从见面那一刻起她就知道了。男人找到了他的女人，女人找到了她的男人。造化在一瞬间便把整桩事料理停当。可现在，文明、礼数、礼仪，随你叫它什么，偏偏要横插一脚，搬出条条框框、成规先例来。

目标就在眼前，望得一清二楚，可偏偏必须沿着指定的那条九曲十八弯的路去抵达。她身为女人，凭着与生俱来的洪荒天性，鄙夷这条路，本想干脆无视它。但她懂得男人，尤其是在两人目光相接的此刻，她懂得柯克；她明白，对他来说，这条路是万万无视不得的。在他看来，这么做的念头本身就显得荒诞不经、绝无可能，说不定还会骇人听闻。男人就是些古怪而拘泥的造物，是先例的奴隶。

他必须得有时间——这是男性的特权；有时间向她展露自己，在她面前昂首阔步，郑重其事地演一出喜剧：靠展览自己的优点、小心掩藏自己的缺点，来向她证明一件她从第一眼起就明了的事——眼前这位，就是她的良人，是造化专为她择定的男人。

===Original===
He would begin by putting on a new suit of clothes and having his hair cut.

She smiled. It was silly and tiresome, but it was funny.

“Will you show me your pictures, Mr. Winfield?” she asked.

“If you’d really care to see them. I’m afraid they’re pretty bad.”

“Exhibit A. Modesty,” thought Ruth.

The journey had begun.

===Chinese===
他的开场，会是置一身新衣裳，再理个发。

她笑了。又傻又烦人，可也真有意思。

「您愿意让我看看您的画吗，温菲尔德先生？」她问。

「只要您真想看。只怕它们糟糕得很。」

「证物甲：谦虚。」露丝想。

旅程开始了。

## III The Misadventure of Steve / 第三章 史蒂夫的飞来横祸



===Original===

Kirk was not the only person whom the sudden change in the financial position of the Winfield family had hit hard. The blighting effects of sudden wealth had touched Steve while Kirk was still in Colombia.

In a sense, it had wrecked Steve’s world. Nobody had told him to stop or even diminish the number of his visits, but the fact remained that, by the time Kirk returned to New York, he had practically ceased to go to the house on Fifth Avenue.

For all his roughness, Steve possessed a delicacy which sometimes almost amounted to diffidence; and he did not need to be told that there was a substantial difference, as far as he was concerned, between the new headquarters of the family and the old. At the studio he had been accustomed to walk in when it pleased him, sure of a welcome; but he had an idea that he did not fit as neatly into the atmosphere of Fifth Avenue as he had done into that of Sixty-First Street; and nobody disabused him of it.

It was perhaps the presence of Mrs. Porter that really made the difference. In spite of the compliments she had sometimes paid to his common sense, Mrs. Porter did not put Steve at his ease. He was almost afraid of her. Consequently, when he came to Fifth Avenue, he remained below stairs, talking pugilism with Keggs.

===Chinese===

温菲尔德家的经济地位骤然改变，被狠狠击中的不止柯克一个。在柯克还在哥伦比亚的时候，飞来横财的祸害就已经波及史蒂夫了。

从某种意义上说，这毁了史蒂夫的世界。没有人叫他不来，甚至也没人叫他少来几趟，但事实是，等到柯克回到纽约时，他实际上已经不去第五大道那座宅子了。

史蒂夫虽然粗豪，却有一种细腻，有时近乎怯于见人；不必谁来说，他也明白，这个家的新大本营和旧大本营相比，对他而言实在大有分别。在画室那会儿，他习惯想什么时候来就什么时候来，进门准受欢迎；可他总觉得，自己融进第五大道的空气，不像当初融进六十一街的空气那么熨帖；而也没有人来给他解开这个心结。

真正造成这种分别的，恐怕还是波特夫人的存在。尽管她有时候夸过史蒂夫有常识，可波特夫人总让他浑身不自在。他简直有点怕她。于是乎，他一到第五大道，就待在楼下，跟凯格斯聊拳击。

===Original===

It was from Keggs that he first learned of the changes that had taken place in the surroundings of William Bannister.

“I’ve ’ad the privilege of serving in some of the best houses in England,” said the butler one evening, as they sat smoking in the pantry, “and I’ve never seen such goings on. I don’t hold with the pampering of children.”

“What do you mean, pampering?” asked Steve.

“Well, Lord love a duck!” replied the butler, who in his moments of relaxation was addicted to homely expletives of the lower London type. “If you don’t call it pampering, what do you call pampering? He ain’t allowed to touch nothing that ain’t been﻿—it’s slipped my memory what they call it, but it’s got something to do with microbes. They sprinkle stuff on his toys and on his clothes and on his nurse; what’s more, and on anyone who comes to see him. And his nursery ain’t what *I* call a nursery at all. It’s nothing more or less than a private ’ospital, with its white tiles and its antiseptics and whatnot, and the temperature just so and no lower nor higher. I don’t call it ’aving a proper faith in Providence, pampering and fussing over a child to that extent.”

===Chinese===

他正是从凯格斯嘴里，头一回听说威廉·班尼斯特的处境发生了哪些变化。

「我有幸在英国几户顶体面的人家当过差，」一天晚上，两人坐在配膳室里抽烟，管家说道，「可我从没见过这等作派。我可不赞成娇惯孩子。」

「你这话怎讲，娇惯？」史蒂夫问。

「嗬，老天爷疼鸭子！」管家答道——他闲下来的时候，就好来两句伦敦下层式的家常口头语。「这要不算娇惯，什么才算娇惯？那孩子什么都不许碰，除非先——那名堂我一时想不起来了，反正跟微生物有点关系。他们往他的玩具上、衣裳上、他保姆身上撒药水儿，还有，撒到每个来看他的人身上。再说，他那间育儿室，压根不是*我*心目中的育儿室。它分明就是一间私家『医院』，白瓷砖、消毒剂一应俱全，温度调得分毫不差，不高也不低。把一个孩子这么娇养、这么折腾，我看这叫对老天爷没个正经的信心。」

===Original===

“You’re stringing me!”

“Not a bit of it, Mr. Dingle. I’ve seen the nursery with my own eyes, and I ’ave my information direct from the young person who looks after the child.”

“But, say, in the old days that kid was about the dandiest little sport that ever came down the pike. You seen him that day I brought him round to say hello to the old man. He didn’t have no nursery at all then, let alone one with white tiles. I’ve seen him come up off the studio floor looking like a coon with the dust. And Miss Ruth tickled to see him like that, too. For the love of Mike, what’s come to her?”

“It’s all along of this Porter,” said Keggs morosely. “She’s done it all. And if,” he went on with sudden heat, “she don’t break her ’abit of addressing me in a tone what the ’umblest dorg would resent, I’m liable to forget my place and give her a piece of my mind. Coming round and interfering!”

===Chinese===

「你逗我呢！」

「一点儿也不逗你，丁格尔先生。那育儿室是我亲眼见过的，我的消息直接来自照看那孩子的大姑娘。」

「可是，我说，早先那小子可是天底下头一份的漂亮小活宝。我领他过来跟老先生打招呼那天，你见过他。那会儿他压根儿没有什么育儿室，更别提带白瓷砖的了。我见过他从画室地板上爬起来，一身的灰，活像只浣熊。露丝小姐那会儿见了他那副模样，还乐得不行呢。看在老天的份上，她这是怎么了？」

「全是这位波特闹的，」凯格斯阴郁地说。「全是她一手弄出来的。还有，」他忽然火气上来，接着说道，「她要是改不掉那副跟我说话的腔调——连天下顶卑微的狗听了都要恼火的腔调——我说不定就忘了自己的身份，好好数落她一顿。跑来横插一杠子！」

===Original===

“Got *your* goat, has she?” commented Steve, interested. “She’s what you’d call a tough proposition, that dame. I used to have my eye on her all the time in the old days, waiting for her to start something. But say, I’d like to see this nursery you’ve been talking about. Take me up and let me lamp it.”

Keggs shook his head.

“I daren’t, Mr. Dingle. It ’ud be as much as my place is worth.”

“But, darn it! I’m the kid’s godfather.”

“That wouldn’t make no difference to that Porter. She’d pick on me just the same. But, if you care to risk it, Mr. Dingle, I’ll show you where it is. You’ll find the young person up there. She’ll tell you more about the child’s ’abits and daily life than I can.”

===Chinese===

「把*你*惹毛啦？」史蒂夫来了兴致，评论道。「那位太太嘛，就是你们所说的难缠的主儿。早先我时刻盯着她，就等她挑事儿。不过我说，你念叨的这间育儿室，我倒想去开开眼。带我上去让我瞅瞅。」

凯格斯摇了摇头。

「我可不敢，丁格尔先生。那可是要砸饭碗的事儿。」

「可是，见鬼！我是那孩子的教父啊。」

「在那位波特看来，这不管用。她照样会拿我是问。不过，你要是愿意担这个风险，丁格尔先生，我可以指给你它在哪儿。那位大姑娘就在楼上。关于这孩子的习性和日常起居，她能告诉你的比我多。」

===Original===

“Good enough,” said Steve.

He had not seen Mamie for some time, and absence had made the heart grow fonder. It embittered him that his meetings with her were all too rare nowadays. She seemed to have abandoned the practice of walking altogether, for, whenever he saw her now she was driving in the automobile with Bill. Keggs’ information about the new system threw some light upon this and made him all the more anxious to meet her now.

It was a curious delusion of Steve’s that he was always going to pluck up courage and propose to Mamie the very next time he saw her. This had gone on now for over two years, but he still clung to it. Repeated failures to reveal his burning emotions never caused him to lose the conviction that he would do it for certain next time.

It was in his customary braced-up, do-or-die frame of mind that he entered the nursery now.

===Chinese===

「成，」史蒂夫说。

他有些日子没见着玛米了，人不常见，心里倒越发惦记。如今他与她见面的时候实在太少，这让他心里发苦。她似乎干脆不再出门散步了，因为如今他每回见她，她都是同比尔一起坐着汽车兜风。凯格斯透露的这套新章程使这事有了几分眉目，也让他此刻愈发急于见她一面。

史蒂夫有个古怪的错觉：他总认定自己下一次见着玛米，准会鼓起勇气向她求婚。这情形已经持续两年多了，可他仍抱住这个信念不放。一次次没能把滚烫的心思说出口，从未让他丢掉「下回准行」的把握。

此刻他走进育儿室，怀里揣的还是那副惯常的抖擞精神、破釜沉舟的心境。
===Original===

His visit to Keggs had been rather a late one and had lasted some time before the subject of the White Hope had been broached, with the result that, when Steve arrived among the white tiles and antiseptics, he found his godson in bed and asleep. In a chair by the cot Mamie sat sewing.

Her eyes widened with surprise when she saw who the visitor was, and she put a finger to her lip and pointed to the sleeper. And, as we have to record another of the long list of Steve’s failures to propose we may say here, in excuse, that this reception took a great deal of the edge off the dashing resolution which had been his up to that moment. It made him feel self-conscious from the start.

“Whatever brings you up here, Steve?” whispered Mamie.

It was not a very tactful remark, perhaps, considering that Steve was the child’s godfather, and, as such, might reasonably expect to be allowed a free pass to his nursery; but Mamie, like Keggs, had fallen so under the domination of Lora Delane Porter that she had grown to consider it almost a natural law that no one came to see Bill unless approved of and personally conducted by her.

===Chinese===

他拜访凯格斯已相当晚，又聊了好一阵才谈到「白人希望」这个话题，结果等史蒂夫置身于白瓷砖与消毒剂之间时，他发现教子已经上床睡着了。玛米坐在童床旁的椅子上做针线。

她看清来客是谁时，吃惊地睁大了眼睛，随即把一根手指压在唇上，指了指熟睡的孩子。既然我们又得记下史蒂夫求婚失败长长名单上的一笔，不妨在此替他辩解一句：这般迎接方式，把他直到那一刻为止的昂扬决心削去了大半，害他从一开始就浑身不自在。

「你怎么会上这儿来，史蒂夫？」玛米悄声问。

这话也许说得不大得体——毕竟史蒂夫是孩子的教父，照理该有一张随时进出育儿室的通行证；可是玛米同凯格斯一样，早已臣服于洛拉·德莱恩·波特的统治之下，以至于渐渐把「未经她认可并亲自引见，任何人不得来看比尔」当成了一条自然法则。

===Original===

Steve did not answer. He was gaping at the fittings of the place in which he found himself. It was precisely as Keggs had described it, white tiles and all.

He was roused from his reflections by the approach of Mamie, or, rather, not so much by her approach as by the fact that at this moment she suddenly squirted something at him. It was cold and wet and hit him in the face before, as he put it to Keggs later, he could get his guard up.

“For the love of﻿—”

“Sh!” said Mamie warningly.

“What’s the idea? What are you handing me?”

===Chinese===

史蒂夫没有答话。他正目瞪口呆地打量着身处之地的各项陈设。一切正如凯格斯所描述的，白瓷砖什么的样样齐全。

把他从沉思中惊醒的是玛米的走近——确切说，倒不是她的走近，而是她此刻突然朝他喷了一股什么东西。那东西又凉又湿，用他后来对凯格斯的说法，还没等他「来得及抬起防守」，就糊了他一脸。

「看在老天的份——」

「嘘！」玛米警示道。

「这是干什么？你给我喷的什么玩意儿？」

===Original===

“I’ve got to. It’s to sterilize you. I do it to everyone.”

“Gee! You’ve got a swell job! Well, go to it, then. Shoot! I’m ready.”

“It’s boric acid,” explained Mamie.

“I shouldn’t wonder. Is this all part of the Porter circus?”

“Yes.”

“Where is she?” inquired Steve in sudden alarm. “Is she likely to butt in?”

===Chinese===

「不得不喷。这是给你消毒。谁来我都喷。」

「嚯！你这差事可真美！行，那就来吧。喷吧！我准备好了。」

「是硼酸，」玛米解释道。

「我一点也不奇怪。这套都是波特马戏团的节目？」

「是。」

「她在哪儿？」史蒂夫忽然警觉起来，问道。「她会不会突然闯进来？」

===Original===

“No. She’s out.”

“Good,” said Steve, and sat down, relieved, to resume his inspection of the room.

When he had finished he drew a deep breath.

“Well!” he said softly. “Say, Mamie, what do you think about it?”

“I’m not paid to think about it, Steve.”

“That means you agree with me that it’s the punkest state of things you ever struck. Well, you’re quite right. It is. It’s a shame to think of that innocent kid having this sort of deal handed to him. Why, just think of him at the studio!”

===Chinese===

「不会。她出去了。」

「好，」史蒂夫说，松了口气坐下，继续视察房间。

看完之后，他深深吸了一口气。

「唉！」他轻声说。「我说，玛米，你对这事儿怎么看？」

「人家雇我，可不是让我对此发表看法的，史蒂夫。」

「这就是说，你跟我一样，觉得这是你见识过的最糟糕透顶的局面。嗯，你说得一点不错。就是这么回事。一想到那个天真的小子落到这般待遇，就叫人心里不是滋味。唉，想想他在画室那会儿的样子吧！」

===Original===

But Mamie, whatever her private views, was loyal to her employers. She refused to be drawn into a discussion on the subject.

“Have you been downstairs with Mr. Keggs, Steve?”

“Yes. It was him that told me about all this. Say, Mame, we ain’t seen much of each other lately.”

“No.”

“Mighty little.”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===

可是玛米无论私下怎么想，都忠于自己的东家。她不肯被拽进这个话题的讨论里。

「你刚才在楼下陪凯格斯先生来着，史蒂夫？」

「对。这些事就是他告诉我的。我说，玛米，咱们近来不大见面了。」

「是啊。」

「见得少极了。」

「嗯。」
===Original===

Having got as far as this, Steve should, of course, have gone resolutely ahead. After all, it is not a very long step from telling a girl in a hushed whisper with a shake in it that you have not seen much of her lately to hinting that you would like to see a great deal more of her in the future.

Steve was on the right lines, and he knew it; but that fatal lack of nerve which had wrecked him on all the other occasions when he had got as far as this undid him now. He relapsed into silence, and Mamie went on sewing.

In a way, if you shut your eyes to the white tiles and the thermometer and the brass knobs and the shower-bath, it was a peaceful scene; and Steve, as he sat there and watched Mamie sew, was stirred by it. Remove the white tiles, the thermometer the brass knobs, and the shower-bath, and this was precisely the sort of scene his imagination conjured up when the business of life slackened sufficiently to allow him to dream dreams.

There he was, sitting in one chair; there was Mamie, sitting in another; and there in the corner was the little white cot﻿—well, perhaps that was being a shade too prophetic; on the other hand, it always came into these dreams of his. There, in short, was everything arranged just as he pictured it; and all that was needed to make the picture real was for him to propose and Mamie to accept him.

===Chinese===

话说到这个份上，史蒂夫理应一鼓作气往前推进才是。毕竟，从压低嗓门、声调微颤地告诉一位姑娘你近来不大见得着她，到暗示今后但愿多多见着她，这一步并不算很远。

史蒂夫路子走对了，他自己也知道；可那致命的怯场——以往每一回他进展到这个地步都被它毁掉——如今又把他毁了。他重新陷入沉默，玛米继续做她的针线。

从某个角度说，只要你对白瓷砖、温度计、铜旋钮和淋浴喷浴视而不见，这还算一幅安宁的图景；史蒂夫坐在那儿望着玛米做针线，心弦为之颤动。搬走白瓷砖、温度计、铜旋钮和淋浴喷浴，这正是他的想象力在人生事务稍歇、容他做梦的当口所幻化出的那类图景。

他坐在一把椅子上；玛米坐在另一把椅子上；墙角还有那张小白床——嗯，这话说得未免有点太像预言了；可话又说回来，这张小床回回都出现在他这些梦里。总之，一切都布置得正如他想象的那般；要把这幅图画变成真的，只差他开口求婚、玛米点头应承了。

===Original===

It was the disturbing thought that the second condition did not necessarily follow on to the first that had kept Steve from taking the plunge for the last two years. Unlike the hero of the poem, he feared his fate too much to put it to the touch, to win or lose it all.

Presently the silence began to oppress Steve. Mamie had her needlework, and that apparently served her in lieu of conversation; but Steve had nothing to occupy him, and he began to grow restless. He always despised himself thoroughly for his feebleness on these occasions; and he despised himself now. He determined to make a big effort.

“Mamie!” he said.

As he was nervous and had been silent so long that his vocal cords had gone off duty under the impression that their day’s work was over, the word came out of him like a husky gunshot. Mamie started, and the White Hope, who had been sleeping peacefully, stirred and muttered.

“S-sh!” hissed Mamie.

===Chinese===

不多时，沉默开始压得史蒂夫喘不过气来。玛米有针线活儿，那显然足以代替谈话；史蒂夫却无事可做，渐渐坐立不安起来。每逢这种场合，他总把自己的窝囊鄙夷个够；此刻他又鄙夷起自己来。他决心豁出去大干一场。

「玛米！」他说。

他本来紧张，又沉默了这么久，声带误以为当天的活儿已经干完、早就下班了，因此这一个词冲出他的喉咙，像一记喑哑的枪响。玛米吓了一跳，睡得正香的「白人希望」也动了动，咕哝出声。

「嘘——！」玛米嗤声道。

史蒂夫整个人泄了气，只觉得今晚时运不济；玛米则焦急地俯身到童床边。不过睡着的孩子没有醒。他咯了一声，叹了口气，又续上了被打断的觉。玛米回到座位上。

===Original===

Steve collapsed with the feeling that it was not his lucky night, while Mamie bent anxiously over the cot. The sleeper, however, did not wake. He gurgled, gave a sigh, then resumed his interrupted repose. Mamie returned to her seat.

“Yes?” she said, as if nothing had occurred, and as if there had been no interval between Steve’s remark and her reply.

Steve could not equal her calmness. He had been strung up when he spoke, and the interruption had undone him. He reflected ruefully that he might have said something to the point if he had been allowed to go straight on; now he had forgotten what he had meant to say.

“Oh, nothing,” he replied.

Silence fell once more on the nursery.

===Chinese===

「嗯？」她说，仿佛什么都没有发生过，仿佛史蒂夫的话与她的回答之间不曾有过任何间隔。

史蒂夫可比不得她这份镇定。他说话时本是绷紧了弦的，这一打岔就把他搅散了。他懊恼地寻思，倘若方才容他一口气说下去，本可以讲出些要紧话来；如今他已经忘了自己想说什么了。

「噢，没什么，」他答道。

沉默再度笼罩了育儿室。

史蒂夫正攒着劲儿准备再发起一轮进攻，楼梯上忽然传来人声。一个声音只是低低的咕哝，另一个却尖利而错不了，正是洛拉·德莱恩·波特那斩钉截铁的音调。这声音让史蒂夫和玛米同时跳了起来。

===Original===

Steve was bracing himself up for another attack when suddenly there came a sound of voices from the stairs. One voice was a mere murmur, but the other was sharp and unmistakable, the incisive note of Lora Delane Porter. It brought Steve and Mamie to their feet simultaneously.

“What’s it matter?” said Steve stoutly, answering the panic in Mamie’s eyes. “It’s not her house, and I got a perfect right to be here.”

“You don’t know her. I shall get into trouble.”

Mamie was pale with apprehension. She knew her Lora Delane Porter, and she knew what would happen if Steve were to be discovered there. It was, as Keggs put it, as much as her place was worth.

For a brief instant Mamie faced a future in which she was driven from Bill’s presence into outer darkness, dismissed, and told never to return. That was what would happen. Sitting and talking with Steve in the sacred nursery at this time of night was a crime, and she had known it all the time. But she had been glad to see Steve again after all this while﻿—if Steve had known how glad, he would certainly have found courage and said what he had so often failed to say﻿—and, knowing that Mrs. Porter was out, she had thought the risk of his presence worth taking. Now, with discovery imminent, panic came upon her.

===Chinese===

「怕什么？」史蒂夫壮着胆子说，回应玛米眼中的惊惶。「这又不是她的房子，我待在这儿是完全正当的。」

「你不了解她。我要倒霉的。」

玛米吓得脸色发白。她深知她那位洛拉·德莱恩·波特的为人，也深知史蒂夫若是被发现在这儿会有什么下场。用凯格斯的话说，那是够砸她饭碗的事儿。

有一小会儿，玛米眼前浮现出这样的未来：她被从比尔身边赶走，逐入外头的黑暗里，遭辞退，并被吩咐永不再来。下场就会是这样。深更半夜坐在神圣的育儿室里陪史蒂夫说话是一桩罪过，这一点她始终心里有数。可是这么久之后能再见到史蒂夫，她心里是高兴的——史蒂夫若是知道她有多高兴，肯定早就鼓起勇气，说出他那许多回都没能说出的话了——况且她知道波特夫人出去了，便觉得为他担这份风险是值得的。如今，被发现就在眼前，恐慌攫住了她。

说话声已经很近了。来人的去处毫无疑问。他们正缓步而来，方向直指育儿室。

===Original===

The voices were quite close now. There was no doubt of the destination of the speakers. They were heading slowly but directly for the nursery.

Steve, not being fully abreast of the new rules and regulations of the sacred apartment, could not read Mamie’s mind completely. He did not know that, under Mrs. Porter’s code, the admission of a visitor during the hours of sleep was a felony in the first degree, punishable by instant dismissal. But Mamie’s face and her brief reference to trouble were enough to tell him that the position was critical, and with the instinct of the trapped he looked round him for cover.

But the White Hope’s nursery was not constructed with a view to providing cover for bulky gentlemen who should not have been there. It was as bare as a billiard-table as far as practicable hiding-places were concerned.

And then his eye caught the waterproof sheet of the shower-bath. Behind that there was just room for concealment.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫没有完全掌握这座神圣公寓的新章程新规条，摸不透玛米的心思。他不知道，按照波特夫人的法典，在睡眠时间放一名访客进来是一级重罪，可判处即刻革职。但玛米的脸色和她那句「要倒霉的」足以告诉他形势危急，于是他凭着落网之兽的本能，环顾四周找掩护。

可是「白人希望」的育儿室在设计上并未考虑为本不该在此的魁梧绅士提供掩护。就切实可用的藏身之处而言，它空得像张台球桌。

这时他的眼睛瞄上了淋浴喷浴的防水帘。那后头刚好有块地方能藏人。

他朝玛米简短地点了个头，算是鼓励，随即朝防水帘扑了过去。他刚隐没，门就开了。
===Original===

With a brief nod of encouragement to Mamie, he leaped at it. The door opened as he disappeared.

Mrs. Porter’s rules concerning visitors, though stringent as regarded Mamie, were capable of being relaxed when she herself was the person to relax them. She had a visitor with her now﻿—a long, severe-looking lady with a sharp nose surmounted by spectacles, who, taking in the white tiles, the thermometer, the cot, and the brass knobs in a single comprehensive glance, observed: “Admirable!”

Mrs. Porter was obviously pleased with this approval. Her companion was a woman doctor of great repute among the advanced apostles of hygiene; and praise from her was praise indeed. She advanced into the room with an air of suppressed pride.

“These tiles are thoroughly cleaned twice each day with an antiseptic solution.”

===Chinese===

波特夫人关于访客的规矩，对玛米而言固然严苛，可一旦放松规矩的人是她本人，这些规矩也并非不可通融。她此刻身边正带着一位访客——一位瘦长、面容严厉的女士，尖鼻子上架着一副眼镜；这位女士把白瓷砖、温度计、童床和铜旋钮一览无余地扫进单独一瞥里，评道：「妙极！」

波特夫人显然对这份嘉许颇为得意。她的同伴是位女医生，在卫生学的先进使徒们中间享有盛名；得她一赞，那可是真格的赞赏。她带着一副按捺着得意的神气走进房来。

「这些瓷砖每天用消毒溶液彻彻底底清洗两遍。」

「正是，」戴眼镜的女士说。

===Original===

“Just so,” said the spectacled lady.

“You notice the thermometer.”

“Exactly.”

“Those knobs you see on the wall have various uses.”

“Quite.”

===Chinese===

「您注意到这温度计了。」

「正是如此。」

「您看到的墙上那些旋钮各有用途。」

「不错。」

两人以极其郑重的神气考察那些旋钮，波特夫人挺身而立、志得意满，另一位则俯身上前，从眼镜后面细细端详。玛米趁她们背对着自己，扭头朝防水帘匆匆投去一瞥。那确是一面妙极的屏风；丝毫瞧不见史蒂夫的影子；可她仍旧抖个不停。

===Original===

They examined the knobs with an air of profound seriousness, Mrs. Porter erect and complacent, the other leaning forward and peering through her spectacles. Mamie took advantage of their backs and turned to cast a hurried glance at the waterproof curtain. It was certainly an admirable screen; no sign of Steve was visible; but nevertheless she did not cease to quake.

“This,” said Mrs. Porter, “controls the heat. This, this, and this are for the ventilation.”

“Just so, just so, just so,” said the doctor. “And this, of course, is for the shower-bath? I understand!”

And, extending a firm finger, she gave the knob a forceful push.

===Chinese===

「这个，」波特夫人说，「管暖气。这个、这个、还有这个是通风用的。」

「正是，正是，正是，」医生说。「那么这个，自然是管淋浴喷浴的了？我明白了！」

说着，她伸出坚决的一根手指，用力按下了那只旋钮。

波特夫人点了点头。

===Original===

Mrs. Porter nodded.

“That is the cold shower,” she said. “This is the hot. It is a very ingenious arrangement, one of Malcolmson’s patents. There is a regulator at the side of the bath which enables the nurse to get just the correct temperature. I will turn on both, and then﻿—”

It was as Mrs. Porter’s hand was extended toward the knob that the paralysis which terror had put upon Mamie relaxed its grip. She had stood by without a movement while the cold water splashed down upon the hidden Steve. Her heart had ached for him, but she had not stirred. But now, with the prospect of allowing him to be boiled alive before her, she acted.

It is generally only on the stage that a little child comes to the rescue of adults at critical moments; but William Bannister was accorded the opportunity of doing so off it. It happened that at the moment of Mrs. Porter’s entry Mamie had been standing near his cot, and she had not moved since. The consequence was that she was within easy reach of him; and, despair giving her what in the circumstances amounted to a flash of inspiration, she leaned quickly forward, even as Mrs. Porter’s finger touched the knob, and gave the round head on the pillow a rapid push.

===Chinese===

「那是冷水喷淋，」她说。「这个是热水。这套装置非常精巧，是马尔科姆森的专利之一。浴缸侧面有个调节器，保姆可以借此调到恰恰正确的温度。我把两个都拧开，然后——」

就在波特夫人的手朝那只旋钮伸过去的时候，恐惧施加给玛米的麻痹松开了它的钳制。冷水泼洒在藏着的史蒂夫身上时，她在一旁一动不动地站着。她的心为他疼过，可她没有动。但现在，眼看着他要在她面前被活活煮熟，她行动了。

小孩子在危急关头出来搭救大人的事，通常只发生在舞台上；可威廉·班尼斯特获得了在舞台之下这么做的机会。碰巧波特夫人进门的当口，玛米正站在他童床近旁，此后一直没有挪动。结果就是，他就在她伸手可及的范围之内；绝望在此时此地给了她一道灵光，就在波特夫人的手指触到旋钮的一瞬，她疾速前倾，朝枕头上那颗圆脑袋猛地一推。

威廉·班尼斯特哼了一声坐起来，揉揉眼睛，一见是生人，便放声大哭。

===Original===

William Bannister sat up with a grunt, rubbed his eyes, and, seeing strangers, began to cry.

It was so obvious to Mrs. Porter and her companion, both from the evidence of their guilty consciences and the look of respectful reproach on Mamie’s face, that the sound of their voices had disturbed the child, that they were routed from the start.

“Oh, dear me! He is awake,” said the lady doctor.

“I am afraid we did not lower our voices,” added Mrs. Porter. “And yet William is usually such a sound sleeper. Perhaps we had better﻿—”

===Chinese===

在波特夫人和她的同伴看来——既有各自内疚的良心作证，又有玛米脸上恭谨责备的神色为凭——分明是她们说话的声音惊扰了孩子，这一点再明显不过，于是她们从一开始便溃不成军。

「哎呀，天哪！他醒了，」女医生说。

「恐怕我们没把嗓门压低，」波特夫人补充道。「可威廉平时睡得那么沉。也许我们最好——」

「正是，」医生说。

===Original===

“Just so,” said the doctor.

“﻿—go downstairs while the nurse gets him off to sleep again.”

“Quite.”

The door closed behind them.

===Chinese===

「正是，」医生说。

「——先下楼去，让保姆再哄他入睡。」

「不错。」

门在她们身后关上了。
---

===Original===

“Oh, Steve!” said Mamie.

The White Hope had gone to sleep again with the amazing speed of childhood, and Mamie was looking pityingly at the bedraggled object which had emerged cautiously from behind the waterproof.

“I got mine,” muttered Steve ruefully. “You ain’t got a towel anywhere, have you, Mame?”

Mamie produced a towel and watched him apologetically as he attempted to dry himself.

===Chinese===

「唉，史蒂夫！」玛米说。

「白人希望」又以孩童惊人的速度睡着了，玛米正怜惜地望着那个从防水帘后头小心翼翼钻出来的、浑身滴水的狼狈家伙。

「我认栽了，」史蒂夫懊丧地咕哝道。「你这儿哪儿有毛巾没有，玛米？」

玛米递上一条毛巾，满怀歉意地看着他试图擦干自己。

===Original===

“I’m so sorry, Steve.”

“Cut it out. It was my fault. I oughtn’t to have been there. Say, it was a bit of luck the kid waking just then.”

“Yes,” said Mamie.

===Chinese===

「真对不起，史蒂夫。」

「快别这么说。是我的错。我本就不该待在那儿。我说，那孩子偏偏那会儿醒，可真是走运。」

「是啊，」玛米说。

===Original===

Observe the tricks that conscience plays us. If Mamie had told Steve what had caused William to wake he would certainly have been so charmed by her presence of mind, exerted on his behalf to save him from the warm fate which Mrs. Porter’s unconscious hand had been about to bring down upon him, that he would have forgotten his diffidence then and there and, as the poet has it, have eased his bosom of much perilous stuff.

But conscience would not allow Mamie to reveal the secret. Already she was suffering the pangs of remorse for having, in however good a cause, broken her idol’s rest with a push that might have given the poor lamb a headache. She could not confess the crime even to Steve.

And if Steve had had the pluck to tell Mamie that he loved her, as he stood before her dripping with the water which he had suffered in silence rather than betray her, she would have fallen into his arms. For Steve at that moment had all the glamour for her of the self-sacrificing hero of a moving-picture film. He had not actually risked death for her, perhaps, but he had taken a sudden cold shower-bath without a murmur﻿—all for her.

===Chinese===

且看良心跟我们玩的把戏。倘若玛米告诉了史蒂夫威廉是怎么醒的，他准会被她的机变深深折服——她为了救他，使他免遭波特夫人那只不知情的手即将加到他身上的暖融融的命运——那份倾倒足以让他当场忘掉羞怯，像诗人所说的那样，把胸中许多凶险的心事倾吐个干净。

可良心不许玛米泄露这个秘密。为了一个再正当不过的缘由，她推醒了她的心肝宝贝，那一推说不定还叫可怜的小羊羔犯了头疼——想到这里，悔恨的刺痛已经缠上了她。这桩罪行她连史蒂夫都没法招认。

倘若史蒂夫有胆量趁站在她面前、滴着那份他为不出卖她而默默承受的清水时，告诉玛米他爱她，她早就扑进他怀里了。因为史蒂夫此刻在她眼中，浑身是电影里那种自我牺牲的英雄的光彩。他倒不曾真的为她冒过死险，或许；可他一声不吭地领受了一场突如其来的冷水淋浴——全是为了她。

===Original===

Mamie was thrilled. She looked at him with the gleaming eyes of devotion.

But Steve, just because he knew that he was wet and fancied that he must look ridiculous, held his peace.

And presently, his secret still locked in his bosom, and his collar sticking limply to his neck, he crept downstairs, avoiding the society of his fellow man, and slunk out into the night where, if there was no Mamie, there were, at any rate, dry clothes.

===Chinese===

玛米心潮激荡。她带着灼灼的倾慕之眼望着他。

可史蒂夫，就因为他知道自己浑身湿透、自忖模样必定可笑，偏偏缄口不言。

不多时，他依然把秘密锁在胸中，衣领软塌塌地贴着脖子，蹑手蹑脚地下了楼，一路避开同类的人群，溜出门去投入夜色——那夜色里虽然没有玛米，至少，还有干衣裳。

## VI The Outcasts / 第六章 被放逐的人

===Original===

Three months of his new life had gone by before Kirk awoke from the stupor which had gripped him as the result of the general upheaval of his world. Ever since his return from Colombia he had honestly been intending to resume his painting, and, attacking it this time in a businesslike way, to try to mould himself into the semblance of an efficient artist.

His mind had been full of fine resolutions. He would engage a good teacher, some competent artist whom fortune had not treated well and who would be glad of the job﻿—Washington Square and its neighbourhood were full of them﻿—and settle down grimly, working regular hours, to recover lost ground.

But the rush of life, as lived on the upper avenue, had swept him away. He had been carried along on the rapids of dinners, parties, dances, theatres, luncheons, and the rest, and his great resolve had gone bobbing away from him on the current.

He had recovered it now and climbed painfully ashore, feeling bruised and exhausted, but determined.

===Chinese===

他的新生活过了三个月，柯克才从那阵麻木中醒过来——他的世界整个翻了个个儿，这麻木便是那场变故留下的。自哥伦比亚归来后，他一直诚心诚意地打算重拾画笔，这一回要拿出办正事的架势来，想把自己塑造出几分能干画家的模样。

他心里装满了堂皇的决心。他要聘一位好老师——某个时运不济、乐得接下这桩差事的称职画家，华盛顿广场（Washington Square）及其左近遍地都是这路人——然后横下心安居下来，按钟点作息，苦干着把荒疏的地盘夺回来。

可是上城大道上那种奔忙的日子把他卷走了。他被晚宴、派对、舞会、剧院、午宴等等的激流裹挟而下，那个宏大的决心便在水面上一起一伏，顺流漂得离他越来越远。

如今他已把它捞了回来，一步一步艰难地爬上了岸，只觉遍体鳞伤、精疲力竭，却心意已决。

---

===Original===

Among the motley crowd which had made the studio a home in the days of Kirk’s bachelorhood had been an artist﻿—one might almost say an ex-artist﻿—named Robert Dwight Penway. An over-fondness for rye whisky at the Brevoort café had handicapped Robert as an active force in the world of New York art. As a practical worker he was not greatly esteemed﻿—least of all by the editors of magazines, who had paid advance cheques to him for work which, when delivered at all, was delivered too late for publication. These, once bitten, were now twice shy of Mr. Penway. They did not deny his great talents, which were, indeed, indisputable; but they were fixed in their determination not to make use of them.

Fate could have provided no more suitable ally for Kirk. It was universally admitted around Washington Square and﻿—grudgingly﻿—downtown that in the matter of theory Mr. Penway excelled. He could teach to perfection what he was too erratic to practise.

Robert Dwight Penway, run to earth one sultry evening in the Brevoort, welcomed Kirk as a brother, as a rich brother. Even when his first impression, that he was to have the run of the house on Fifth Avenue and mix freely with touchable multimillionaires, had been corrected, his altitude was still brotherly. He parted from Kirk with many solemn promises to present himself at the studio daily and teach him enough art to put him clear at the top of the profession. “Way above all these other dubs,” asserted Mr. Penway.

Robert Dwight Penway’s attitude toward his contemporaries in art bore a striking resemblance to Steve’s estimate of his successors in the middleweight department of the American prize-ring.

Surprisingly to those who knew him, Mr. Penway was as good as his word. Certainly Kirk’s terms had been extremely generous; but he had thrown away many a contract of equal value in his palmy days. Possibly his activity was due to his liking for Kirk; or it may have been that the prospect of sitting by with a cigar while somebody else worked, with nothing to do all day except offer criticism, and advice, appealed to him.

===Chinese===

在柯克单身时代把画室当家的那群三教九流之辈里，有一个画家——简直可以称之为前画家——名叫罗伯特·德怀特·佩尼韦（Robert Dwight Penway）。在布雷沃特（Brevoort）咖啡馆对黑麦威士忌爱得过了头，拖累了罗伯特，使他成不了纽约艺术界的一支活跃力量。作为实干者，他不大受人推重——最不推重他的，莫过于各家杂志的编辑：他们曾预付支票向他约稿，而那些活计即便交了卷，也总是迟得无法刊出。一朝被蛇咬，这些编辑如今见了佩尼韦先生，十年怕井绳。他们并不否认他的巨大才华——那的确无可争议——却铁了心不肯再加以任用。

命运再也替柯克寻不出比这更合适的帮手了。华盛顿广场一带人人公认——下城的人也公认，只是不情不愿——在理论这门功夫上，佩尼韦先生堪称翘楚。他自己反复无常、做不来的事，他教起来却能尽善尽美。

一个闷热的傍晚，罗伯特·德怀特·佩尼韦在布雷沃特被柯克寻到了踪迹，他把柯克当兄弟欢迎——当阔兄弟欢迎。及至他起初的印象遭到更正，明白自己并不能随意出入第五大道那座宅子、随手结交摸得着的千万富翁，他的态度依然亲如手足。分手时他再三向柯克庄严许诺，定将每日到画室露面，教给他的艺术足够让他稳坐本行头把交椅。「远远高过其余那些草包。」佩尼韦先生断言。

罗伯特·德怀特·佩尼韦对艺术界同辈的态度，同史蒂夫对美国拳坛中量级一班后进的估量，像得出奇。

让认识他的人大感意外的是，佩尼韦先生这回竟说话算话。诚然，柯克开出的条件极其优厚；可在他走红的日子里，同等价钱的合同他弃掉的不知有多少。他的勤勉，或许出于他对柯克的喜欢；也可能是这般前景别有吸引力：别人埋头干活，自己叼着雪茄坐在旁边，成天无事，只管提提批评、给给忠告。

===Original===

At any rate, he appeared at the studio on the following afternoon, completely sober and excessively critical. He examined the canvases which Kirk had hauled from shelves and corners for his inspection. One after another he gazed upon them in an increasingly significant silence. When the last one was laid aside he delivered judgment.

“Golly!” he said.

Kirk flushed. It was not that he was not in complete agreement with the verdict. Looking at these paintings, some of which he had in the old days thought extremely good, he was forced to admit that “Golly” was the only possible criticism.

He had not seen them for a long time, and absence had enabled him to correct first impressions. Moreover, something had happened to him, causing him to detect flaws where he had seen only merits. Life had sharpened his powers of judgment. He was a grown man looking at the follies of his youth.

===Chinese===

无论如何，第二天下午他在画室露面了，头脑完全清醒，眼光格外挑剔。柯克从架子上和墙角里拖出一堆画布供他检阅。他一幅接一幅地凝视，沉默得越来越意味深长。最后一幅搁到一旁时，他宣布了裁决。

「老天爷！」他说。

柯克涨红了脸。倒不是他不完全服膺这个判词。看着这些画——其中几幅他当年还自认为好得很——他不得不承认，「老天爷」是唯一说得出的批评。

他好久没见过它们了，久别使他得以修正第一印象。况且，他身上起了某种变化，从前只见好处的地方，如今他能挑出毛病了。生活磨利了他的判断力。他如今是个成年人，正在打量自己年轻时的荒唐产物。

===Original===

“Burn them!” said Mr. Penway, lighting a cigar with the air of one restoring his tissues after a strenuous ordeal. “Burn the lot. They’re awful. Darned amateur nightmares. They offend the eye. Cast them into a burning fiery furnace.”

Kirk nodded. The criticism was just. It erred, if at all, on the side of mildness. Certainly something had happened to him since he perpetrated those daubs. He had developed. He saw things with new eyes.

“I guess I had better start right in again at the beginning,” he said.

“Earlier than that,” amended Mr. Penway.

===Chinese===

「烧了它们！」佩尼韦先生说着点起一支雪茄，那副气派，像刚熬过一场苦刑、正在恢复元气。「一股脑儿全烧了。糟透了。不折不扣的业余梦魇。刺人眼目。把它们扔进烈火熊熊的窑里去吧。」

柯克点了点头。这批评是公道的。若有偏差，也只会失之于宽。自从涂出那些拙劣之作，他身上确实起了变化。他长进了。他用新的眼睛看事物了。

「我看我还是干脆从头再来一遍的好。」他说。

「比那还早。」佩尼韦先生修正道。

---

===Original===

So Kirk settled down to a routine of hard work; and, so doing, drove another blow at the wedge which was separating his life from Ruth’s. There were days now when they did not meet at all, and others when they saw each other for a few short moments in which neither seemed to have much to say.

Ruth had made a perfunctory protest against the new departure.

“Really,” she said, “it does seem absurd for you to spend all your time down at that old studio. It isn’t as if you had to. But, of course, if you want to﻿—”

And she had gone on to speak of other subjects. It was plain to Kirk that his absence scarcely affected her. She was still in the rapids, and every day carried her farther away from him.

It did not hurt him now. A sort of apathy seemed to have fallen on him. The old days became more and more remote. Sometimes he doubted whether anything remained of her former love for him, and sometimes he wondered if he still loved her. She was so different that it was almost as if she were a stranger. Once they had had everything in common. Now it seemed to him that they had nothing﻿—not even Bill.

===Chinese===

于是柯克安居下来，过上了苦干的规律日子；而这一来，又朝那道正把他的生活与露丝的生活劈开的楔子，重重敲进了一锤。如今有些日子他们干脆见不着面，另一些日子也只匆匆会面片刻，彼此似乎都无话可说。

对这一新动向，露丝敷衍地抗议过一回。

「说真的，」她说，「你成天耗在下面那间旧画室里，实在有点可笑。又不是非这样不可。不过，当然啦，你要是想——」

说罢她便谈起别的话题去了。柯克看得分明，他不在家这事几乎触动不了她。她还在那道激流里，一天一天漂得离他更远。

这如今已不叫他痛苦了。一种麻木似乎落到了他身上。旧日越来越遥远。有时他怀疑她从前对他的爱可还剩下些什么，有时他又怀疑自己是否还爱着她。她变得如此厉害，几乎同陌生人无异。他们曾经彼此共有过一切。如今在他看来，他们之间什么也不剩了——连比尔也不剩。

===Original===

He did not brood upon it. He gave himself no time for that. He worked doggedly on under the blasphemous but efficient guidance of Mr. Penway. He was becoming a man with a fixed idea﻿—the idea of making good.

He began to make headway. His beginnings were small, but practical. He no longer sat down when the spirit moved him to dash off vague masterpieces which might turn into something quite unexpected on the road to completion; he snatched at anything definite that presented itself.

Sometimes it was a couple of illustrations to a short story in one of the minor magazines, sometimes a picture to go with an eulogy of a patent medicine. Whatever it was, he seized upon it and put into it all the talent he possessed. And thanks to the indefatigable coaching of Robert Dwight Penway, a certain merit was beginning to creep into his work. His drawing was growing firmer. He no longer shirked difficulties.

Mr. Penway was good enough to approve of his progress. Being free from any morbid distaste for himself, he attributed that progress to its proper source. As he said once in a moment of expansive candour, he could, given a free hand and something to drink and smoke while doing it, make an artist out of two sticks and a lump of coal.

“Why, I’ve made *you* turn out things that are like something on earth, my boy,” he said proudly. “And that,” he added, as he reached out for the bottle of Bourbon which Kirk had provided for him, “is going some.”

Kirk was far too grateful to resent the slightly unflattering note a more spirited man might have detected in the remark.

===Chinese===

他不为此苦苦思量。他不给自己留那工夫。他在佩尼韦先生那亵渎却高效的指点下顽强地干下去。他正在变成一个怀着执念的人——执念便是要干出个名堂来。

他开始有了进展。起步虽小，却脚踏实地。他不再心血来潮便坐下来挥洒什么含糊的大作——那种东西在通往完工的半道上会变成什么，全然无从预料；如今但有一件确切的活计送上门，他便一把抓住。

有时是给某家二流杂志的短篇小说配两幅插图，有时是给一篇吹捧专利成药的颂文配一幅画。无论是什么，他都一把抓住，把身怀的全部才华倾注进去。多亏罗伯特·德怀特·佩尼韦不知疲倦的点拨，某种长处开始悄悄渗进他的作品。他的素描日渐扎实。他不再回避难点了。

佩尼韦先生屈尊认可了他的进步。他对自己并无病态的自厌，于是将这进步归到了正当的源头——他自己身上。正如他某回心胸敞亮、坦率之至时所说：只要放手让他干，再给他点喝的抽的，两根木棍加一块煤，他也能造出一个画家来。

「瞧，我不就把*你*调教得画出了些像样的东西吗，我的小伙子。」他骄傲地说。「而这，」他一面伸手去够柯克替他备下的那瓶波本威士忌，一面补充道，「可算得上一桩本事了。」

柯克感激还来不及，无心去计较这话里那一点不甚动听的弦外之音——换个心气高些的人，是会听出来的。

---

===Original===

Only once during those days did Kirk allow himself to weaken and admit to himself how wretched he was. He was drawing a picture of Steve at the time, and Steve had the sympathy which encourages weakness in others.

It was a significant sign of his changed attitude towards his profession that he was not drawing Steve as a figure in an allegorical picture or as *Apollo* or *The Toiler*, but simply as a well-developed young man who had had the good sense to support his nether garments with Middleton’s Undeniable Suspenders. The picture, when completed, would show Steve smirking down at the region of his waistline and announcing with pride and satisfaction: “They’re Middleton’s!” Kirk was putting all he knew into the work, and his face, as he drew, was dark and gloomy.

Steve noted this with concern. He had perceived for some time that Kirk had changed. He had lost all his old boyish enjoyment of their sparring-bouts, and he threw the medicine-ball with an absent gloom almost equal to Bailey’s.

It had not occurred to Steve to question Kirk about this. If Kirk had anything on his mind which he wished to impart he would say it. Meanwhile, the friendly thing for him to do was to be quiet and pretend to notice nothing.

It seemed to Steve that nothing was going right these days. Here was he, chafing at his inability to open his heart to Mamie. Here was Kirk, obviously in trouble. And﻿—a smaller thing, but of interest, as showing how universal the present depression was﻿—there was Bailey Bannister, equally obviously much worried over something or other.

For Bailey had reinstated Steve in the place he had occupied before old John Bannister had dismissed him, and for some time past Steve had marked him down as a man with a secret trouble. He had never been of a riotously cheerful disposition, but it had been possible once to draw him into conversation at the close of the morning’s exercises. Now he hardly spoke. And often, when Steve arrived in the morning, he was informed that Mr. Bannister had started for Wall Street early on important business.

===Chinese===

那些日子里，柯克只有一回容许自己软弱，向自己承认他有多痛苦。当时他正在画一张史蒂夫的像，而史蒂夫身上正有那种鼓励旁人软弱下去的同情心。

他没有把史蒂夫画成寓意画里的人物，没有画成*阿波罗*（Apollo）或*《劳作者》*（The Toiler），而只是画一个体格健壮的青年，此君深谋远虑，懂得用米德尔顿（Middleton）牌「不容置疑」背带吊住自己的裤子：这一转变清楚地标志着他对本行态度的变化。这幅画完成之后，画面上的史蒂夫将俯视着自己的腰际傻笑，又骄傲又满足地宣告：「这是米德尔顿牌的！」柯克把浑身解数都使在这件活计上，而他边画，脸上的神色却阴沉沉的。

史蒂夫看在眼里，暗暗担忧。他早察觉柯克变了。对两人切磋拳脚，柯克昔日那股孩子气的兴致已荡然无存；如今扔起药球，也是一副心不在焉的愁容，那郁劲儿几乎赶得上贝利了。

史蒂夫没想到要为此盘问柯克。柯克心里若有想倾吐的事，自己自会说。眼下，尽朋友之谊的做法，就是安安静静，只当什么也没瞧见。

在史蒂夫看来，这些日子就没有一件事顺当。他自己，正为没法向玛米掏出心窝子而窝火。柯克呢，明摆着有了麻烦。还有——这桩事小些，但颇有趣味，可见眼下的消沉何等普遍——贝利·班尼斯特也明摆着在为什么事忧心忡忡。

原来贝利已让史蒂夫官复原职，回到老约翰·班尼斯特将他辞退之前所占的位置；近来一段时日，史蒂夫已认定他是个藏着隐忧的人。他从来算不得欢天喜地的性子，可从前晨练收尾时，好歹还能把他拉进话头聊几句。如今他几乎不开口了。而且常常史蒂夫一早赶到，就被告知班尼斯特先生因要务一早动身去华尔街了。

===Original===

These things troubled Steve. His simple soul abhorred a mystery.

But it was the case of Kirk that worried him most, for he half guessed that the latter’s gloom had to do with Ruth; and he worshipped Ruth.

Kirk laid down his sketch and got up.

“I guess that’ll do for the moment, Steve,” he said.

Steve relaxed the attitude of proud satisfaction which he had assumed in order to do justice to the Undeniable Suspenders. He stretched himself and sat down.

“You certainly are working to beat the band just now, squire,” he remarked.

===Chinese===

这些事都让史蒂夫犯愁。他这副简单的性灵，最厌恶神秘。

而最让他揪心的还是柯克的事，因为他隐约猜到，后者的阴郁同露丝有关；而露丝，是他敬若神明的人。

柯克放下速写，站起身来。

「我看这会儿就先到这儿吧，史蒂夫。」他说。

史蒂夫松弛下来——为了对得起那「不容置疑」背带，他方才一直端着一副志得意满的姿势。他伸展了一下身子，坐下了。

「你这一阵子干起活来可真是豁出命去了，小乡绅。」他评论道。

===Original===

“It’s a pretty good thing, work, Steve,” said Kirk. “If it does nothing else, it keeps you from thinking.”

He knew it was feeble of him, but he was powerfully impelled to relieve himself by confiding his wretchedness to Steve. He need not say much, he told himself plausibly﻿—only just enough to lighten the burden a little.

He would not be disloyal to Ruth﻿—he had not sunk to that﻿—but, after all Steve was Steve. It was not like blurting out his troubles to a stranger. It would harm nobody, and do him a great deal of good, if he talked to Steve.

He relit his pipe, which had gone out during a tense spell of work on the suspenders.

“Well, Steve,” he said, “what do you think of life? How is this best of all possible worlds treating you?”

Steve deposed that life was pretty punk.

===Chinese===

「工作是个好东西啊，史蒂夫。」柯克说，「它就算没有别的用处，至少能叫你不胡思乱想。」

他知道自己这样未免窝囊，可他有一股强烈的冲动，想把苦水倒给史蒂夫，好轻快轻快。不必说得太多，他给自己找着说得通的理由——只消那么几句，把担子减轻一点就行。

他不会做对不住露丝的事——还没堕落到那个地步——可是，史蒂夫终究是史蒂夫。这不同于朝一个陌生人倒苦水。同史蒂夫谈谈，谁也伤不着，他自己却大有好处。

他重新点着烟斗——方才埋头攻那副背带，烟斗早灭了。

「喂，史蒂夫，」他说，「你对人生有何高见？这个一切可能世界中最好的世界，待你如何呀？」

史蒂夫作证道，人生糟透了。

===Original===

“You’re a great describer, Steve. You’ve hit it first time. Punk is the word. It’s funny, if you look at it properly. Take my own case. The superficial observer, who is apt to be a bonehead, would say that I ought to be singing psalms of joy. I am married to the woman I wanted to marry. I have a son who, not to be fulsome, is a perfectly good sort of son. I have no financial troubles. I eat well. I have ceased to tremble when I see a job of work. In fact, I have advanced in my art to such an extent that shrewd business men like Middleton put the pictorial side of their Undeniable Suspenders in my hands and go off to play golf with their minds easy, having perfect confidence in my skill and judgment. If I can’t be merry and bright, who can? Do you find me merry and bright, Steve?”

“I’ve seen you in better shape,” said Steve cautiously.

“I’ve felt in better shape.”

Steve coughed. The conversation was about to become delicate.

“What’s eating you, colonel?” he asked presently.

Kirk frowned in silence at the Undeniable for a few moments. Then the pent-up misery of months exploded in a cascade of words. He jumped up and began to walk restlessly about the studio.

===Chinese===

「你可真是个了不起的形容家，史蒂夫。头一炮就打中了。糟透了，就是这个字眼。细细想来，这事其实滑稽得很。就拿我自己来说吧。肤浅的看客——这种人多半是榆木脑袋——准会说我该高唱欢乐的赞美诗才是。我娶了我想娶的女人。我有个儿子，不说奉承话，是顶呱呱的儿子。我在钱财上没有愁事。我吃得香。我见了活计不再发怵。事实上，我的艺术已精进到这般地步，连米德尔顿那样精明的生意人，都把『不容置疑』背带的画图一面交到我手里，然后揣着对我手艺和眼力的十足信心，安安心心打高尔夫球去了。连我都快活不起来、明媚不起来，还有谁能快活？你觉得我快活吗、明媚吗，史蒂夫？」

「我见过你气色更好的时候。」史蒂夫审慎地说。

「我自己也觉得那会儿更好。」

史蒂夫咳嗽了一声。谈话就要进入微妙的境地了。

「什么事在折腾你呢，上校？」过了一会儿，他问。

柯克对着那「不容置疑」默然皱了一阵眉头。随即，积压数月的苦楚化作一挂话语倾泻而出。他跳起身，开始在画室里焦躁地踱步。

===Original===

“Damn it! Steve, I ought not to say a word, I know. It’s weak and cowardly and bad taste and everything else you can think of to speak of it﻿—even to you. One’s supposed to stand this sort of roasting at the stake with a grin, as if one enjoyed it. But, after all, you *are* different. It’s not as if it was anyone. You *are* different, aren’t you?”

“Sure.”

“Well, you know what’s wrong as well as I do.”

“Surest thing you know. It’s hit me, too.”

“How’s that?”

“Well, things ain’t the same. That’s about what it comes to.”

===Chinese===

「见鬼！史蒂夫，我一个字也不该说的，这我知道。这软弱、这怯懦、这没分寸，还有你能想得出的别的什么毛病，全叫它占全了——哪怕是跟你说。照规矩，人受了这种火刑烤炙也该咧嘴一笑，仿佛乐在其中才是。可是说到底，你*就是*不一样。又不是对随便什么人开口。你*确实*不一样，不是吗？」

「那是自然。」

「那么，毛病出在哪儿，你跟我一样清楚。」

「这还用说。这一下也砸着我了。」

「这话怎讲？」

「唉，事情全变味儿了。归根结底就是这么回事。」
===Original===

Kirk stopped and looked at him. His expression was wistful. “I ought not to be talking about it.”

“You go right ahead, squire,” said Steve soothingly. “I know just how you feel, and I guess talking’s not going to do any harm. Act as if I wasn’t here. Look on it as a monologue. I don’t amount to anything.”

“When did you go to the house last, Steve?”

Steve reflected.

“About a couple of weeks ago, I reckon.”

===Chinese===

柯克停下脚步，望着他，一脸怅惘。「我不该谈论这个的。」

「你只管往下说，小乡绅。」史蒂夫宽慰道，「你的滋味我一清二楚，况且我想，说说也没什么坏处。只当我不在这儿。就当是一段独白。我不算数。」

「你上一回上那宅子去，是什么时候，史蒂夫？」

史蒂夫想了想。

「我估摸着，约莫两个礼拜前吧。」
===Original===

“See the kid?”

Steve shook his head.

“Seeing his nibs ain’t my long suit these days. I may be wrong, but I got the idea there was a deadline for me about three blocks away from the nursery. I asked Keggs was the coast clear, but he said the Porter dame was in the ring, so I kind of thought I’d better away. I don’t seem to fit in with all them white tiles and thermometers.”

“You used to see him every day when we were here. And you didn’t seem to contaminate him, as far as anyone could notice.”

There was a silence.

===Chinese===

「见着那小子了？」

史蒂夫摇了摇头。

「如今想见那位小爷，可算不上我的拿手戏。兴许是我错疑了，可我总觉得，离育儿室还差着大约三个街区呢，人家就给我划了条死线。我问过凯格斯那边清不清净，可他说波特太太正在场子里坐镇，我寻思还是走为上策。我跟那一屋子白瓷砖和温度计，好像天生不对付。」

「从前我们住在这儿的时节，你天天都见得着他。而且就旁人看得出的来说，你似乎也没把什么病气过给他。」

一阵沉默。
===Original===

“Do you see him often, colonel?”

Kirk laughed.

“Oh, yes. I’m favoured. I pay a state visit every day. Think of that! I sit in a chair at the other end of the room while Mrs. Porter stands between to see that I don’t start anything. Bill plays with his sterilized bricks. Occasionally he and I exchange a few civil words. It’s as jolly and sociable as you could want. We have great times.”

“Say, on the level, I wonder you stand for it.”

“I’ve got to stand for it.”

“He’s your kid.”

===Chinese===

「你倒是常常见他吗，上校？」

柯克笑了。

「哦，常见。我是受优待的。我每天进行一次国事访问。你想想吧！我坐在房间另一头的一把椅子里，波特太太居中站着，监着别让我起什么幺蛾子。比尔玩他那消过毒的积木。偶尔，他和我之间也客气地寒暄几句。要多快活有多快活，要多亲热有多亲热。我们过的可是好日子啊。」

「我说，凭良心讲，我真纳闷你怎么受得了。」

「受不了也得受。」

「他是你儿子啊。」
===Original===

“Not exclusively. I have a partner, Steve.”

Steve snorted dolefully.

“Ain’t it hell the way things break loose in this world!” he sighed. “Who’d have thought two years ago﻿—”

“Do you make it only two? I should have put it at about two thousand.”

“Honest, squire, if anyone had told me then that Miss Ruth had it in her to take up with all these fool stunts﻿—”

“Well, I can’t say I was prepared for it.”

===Chinese===

「又不是我独一份的。我还有位合伙人呢，史蒂夫。」

史蒂夫悲切地哼了一声。

「这世上的事，说散架就散架，真是造孽啊！」他叹道，「谁能想得到，两年前——」

「你才折算成两年？我看怎么也得有两千年。」

「不骗你，小乡绅，那时候要有人告诉我，露丝小姐骨子里竟会迷上这帮蠢把戏——」

「唉，我也不能说自己早有防备。」
===Original===

Steve coughed again. Kirk was in an expansive mood this afternoon, and the occasion was ideal for the putting forward of certain views which he had long wished to impart. But, on the other hand, the subject was a peculiarly delicate one. It has been well said that it is better for a third party to quarrel with a buzz-saw than to interfere between husband and wife; and Steve was constitutionally averse to anything that savoured of butting in.

Still, Kirk had turned the talk into this channel. He decided to risk it.

“If I were you,” he said, “I’d get busy and start something.”

“Such as what?”

Steve decided to abandon caution and speak his mind. Him, almost as much as Kirk, the existing state of things had driven to desperation. Though in a sense he was only a spectator, the fact that the altered conditions of Kirk’s life involved his almost complete separation from Mamie gave him what might be called a stake in the affair. The brief and rare glimpses which he got of her nowadays made it absolutely impossible for him to conduct his wooing on a businesslike basis. A diffident man cannot possibly achieve any success in odd moments. Constant propinquity is his only hope.

That fact alone, he considered, almost gave him the right to interfere. And, apart from that, his affection for Kirk and Ruth gave him a claim. Finally, he held what was practically an official position in the family councils on the strength of being William Bannister Winfield’s godfather.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫又咳了一声。这天下午，柯克正怀着倾吐的兴致，正是亮出某些他早想进言的见解的天赐良机。可话又说回来，这题目委实万分微妙。有句名言说得好：第三者宁可去跟圆锯吵架，也休想插手人家夫妻之间的事；而史蒂夫天生就避忌一切带有多管闲事气味的行为。

不过，话头毕竟是柯克自己引进这条渠道的。他决定冒一冒险。

「我要是你，」他说，「就赶紧行动，闹出点动静来。」

「比如说？」

史蒂夫决定抛开谨慎，直抒胸臆。眼下这个局面，几乎把他也逼到了和柯克不相上下的绝境。虽然从某种意义上说，他只是个旁观者，但柯克生活的变故意味着他几乎与玛米暌隔两地，这就给了他一件可称之为利害攸关的事。如今他只能偷空匆匆见她一两眼，想按部就班地走求爱的路数，是断无可能的了。一个脸皮薄的人，零敲碎打地抽空献殷勤，绝无成功的指望。朝夕相守才是他唯一的希望。

单凭这一条，在他看来，就几乎给了他插手的权利。何况除此之外，他对柯克和露丝的感情也给了他一份发言权。末了，凭着威廉·班尼斯特·温菲尔德（William Bannister Winfield）教父的身份，他在这个家庭的议事会上，几乎是占着一个正式席位。
===Original===

He loved William Bannister as a son, and it had been one of his favourite day dreams to conjure up a vision of the time when he should be permitted to undertake the child’s physical training. He had toyed lovingly with the idea of imparting to this promising pupil all that he knew of the greatest game on earth. He had watched him in the old days staggering about the studio, and had pictured him grown to his full strength, his muscles trained, his brain full of the wisdom of one who, if his mother had not kicked, would have been middleweight champion of America.
