He had resigned himself to the fact that the infant’s social status made it impossible that he should be the real White Hope whom he had once pictured beating all comers in the roped ring; but, after all, there was a certain mild fame to be acquired even by an amateur. And now that dream was over﻿—unless Kirk could be goaded into strong action in time.

“Why don’t you sneak the kid away somewhere?” he suggested. “Why don’t you go right in at them and say: ‘It’s my kid, and I’m going to take him away into the country out of all this white-tile stuff and let him roll in the mud same as he used to.’ Why, say, there’s that shack of yours in Connecticut, just made for it. That kid would have the time of his life there.”

“You think that’s the solution, do you, Steve?”

“I’m dead sure it is.” Steve’s voice became more and more enthusiastic as the idea unfolded itself. “Why, it ain’t only the kid I’m thinking of. There’s Miss Ruth. Say, you don’t mind me pulling this line of talk?”

“Go ahead. I began it. What about Miss Ruth?”

===Chinese===

他爱威廉·班尼斯特，如同爱亲生儿子。他最爱做的白日梦之一，便是畅想有朝一日获准主持这孩子的体育锻炼。他曾津津有味地盘算，要把自己对那项世上最伟大的运动所知的一切，倾囊传授给这位前程远大的学生。他看着这孩子当年在画室里摇摇晃晃地扑腾，不禁想象他长成一副魁伟的身板、练出一身筋骨、脑子里装满那个人的智慧——那人若非当年母亲死活拦着，本该是全美中量级冠军。

这孩子的社会地位，决定了他成不了真正的「白人希望」，成不了史蒂夫曾想象的那副在围绳拳台上打遍各路来客的身手——这一点他已想开认命了；可是话又说回来，即便是业余选手，也照样挣得着一份小小的名声。而如今，连这个梦也完了——除非能把柯克刺激得及时采取强有力的行动。

「你干吗不悄悄把小子带走，寻个地方猫起来？」他出主意道，「你干吗不径直冲到他们跟前，说：『这是我儿子，我要带他下乡，脱离这一整套白瓷砖的玩意儿，让他像从前那样在泥地里打滚。』嘿，我说，你在康涅狄格不是有座小木屋吗，简直现成。那孩子到了那儿，非乐出个样儿来不可。」

「你认为这就是解决办法，是吗，史蒂夫？」

「我拿得死死的。」随着这主意越铺越开，史蒂夫的声音也越来越热切。「嘿，我心里惦记的可不光是小子。还有露丝小姐呢。呃，你不介意我顺着这个话头说下去吧？」

「说吧。话头是我先起的。露丝小姐怎么了？」
===Original===

“Well, you know just what’s the matter with her. She’s let this society game run away with her. I guess she started it because she felt lonesome when you were away; and now it’s got her and she can’t drop it. All she wants is a jolt. It would slow her up and show her just where she was. She’s asking for it. One good, snappy jolt would put the whole thing right. And this thing of jerking the kid away to Connecticut would be the right dope, believe *me*.”

Kirk shook his head.

“It wouldn’t do, Steve. It isn’t that I don’t want to do it; but one must play to the rules. I can’t explain what I mean. I can only say it’s impossible. Let’s think of a parallel case. When you were in the ring, there must have been times when you had a chance of hitting your man low. Why didn’t you do it? It would have jolted him, all right.”

“Why, I’d have lost on a foul.”

“Well, so should I lose on a foul if I started the sort of roughhouse you suggest.”

“I don’t get you.”

===Chinese===

「唉，她的毛病在哪儿，你一清二楚。她由着这场交际游戏牵着跑，收不住缰了。我猜，你出门那阵她心里孤单，才沾上这口的；如今这东西拿住了她，她丢不开了。她缺的就是一记猛击。那一下能叫她缓过劲儿来，看清自己到底站在哪儿。她自己正等着这一下呢。一记干脆利落的好猛击，保管样样归位。把孩子猛地拽去康涅狄格这一手，就是对症的药，相信*我*。」

柯克摇了摇头。

「那行不通，史蒂夫。不是我不想干；而是人总得按规矩来。我讲不清我的意思。我只能说，这不可能。咱们找个类似的情形说说。你在拳台上那些年，总有过机会，能朝你那对手腰带底下来一记的吧。你干吗不下手？那一记保管叫他吃不消。」

「那还用说，我会犯规格出局呀。」

「这就对了。我要是照你说的那样大闹一场，也同样会犯规格出局。」

「我没听懂。」
===Original===

“Well, if you want it in plain English, Ruth would never forgive me. Is that clear enough?”

“You’re dead wrong, boss,” said Steve excitedly. “I know her.”

“I thought I did. Well, anyway, Steve, thanks for the suggestion; but, believe me, nothing doing. And now, if you feel like it, I wish you would resume your celebrated imitation of a man exulting over the fact that he is wearing Middleton’s Undeniable. There isn’t much more to do, and I should like to get through with it today, if possible. There, hold that pose. It’s exactly right. The honest man gloating over his suspenders. You ought to go on the stage, Steve.”

===Chinese===

「好吧，你要是想听大白话：露丝永远不会原谅我。这下够明白了吧？」

「你可是错大发了，老板。」史蒂夫激动地说，「我了解她。」

「我原也以为自己了解。好了，不管怎么说，多谢你的提议，史蒂夫；可是相信我，没门儿。现在呢，你要是歇够了，就请你继续表演你那段出名的模仿——学一个男子汉，正为自己穿着米德尔顿牌『不容置疑』背带而喜不自胜。要画的没剩多少了，可能的话，我想今天把它了结。对，保持住那个姿势。一丝不差。一个老实巴交的汉子，正对着自己的背带沾沾自喜。你真该去登台演戏的，史蒂夫。」

## V The Real Thing / 第五章 真材实料

===Original===

Kirk sat in the nursery with his chin on his hands, staring gloomily at William Bannister. On the floor William Bannister played some game of his own invention with his box of bricks.
They were alone. It was the first time they had been alone together for two weeks. As a rule, when Kirk paid his daily visit, Lora Delane Porter was there, watchful and forbidding, prepared, on the slightest excuse, to fall upon him with rules and prohibitions. Today she was out, and Kirk had the field to himself, for Mamie, whose duty it was to mount guard, and who had been threatened with many terrible things by Mrs. Porter if she did not stay on guard, had once more allowed her too sympathetic nature to get the better of her and had vanished.
Kirk was too dispirited to take advantage of his good fortune. He had a sense of being there on parole, of being on his honour not to touch. So he sat in his chair, and looked at Bill; while Bill, crooning to himself, played decorously with bricks.
The truth had been a long time in coming home to Kirk, but it had reached him at last. Ever since his return he had clung to the belief that it was a genuine conviction of its merits that had led Ruth to support her aunt’s scheme for Bill’s welfare. He himself had always looked on the exaggerated precautions for the maintenance of the latter’s health as ridiculous and unnecessary; but he had acquiesced in them because he thought that Ruth sincerely believed them indispensable.

===Chinese===

柯克坐在育儿室里，双手托着下巴，闷闷不乐地盯着威廉·班尼斯特。地板上，威廉·班尼斯特正用他那盒积木玩着一种自己发明的游戏。
屋里只有他们父子两个。两个星期以来，这还是头一回。按老规矩，柯克每日前来探望时，洛拉·德莱恩·波特总在场，又警觉又威严，只消有个最小的由头，便预备拿清规戒律和种种禁令朝他扑将过来。今天她出门了，柯克得以独享这片阵地——本来该站岗把守的是玛米，她若不坚守岗位，波特夫人威胁过要叫她吃不了兜着走，可她那副过分好心的天性又一次占了上风，人已经没影儿了。
柯克心灰意懒，竟无福消受这份好运。他自觉是假释在此，凭名誉担保不许动手。于是他坐在自己的椅子里，望着比尔；比尔则哼哼唧唧地自得其乐，规规矩矩地玩着积木。
真相花了很长时间才在柯克心里落定，但它终究来了。自打回来，他一直抱定一个信念：露丝之所以支持姑妈那套为比尔福祉打算的方案，是真心信服它的好处。他自己呢，向来把那些为维护小家伙健康而设的夸张防备看作又可笑又多余；但他默许了，因为他以为露丝当真相信这些防备缺一不可。

===Original===

After all, he had not been there when Bill so nearly died, and he could understand that the shock of that episode might have distorted the judgment even of a woman so well balanced as Ruth. He was quite ready to be loyal to her in the matter, however distasteful it might be to him.
But now he saw the truth. A succession of tiny incidents had brought light to him. Ruth might or might not be to some extent genuine in her belief in the new system, but her chief motive for giving it her support was something quite different. He had tried not to admit to himself, but he could do so no longer. Ruth allowed Mrs. Porter to have her way because it suited her to do so; because, with Mrs. Porter on the premises, she had more leisure in which to amuse herself; because, to put it in a word, the child had begun to bore her.
Everything pointed to that. In the old days it had been her chief pleasure to be with the boy. Their walks in the park had been a daily ceremony with which nothing had been allowed to interfere. But now she always had some excuse for keeping away from him.
Her visits to the nursery, when she did go there, were brief and perfunctory. And the mischief of it was that she always presented such admirable reasons for abstaining from Bill’s society, when it was suggested to her that she should go to him, that it was impossible to bring her out into the open and settle the matter once and for all.

===Chinese===

毕竟，比尔险些丧命那阵子他不在场，他能理解那场虚惊的打击足以扭曲一个人的判断力，哪怕那人是露丝这般头脑端正的女人。在这件事上，他满心打算始终站在她一边，不管自己心里有多不痛快。
可如今他看清了真相。一连串鸡毛蒜皮的小事给他带来了光明。露丝对新制度或许在某种程度上也算真心相信，或许不算，但她支持它的主要动机却全然是另一回事。他一直竭力不向自己承认，如今再也不能了。露丝听任波特夫人自行其是，是因为这样对她合适；因为有波特夫人坐镇宅中，她便有更多可供自娱的闲暇；一句话概括，因为这孩子已经开始叫她腻烦了。
桩桩件件都指向这一点。从前，陪这孩子是她头一桩乐事。母子俩在公园里散步是每日的典礼，什么事情都不许搅扰。可如今她总有个借口避着他。
她到育儿室去的时候——她当真去的话——也是来去匆匆、敷衍了事。糟就糟在，每逢有人劝她到比尔那儿去，她总能端出一套无可指摘的理由，叫人没法把话挑明，一劳永逸地了结这件事。

===Original===

Patience was one of the virtues which set off the defects in Kirk’s character; but he did not feel very patient now as he sat and watched Bill playing on the floor.
“Well, Bill, old man, what do you make of it all?” he said at last.
The child looked up and fixed him with unwinking eyes. Kirk winced. They were so exactly Ruth’s eyes. That wide-open expression when somebody, speaking suddenly to her, interrupted a train of thought, was one of her hundred minor charms.
Bill had reproduced it to the life. He stared for a moment; then, as if there had been some telepathy between them, said: “I want mummy.”
Kirk laughed bitterly.
“You aren’t the only one. I want mummy, too.”

===Chinese===

忍耐本是柯克品性中足以衬掩种种缺点的一项美德；可此刻他坐在那儿，望着比尔在地板上玩耍，却觉得自己没多少忍耐可言了。
「喂，比尔，老伙计，这一切你都作何感想？」他终于开口道。
孩子抬起头，一眨不眨地定睛望着他。柯克心里一颤。那双眼睛活脱脱正是露丝的眼睛。有人冷不丁开口同她说话、打断了她的思绪时，她脸上那种瞪圆了眼的神情，正是她百般细微迷人处中的一处。
比尔把这神情复刻得惟妙惟肖。他盯了片刻；接着，仿佛父子之间果真有什么心电感应似的，说道：「我要妈咪。」
柯克苦笑一声。
「想妈咪的可不止你一个。我也想妈咪。」

===Original===

“Where is mummy?”
“I couldn’t tell you exactly. At a luncheon-party somewhere.”
“What’s luncheon-party?”
“A sort of entertainment where everybody eats too much and talks all the time without ever saying a thing that’s worth hearing.”
Bill considered this gravely.
“Why?”

===Chinese===

「妈咪在哪儿？」
「确切的我可说不上。在哪儿赴午宴吧。」
「什么叫赴午宴？」
「一种招待会：人人吃得过量，嘴巴不停地说，可从头到尾说不出一句值得一听的话。」
比尔郑重其事地琢磨了一会儿。
「为什么？」

===Original===

“Because they like it, I suppose.”
“Why do they like it?”
“Goodness knows.”
“Does mummy like it?”
“I suppose so.”
“Does mummy eat too much?”

===Chinese===

「我猜是因为他们喜欢吧。」
「他们为什么喜欢？」
「天晓得。」
「妈咪喜欢吗？」
「我猜喜欢吧。」
「妈咪也吃得过量吗？」

===Original===

“She doesn’t. The others do.”
“Why?”
William Bannister’s thirst for knowledge was at this time perhaps his most marked characteristic. No encyclopaedia could have coped with it. Kirk was accustomed to do his best, cheerfully yielding up what little information on general subjects he happened to possess, but he was like Mrs. Partington sweeping back the Atlantic Ocean with her broom.
“Because they’ve been raised that way,” he replied to the last question. “Bill, old man, when you grow up, don’t you ever become one of these fellows who can’t walk two blocks without stopping three times to catch up with their breath. If you get like that mutt Dana Ferris you’ll break my heart. And you’re heading that way, poor kid.”
“What’s Ferris?”
“He’s a man I met at dinner the other night. When he was your age he was the richest child in America, and everybody fussed over him till he grew up into a wretched little creature with a black moustache and two chins. You ought to see him. He would make you laugh; and you don’t get much to laugh at nowadays. I guess it isn’t hygienic for a kid to laugh. Bill, honestly﻿—what *do* you think of things? Don’t you ever want to hurl one of those sterilized bricks of yours at a certain lady? Or has she taken all the heart out of you by this time?”

===Chinese===

「她不。别人才那样。」
「为什么？」
威廉·班尼斯特的求知欲，眼下恐怕要算他最显著的特性了。哪一部百科全书也招架不住它。柯克惯于尽力应付，乐呵呵地把自己碰巧掌握的那点儿百科知识倾囊相授，可他那份苦功，好比帕丁顿夫人（Mrs. Partington）挥着扫帚往回扫大西洋。
「因为他们从小就是这么养大的，」他回答了最后一个问题。「比尔，老伙计，你长大了，千万别变成那种家伙——走不上两个街区就得停下三回缓气。你要是变得跟达纳·费里斯（Dana Ferris）那头蠢驴一个样，我的心都要碎了。而你正朝那条道上走呢，可怜的孩子。」
「什么叫费里斯？」
「是我前几天晚饭上认识的一个家伙。他像你这么大的时候，是全美国最富有的孩子，人人把他捧得天上地下，结果他长大成了个可怜巴巴的小东西，留着两撇黑胡子，顶着两个下巴。你真该见见他。他能逗你发笑；你如今难得有东西可笑。我猜小孩子发笑是不合卫生的。比尔，说句老实话——你对这一切究竟*是*怎么想的？你难道从没想拿你那些消过毒的积木里的一块，朝某位太太身上掷过去？还是说到如今，她已经把你的胆气全掏空了？」

===Original===

This was beyond Bill, as Kirk’s monologues frequently were. He changed the subject.
“I wish I had a cat,” he said, by way of starting a new topic.
“Well, why haven’t you a cat? Why haven’t you a dozen cats if you want them?”
“I asked Aunty Lora could I have a cat, and she said: ‘Certainly not, cats are﻿—cats are﻿—’ ”
“Unhygienic?”
“What’s that?”

===Chinese===

这话超出了比尔的理解力，柯克的独白十有八九都是如此。他另起话头。
「我想要一只猫，」他说，权当另起一个新话题。
「嗐，你怎么会没有猫？你要是想要，怎么不养上一打猫？」
「我问过洛拉姑妈能不能要一只猫，她说：『当然不行，猫是——猫是——』」
「『不卫生』？」
「那是什么？」
===Original===

“It’s what your Aunt Lora might think a cat was. Or did she say pestilential?”
“I don’t amember.”
“But she wouldn’t let you have one?”
“Mamie said a cat might scratch me.”
“Well, you wouldn’t mind that?” said Kirk anxiously.

===Chinese===

「就是你洛拉姑妈对猫的看法，她没准儿就认定猫是那种东西。要么，她说的是『瘟疫性的』？」
「我记不得了嘛。」
「可她就是不肯让你养？」
「玛米说猫可能会挠我。」
「嗐，挠一下你也不在乎的，对吧？」柯克焦急地问。

===Original===

He had come to be almost morbidly on the lookout for evidence which might go to prove that this cotton-wool existence was stealing from the child the birthright of courage which was his from both his parents. Much often depends on little things, and, if Bill had replied in the affirmative to the question, it would probably have had the result of sending Kirk there and then raging through the house conducting a sort of War of Independence.
The only thing that had kept him from doing so before was the reflection that Mrs. Porter’s system could not be definitely taxed with any harmful results. But his mind was never easy. Every day found him still nervously on the alert for symptoms.
Bill soothed him now by answering “No” in a very decided voice. All well so far, but it had been an anxious moment.
It seemed incredible to Kirk that the life he was leading should not in time turn the child into a whimpering bundle of nerves. His conversations with Bill were, as a result, a sort of spiritual parallel to the daily taking of his temperature with the thermometer. Sooner or later he always led the talk round to some point where Bill must make a definite pronouncement which would show whether or not the insidious decay had begun to set in.

===Chinese===

他如今几乎是病态地四下搜寻证据，想证明这种裹在药棉里的日子正在把孩子生来继承的胆量偷走——那份胆量，是他从父母双方得来的与生之权。大事每每系于小事；倘若比尔方才对这个问题给了肯定的回答，其结果多半是柯克当场便怒冲冲地满宅杀将起来，发动一场独立战争。
先前他之所以没有这么干，唯一的考虑是：波特夫人的制度还罗织不出什么确凿的害处。可他的心从来不曾安定。每一天都发现他仍在提心吊胆地警戒着种种征兆。
比尔此刻用一个十分干脆的「不」字安抚了他。到目前为止一切良好，只是方才那一刻着实叫人捏了把汗。
在柯克看来，这孩子过的这种日子，若不把他迟早变成一包哼哼唧唧的神经，简直不可思议。因此，他同比尔的谈话便成了每天拿体温计试体温在精神层面的对应物。他总会或迟或早地把话头引到某个关口，叫比尔非做一番明确的宣示不可，好看出那暗中的衰朽究竟动了身没有。

===Original===

So far all appeared to be well. In earlier conversations Bill, subtly questioned, had stoutly maintained that he was not afraid of Indians, dogs, pirates, mice, cows, June-bugs, or noises in the dark. He had even gone so far as to state that if an Indian chief found his way into the nursery he, Bill, would chop his head off. The most exacting father could not have asked more. And yet Kirk was not satisfied: he remained uneasy.
It so happened that this afternoon Bill, who had had hitherto to maintain his reputation for intrepidity entirely by verbal statements, was afforded an opportunity of providing a practical demonstration that his heart was in the right place. The game he was playing with the bricks was one that involved a certain amount of running about with a puffing accompaniment of a vaguely equine nature. And while performing this part of the programme he chanced to trip. He hesitated for a moment, as if uncertain whether to fall or remain standing; then did the former with a most emphatic bump.
He scrambled up, stood looking at Kirk with a twitching lip, then gave a great gulp, and resumed his trotting. The whole exhibition of indomitable heroism was over in half a minute, and he did not even bother to wait for applause.
The effect of the incident on Kirk was magical. He was in the position of an earnest worshipper who, tortured with doubts, has prayed for a sign. This was a revelation. A million anti-Indian statements, however resolute, were nothing to this.

===Chinese===

到目前为止，一切似乎良好。早先的几次谈话里，比尔在旁敲侧击的盘问下曾一口咬定：他不怕印第安人、狗、海盗、耗子、奶牛、金龟子，也不怕黑地里的响动。他甚至把话说到了那个地步：倘若有个印第安酋长摸进育儿室，他比尔就砍下他的脑袋。最挑剔的父亲也不能要求更多了。然而柯克并不满足：他心里依旧不踏实。
也是合该有事，这天下午，迄今为止一直全凭口头声明维持无畏名声的比尔，得了一个机会，实地演示一番自己的心长没长对地方。他用积木玩的那套游戏里有一定分量的跑动，跑时还伴着一种隐约近乎马匹的喷鼻声。正执行节目这一部分时，他偶然绊了一跤。他迟疑了片刻，仿佛拿不准该摔倒还是站稳；随后他选择了前者，摔了个毫不含糊的大跟头，砰然有声。
他手脚并用地爬起来，站在那儿望着柯克，嘴唇直哆嗦，随后猛地咽了一大口，又颠颠地小跑起来。这场不屈不挠的英雄气概的整个演示前后不过半分钟，他甚至懒得停下来等一声喝彩。
这件事在柯克身上产生的效力如有神助。他正处在一位被疑虑煎熬、祈求预兆的虔诚信徒的地位上。这是一场显圣。一百万句反印第安人宣言，说得再有决心，也比不上这一跤。

===Original===

This was the real thing. Before his eyes this super-child of his had fallen in a manner which might quite reasonably have led to tears; which would, Kirk felt sure, have produced bellows of anguish from every other child in America. And what had happened? Not a moan. No, sir, not one solitary cry. Just a gulp which you had to strain your ears to hear, and which, at that, might have been a mere taking-in of breath such as every athlete must do, and all was over.
This child of his was the real thing. It had been proved beyond possibility of criticism.
There are moments when a man on parole forgets his promise. All thought of rules and prohibitions went from Kirk. He rose from his seat, grabbed his son with both hands, and hugged him. We cannot even begin to estimate the number of bacilli which must have rushed, whooping with joy, on to the unfortunate child. Under a microscope it would probably have looked like an Old Home Week. And Kirk did not care. He simply kept on hugging. That was the sort of man he was﻿—thoroughly heartless.
“Bill, you’re great!” he cried.

===Chinese===

这才是真材实料。就在他眼前，他这个超凡的孩子摔了那样一跤——按理那一跤完全可以摔出眼泪来；柯克敢担保，全美国随便换哪个孩子，都得嚎出一片哀声。结果呢？一声呜咽也没有。没有，先生，孤零零一声哭喊也没有。只有一下吞咽，你得竖起耳朵才听得见，而且就那一下，说不定不过是换一口气——每个运动员都非换不可的那种——然后一切就完事了。
他这个孩子是真材实料。这一点已经证明得无可指摘。
假释中的人也有忘了誓言的时刻。什么规章制度、禁令条条，全从柯克脑子里飞了。他从座位上站起，双手一把抓住儿子，紧紧搂在怀里。涌向那不幸孩子的细菌想必数不胜数，一路欢呼怪叫——具体数目我们连着手估算都办不到。放在显微镜底下看，多半像一场热热闹闹的返乡同乐会（Old Home Week）。柯克满不在乎。他只管一个劲儿地搂。他就是这种人——彻头彻尾地没心没肺。
「比尔，你真了不起！」他喊道。

===Original===

Bill had been an amazed party to the incident. Nothing of this kind had happened to him for so long that he had forgotten there were children to whom this sort of thing did happen. Then he recollected a similar encounter with a bearded man down in the hall when he came in one morning from his ride in the automobile. A moment later he had connected his facts.
This man who had no beard was the same man as the man who had a beard, and this behaviour was a personal eccentricity of his. The thought crossed his mind that Aunty Lora would not approve of this.
And then, surprisingly, there came the thought that he did not care whether Aunty Lora approved or not. *He* liked it, and that was enough for him.
The seeds of revolt had been sown in the bosom of William Bannister.

===Chinese===

比尔是这场事变里目瞪口呆的一方。这类事情太久没有临到他头上，以致他忘了世上还有常常摊上这类事的孩子。接着他记起一次类似的遭遇：某天早晨他坐汽车兜风回来，楼下大厅里有个留胡子的人同他有过这么一回。片刻之后，他把两条事实联系起来了。
这个没有胡子的人，就是那个有胡子的人，而这种行为是他个人的一桩怪癖。他心里掠过一个念头：洛拉姑妈不会赞成这个。
随后，出乎意料地又来了一个念头：洛拉姑妈赞成不赞成，他才不管呢。*他*喜欢，这对他来说就足够了。
反叛的种子，就这样播进了威廉·班尼斯特的胸膛。

===Original===

It happened that Ruth, returning from her luncheon-party, looked in at the nursery on her way upstairs. She was confronted with the spectacle of Bill seated on Kirk’s lap, his face against Kirk’s shoulder. Kirk, though he had stopped speaking as the door opened, appeared to be in the middle of a story, for Bill, after a brief glance at the newcomer, asked: “What happened then?”
“Kirk, really!” said Ruth.
Kirk did not appear in the least ashamed of himself.
“Ruth, this kid is the most amazing kid. Do you know what happened just now? He was running along and he tripped and came down flat. And he didn’t even think of crying. He just picked himself up, and﻿—”
“That was very brave of you, Billy. But, seriously, Kirk, you shouldn’t hug him like that. Think what Aunt Lora would say!”

===Chinese===

也是碰巧，露丝赴完午宴回来，上楼途中往育儿室里张了一张。她迎面撞见的场面是：比尔坐在柯克膝上，脸贴着柯克的肩膀。门开时柯克虽然住了口，看样子却正讲到故事半截，因为比尔朝来人略略一瞥之后便问：「后来怎么样了？」
「柯克，你真是的！」露丝说。
柯克看上去没有一丝半点自惭形秽的意思。
「露丝，这孩子是最了不得的孩子。你知道刚才出了什么事吗？他跑着跑着绊了一下，扑通摔了个结实。他连哭都没想到哭。自己爬起来，接着就——」
「你真勇敢，比利。不过，说正经的，柯克，你不该那样搂他。想想洛拉姑妈会说什么！」

===Original===

“Aunt Lora be﻿—Bother Aunt Lora!”
“Well, I won’t give you away. If she heard, she would write a book about it. And she was just starting to come up when I was downstairs. We came in together. You had better fly while there’s time.”
It was sound advice, and Kirk took it.
It was not till some time later, going over the incident again in his mind, he realized how very lightly Ruth had treated what, if she really adhered to Mrs. Porter’s views on hygiene, should have been to her a dreadful discovery. The reflection was pleasant to him for a moment; it seemed to draw Ruth and himself closer together; then he saw the reverse side of it.
If Ruth did not really believe in this absurd hygienic nonsense, why had she permitted it to be practised upon the boy? There was only one answer, and it was the one which Kirk had already guessed at. She did it because it gave her more freedom, because it bored her to look after the child herself, because she was not the same Ruth he had left at the studio when he started with Hank Jardine for Colombia.

===Chinese===

「让洛拉姑妈见——嗐，别提洛拉姑妈了！」
「好啦，我不会告发你的。她要是听见了，准会为此写出一本书来。方才我在楼下的时候，她正要上楼来。我们是一道进门的。趁还来得及，你快飞吧。」
这是一条金玉良言，柯克从善如流。
直到过了一阵、在心里把这件事重新过了一遍，他才意识到，露丝把这件事处理得何等轻描淡写——倘若她当真信奉波特夫人的卫生观点，这本该是她一场可怕的发现。这个念头让他一时觉得愉快；它似乎把露丝和他自己拉得更近了；随后他看到了它的反面。
倘若露丝并不真信这套荒唐的卫生呓语，那她为什么听任它在这孩子身上试行？答案只有一个，而且正是柯克早已猜到的那个。她这样做，是因为这给了她更多自由，因为亲自照看孩子叫她腻烦，因为她已经不是他随汉克·贾丁动身去哥伦比亚那天留在画室里的那个露丝了。

## XIV The Sixty-First Street Cyclone / 第十四章 第六十一街旋风

===Original===

It was past seven o’clock when Kirk, bending over the wheel, with Mamie at his side came in sight of the shack. The journey had been checked just outside the city by a blowout in one of the back tyres. Kirk had spent the time, while the shirt-sleeved rescuer from the garage toiled over the injured wheel, walking up and down with a cigar. Neither he nor Mamie had shown much tendency towards conversation. Mamie was habitually of a silent disposition, and Kirk’s mind was too full of his thoughts to admit of speech.
Ever since he had read Steve’s telegram he had been in the grip of a wild exhilaration. He had not stopped to ask himself what this mad freak of Steve’s could possibly lead to in the end﻿—he was satisfied to feel that its immediate result would be that for a brief while, at any rate, he would have his son to himself, away from all the chilling surroundings which had curbed him and frozen his natural feelings in the past.
He tried to keep his mind from dwelling upon Ruth. He had thought too much of her of late for his comfort. Since they had parted that day of the thunderstorm the thought that he had lost her had stabbed him incessantly. He had tried to tell himself that it was the best thing they could do, to separate, since it was so plain that their love had died; but he could not cheat himself into believing it.
It might be true in her case﻿—it must be, or why had she let him go that afternoon?﻿—but, for himself, the separation had taught him that he loved her as much as ever, more than ever. Absence had purified him of that dull anger which had been his so short a while before. He looked back and marvelled that he could ever have imagined for a moment that he had ceased to love her.
Now, as he drove along the empty country roads, he forced his mind to dwell, as far as he could, only upon his son. There was a mist before his eyes as he thought of him. What a bully lad he had been! What fun they had had in the old days! But that brought his mind back to Ruth, and he turned his mind resolutely to the future again.

===Chinese===

柯克俯身握着方向盘，玛米坐在他身旁，当小木屋映入眼帘时，已是七点过后了。就在城外不远处，一只后胎放了炮，行程被耽搁下来。修车厂那位挽着衬衫袖子的救兵埋头对付伤胎的当儿，柯克叼着雪茄来回踱步消磨时光。他和玛米都没什么攀谈的兴致。玛米平日就是个沉默寡言的性子，柯克则满腹心事，腾不出说话的空当。
自打读到史蒂夫的电报，他就被一阵狂喜攥住了。史蒂夫这番疯狂的异想天开，到头来究竟会引出什么局面，他没有停下来细问自己——他只满足于这样一种感觉：眼下的直接结果是，至少在短短一阵子里，儿子将归他独自享有，远离那些往日钳制他、冻僵他天然感情的冰冷环境。
他竭力不去多想露丝。近来他想她想得太多，心里好不受用。自打雷雨天分手那天起，「自己已经失去她了」这个念头便无休无止地刺痛着他。他曾试图告诉自己：既然两人的爱情分明已经死了，那么分开便是他们所能采取的最好办法；可他骗不了自己，怎么也信不进这一套。
就她而言，这或许是真的——必定是真的，不然那天下午她怎会放他走？——可就他自己而言，这场分离让他明白：他爱她一如既往，甚至比以往更深。别离涤净了他那股闷怒——不久之前，那股闷怒还盘踞在他心里。回首前尘，他惊异自己居然曾有一时半刻相信过自己不再爱她。
此刻，他行驶在空荡荡的乡间道路上，强迫自己尽量只想着儿子。一想到儿子，他眼前便蒙起一层雾。从前的他是多棒的一个小子啊！往昔他们爷俩曾有过多少快活日子！可这一想又把心思引回露丝身上，于是他毅然把心思重新拨向未来。

===Original===

He chuckled silently as he thought of Steve. Of all the mad things to do! What had made him think of it? How had such a wild scheme ever entered his head? This, he supposed, was what Steve called punching instead of sparring. But he had never given him credit for the imagination that could conceive a punch of this magnitude.
And how had he carried it out? He could hardly have broken into the house. Yet that seemed the only way in which it could have been done.
From Steve his thoughts returned to William Bannister. He smiled again. What a time they would have﻿—while it lasted! The worst of it was, it could not last long. Tomorrow, he supposed, he would have to take the child back to his home. He could not be a party to this kidnapping raid for any length of time. This must be looked on as a brief holiday, not as a permanent relief.
That was the only flaw in his happiness as he stopped the car at the door of the shack, for by now he had succeeded at last in thrusting the image of Ruth from his mind.

===Chinese===

一想到史蒂夫，他便暗自窃笑。这真是天字第一号的疯事！他怎么会想起这一招？这么个异想天开的计策，究竟是怎么钻进他脑袋的？他琢磨，这大概就是史蒂夫所说的「打实拳，不打虚招」了。可他从来没给过史蒂夫这样的信用：他竟有能构想出这般大手笔一击的想象力。
再说，他是怎么把这事办成的呢？总不至于硬闯进那座宅子吧。可除此之外，似乎又别无他途。
想到史蒂夫，思绪又回到威廉·班尼斯特身上。他再次笑了。趁它还延续着，这段日子该有多美！糟糕的是，它长不了。他估摸着，明天他就得把孩子送回他家里去了。这场绑票奇袭，他可不能同流太久。这只能看作一个短暂的假日，而不是一劳永逸的解脱。
他把车停在小木屋门口，此刻他这份幸福里唯一的瑕疵便在于此，因为到这时，他总算成功地把露丝的影子从心头撵了出去。

===Original===

There was a light in the ground-floor window. He raised his head and shouted:
“Steve!”
The door opened.
“Hello, Kirk. That you? Come along in. You’re just in time for the main performance.”
He caught sight of Mamie standing beside Kirk.

===Chinese===

底层窗户里透着灯光。他仰起头喊道：
「史蒂夫！」
门开了。
「嘿，柯克。是你吗？进来吧。你来得正巧，正戏马上开演。」
他一眼瞥见柯克身旁站着的玛米。

===Original===

“Who’s that?” he cried. For a moment he thought it was Ruth, and his honest heart leaped at the thought that his scheme had worked already and brought Kirk and her together again.
“It’s me, Steve,” said Mamie in her small voice. And Steve, as he heard it, was seized with the first real qualm he had had since he had embarked upon his great adventure.
As Kirk had endeavoured temporarily to forget Ruth, so had he tried not to think of Mamie. It was the only thing he was ashamed of in the whole affair, the shock he must have given her.
“Hello, Mamie,” he said sheepishly, and paused. Words did not come readily to him.
Mamie entered the house without speaking. It seemed to Steve that invective would have been better than this ominous silence. He looked ruefully at her retreating back and turned to greet Kirk.

===Chinese===

「那是谁？」他叫道。有一瞬他以为是露丝，想到自己的妙计已然奏效、把柯克和她重新撮合到了一处，他这颗老实人的心不由得狂跳起来。
「是我，史蒂夫。」玛米细声细气地说。史蒂夫一听这嗓音，自打踏上伟大冒险的征途以来，心头第一次实实在在地打了个突。
正如柯克竭力暂时忘掉露丝一样，他也一直竭力不去想玛米。这是他在整桩事里唯一感到愧疚的地方——自己必定让她大大受了一场惊吓。
「你好啊，玛米。」他讪讪地说，随即语塞。要他利利索索地开口，可不是件容易事。
玛米一言不发地进了屋。在史蒂夫看来，挨一顿臭骂反倒比这不祥的沉默来得好受。他怏怏地望着她远去的背影，转过身来招呼柯克。

===Original===

“You’re mighty late,” he said.
“I only got your telegram toward the end of the afternoon. I had been away all day. I came here as fast as I could hit it up directly I read it. We had a blowout, and that delayed us.”
Steve ventured a question.
“Say, Kirk, why ‘us,’ while we’re talking of it? How does Mamie come to be here?”
“She insisted on coming. It seems that everybody in the house was away today, so she tells me, so she came round to me with your note.”

===Chinese===

「你们可真够晚的。」他说。
「你的电报，我挨到下午快收尾才收到。我出去了整整一天。一读完，我就一路玩儿命地往这儿赶。半道上车胎放了炮，又耽搁了一阵。」
史蒂夫壮着胆子问了一句。
「我说柯克，趁这会儿正说到这茬儿——怎么个『咱们』法？玛米是怎么到这儿来的？」
「她非要跟来不可。照她说，今天宅子里的人都外出了，于是她就拿着你的条子找上了我。」

===Original===

“I guess this has put me in pretty bad with Mamie,” observed Steve regretfully. “Has she been knocking me on the trip?”
“Not a word.”
Steve brightened, but became subdued again next moment.
“I guess she’s just saving it,” he said resignedly.
“Steve, what made you do it?”

===Chinese===

「我琢磨着，这一下我在玛米跟前可栽透了。」史蒂夫懊丧地说。「这一路上她数落我没有？」
「一个字也没数落。」
史蒂夫一听亮堂了，可转眼又蔫了下去。
「我琢磨，她这是攒着回头一块儿算呢。」他听天由命地说。
「史蒂夫，你干吗要来这么一手？」

===Original===

“Oh, I reckoned you could do with having the kid to yourself for a spell,” said Steve awkwardly.
“You’re all right, Steve. But how did you manage it? I shouldn’t have thought it possible.”
“Oh, it wasn’t so hard, that part. I just hid in the house, and﻿—but say, let’s forget it; it makes me feel kind of mean, somehow. It seems to me I may have lost Mamie her job. It’s mighty hard to do the right thing by everyone in this world, ain’t it? Come along in and see the kid. He’s great. Are you feeling ready for supper? Him and me was just going to start.”

===Chinese===

「哦，我寻思，让你自个儿跟孩子过上一阵，正合你的心意。」史蒂夫讪讪地说。
「你真够朋友，史蒂夫。可你是怎么弄成的？我原以为这根本办不到。」
「哦，那一步倒不难。我就是藏在宅子里，然后——哎，算了，咱们别提了；不知怎么，一提我就觉得自己有点下作。我看我这下子兴许害得玛米丢了饭碗。这世上要想把每个人都照料周全，可真难哪，是不是？进来瞧瞧那小子吧。他棒极了。你预备好吃晚饭了吗？他跟我正要开动呢。」

===Original===

It occurred to Kirk for the first time that he was hungry.
“Have you got anything to eat, Steve?”
Steve brightened again.
“Have we?” he said. “We’ve got everything there is in Connecticut! Why, say, we’re celebrating. This is our big day. Know what’s happened? Why﻿—”

===Chinese===

柯克这才头一回觉出自己饿了。
「你这儿有吃的吗，史蒂夫？」
史蒂夫又亮堂起来。
「有没有？」他说。「康涅狄格有的东西，咱这儿是应有尽有！嘿，跟你说，咱们这正过节呢。今天是咱们的大日子。知道出什么事了吗？嘿——」

===Original===

He stopped short, as if somebody had choked him. They had gone into the sitting-room while he was speaking. The table was laid for supper. A chafing-dish stood at one end, and the remainder of the available space was filled with a collection of foods, from cold chicken to candy, which did credit to Steve’s imagination.
But it was not the sight of these that checked his flow of speech. It was the look on Mamie’s face as he caught sight of it in the lamplight. The White Hope was sitting at the table in the attitude of one who has heard the gong and is anxious to begin; while Mamie, bending over him, raised her head as the two men entered and fixed Steve with a baleful stare.
“What have you been doing to the poor mite?” she demanded fiercely, “to get his face scratched this way?”
There was no doubt about the scratch. It was a long, angry red line running from temple to chin. The White Hope, becoming conscious of the fact that the attention of the public was upon him, and diagnosing the cause, volunteered an explanation.
“Bad boy,” he said, and looked meaningly again at the candy.

===Chinese===

他陡然收住话头，像被人掐住了脖子似的。说话间他们已进了起居室。晚饭的桌子已经摆好。一端立着一只小火锅，余下的空处全让各色吃食占满了，从冷鸡肉到糖果，样样都有，足见史蒂夫的想象力功不可没。
可让他话头卡住的并非这些吃食，而是灯光下映入眼帘的玛米脸上的那副神色。「白人希望」正坐在桌旁，那架势像一个听见了开饭锣、急欲动手的人；玛米俯身照看着他，两位男士进来时她抬起头，恶狠狠地盯住了史蒂夫。
「你对这可怜的小不点儿干了什么？」她怒气冲冲地质问，「把他的脸抓成这样？」
抓痕确凿无疑。那是一道长长的、怒赤赤的红杠子，从太阳穴一直划到下巴。「白人希望」觉察到公众的注意力落在了自己身上，又诊断出了个中缘由，便主动作出解释。
「坏孩子。」他说着，又意味深长地瞧了瞧糖果。

===Original===

“What does he mean by ‘bad boy’?”
“Just what he says, Mamie, honest. Gee! you don’t think *I* done it, do you?”
“Have you been letting the precious lamb *fight*?” cried Mamie, her eyes two circles of blue indignation.
Steve’s enthusiasm overcame his sense of guilt. He uttered a whoop.
“*Letting* him! Gee! Listen to her! Why, say, that kid don’t have to be let! He’s a scrapper from Swatville-on-the-Bingle. Honest! That’s what all this food is about. We’re celebrating. This is a little supper given in his honour by a few of his admirers and backers, meaning me. Why, say, Kirk, that kid of yours is just the greatest thing that ever happened. Get that chafing-dish going and I’ll tell you all about it.”

===Chinese===

「他说的『坏孩子』是什么意思？」
「就是他说的那个意思，玛米，不骗你。天哪！你不会以为是*我*干的吧？」
「你一直让这宝贝小羊羔*打架*？」玛米嚷道，两只蓝眼睛瞪成了两圈义愤。
史蒂夫的兴头压倒了他的负罪感。他嗷地喊了一嗓子。
「*让*他打架？天哪！听听她这话！嘿，跟你说，那小子哪用得着人让！他是宾格尔河畔斯沃特维尔（Swatville-on-the-Bingle）来的打架好手。不骗你！这一桌子吃食就是为这个。咱们在庆祝呢。这是他的几位崇拜者兼后台老板——也就是鄙人——为他设的小小晚宴。嘿，我说柯克，你家那小子简直是开天辟地以来最了不起的一桩事。把火锅点上，我这就把原委一五一十说给你听。」

===Original===

“How did he come by that scratch?” said Mamie, coldly sticking to her point.
“I’ll tell you quick enough. But let’s start in on the eats first. You wouldn’t keep a coming champ waiting for his grub, would you? Look how he’s lamping that candy.”
“Were you going to let the poor mite stuff himself with candy, Steve Dingle?”
“Sure. Whatever he says goes. He owns the joint after this afternoon.”
Mamie swiftly removed the unwholesome delicacy.

===Chinese===

「那抓痕到底是怎么来的？」玛米说，冷冷地咬住她的题目不放。
「这就告诉你，快得很。可咱们先动手吃起来。你总不至于让一位未来的拳王饿着肚子等饭吧？瞧他那双眼睛，直勾勾地钉在那糖果上呢。」
「你刚才是不是打算让这可怜的小不点儿拿糖果撑破肚皮，史蒂夫·丁格尔？」
「那当然。他说什么就是什么。打今天下午起，这座宅子就归他当家做主了。」
玛米眼疾手快，把这份有害无益的美味撤走了。

===Original===

“The idea!”
Kirk was busying himself with the chafing-dish.
“What have you got in here, Steve?”
“Lobster, colonel. I had to do thirty miles to get it, too.”
Mamie looked at him fixedly.
“Were you going to feed lobster to this child?” she asked with ominous calm. “Were you intending to put him to bed full of broiled lobster and marshmallows?”

===Chinese===

「亏你想得出！」
柯克正忙着摆弄火锅。
「你这锅里煮的什么，史蒂夫？」
「龙虾，上校。为了弄到它，我还足足跑了三十英里的路呢。」
玛米直勾勾地盯着他。
「你刚才是不是打算拿龙虾喂这孩子？」她问，平静得令人心惊。「你是不是存心让他揣着一肚子烤龙虾和果汁软糖上床？」

===Original===

“Nix on the rough stuff, Mamie,” pleaded the embarrassed pugilist. “How was I to know what kids feed on? And maybe he would have passed up the lobster at that and stuck to the sardines.”
“Sardines!”
“Ain’t kids allowed sardines?” said Steve anxiously. “The guy at the store told me they were wholesome and nourishing. It looked to me as if that ought to hit young Fitzsimmons about right. What’s the matter with them?”
“A little bread-and-milk is all that he ever has before he goes to bed.”
Steve detected a flaw in this and hastened to make his point.

===Chinese===

「这种狠话可饶了我吧，玛米。」窘迫的拳师央求道。「小孩子家吃什么，我哪儿知道？话说回来，没准儿他还看不上龙虾，专攻沙丁鱼呢。」
「沙丁鱼！」
「小孩子不许吃沙丁鱼吗？」史蒂夫急切地问。「铺子里那家伙跟我说，这玩意儿又卫生又滋养。依我看，正对小菲茨西蒙斯（young Fitzsimmons）的脾胃。它们有什么不妥？」
「他上床之前，从来只吃一点点牛奶泡面包。」
史蒂夫从中挑出了一个漏洞，忙不迭地亮出他的道理。

===Original===

“Sure,” he said, “but he don’t win the bantamweight champeenship of Connecticut every night.”
“Is that what he’s done today, Steve?” asked Kirk.
“It certainly is. Ain’t I telling you?”
“That’s the trouble. You’re not. You and Mamie seem to be having a discussion about the nourishing properties of sardines and lobster. What has been happening this afternoon?”
“Bad boy,” remarked William Bannister with his mouth full.

===Chinese===

「没错，」他说，「可他也不是天天晚上都能赢下康涅狄格雏量级拳王头衔的呀。」
「他今天干成的就是这个吗，史蒂夫？」柯克问。
「可不是嘛。我不是正要告诉你吗？」
「毛病就出在这儿。你并没有在讲。你和玛米倒像是在开一场沙丁鱼与龙虾滋养价值的讨论会。今天下午到底出了什么事？」
「坏孩子。」威廉·班尼斯特满嘴食物地发表意见。

===Original===

“That’s right,” said Steve. “That’s it in a nutshell. Say, it was this way. It seemed to me that, having no kid of his own age to play around with, his nibs was apt to get lonesome, so I asked about and found that there was a guy of the name of Whiting living near here who had a kid of the same age or thereabouts. Maybe you remember him? He used to fight at the featherweight limit some time back. Called himself Young O’Brien. He was a pretty good scrapper in his time, and now he’s up here looking after some gent’s prize dogs.
“Well, I goes to him and borrows his kid. He’s a scrappy sort of kid at that and weighs ten pounds more than his nibs; but I reckoned he’d have to do, and I thought I could stay around and part ’em if they got to mixing it.”
Mamie uttered an indignant exclamation, but Kirk’s eyes were gleaming proudly.
“Well?” he said.
Steve swallowed lobster and resumed.

===Chinese===

「正是。」史蒂夫说。「一句话就这么回事。听着，事情是这样的。我寻思，这孩子身边没有同龄的玩伴，咱们这位小爷难免寂寞，于是我四处打听，发现附近住着个姓怀廷（Whiting）的家伙，有个岁数相仿的娃。也许你还记得他？前些年他一直在羽量级的限重上打拳，自号「小奥布赖恩」（Young O’Brien）。当年也算把好手，如今在这边替一位老爷子照看宝贝狗。
「于是我就上他家，把他的娃借来了。那娃也是个好斗的种，还比咱们小爷重十磅；可我寻思凑合着用吧，我还可以守在旁边，他们要是真打上了，我好把他们拆开。」
玛米愤愤地惊呼了一声，柯克的眼睛却骄傲地发亮。
「后来呢？」他说。
史蒂夫吞下一口龙虾，接着讲。

===Original===

“Well, you know how it is. You meet a guy who’s been in the same line of business as yourself and you find you’ve got a heap to talk about. I’d never happened across the gink Whiting, but I knew of him, and, of course, he’d heard of me, and we got to discussing things. I seen him lose on a foul to Tommy King in the eighteenth round out in Los Angeles, and that kept us busy talking, him having it that he hadn’t gone within a mile of fouling Tommy and me saying I’d been in a ring-seat and had the goods on him same as if I’d taken a snapshot. Well, we was both getting pretty hot under the collar about it when suddenly there’s the blazes of a noise behind us, and there’s the two kids scrapping all over the lot. The Whiting kid had started it, mind you, and him ten pounds heavier than Bill, and tough, too.”
The White Hope confirmed this.
“Bad boy,” he remarked, and with a deep breath resumed excavating work on a grapefruit.
“Well, I was just making a jump to separate them when this Whiting gook says, ‘Betcha a dollar my kid wins!’ and before I knew what I was doing I’d taken him. It wasn’t that that stopped me, though. It was his saying that his kid took after his dad and could eat up anything of his own age in America. Well, darn it, could I take that from a slob of a mixed-ale scrapper when it was handed out at the finest kid that ever came from New York?”
“Of course not,” said Kirk indignantly, and even Mamie forbore to criticize. She bent over the White Hope and gave his grapefruit-stained cheek a kiss.

===Chinese===

「后来，你也知道那股劲儿。碰上一位跟你同行干过的人，你就发现有的是可谈的。怀廷那家伙我从没照过面，可我久闻其名，他自然也听说过我，我们就聊开了。我看过他在洛杉矶（Los Angeles）打汤米·金（Tommy King），第十八回合因犯规被判负，就这一桩事够我们谈上半天：他一口咬定自己离犯规汤米还差着一英里远，我说我那时坐的是拳台边的座席，手里攥着他的真凭实据，就跟拍了快照一样。我们俩正越说火气越旺，忽然背后炸开一声天崩地裂的响动，两个娃已经满院子扭打成一团了。你可听清楚了，是那怀廷家的娃先动的手，他还比比尔重十磅，皮实着呢。」
「白人希望」对此予以证实。
「坏孩子。」他评论道，随后深吸一口气，继续对一只葡萄柚展开挖掘工程。
「当时我正要一个箭步上去拆开他们，那怀廷家伙来了一句：『赌一块钱我家娃赢！』还没等明白过来自己在干什么，我已经把注接下了。不过，让我止步的还不是这个。是他接着吹嘘，说他家娃随他爹，全美国同龄的娃没一个不被他收拾的。嘿，真见鬼，这话是当着纽约有史以来最出挑的孩子撂下的，说话的还是个喝混合啤酒的三流拳手，我能咽下这口气吗？」
「当然咽不下。」柯克愤然道，连玛米也暂且按下了指责。她俯下身，在「白人希望」沾着葡萄柚汁的脸颊上亲了一口。

===Original===

“Well, I *should* say not!” cried Steve. “I just hollered to his nibs, ‘Soak it to him, kid! for the honour of No. 99’; and, believe me, the young bearcat sort of gathered himself together, winked at me, and began to hammer the stuffing out of the scrappy kid. Say, there wasn’t no sterilized stuff about his work. You were a regular germ, all right, weren’t you squire?”
“Germ,” agreed the White Hope. He spoke drowsily.
“Gee!” Steve resumed his saga in a whirl of enthusiasm. “Gee! if they’re right to start with, if they’re born right, if they’ve got the grit in them, you can’t sterilize it out of ’em if you use up half the germ-killer in the country. From the way that kid acted you’d have thought he’d been spending the last year in a training-camp. The other kid rolled him over, but he come up again as if that was just the sort of stuff he liked, and pretty soon I see that he’s uncovered a yellow streak in the Whiting kid as big as a barn door. You were on it, weren’t you, colonel?”
But the White Hope had no remarks to offer this time. His head had fallen forward and was resting peacefully in his grapefruit.
“He’s asleep,” said Mamie.

===Chinese===

「那还用说，*当然*咽不下！」史蒂夫嚷道。「我冲咱们小爷大喊：『揍他，小子！为 99 号的荣誉！』；跟你说，那头小猛崽把身子一攒，冲我眨了眨眼，就动手把那好斗的娃打得没了魂儿。嘿，他那套拳路里可没有一丁点儿消过毒的成分。你是个不折不扣的细菌，没错吧，小乡绅？」
「细菌。」「白人希望」表示赞同。他说得迷迷糊糊。
「天哪！」史蒂夫满怀热忱，重新续起他的史诗。「天哪！只要他们底子是好的，只要他们生得端正，只要骨子里有那股狠劲儿，你就是把全国一半的杀菌剂都用上，也别想把它消毒掉。瞧那小子打出来的做派，你会以为他过去一年是在训练营里熬出来的。那娃把他掀翻在地，他爬起来那股劲儿，倒好像正中下怀，不多时我就看出，他挖出了怀廷家娃身上一条谷仓大门那么宽的怯懦黄筋。你当时在场吧，上校？」
可这一回「白人希望」没有高见要发表。他的脑袋已经向前栽了下去，安安稳稳地歇在葡萄柚里了。
「他睡着了。」玛米说。

===Original===

She picked him up gently and carried him out.
“He’s a champeen at that too,” said Steve. “I had to pull him out of the hay this morning. Well, I guess he’s earned it. He’s had a busy day.”
“What happened then, Steve?”
“Why, after that there wasn’t a thing to it. Whiting, poor simp, couldn’t see it. ‘Betcha ten dollars my kid wins,’ he hollers. ‘He’s got him going.’ ‘Take you,’ I shouts; and at that moment the scrappy kid sees it’s all over, so he does the old business of fouling, same as his pop done when he fought Tommy King. It’s in the blood, I guess. He takes and scratches poor Bill on the cheek.”
“That was enough for me. I jumps in. ‘All over,’ I says. ‘My kid wins on a foul.’ ‘Foul nothing,’ says Whiting. ‘It was an accident, and you lose because you jumped into the fight, same as Connie McVey did when Corbett fought Sharkey. Think you can get away with it, pulling that old-time stuff?’ I didn’t trouble to argue with him. ‘Oh,’ I says, ‘is that it? Say, just take a slant at your man. If you don’t stop him quick he’ll be in Texas.’

===Chinese===

她轻轻抱起他，抱了出去。
「论睡觉他也是位冠军。」史蒂夫说。「今儿早上还是我把他从被窝里薅出来的呢。唔，我看这也是他挣来的。他这一天够忙的。」
「后来又怎么样了，史蒂夫？」
「嘿，打那以后就没什么可说的了。怀廷那可怜的呆子，就是看不清形势。『赌十美元我家娃赢，』他嚷嚷。『他已经把他打蔫了。』『跟你赌，』我喊道；就在这节骨眼上，那好斗的娃眼看大势已去，就使出犯规的老一套，跟他爹当年打汤米·金那回一模一样。我琢磨这是胎里带的。他伸手就照可怜的比尔腮帮子上抓了一把。」
「这就够我受的了。我一个箭步跳进去。『完事了，』我说。『我家娃凭对方犯规获胜。』『犯规，没影儿的事，』怀廷说。『那是失手。你输了，是因为你插手了打架，就跟科贝特（Corbett）打沙基（Sharkey）那回康尼·麦克维（Connie McVey）插手是一路货。搬出这种老古董，你以为就能蒙混过关？』我犯不着跟他费口舌。『哦，』我说，『是这么回事？我说，你回头瞅一眼你家那位。你要是不赶紧拦住，他都要跑到得克萨斯（Texas）了。』

===Original===

“For the scrappy kid was beating it while the going was good and was half a mile away, running hard. Well, that was enough even for the Whiting guy. ‘I guess we’ll call it a draw,’ he says, ‘and all bets off.’ I just looks at him and says, quite civil and polite: ‘You darned half-baked slob of a roughhouse scrapper,’ I says, ‘it ain’t a draw or anything like it. My kid wins, and I’ll trouble you now to proceed to cash in with the dough, or else I’m liable to start something.’ So he paid up, and I took the White Hope indoors and give him a wash and brush-up, and we cranks up the bubble and hikes off to the town and spends the money on getting food for the celebration supper. And what’s over I slips into the kid’s pocket and says: ‘That’s your first winner’s end, kid, and you’ve earned it.’ ”
Steve paused and filled his glass.
“I’m on the wagon as a general thing nowadays,” he said; “but I reckon this an occasion. Right here is where we drink his health.”
And, overcome by his emotion, he burst into discordant song.
“Fo‑or he’s a jolly good fellow,” bellowed Steve. “For he’s a jolly good fellow. For he’s﻿—”

===Chinese===

「原来那好斗的娃正趁脚底下方便撒腿开溜，一路狂奔，已经跑出半英里地了。唔，这一来，连怀廷那家伙都没得可说了。『我看这就算平局，』他说，『赌注一律作废。』我直瞪着他说——说得客客气气、文质彬彬：『你这个半生不熟的浑球、乱打王八拳的莽汉，』我说，『这算不得平局，也差着十万八千里呢。我家娃赢了，现在有劳你把票子掏出来，不然我保不齐要起点什么事儿。』于是他付了钱，我把「白人希望」领进屋，给他洗刷拾掇了一番，我们摇着曲柄发动汽车，一路开进城去，把这笔钱花在置办庆祝晚饭的吃食上。剩下的我偷偷塞进那小子的口袋，说：『这是你头一份赢拳的进项，小子，是你自个儿挣来的。』」
史蒂夫顿了顿，斟满自己的杯子。
「照眼下的常规，我平日是滴酒不沾的，」他说；「可我掂量着今天算个日子。就趁现在，咱们为他的健康干一杯。」
情绪一涌上来，他便扯开嗓子唱起歌来，荒腔走板。
「因——为他是个大好人，」史蒂夫吼道。「因为他是个大好人。因为他——」

===Original===

There was a sound of quick footsteps outside, and Mamie entered the room like a small whirlwind.
“Be quiet!” she cried. “Do you want to wake him?”
“Wake him?” said Steve. “You can’t wake that kid with dynamite.”
He raised his glass.

===Chinese===

门外响起一阵急促的脚步声，玛米像个小旋风似的冲进屋来。
「安静点！」她喊道。「你想把他吵醒吗？」
「吵醒他？」史蒂夫说。「拿炸药也炸不醒那小子。」
他举起了杯子。

===Original===

“Ladeez’n gentlemen, the boy wonder! Here’s to him! The bantamweight champeen of Connecticut. The Sixty-First Street Cyclone! The kid they couldn’t sterilize! The White Hope!”
“The White Hope!” echoed Kirk.
“Fo‑or he’s a jolly good fellow﻿—” sang Steve.
“Be quiet!” said Mrs. Porter from the doorway, and Steve, wheeling round, caught her eye and collapsed like a pricked balloon.

===Chinese===

「女士们、先省们，少年奇才！敬他！康涅狄格雏量级拳王！第六十一街旋风！消毒消不掉的小子！「白人希望」！」
「白人希望！」柯克应声道。
「因——为他是个大好人——」史蒂夫唱道。
「安静点！」波特夫人在门口喝道。史蒂夫霍地转过身，撞上她的目光，登时像被扎了一针的气球，瘪了下去。

## IX The White Hope is Turned Down / 第九章 「白人希望」吃了闭门羹

===Original===

William Bannister Winfield was the most wonderful child. Of course, you had to have a certain amount of intelligence to see this. To the vapid and irreflective observer he was not much to look at in the early stages of his career, having a dough-like face almost entirely devoid of nose, a lacklustre eye, and the general appearance of a poached egg. His immediate circle of intimates, however, thought him a model of manly beauty; and there was the undeniable fact that he had come into the world weighing nine pounds. Take him for all in all, a lad of promise.

Kirk’s sense of being in a dream continued. His identity seemed to have undergone a change. The person he had known as Kirk Winfield had disappeared, to be succeeded by a curious individual bubbling over with an absurd pride for which it was not easy to find an outlet. Hitherto a rather reserved man, he was conscious now of a desire to accost perfect strangers in the street and inform them that he was not the ordinary person they probably imagined, but a father with an intensely unusual son at home, and if they did not believe him they could come right along and see for themselves.

The only flaw in his happiness at the moment was the fact that his circle of friends was so small. He had not missed the old brigade of the studio before, but now the humblest of them would have been welcome, provided he would have sat still and listened. Even Percy Shanklyn would have been acceptable as an audience.

Steve, excellent fellow, was always glad to listen to him on his favourite subject. He had many long talks with Steve on the question of William’s future. Steve, as the infant’s godfather, which post he had claimed and secured at an early date, had definite views on the matter.

===Chinese===

威廉·班尼斯特·温菲尔德是个再奇妙不过的孩子。当然，要看出这一点，你自己得先有几分智力。在那些头脑空泛、不知反省的旁观者眼里，他生涯初期的模样实在没什么看头：一张面团似的脸，鼻子几乎全然缺席，一双眼睛黯淡无光，通身上下一副水煮蛋的观感。然而他身边最内圈的一小撮至亲，却把他奉为男子汉美的典范；何况还有一件无可否认的事实——他来到人世时，体重足足九磅。通盘看来，是个前程远大的孩子。

柯克那种身在梦中的感觉持续不散。他的身份似乎经历了一场变革。他一向熟悉的那位柯克·温菲尔德已经消失，接替其位的是一个古怪的家伙，满腔泛滥着一种荒唐的自豪，却苦于找不到宣泄的出口。他本来是个相当矜持的人，如今却常常生出一种冲动，想在街上拦住全然陌生的人，告知对方：自己并非他们八成想象中的那种庸常之辈，而是一位父亲，家中养着一个绝非凡品的儿子；他们若是不信，大可这就跟他走一趟，亲眼去看看。

眼下他的幸福美中只有一桩不足：朋友圈子实在太小。画室那帮老伙伴，他从前倒不曾想念，可如今其中最微末的一位，只要肯安坐静听，也会大受欢迎。就连珀西·尚克林，拿他当听众也是可以将就的。

史蒂夫这位好样的，一向乐于听他讲这个最心爱的话题。关于威廉的前途，他同史蒂夫进行过多次长谈。史蒂夫身为婴儿的教父——这一职位他早早便下手认领，并且稳稳到手——对这事自有明确的主张。

===Original===

Here, held Steve, was the chance of a lifetime. With proper training, a baby of such obvious muscular promise might be made the greatest fighter that ever stepped into the ring. He was the real White Hope. He advised Kirk to direct William’s education on the lines which would insure his being, when the time was ripe, undisputed heavyweight champion of the world. To Steve life outside the ring was a poor affair, practically barren of prizes for the ambitious.

Mrs. Lora Delane Porter, eyeing William’s brow, of which there was plenty, he being at this time extremely short of hair, predicted a less robust and more intellectual future for him. Something more on the lines of president of some great university or ambassador at some important court struck her as his logical sphere.

Kirk’s view was that he should combine both careers and be an ambassador who took a few weeks off every now and then in order to defend his champion’s belt. In his spare time he might paint a picture or two.

Ruth hesitated between the army, the navy, the bar, and business. But everyone was agreed that William was to be something special.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫认为，此乃百年难遇的良机。只要训练得当，一个筋骨前程如此昭然的婴儿，大可造就成踏上拳台以来的头一号拳手。他才是真正的「白人希望」。他建议柯克把威廉的教育引上这样一条路线，以确保时机一到，他便成为无可争议的世界重量级冠军。在史蒂夫看来，拳台之外的人生实在寒碜，对胸怀大志者而言，那里几乎寸奖难寻。

洛拉·德莱恩·波特夫人端详着威廉的额头——额头有的是，因为他这时头发稀少得很——为他预言了一个不那么孔武、却更近文墨的未来。在她看来，他合乎逻辑的天地，更近乎执掌某所伟大学府的校长，或出使某重要宫廷的大使。

柯克的看法是两副担子一肩挑：做一名大使，隔三差五告上几个星期的假，回去卫冕他的冠军腰带。闲暇之余，不妨再画上一两幅画。

露丝则在陆军、海军、律师、经商之间踌躇难决。但众口一词的是：威廉注定要成为不同凡响的人物。

===Original===

This remarkable child had a keen sense of humour. Thus he seldom began to cry in his best vein till the small hours of the morning; and on these occasions he would almost invariably begin again after he had been officially pronounced to be asleep. His sudden grab at the hair of any adult who happened to come within reach was very droll, too.

As to his other characteristics, he was of rather an imperious nature. He liked to be waited on. He wanted what he wanted when he wanted it. The greater part of his attention being occupied at this period with the important duty of chewing his thumb, he assigned the drudgery of life to his dependants. Their duties were to see that he got up in the morning, dressed, and took his tub; and after that to hang around on the chance of general orders.

Any idea Kirk may have had of resuming his work was abandoned during these months. No model, young and breezy or white-haired and motherly, passed the studio doors. Life was far too interesting for work. The canvas which might have become *Carmen* or *A Reverie* or even *The Toreador’s Bride* lay unfinished and neglected in a corner.

It astonished Kirk to find how strong the paternal instinct was in him. In the days when he had allowed his mind to dwell upon the abstract wife he had sometimes gone a step further and conjured up the abstract baby. The result had always been to fill him with a firm conviction that the most persuasive of wild horses should not drag him from his bachelor seclusion. He had had definite ideas on babies as a class. And here he was with his world pivoting on one of them. It was curious.

===Chinese===

这个非凡的孩子幽默感极为敏锐。譬如，他很少在凌晨时分之前拿出最佳水准开嗓痛哭；而且每逢这种场合，等他被正式宣布入睡之后，几乎必定重新开嗓。此外，他冷不丁一把薅住任何恰好够得着的大人的头发，也滑稽得很。

至于其他品性，他的性子颇为专横。他喜欢被人侍候。他想要的东西，他想要的时候就得要到。这个时期，他大部分注意力都被咀嚼拇指这一重要职责占据，便把生活中的苦差一并派给了手下的依附者们。他们的职责是：确保他早晨起床、穿衣、洗盆浴；此后便守在近旁，听候总命令随时颁发。

这几个月里，柯克纵然起过重操旧业的念头，也一并打消了。再没有模特儿——不论青春爽朗的，还是白发慈祥的——跨进画室的门。生活实在有趣得过分，容不得工作。那幅本可能成为*《卡门》*、*《幻想曲》*乃至*《斗牛士的新娘》*的画布，未竟其功，被弃在角落里无人问津。

柯克惊讶地发现，自己身上做父亲的本能竟如此强劲。当年他任由心思在那位「抽象的妻子」身上流连时，有时还会更进一层，招来一个「抽象的婴孩」。其结果无一例外：他心中便坚定地认定，便是天下最善游说的野马，也休想把他从单身幽居中拖出来。他对婴孩这个阶层原有明确的见解。而如今，他的整个世界偏偏绕着其中一个打转。真是不可思议。

===Original===

The White Hope, as Steve called his godson﻿—possibly with the idea of influencing him by suggestion﻿—grew. The ailments which attacked lesser babies passed him by. He avoided croup, and even whooping-cough paid him but a flying visit hardly worth mentioning. His first tooth gave him a little trouble, but that is the sort of thing which may happen to anyone; and the spirited way in which he protested against the indignity of cutting it was proof of a high soul.

Such was the remarkableness of this child that it annoyed Kirk more and more that he should be obliged to give the exhibition of his extraordinary qualities to so small an audience. Ruth felt the same; and it was for this reason that the first overtures were made to the silent camp which contained her father and her brother Bailey.

Since that evening in the library there had come no sign from the house on Fifth Avenue that its inmates were aware of her existence. Life had been too full till now to make this a cause of trouble to her; but with William Bannister becoming every day more amazing the desire came to her to try and heal the breach. Her father had so ordered his life in his relation to his children that Ruth’s affection was not so deep as it might have been; but, after all, he was William Bannister’s grandfather, and, as such, entitled to consideration.

It was these reflections that led to Steve’s state visit to John Bannister﻿—probably the greatest fiasco on record.

===Chinese===

「白人希望」——史蒂夫如此称呼他的教子，或许存了借暗示潜移默化之心——渐渐长大了。侵袭寻常婴儿的种种毛病，都与他擦身而过。哮吼他躲过了，连百日咳也只对他作了一次匆匆的造访，不值一提。第一颗牙给他添了点小麻烦，可这种事谁都可能摊上；何况他抗议出牙这份屈辱时那股烈性劲儿，正是一颗高贵灵魂的明证。

这孩子的卓绝竟至于此，以致柯克越来越恼火：如此不凡的品质，竟不得不展示给这么一小撮观众。露丝同感；正是为此，才有了向那座沉默的阵营——里头驻扎着她的父亲和她的哥哥贝利——的第一次示好。

自书房里的那个夜晚以来，第五大道那所房子再无半点迹象表明屋里的人知道世上有她这个人。直到如今，日子一直太满，这还够不上她的一桩心事；可眼见威廉·班尼斯特一天比一天惊人，她起了念头，想试着弥合这道裂痕。她父亲安排自己与子女关系的那套方式，早注定了露丝对他的感情不及本可能的深厚；但不管怎样，他总是威廉·班尼斯特的外祖父，仅凭这一条，便有权受到体恤。

正是这些思量，引出了史蒂夫对约翰·班尼斯特的那次国事访问——大约是有史以来最惨重的一次惨败。

===Original===

Steve had been selected for the feat on the strength of his having the right of entry to the Fifth Avenue house, for John Bannister was still obeying his doctor’s orders and taking his daily spell of exercise with the pugilist﻿—and Steve bungled it hopelessly.

His task was not a simple one. He was instructed to employ tact, to hint rather than to speak, to say nothing to convey the impression that Ruth in any way regretted the step she had taken, to give the idea that it was a matter of complete indifference to her whether she ever saw her father again or not, yet at the same time to make it quite clear that she was very anxious to see him as soon as possible.

William Bannister, grown to maturity and upholding the interests of his country as ambassador at some important court, might have jibbed at the mission.

William Bannister was to accompany Steve and be produced dramatically to support verbal arguments. It seemed to Ruth that for her father to resist William when he saw him was an impossibility. William’s position was that of the ace of trumps in the cards which Steve was to play.

===Chinese===

这项壮举之所以选中史蒂夫，凭的是他有出入第五大道宅邸的权利，因为约翰·班尼斯特仍遵着医嘱，每天同这位拳手锻炼一阵——结果史蒂夫把事情办得一塌糊涂，不可救药。

他的任务并不简单。他领到的指示是：要运用圆滑，只可暗示、不可明说；万不可透露出露丝对自己所走的这一步有半分悔意的印象；要让人觉得，此生再见不见得到父亲，于她完全是件无所谓的事；可与此同时，又必须让人看得清清楚楚：她巴不得尽早见到他。

威廉·班尼斯特若已长大成人，正以某重要宫廷大使的身份捍卫着国家利益，接下这桩使命时说不定还要踌躇一下呢。

威廉·班尼斯特则要随史蒂夫同行，到时候戏剧性地亮将出来，为口头论证助威。在露丝看来，她父亲见了威廉还能抗拒，是万万不可能的事。威廉在这副牌里的地位，正是史蒂夫将要打出的那张王牌爱司。

===Original===

Steve made a few objections. His chief argument against taking up the post assigned to him was that he was a roughneck, and that the job in question was one which no roughneck, however gifted in the matter of left hooks, could hope to carry through with real success. But he yielded to pressure, and the expedition set out.

William Bannister at this time was at an age when he was beginning to talk a little and walk a little and take a great interest in things. His walking was a bit amateurish, and his speech rather hard to follow unless you had the key to it. But nobody could have denied that his walk, though staggery, was a genuine walk, and his speech, though limited, genuine speech, within the meaning of the act.

He made no objections to the expedition. On being told that he was going to see his grandpa he nodded curtly and said: “Gwa-wah,” after his custom. For, as a conversationalist, perhaps the best description of him is to say that he tried hard. He rarely paused for a word. When in difficulties he said something; he did not seek refuge in silence. That the something was not always immediately intelligible was the fault of his audience for not listening more carefully.

Perhaps the real mistake of the expedition was the nature of its baggage. William Bannister had stood out for being allowed to take with him his wheelbarrow, his box of bricks, and his particular favourite, the dying pig, which you blew out and then allowed to collapse with a pleasing noise. These properties had struck his parents as excessive, but he was firm; and when he gave signs of being determined to fight it out on these lines if it took all the summer, they gave in.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫提了几条异议。他反对出任这一职位的主要论据是：他是个粗人；而眼下这桩差事，凡是粗人——不论左钩拳上的天分有多高——都休想指望办得真正圆满。但他终究屈从于压力，远征队开拔了。

威廉·班尼斯特这时正到了这样一个年纪：开始会讲一点话、会走一点路，并对万事万物怀着浓厚的兴趣。他走路还带几分业余味道，他的言语若没有解码的钥匙也颇难听懂。但谁都无法否认：他的步态虽摇摇晃晃，却是如假包换的走路；他的言语虽为数有限，却是货真价实的言语——在法令条文的意义上。

这次远征真正的失策，或许在于行装的性质。威廉·班尼斯特坚持要求获准随身带上他的独轮手推车、他的积木盒，还有他的心头至好——那只垂死猪：先把它吹胀，再任它瘪塌下去，发出一声悦耳的声响。这些行头在他父母看来未免过了头，可他毫不动摇；及至他摆出架势，表明哪怕搭上整个夏天也要在这条战线上周旋到底，他们便让步了。

把威廉偷运进外祖父的宅子，史蒂夫毫无困难。这孩子在楼下的用人堆里极得人缘。他的铁杆盟友，是那位英国管家凯格斯。

===Original===

Steve had no difficulty in smuggling William into his grandfather’s house. He was a great favourite below stairs there. His great ally was the English butler, Keggs.

Keggs was a stout, dignified, pigeon-toed old sinner, who cast off the butler when not on duty and displayed himself as something of a rounder. He was a man of many parts. It was his chief relaxation to look in at Broadway hotels while some big fight was in progress out West to watch the ticker and assure himself that the man he had backed with a portion of the loot which he had accumulated in the form of tips was doing justice to his judgment, for in private Keggs was essentially the sport.

It was this that so endeared Steve to him. A few years ago Keggs had won considerable sums by backing Steve, and the latter was always given to understand that, as far as the lower regions of it were concerned, the house on Fifth Avenue was open to him at all hours.

Today he greeted Steve with enthusiasm and suggested a cigar in the pantry before the latter should proceed to his work.

===Chinese===

凯格斯是个矮胖、体面、内八字脚的老滑头，一下班便把管家这层皮一撂，露出几分吃喝玩乐的老做派。他是个多面手。每逢西部有大赛进行，他最爱到百老汇的各家旅馆里坐坐，盯着行情收报机，好确认自己用小费积攒下的那笔外财的一部分所押的拳手没有辜负自己的眼力——因为私底下的凯格斯，骨子里是个地道的老赌客。

也正是这一点，使史蒂夫在他心里如此可亲。几年前，凯格斯押史蒂夫的注赢过相当可观的钱数，于是后者一向得到这样的体谅：只要不出楼下这片地界，第五大道这座宅门随时为他敞开。

这一天，他热络地迎接史蒂夫，并提议趁后者着手办事之前，先到配膳室抽上一支雪茄。

「他还没得空见您呢，丁格尔先生。他正在里头过目些文件——哎呀我的老天，这是哪位呀？」


===Original===

“He ain’t ready for you yet, Mr. Dingle. He’s lookin’ over some papers in﻿—for goodness’ sake, who’s this?”

He had caught sight of William Bannister, who having wriggled free of Steve, was being made much of by the maids.

“The kid,” said Steve briefly.

“Not﻿—”

Steve nodded.

“Sure. His grandson.”

===Chinese===

他瞧见了威廉·班尼斯特——小家伙已从史蒂夫手里挣脱，正被一帮女仆你一言我一语地宠着。

「这小家伙。」史蒂夫三言两语地说。

「该不会是——」

史蒂夫点了点头。

「没错。他的外孙。」

凯格斯的神色愈发郑重。

===Original===

Keggs’ solemnity increased.

“You aren’t going to take him upstairs with you?”

“Surest thing you know. That’s why I brought him.”

“Don’t you do it, Mr. Dingle. ’E’s in an awful temper this morning﻿—he gets worse and worse﻿—he’ll fire you as soon as look at you.”

“Can’t be helped. I’ve got me instructions.”

“You always were game,” said Keggs admiringly. “I used to see that quick enough before you retired from active work. Well, good luck to you, Mr. Dingle.”

===Chinese===

「您总不至于要带他上楼去吧？」

「那是板上钉钉的事。我就是为这才带他来的。」

「您可别这么干，丁格尔先生。他今儿一早火气大得吓人——一天凶过一天——他瞧您一眼就能把您轰出门去。」

「没法子。我领了指令。」

「您向来有种，」凯格斯叹服地说。「您还没退出现役那阵子，我一眼就瞧得出。好吧，祝您走运，丁格尔先生。」

史蒂夫抱起威廉·班尼斯特，抄齐独轮车、积木盒和垂死猪，向健身房进发。

===Original===

Steve gathered up William Bannister, the wheelbarrow, the box of bricks, and the dying pig and made his way to the gymnasium.

The worst of these prearranged scenes is that they never happen just as one figured them in one’s mind. Steve had expected to have to wait a few minutes in the gymnasium, then there would be a step outside and the old man would enter. The beauty of this, to Steve’s mind, was that he himself would be “discovered,” as the stage term is; the onus of entering and opening the conversation would be on Mr. Bannister. And, as everybody who has ever had an awkward interview knows, this makes all the difference.

But the minutes passed, and still no grandfather. The nervousness which he had with difficulty expelled began to return to Steve. This was exactly like having to wait in the ring while one’s opponent tried to get one’s goat by dawdling in the dressing room.

An attempt to relieve himself by punching the ball was a dismal failure. At the first bang of the leather against the wood William Bannister, who had been working in a preoccupied way at the dying pig, threw his head back and howled, and would not be comforted till Steve took out the rope and skipped before him, much as dancers used to dance before oriental monarchs in the old days.

Steve was just saying to himself for the fiftieth time that he was a fool to have come, when Keggs arrived with the news that Mr. Bannister was too busy to take his usual exercise this morning and that Steve was at liberty to go.

===Chinese===

这类预先编排好的场面，最糟的一点便是：它们从来不会照你心里预演的那样发生。史蒂夫原以为得在健身房里候上几分钟，随后门外脚步声起，老爷子应声而入。在史蒂夫看来，妙就妙在他自己将处于「被看见」的位置——这是舞台术语；进门与开腔的重担便全压在班尼斯特先生身上。而凡是有过尴尬会面经历的人都知道，这一来一去，差别可就大了。

可是一分钟又一分钟过去，外祖父仍无踪影。他好不容易驱逐出去的紧张，又开始回到史蒂夫身上。这情形，活脱脱像站在拳台里干等，而对手赖在更衣室里磨磨蹭蹭，存心撩拨你的火气。

他想打打吊球排遣排遣，结果一败涂地。皮球头一回砰地撞上木板，方才还在专心致志伺候垂死猪的威廉·班尼斯特便仰起头嚎啕大哭，怎么哄也不见好，直到史蒂夫掏出跳绳，在他面前跳将起来——那做派，颇有几分旧日在东方君主驾前献舞的舞者的意思。

史蒂夫正第五十遍在心里念叨自己真是个傻瓜不该来，凯格斯到了，捎来消息说：班尼斯特先生今早事务太忙，不能照常锻炼，史蒂夫尽可自便离去。

史蒂夫到底没走，这一点足为他人品作证。他巴不得就此撤退，因为老爷子是天下少数几个能令他生出近似恐惧之感的人物。可任务未成，他不能就这么乖乖回画室去。

===Original===

It speaks well for Steve’s character that he did not go. He would have given much to retire, for the old man was one of the few people who inspired in him anything resembling fear. But he could not return tamely to the studio with his mission unaccomplished.

“Say, ask him if he can see me for a minute. Say it is important.”

Keggs’ eye rested on William Bannister, and he shook his head.

“I shouldn’t, Mr. Dingle. Really I shouldn’t. You don’t know what an ugly mood he’s in. Something’s been worrying him. It’s what you might call courting disaster.”

“Gee! Do you think I *want* to do it? I’ve just got to. That’s all there is to it.”

A few moments later Keggs returned with the news that Mr. Bannister would see Dingle in the library.

===Chinese===

「喂，去问问他，能不能见我一分钟。就说是要紧事。」

凯格斯的目光落在威廉·班尼斯特身上，摇了摇头。

「换了我才不呢，丁格尔先生。真的才不呢。您不知道他这阵子心情有多坏。一直有桩什么事在烦他。这简直是人们常说的自寻祸事。」

「天哪！你当我*想*去？我是非去不可。就这么回事。」

片刻之后，凯格斯回来了，说班尼斯特先生愿在书房见丁格尔。

「走吧，小家伙，」史蒂夫说。「把超载的行李递给我拿着，咱们这就动身。」

===Original===

“Come along, kid,” said Steve. “Gimme hold of the excess baggage, and let’s get a move on.”

So in the end it was Mr. Bannister who was discovered and Steve who made the entrance. And, as Steve pointed out to Kirk later, it just made all the difference.

The effect of the change on Steve was to make him almost rollicking in his manner, as if he and Mr. Bannister were the nucleus of an Old Home Week celebration or two old college chums meeting after long absence. Nervousness, on the rare occasions when he suffered from it, generally had that effect on him.

He breezed into the library, carrying the wheelbarrow, the box of bricks, and the dying pig, and trailing William in his wake. William’s grandfather was seated with his back to the door, dictating a letter to one of his secretaries.

He looked up as Steve entered. He took in Steve and William in a rapid glance and guessed the latter’s identity in an instant. He had expected something of this sort ever since he had heard of his grandson’s birth. Indeed, he had been somewhat surprised that the visit had not occurred before.

He betrayed no surprise.

===Chinese===

于是到头来，「被看见」的成了班尼斯特先生，而进场开腔的反倒是史蒂夫。正如史蒂夫事后向柯克指出的，这一来一去，差别可就大了。

这番变故落在史蒂夫身上，效果是使他的做派几乎称得上手舞足蹈，仿佛他和班尼斯特先生是一场返乡同乐会的核心人物，或是久别重逢的两个老同学。紧张感——他难得受它光顾——一旦沾上，通常对他都有这般效用。

他一阵风似的进了书房，手里拎着独轮车、积木盒和垂死猪，身后拖着威廉。威廉的外祖父背对房门坐着，正对一位秘书口授信函。

史蒂夫进门时，他抬起眼来。一道急速的目光扫过史蒂夫和威廉，立时猜中了后者的身份。自打听说外孙降生，他便料到会有这么一出。说实在的，这一趟迟迟不来，他反倒有些纳闷。

他未露半分惊讶。

「稍等，丁格尔。」他说罢，又转向秘书。一丝淡淡的冷笑在他脸上一掠而过。

===Original===

“One moment, Dingle,” he said, and turned to the secretary again. A faint sneer came and went on his face.

The delay completed Steve’s discomfiture. He placed the wheel harrow on the floor, the box of bricks on the wheelbarrow, and the dying pig on the box of bricks, whence it was instantly removed and inflated by William.

“ ‘Referring to your letter of the eighth﻿—’ ” said Mr. Bannister in his cold, level voice.

He was interrupted by the incisive cry of the dying pig.

“Ask your son to be quiet, Dingle,” he said impassively.

Steve was staggered.

===Chinese===

这一耽搁，把史蒂夫的窘态补了个十足。他把独轮车搁在地板上，把积木盒搁在独轮车上，把垂死猪搁在积木盒上——转眼便被威廉取下来吹胀了。

「『关于贵方八日的来函——』」班尼斯特先生用他那冷而平直的嗓音说道。

他的口授被垂死猪一声尖利的啼叫打断。

「请你的儿子安静些，丁格尔。」他不动声色地说。

史蒂夫懵住了。

「我说，这可不是我儿子，老爷，」他随口开腔道。

===Original===

“Say, this ain’t my son, squire,” he began breezily.

“Your nephew, then, or whatever relation he happens to be to you.”

He resumed his dictation. Steve wiped his forehead and looked helplessly at the White Hope, who, having discarded the dying pig, was now busy with the box of bricks.

Steve wished he had not come. He was accustomed to the primitive exhibition of emotions, having moved in circles where the wrathful expressed their wrath in a normal manner.

Anger which found its expression in an exaggerated politeness was out of his line and made him uncomfortable.

After what seemed to him a century, John Bannister dismissed the secretary. Even then, however, he did not come immediately to Steve. He remained for a few moments writing, with his back turned. Then, just when Steve had given up hope of ever securing his attention, he turned suddenly.

===Chinese===

「我说，这可不是我儿子，老爷，」他随口开腔道。

「那便是你的侄子，或者管他与你是什么亲戚关系。」

他继续口授。史蒂夫抹了把额头，一筹莫展地望着「白人希望」——后者已撇下垂死猪，正忙着摆弄积木盒。

史蒂夫懊悔不该来。他见惯了直截了当的情感表露，因为他出没的那些圈子里，动怒的人发怒都发得规规矩矩。

那种以夸张的客气来表情达意的愤怒，超出他的行当，令他浑身不自在。

挨过了在他感觉里足有一个世纪的时间，约翰·班尼斯特才打发走秘书。然而即便那时，他也没有立刻转向史蒂夫，仍旧背过身去写了片刻。直到史蒂夫对博得他的注意已彻底不抱希望，他才猛然转过身来。

===Original===

“Well?”

“Say, it’s this way, colonel,” Steve had begun, when a triumphant cry from the direction of the open window stopped him. The White Hope was kneeling on a chair, looking down into the street.

“Bix,” he explained over his shoulder.

“Kindly ring the bell, Dingle,” said Mr. Bannister, unmoved. “Your little nephew appears to have dropped his bricks into Fifth Avenue.”

In answer to the summons Keggs appeared. He looked anxious.

“Keggs,” said Mr. Bannister, “tell one of the footmen to go out into the avenue and pick up some wooden bricks which he will find there. Dingle’s little brother has let some fall.”

===Chinese===

「有何见教？」

「我说，是这么回事，上校，」史蒂夫刚起了个头，敞开的窗户那方向一声得意的欢呼便把他截住了。「白人希望」正跪在一把椅子上，俯瞰着街面。

「积积。」他扭过头来解释道。

「劳驾按铃，丁格尔。」班尼斯特先生不为所动地说。「你的小侄子似乎把他的积木掉到第五大道上去了。」

铃声一唤，凯格斯应声而至。他面有忧色。

「凯格斯，」班尼斯特先生说，「吩咐一个男仆到大道上去，把他将在那儿发现的几块木制积木捡回来。丁格尔的小弟弟掉了些下去。」

===Original===

As Keggs left the room Steve’s pent-up nervousness exploded in a whirl of words.

“Aw say, boss, quit yer kiddin’. You know this kid ain’t anything to do with me. Why, say, how would he be any relation of a roughneck like me? Come off the roof, bo. You know well enough who he is. He’s your grandson. On the level.”

Mr. Bannister looked at William, now engaged in running the wheelbarrow up and down the room, emitting the while a curious sound, possibly to encourage an imaginary horse. The inspection did not seem to excite him or afford him any pleasure.

“Oh!” he said.

Steve was damped, but resumed gamely:

===Chinese===

凯格斯一离开房间，史蒂夫憋了许久的紧张便化作一连串旋风似的话头炸了开来。

「嗐，我说，老板，别逗闷子了。您明知这小家伙跟我半点不沾边。再说了，他怎么会是我这么个粗人的什么亲戚？快别打马虎眼啦，老兄。他是谁，您心里明白得很。他是您的外孙。句句是实话。」

班尼斯特先生看了看威廉——他此刻正推着独轮车在屋里来回奔忙，嘴里同时发出一种古怪的声响，多半是在给一匹假想中的马鼓劲。这一番检阅似乎既未令他激动，也未给他丝毫愉快。

「哦！」他说。

史蒂夫像被兜头浇了盆冷水，但仍鼓起勇气接着说：

===Original===

“Say, boss, this is the greatest kid on earth. I’m not stringing you, honest. He’s a wonder. On the level, did you ever see a kid that age with a pair of shoulders on him like what this kid’s got? Say, squire, what’s the matter with calling the fight off and starting fair? Miss Ruth would be tickled to death if you would. Can the rough stuff, colonel. I know you think you’ve been given a raw deal, Kirk chipping in like that and copping off Miss Ruth, but for the love of Mike, what does it matter? You seen for yourself what a dandy kid this is. Well, then, check your grouch with your hat. Do the square thing. Have out the auto and come right round to the studio and make it up. What’s wrong with that, colonel? Honest, they’d be tickled clean through.”

At this point Keggs entered, followed by a footman carrying wooden bricks.

“Keggs,” said Mr. Bannister, “telephone for the automobile at once﻿—”

“That’s the talk, colonel,” cried Steve joyfully. “I know you were a sport.”

===Chinese===

「我说，老板，这是天底下头一号的好孩子。我不诓您，真的。他是个奇才。凭实说，这岁数的孩子，您可曾见过谁长着他这样一副肩膀？喂，老爷，把这场拳赛取消、清清爽爽重新开局，如何？您若肯，露丝小姐准会乐上天去。收起那副狠劲儿吧，上校。我知道您觉得自己挨了一记闷棍——柯克横插一杠，把露丝小姐娶走了——可看在老天的份上，这又打什么紧？这孩子多么出挑，您亲眼见了。那么，就把您那股怨气跟帽子一道寄存了吧。行件方正事。把汽车唤出来，这就上画室去，与他们言归于好。这有什么不妥，上校？说真的，他们准会从里到外乐个通透。」

正说到这里，凯格斯进来了，身后跟着一个捧着木制积木的男仆。

「凯格斯，」班尼斯特先生说，「马上打电话叫汽车——」

「这就对啦，上校！」史蒂夫喜不自胜地喊道。「我早知道您是条痛快的好汉。」

===Original===

“﻿—﻿—﻿to take me down to Wall Street.”

Keggs bowed.

“Oh Keggs,” said Mr. Bannister, as he turned to leave.

“Sir?”

“Another thing. See that Dingle does not enter the house again.”

And Mr. Bannister resumed his writing, while Steve, gathering up the wheelbarrow, the box of bricks, and the dying pig, took William by the hand and retreated.

===Chinese===

「——好送我上华尔街去。」

凯格斯躬身领命。

「哦，凯格斯。」班尼斯特先生转身欲行时说。

「先生？」

「还有一件事。务必别让丁格尔再进这座宅子。」

班尼斯特先生于是重新伏案写字，而史蒂夫收拢起独轮车、积木盒和垂死猪，牵起威廉的手，撤退而去。

---

===Original===

That terminated Ruth’s attempts to conciliate her father.

There remained Bailey. From Bailey she was prepared to stand no nonsense. Meeting him on the street, she fairly kidnapped him, driving him into a taxicab and pushing him into the studio, where he was confronted by his nephew.

Bailey came poorly through the ordeal. William Bannister, a stern critic, weighed him up in one long stare, found him wanting, and announced his decision with all the strength of powerful lungs. In the end he had to be removed, hiccupping, and Bailey, after lingering a few uneasy moments making conversation to Kirk, departed, with such a look about the back of him as he sprang into his cab that Ruth felt that the visit was one which would not be repeated.

She went back into the studio with a rather heavy heart. She was fond of Bailey.

The sight of Kirk restored her. After all, what had happened was only what she had expected. She had chosen her path, and she did not regret it.

===Chinese===

露丝与父亲修好的种种尝试，就此告终。

剩下的还有贝利。对付贝利，她早打定主意，决不容他耍半点花腔。她在街上撞见了他，索性干了一票绑架：把他塞进出租车，再推进画室，让他与自己的外甥直面相对。

贝利这场考验考得甚是狼狈。威廉·班尼斯特这位严苛的批评家，长盯一眼便把他掂量了个透，认定他不合格，随即以强劲肺力的全部声势宣布了这项裁决。到头来，只得把嗝儿连声的他抱走了事；贝利又局促不安地捱了片刻，同柯克勉强扯了几句闲话，便告辞而去——他纵身跳上马车时背影里透出的那副神气，叫露丝断定，这类拜访是不会有第二回的了。

她回到画室里，心头颇为沉重。她是喜欢贝利的。

一见到柯克，她便宽慰复原了。毕竟，发生的一切不过正合她的预料。路是她自己拣的，她并不后悔。

## XVI The White Hope Link / 第十六章 「白人希望」的纽带

===Original===

The White Hope slept. The noise of the departing car, which had roused the birds, had made no impression on him. As Steve had said, dynamite could not do it. He slumbered on, calmly detached, unaware of the remarkable changes which, in the past twenty-four hours, had taken place in his life. An epoch had ended and a new one begun, but he knew it not.

And probably, if Kirk and Ruth, who were standing at his bedside, watching him, had roused him and informed him of these facts, he would have displayed little excitement. He had the philosophical temperament. He took things as they came. Great natural phenomena, like Lora Delane Porter, he accepted as part of life. When they were in his life, he endured them stoically. When they went out of it, he got on without them. Marcus Aurelius would have liked William Bannister Winfield. They belonged to the same school of thought.

The years have a tendency to destroy this placidity towards life and to develop in man a sense of gratitude to fate for its occasional kindnesses; and Kirk, having been in the world longer than William Bannister, did not take the gifts of the gods so much for granted. He was profoundly grateful for what had happened. That Lora Delane Porter should have retired from active interference with his concerns was much; but that he should have had the incredible good fortune to be freed from the burden of John Bannister’s money was more.

If ever money was the root of all evil, this had been. It had come into his life like a poisonous blight, withering and destroying wherever it touched. It had changed Ruth; it had changed William Bannister; it had changed himself; it was as if the spirit of the old man had lived on, hating him and working him mischief. He always had superstitious fear of it; and events had proved him right.

===Chinese===

「白人希望」睡着了。汽车离去时的声响惊起了群鸟，对他却未起丝毫作用。正如史蒂夫说过的，就是炸药也办不到。他继续酣睡，安然物外，浑然不觉过去二十四小时里他的生活中发生了何等惊人的变故。一个时代结束了，一个新的时代开始了，他却毫不知情。

而且多半，倘若守在床边望着他的柯克和露丝把他唤醒，把这桩桩事实讲给他听，他也不会表现出多少兴奋来。他天生一副哲人气性。凡事来了什么便受什么。对洛拉·德莱恩·波特这类伟大的自然现象，他当作人生的一部分接受下来。它们在他的生活里时，他坚忍地承受；它们离开时，他照样过活。马可·奥勒留（Marcus Aurelius）见了威廉·班尼斯特·温菲尔德准会喜欢。他们同属一个思想门派。

岁月惯会磨蚀这种对待人生的恬淡，而教人对命运偶尔的仁慈生出感激之心；柯克在这个世界上活得比威廉·班尼斯特久，便不曾把诸神的馈赠全然视作理所当然。他对已发生的一切深怀感激。洛拉·德莱恩·波特从此退出、不再积极插手他的事务，这已是莫大的幸事；而他竟有这般不可思议的好运，得以卸下约翰·班尼斯特那笔钱的重负，那就更是莫大的幸事了。

若说金钱真是万恶之源，这一笔便是明证。它进入他的生活，像一场毒疫，所触之处尽皆枯萎凋残。它改变了露丝，改变了威廉·班尼斯特，也改变了他自己；仿佛老头儿的阴魂不散，恨着他，与他作祟为祸。他一向对它怀有迷信般的恐惧，而事态证明他恐惧得有理。

===Original===

And now the cloud had rolled away. A few crowded hours of Bailey’s dashing imbecility had removed the curse forever.

He was alone with Ruth and his son in a world that contained only them, just as in the old days of their happiness. There was something symbolic, something suggestive of the beginning of a new order of things, in their isolation at this very moment. Steve had gone. Only he and Ruth and the child were left.

The child﻿—the White Hope﻿—he was the real hero of the story, the real principal of the drama of their three lives. He was the link that bound them together, the force that worked for coherence and against chaos. He stood between them, his hands in theirs; and while he did so there could be no parting of the ways. His grip was light, but as strong as steel. Time would bring troubles, moods, misunderstandings, for they were both human; but, while that grip held, there could be no gulf dividing Ruth and himself, as it had divided them in the past.

He faced the future calmly, with open eyes. It would be rough going at first, very rough going. It meant hard work, incessant work. No more vague masterpieces which might or might not turn into *Carmen* or *The Spanish Maiden*. No more delightful idle days to be loafed through in the studio or the shops. No more dreams, seen hazily through the smoke of a cigar, as he lay on the couch and stared at the ceiling, of what he would do tomorrow. Tomorrow must look after itself. His business was with the present and the work of the present.

===Chinese===

而如今，乌云散尽了。贝利那番雷厉风行的愚行，短短几个钟头，便把这诅咒永远解除了。

他与露丝和儿子单独在一起，处在一个只装得下他们三人的世界里，一如当年他们幸福的旧时光。此时此刻他们的这份孤立，颇有一种象征的意味，暗示着一种新秩序的开端。史蒂夫走了。只剩下他、露丝和孩子。

这孩子——「白人希望」——他才是这个故事里真正的英雄，他们三人人生这出戏里真正的主角。他是把两人联结在一起的纽带，是为凝聚效力、与涣散作对的力量。他站在他们中间，两只手各握在一只大手里；只要他这样站着，他们之间就不会有分道扬镳的可能。他的手握得轻轻的，却坚如钢铁。岁月自会带来烦恼、心绪、误会，因为他们两个都是凡人；但只要这一握不曾松开，露丝与他之间便不可能再有鸿沟横亘，像过去那样把他们隔开。

他睁着双眼，平静地直面未来。起初的路会很难走，非常难走。那意味着苦干，无休无止的苦干。再没有那些含糊其辞的杰作了——那种也许会、也许不会变成*《卡门》*或*《西班牙少女》*的东西。再没有可以在画室里、商店里闲混过去的美妙的偷闲之日。再没有躺在长沙发上盯着天花板、隔着雪茄的烟雾依稀望见的、明天要如何如何的梦。明天自会照料它自己。他要管的只是当下，只是当下的工作。

===Original===

He braced himself to the fight, confident of his power to win. He had found himself.

Bill stirred in his sleep and muttered. Ruth bent over him and kissed the honourable scratch on his cheek.

“Poor little chap! You’ll wake up and find that you aren’t a millionaire baby after all! I wonder if you’ll mind. Kirk, do *you* mind?”

===Chinese===

他振作精神迎向这一战，自信有力取胜。他已经找到了自己。

比尔在睡梦中动了动，咕哝了一声。露丝俯下身去，吻了吻他脸颊上那道光荣的抓痕。

「可怜的小家伙！等你一觉醒来，会发现自己到底不是百万富翁宝宝了！不知你会不会在意。柯克，*你*在意吗？」

===Original===

“Mind!”

“I don’t,” said Ruth. “I think it will be rather fun being poor again.”

“Who’s poor?” said Kirk stoutly. “I’m not. I’ve got you and I’ve got Bill. Do you remember﻿—ages ago﻿—what that Vince girl, the model, you know, said that her friend had called me? A plute. That’s me. I’m the richest man in the world.”

===Chinese===

「在意！」

「我不在意，」露丝说。「我倒觉得，重新穷起来会挺有意思呢。」

「谁穷？」柯克理直气壮地说。「我不穷。我有你，还有比尔。你还记得吗——很久以前——那个文斯姑娘，你知道的，就是那个模特儿，说她朋友管我叫什么来着？阔佬。说的就是我。我是世界上最有钱的人。」

## IV The Widening Gap / 第四章 渐行渐远

===Original===

The new life hit Kirk as a wave hits a bather; and, like a wave, swept him off his feet, choked him, and generally filled him with a feeling of discomfort.
He should have been prepared for it, but he was not. He should have divined from the first that the money was bound to produce changes other than a mere shifting of headquarters from Sixty-First Street to Fifth Avenue. But he had deluded himself at first with the idea that Ruth was different from other women, that she was superior to the artificial pleasures of the Society which is distinguished by the big S.
In a moment of weakness, induced by hair-ruffling, he had given in on the point of the hygienic upbringing of William Bannister; but there, he had imagined, his troubles were to cease. He had supposed that he was about to resume the old hermit’s-cell life of the studio and live in a world which contained only Ruth, Bill, and himself.
He was quickly undeceived. Within two days he was made aware of the fact that Ruth was in the very centre of the social whirlpool and that she took it for granted that he would join her there. There was nothing of the hermit about Ruth now. She was amazingly undomestic.
Her old distaste for the fashionable life of New York seemed to have vanished absolutely. As far as Kirk could see, she was always entertaining or being entertained. He was pitched headlong into a world where people talked incessantly of things which bored him and did things which seemed to him simply mad. And Ruth, whom he had thought he understood, revelled in it all.
At first he tried to get at her point of view, to discover what she found to enjoy in this lunatic existence of aimlessness and futility. One night, as they were driving home from a dinner which had bored him unspeakably, he asked the question point-blank. It seemed to him incredible that she could take pleasure in an entertainment which had filled him with such depression.

===Chinese===

新生活扑向柯克，就像巨浪扑向戏水的人；而且同巨浪一样，把他掀了个底朝天，呛得他透不过气来，总之叫他浑身不自在。
他本该对此有所准备，可他没有。他本该从一开始就料到：这笔钱注定带来的变化，绝不仅仅是大本营从第六十一街搬到第五大道而已。可他起初一直哄骗自己，认定露丝与别的女人不同，认定她超然于那个凭首字母大写来彰显身份的「社交界」（Society）的种种虚浮享乐之外。
有一阵子——在被揉乱头发揉得心软的一刻——他在威廉·班尼斯特的卫生保健教养问题上让了步；但他原以为，麻烦到此为止。他还以为，自己即将重回画室里那种隐士斗室般的旧日生活，在一个只装得下露丝、比尔和他自己的世界里过日子。
幻灭来得很快。不出两天，他便明白了：露丝正处在这社交漩涡的正中心，而且她理所当然地认定他会陪她一道待在那儿。如今的露丝身上再没有半点隐士气。她不爱居家，不爱到了惊人的地步。
她对纽约时髦生活的旧日厌憎，似乎消失得干干净净。在柯克看来，她不是在宴客，就是在赴宴。他自己则被一头掼进一个世界，那里的人们没完没了地谈论着令他厌倦的事，做着在他看来简直是发疯的事。而露丝——这个他自以为了解的露丝——却对这一切乐在其中。
起初他还试着去理解她的立场，想弄明白这种既无目标又无意义的疯子生涯里，究竟有什么可让她享受的。一天晚上，他们赴宴归来——那场宴席让他厌烦得无以复加——汽车一开动，他便单刀直入地提出了问题。一个让他满心沮丧的消遣，她竟能从中取乐，在他看来简直不可思议。

===Original===

“Ruth,” he said impulsively, as the car moved off, “what do you see in this sort of thing? How can you stand these people? What have you in common with them?”
“Poor old Kirk. I know you hated it tonight. But we shan’t be dining with the Baileys every night.”
Bailey Bannister had been their host on that occasion, and the dinner had been elaborate and gorgeous. Mrs. Bailey was now one of the leaders of the younger set. Bailey, looking much more than a year older than when Kirk had seen him last, had presided at the head of the table with great dignity, and the meeting with him had not contributed to the pleasure of Kirk’s evening.
“Were you awfully bored? You seemed to be getting along quite well with Sybil.”
“I like her. She’s good fun.”
“She’s certainly having good fun. I’d give anything to know what Bailey really thinks of it. She is the most shockingly extravagant little creature in New York. You know the Wilburs were quite poor, and poor Sybil was kept very short. I think that marrying Bailey and having all this money to play with has turned her head.”

===Chinese===

「露丝，」汽车开动时，他冲口而出，「你到底看上这种生活哪一点了？这些人你怎么受得了的？你跟他们有什么共同之处？」
「可怜的柯克。我知道你今晚受罪了。可我们也不会天天晚上都在贝利家吃饭呀。」
那晚做东的正是贝利·班尼斯特，席面极尽铺张华丽。贝利太太如今已是青年社交圈里的领衔人物之一。贝利本人——看上去比柯克上次见到时苍老了一年不止——气度俨然地端坐主位主持大局，而这次重逢，实在无补于柯克当晚的兴致。
「闷坏了吧？我看你跟西比尔处得挺不错嘛。」
「我喜欢她。她很有趣。」
「她确实玩得尽兴。要我拿什么都行，只求知道贝利心里到底怎么想。她是全纽约最骇人听闻的挥霍小东西。你知道的，威尔伯家原本相当穷，可怜的西比尔手头一直很紧。我看是嫁给贝利、又有这么大一笔钱可供摆弄，把她乐昏了头。」

===Original===

It struck Kirk that the criticism applied equally well to the critic.
“She does the most absurd things. She gave a freak dinner when you were away that cost I don’t know how much. She is always doing something. Well, I suppose Bailey knows what he is about; but at her present pace she must be keeping him busy making money to pay for all her fads. You ought to paint a picture of Bailey, Kirk, as the typical patient American husband. You couldn’t get a better model.”
“Suggest it to him, and let me hide somewhere where I can hear what he says. Bailey has his own opinion of my pictures.”
Ruth laughed a little nervously. She had always wondered exactly what had taken place that day in the studio, and the subject was one which she was shy of exhuming. She turned the conversation.
“What did you ask me just now? Something about﻿—”
“I asked you what you had in common with these people.”

===Chinese===

柯克忽然觉得，这番批评用在批评者本人身上，也同样贴切。
「她尽干荒唐事。你不在的时候，她办了一场别出心裁的怪宴，花了多少钱我都不敢想。她就没个消停的时候。唉，我猜贝利自己心里有数；可照她眼下这个花法，他准得忙个半死地挣钱，才够付她那些心血来潮的玩意儿。柯克，你真该画一幅贝利，就画那种典型的、有耐心的美国丈夫。再找不到比他更好的模特了。」
「你向他提提看，再让我找个地方躲起来，听听他怎么说。贝利对我的画自有他的一套看法。」
露丝笑得有点不自然。那天画室里到底发生了什么，她一直想弄个明白，而这个题目她又不敢去刨根问底。于是她把话岔开了。
「你刚才问我什么来着？好像是关于——」
「我问你，你跟这些人有什么共同之处。」

===Original===

Ruth reflected.
“Oh, well, it’s rather difficult to say if you put it like that. They’re just people, you know. They are amusing sometimes. I used to know most of them. I suppose that is the chief thing which brings us together. They happen to be there, and if you’re travelling on a road you naturally talk to your fellow travellers. But why? Don’t you like them? Which of them didn’t you like?”
It was Kirk’s turn to reflect.
“Well, that’s hard to answer, too. I don’t think I actively liked or disliked any of them. They seemed to me just not worth while. My point is, rather, why are we wasting a perfectly good evening mixing with them? What’s the use? That’s my case in a nutshell.”
“If you put it like that, what’s the use of anything? One must do something. We can’t be hermits.”

===Chinese===

露丝沉吟起来。
